## V A Fantasy of Fear / 五 恐惧的幻想

===Original===
The satyr beside Pat was leaning toward her; the arm about her was tightening with a brutal ruthlessness, and while still staring in fascination at the incredible eyes, she realized that another arm and a white hand was moving relentlessly, exploratively, toward her body. It was the cold touch of this hand as it slipped over her silk-sheathed legs that broke the chilling spell of her fascination.

“Nick!” she screamed. “Nick!” She had a curious sensation of calling him back from far distances, the while she strove with both hands and all her strength to press him back from her. But the ruthless force of his arms was overcoming her resistance; she saw the red eyes a hand’s breadth from her own.

“Nick!” she sobbed in terror.

There was a change. Abruptly, she was looking into Nick’s eyes, bloodshot, frightened, puzzled, but indubitably Nick’s eyes. The flaming orbs of the demon were no more; it was as if they had receded into Nick’s head. The arm about her body relaxed, and they were staring at each other in a medley of consternation, amazement and unbelief. The youth drew back, huddled in his corner of the car, and Pat, breathing in sobs, smoothed out her rumpled apparel with a convulsive movement.

===Chinese===
帕特身边的羊怪正朝她倾过身来；环着她的那条手臂带着残暴的狠劲越收越紧，而她仍旧出神似的盯着那双令人难以置信的眼睛，与此同时意识到另一条手臂和一只白皙的手正冷酷地、探察似的向她的身体摸来。正是这只手滑过她丝袜裹着的双腿时的冰冷触感，才惊破了她身上那股寒意森森的迷咒。

“尼克！”她尖叫起来。“尼克！”她有种奇异的感觉，仿佛正把他从遥远的地方唤回来，同时又用双手拼尽全力想把他从自己身上推开。可他双臂那股无情的蛮力正压过她的抵抗；她看见那双红眼睛离她自己的一双只剩一掌之遥。

“尼克！”她惊恐地啜泣道。

变化来了。她猛然发觉自己望进的是尼克的眼睛——布满血丝、惊惶、困惑，却千真万确是尼克的眼睛。恶魔那对烈焰般的眼球已无影无踪，仿佛缩回了尼克的头颅深处。环着她身体的手臂松了下来，两人面面相觑，惊愕、骇异与难以置信搅作一团。青年缩了回去，蜷在车厢里他那一侧的角落；帕特抽噎着喘气，一阵痉挛似的抚平了自己弄皱的衣裳。

===Original===
“Pat!” he gasped. “Oh, my God! He couldn’t have﻿—” He paused abruptly. The girl gazed at him without reply.

“Pat, Dear,” he spoke in a low, tense murmur, “I’m﻿—sorry. I don’t know﻿—I don’t understand how﻿—”

“Never mind,” she said, regaining a vestige of her customary composure. “It’s﻿—all right, Nick.”

“But﻿—oh, Pat﻿—!”

“It was that near accident,” she said. “That upset you﻿—both of us, I mean.”

“Yes!” he said eagerly. “That’s what it was, Pat. It must have been that, but Dear, can you forgive? Do you want to forgive me?”

===Chinese===
“帕特！”他倒吸一口气。“哦，上帝！他不可能——”他猛地打住。姑娘凝视着他，没有作答。

“帕特，亲爱的，”他低声而紧张地喃喃道，“对不起——我不知道——我不明白怎么会——”

“别放在心上，”她说，寻回了一丝平日的镇定。“没——没事的，尼克。”

“可是——哦，帕特——！”

“是刚才那场险些撞上的事故，”她说，“那让你乱了方寸——我是说，我们俩都乱了。”

“对！”他急切地说，“就是这么回事，帕特。一定是那个缘故。可是亲爱的，你能原谅吗？你愿意原谅我吗？”

===Original===
“It’s all right,” she repeated. “After all, you just complimented my legs, and I guess I can stand that. It’s happened before, only not quite so﻿—convincingly!”

“You’re sweet, Pat!”

“No; I just love you Nick.” She felt a sudden pity for the misery in his face. “Kiss me, Nick﻿—only gently.”

He pressed his lips to hers, very lightly, almost timidly. She lay back against the seat for a moment, her eyes closed.

“That’s you again,” she murmured. “This other﻿—wasn’t.”

===Chinese===
“没事的，”她重复道，“说到底，你不过是称赞了我的腿，我想这点我还受得住。这种事从前也有过，只是从没这么——情真意切！”

“你真可爱，帕特！”

“不；我只是爱你，尼克。”望着他脸上的痛苦，她忽然心生怜惜。“吻我吧，尼克——只要温柔些。”

他把唇贴上她的唇，非常轻，轻得近乎羞怯。她向后靠进座椅，闭了一会儿眼睛。

“这才是你，”她喃喃道，“先前那个——不是。”

===Original===
“Please, Pat! Don’t refer to it﻿—not ever.”

“But it wasn’t you, Nick. It was just the strain of that narrow escape. I don’t hold it against you.”

“You’re﻿—Lord, Pat, I don’t deserve you. But you know that I﻿—I myself﻿—could never touch you except in tenderness, even in reverence. You’re too dainty, too lovely, too spirited, to be hurt, or to be held roughly, against your will. You know I feel that way about you, don’t you?”

“Of course. It was nothing, Nick. Forget it.”

“If I can,” he said somberly. He switched on the engine, backed out upon the pavement, and turned the car toward the glow that marked Chicago. Neither of them spoke as the machine hummed over the arching bridge and down the slope, where, so few minutes before, the threat of accident had thrust itself at them.

“We won’t see a moon tonight,” said Pat in a small voice, after an interval. “We’ll never check up on Dr. Carl’s astronomy.”

===Chinese===
“求你了，帕特！别提这件事——永远别提。”

“可那不是你，尼克。那不过是死里逃生后的紧张失态。我不会记你的仇。”

“你真是——天哪，帕特，我配不上你。可你知道，我——我本人——绝不可能带着别的什么碰你，只有温柔，甚至只有敬重。你太娇美、太可爱、太有灵性，不该受伤害，也不该在违背你心意时被人粗暴地搂住。你知道我对你是这份心，对不对？”

“当然。没什么大不了的，尼克。忘了吧。”

“只要我办得到，”他阴郁地说。他发动引擎，倒车驶上路面，把车头对准标出芝加哥所在的那片光晕。汽车嗡嗡驶过高拱的桥、驶下坡道——就在几分钟前，车祸的威胁正是在那里猝然朝他们逼来。两人谁也没有说话。

“今晚看不到月亮了，”隔了一阵，帕特小声说。“永远没法验证卡尔医生的天文学喽。”

===Original===
“You don’t want to tonight, Pat, do you?”

“I guess perhaps we’d better not,” she replied. “We’re both upset, and there’ll be other nights.”

Again they were silent. Pat felt strained, shaken; there was something uncanny about the occurrence that puzzled her. The red eyes that had glared out of Nick’s face perplexed her, and the curious rasping voice he had used still sounded inhumanly in her memory. Out of recollection rose still another mystery.

“Nick,” she said, “what did you mean﻿—then﻿—when you said there was danger and you came to save me?”

“Nothing,” he said sharply.

===Chinese===
“你今晚也不想看了，对吗，帕特？”

“我看我们最好还是别去，”她答道，“我们俩都心绪不宁，往后还有的是夜晚呢。”

两人再度沉默。帕特觉得紧绷绷的，心有余悸；这桩事里有一种说不出的诡秘让她纳闷。从尼克脸上瞪视出来的那双红眼睛令她百思不解，他当时那副刺耳的怪嗓音至今仍不似人声地回响在她记忆里。回忆中又升起另一重谜。

“尼克，”她说，“刚才——你说有危险、赶来救我的时候，到底是什么意思？”

“没什么，”他口气很冲地说。

===Original===
“And then, afterwards, you started to say something about ‘He couldn’t have﻿—’. Who’s ‘he’?”

“It meant nothing, I tell you. I was frantic to think you might have been hurt. That’s all.”

“I believe you, Honey,” she said, wondering whether she really did. The thing was beginning to grow hazy; already it was assuming merely the proportions of an upheaval of youthful fervor. Such occurrences were not unheard of, though never before had it happened to Patricia Lane! Still, even that was conceivable, far more conceivable than the dark, unformed, inchoate suspicions she had been harboring. They hadn’t even been definite enough to be called suspicions; indefinite apprehensions came closer.

And yet﻿—that strange, wild face that had formed itself of Nick’s fine features, and the terrible red eyes! Were they elements in a picture conjured out of her own imagination? They must be, of course. She had been frightened by that hairbreadth escape, and had seen things that didn’t exist. And the rest of it﻿—well, that might be natural enough. Still, there was something﻿—she knew that; Nick had admitted it.

Horker’s words concerning Nick’s father rose in her mind. Suspected of being crazy! Was that it? Was that the cause of Nick’s curious reluctance where she was concerned? Was the face that had glared at her the visage of a maniac? It couldn’t be. It couldn’t be, she told herself fiercely. Not her fine, tender, sensitive Nick! And besides, that face, if she hadn’t imagined it, had been the face, not of a lunatic, but of a devil. She shook her head, as if to deny her thoughts, and placed her hand impulsively on Nick’s.

===Chinese===
“还有，后来你起了个头，说什么‘他不可能——’。那个‘他’是谁？”

“毫无意义，我告诉你。一想到你也许会受伤，我都急疯了。就这么些。”

“我相信你，亲爱的，”她说，心里却在嘀咕自己是不是真信。这件事开始变得朦胧；在她心里，它已经缩成一场青春血气的失态骚动。这种事并非闻所未闻，尽管它此前还从未落到过帕特里夏·莱恩头上！不过连那样都还说得通，远比她一直暗怀的那些阴郁、无形、混沌的疑心说得通。那些念头甚至还没清晰到配称“疑心”；说是不成形的忧惧倒更贴切。

然而——那张在尼克清秀眉目上幻化出来的狰狞狂野的脸，还有那双可怕的红眼睛！它们难道是她自己的想象凭空变出来的图景？当然只能是。她被那千钧一发的险情吓破了胆，看见了子虚乌有的东西。至于其余的——嗯，倒也够自然了。可总有那么点什么——这她知道；尼克自己就承认过。

霍克医生那些关于尼克父亲的话浮上她的心头。涉嫌精神失常！是这么回事吗？这就是尼克在她面前那份古怪拘谨的缘由吗？刚才瞪视她的那张脸，是一张疯子的脸吗？不可能。不可能，她狠狠地告诉自己。绝不是她那个优秀的、温柔的、敏感的尼克！再说，那张脸——倘若不是她想象出来的——也不是疯子的脸，而是魔鬼的脸。她摇了摇头，仿佛要摇掉这些念头，又情不自禁把手搭上尼克的手。

===Original===
“I don’t care,” she said. “I love you, Nick.”

“And I you,” he murmured. “Pat, I’m sorry about spoiling this evening. I’m sorry and ashamed.”

“Never mind, Honey. There’ll be others.”

“Tomorrow?”

“No,” she said. “Mother and I are going out to dinner. And Friday we’re having company.”

===Chinese===
“我不在乎，”她说，“我爱你，尼克。”

“我也爱你，”他低声道，“帕特，今晚给搅成这样，对不起。我又抱歉又惭愧。”

“别放在心上，亲爱的。往后有的是。”

“明天行吗？”

“不行，”她说，“妈妈和我要出门吃晚饭。星期五家里又要请客。”

===Original===
“Really, Pat? You’re not just trying to turn me off gently.”

“Really, Nick. Try asking me for Saturday evening and see!”

“You’re asked, then.”

“And it’s a date.” Then, with a return of her usual insouciance, she added. “If you’re on good behavior.”

“I will be. I promise.”

===Chinese===
“真的吗，帕特？你不会只是想客客气气地打发我吧。”

“真的，尼克。你约我星期六晚上试试看！”

“那可就约定了。”

“一言为定。”随后，她恢复了惯常的洒脱，又补上一句：“前提是你得规规矩矩。”

“我会的。我保证。”

===Original===
“I hope so,” said Pat. An inexplicable sense of foreboding had come over her; despite her self-given assurances, something unnameable troubled her. She gave a mental shrug, and deliberately relegated the unpleasant cogitations to oblivion.

The car turned into Dempster Road; the lights of the teeming roadhouses, dance halls, roadside hamburger and barbecue stands flashed by. There were many cars here; there was no longer any impression of solitude now, in the overflow from the vast city in whose shadow they moved. The incessant flow of traffic gave the girl a feeling of security; these were tangible things about her, and once more the memory of that disturbing occurrence became dim and dreamlike. This was Nick beside her, gentle, intelligent, kind; had he ever been otherwise? It seemed highly unreasonable, a fantasy of fear and the hysteria of the moment.

“Hungry?” asked Nick unexpectedly.

“I could use a barbecue, I guess. Beef.”

===Chinese===
“但愿如此，”帕特说。一阵莫名的凶兆攫住了她；尽管她给自己吃下了定心丸，还是有种说不出的东西让她不安。她在心里耸了耸肩，存心把那些不愉快的盘算丢进遗忘里去。

汽车拐上登普斯特路；人声鼎沸的路边酒馆、舞厅、汉堡摊和烤肉摊的灯火一一闪过。这里车马繁多；身处这座大都会漫溢出来的滚滚车流之中，再无半点孤寂之感。川流不息的车辆给了姑娘一种安全感；四周都是摸得着看得见的事物，那桩恼人往事的记忆重又变得黯淡、恍如梦中。身边这个就是尼克，温柔、聪慧、和善；他难道有过别的样子吗？方才的种种似乎全无道理，不过是一场恐惧的幻想、一时片刻的歇斯底里。

“饿了吧？”尼克忽然问。

“我想来份烤肉。牛肉的。”

===Original===
The car veered to the graveled area before a brightly lit stand. Nick gave the order to an attendant. He chuckled as Pat, with the digestive disregard of youth attacked the greasy combination.

“That’s like a humming bird eating hay!” he said. “Or better, like a leprechaun eating that horse-meat they can for dogs.”

“You might as well discover that I don’t live on honey and rose-petals,” said Pat. “Not even on caviar and terrapin﻿—at least, not exclusively. I leave the dainty palate for Mother to indulge.”

“Which is just as well. Hamburger and barbecue are more easily budgeted.”

“Nicholas,” said the girl, tossing the paper napkin out of the car window, “is that an indirect and very evasive proposal of marriage?”

===Chinese===
汽车一拐，驶进一处灯火通明的摊子前的碎石场地。尼克向伙计点了菜。帕特拿出年轻人满不在乎的肠胃，向那份油腻的大杂烩发起进攻，他瞧着直乐。

“这活像蜂鸟吃干草！”他说，“或者说得更妙，像爱尔兰小矮妖吃他们罐装喂狗的马肉！”

“你不妨顺便弄清楚，我不是靠蜂蜜和玫瑰花瓣过活的，”帕特说，“也不是靠鱼子酱和甲鱼——至少，不全靠。精致讲究的口味，留给妈妈去受用好了。”

“这样正好。汉堡和烤肉更经得起精打细算。”

“尼古拉斯，”姑娘把纸巾扔出车窗，说道，“这算不算一场拐弯抹角、闪烁其词的求婚？”

===Original===
“You know it could be, if you wished it!”

“And do I?” she said, assuming a pensive air. “I wonder. Suppose we say I’ll let you know later.”

“And meanwhile?”

“Oh, meanwhile we can be sort of engaged. Just the way we’ve been.”

“You’re sweet, Pat,” he murmured, as the car edged into the line of traffic. “I don’t know just how to convey my appreciation, but it’s there!”

===Chinese===
“你知道的，只要你愿意，它就可以算！”

“那我愿意吗？”她摆出一副若有所思的样子，“说不准。要不这样，我们就算我以后再告诉你。”

“那眼下呢？”

“哦，眼下嘛，我们可以算是订了婚的那种。就像我们一直以来这样。”

“你真可爱，帕特，”汽车缓缓汇入车流，他喃喃说，“我不知道该怎样表达我的感激——可它实实在在在那儿！”

===Original===
The buildings drew more closely together; the road was suddenly a lighted street, and then, almost without realizing it, they were before Pat’s home. Nick walked beside her to the door; he stood facing her hesitantly.

“Good night, Pat,” he said huskily. He leaned down, kissing her very gently, turned, and departed.

The girl watched him from the open doorway, following the lights of his car until they vanished down the street. Dear, sweet Nick! Then the disturbing memory of that occurrence of the evening returned; she frowned in perplexity as the thought rose. That was all of a piece with the puzzling character of him, and the curious veiled references he’d made. References to what? She didn’t know, couldn’t imagine. Nick had said he didn’t know either, which added still another quirk to the maze.

She thought of Dr. Horker’s words. With the thought, she glanced at his house, adjacent to her own home. A light gleamed in the library; he was still awake. She closed the door behind her, and darted across the narrow strip of lawn to his porch. She rang the bell.

“Good evening, Dr. Carl,” she said as the massive form of Horker appeared. She puckered her lips impudently at him as she slipped by him into the house.

===Chinese===
楼房渐渐密集起来；道路倏然成了一条灯火通明的街道，接着，几乎不知不觉间，他们已到了帕特家门口。尼克陪她走到门前，迟疑地对着她站定。

“晚安，帕特，”他嗓音沙哑地说。他俯下身，十分轻柔地吻了她，转过身，走了。

姑娘站在敞开的门口望着他，目光追着他的车灯，直到它们消失在街尾。亲爱的、可爱的尼克！随即，今晚那桩恼人事的记忆又回来了；念头一起，她困惑地皱起眉。这跟他那叫人捉摸不透的性子、跟他那些遮遮掩掩的古怪影射正是一路货。影射什么？她不知道，也想象不出。尼克说他也不知道，这又给这座迷宫添了一道弯。

她想起霍克医生的话。想到这里，她朝紧挨着自家的那栋房子瞥了一眼。书房里亮着灯；他还没睡。她关上身后的门，一溜烟跑过那窄窄一条草坪，上了他家的门廊。她按响门铃。

“晚上好，卡尔医生，”霍克医生魁梧的身影出现时，她说。她从他身边溜进屋去，还冲他没大没小地撅了撅嘴。

## XV A Modern Mr. Hyde / 十五 现代海德先生

===Original===
“I don’t remember when I first noticed it,” began Nick in a low voice, “but I’m two people. I’m me, the person who’s talking to you now, and I’m﻿—another.”

Pat, looking very pale and serious in the dusky light, said nothing at all. She simply gazed at him silently, without the slightest trace of surprise in her wide dark eyes.

“This is the real me,” proceeded Nick miserably. “The other is an outsider, that has somehow contrived to grow into me. He is different; cold, cruel, utterly selfish, and not exactly﻿—human. Do you understand?”

“Y﻿—Yes,” said the girl, fighting to control her voice. “Sort of.”

===Chinese===
“我不记得最初是什么时候察觉到的，”尼克低声开口，“可我是两个人。一个是现在的我，也就是正在和你说话的这个人；还有一个是——另外一个。”

帕特在昏暗的光线里显得脸色苍白、神情凝重，一句话也没说。她只是默默地凝视着他，那双又大又黑的眼睛里没有丝毫惊讶的痕迹。

“这个才是真正的我，”尼克凄惨地接着说，“另一个是个外来者，不知怎么设法长进了我的身体里。他不一样；冷酷、残忍、极端自私，而且不算是——人。你明白吗？”

“是——是的，”姑娘竭力控制着自己的声音说，“算是明白吧。”

===Original===
“This is a struggle that has continued for a long time,” he pursued. “There were times in childhood when I remember punishments for offenses I never committed, for nasty little meannesses *he* perpetrated. My mother, and after her death, my tutoress, thought I was lying when I tried to explain; they thought I was trying to evade responsibility. After a while I learned not to explain; I learned to accept my punishments doggedly, and to fight this other when he sought dominance.”

“And could you?” asked Pat, her voice frankly quavery. “Could you fight him?”

“I was the stronger; I could win﻿—usually. He slipped into consciousness as wilful, mean little impulses, nasty moods, unreasoning hates and such unpleasant things. But I was always the stronger: I learned to drive him into the background.”

“You said you *were* the stronger,” she mused. “What does that mean, Nick?”

“I’ve always been the stronger; I am now. But recently, Pat﻿—I think it’s since I fell in love with you﻿—the struggle has been on evener terms. I’ve weakened or he’s gained. I have to guard against him constantly; in any moment of weakness he may slip in, as on our ride last week, when we had that near accident. And again Saturday.” He turned appealing eyes on the girl. “Pat, do you believe me?”

===Chinese===
“这是一场旷日持久的争斗，”他继续说道，“小时候有多少回，我因为从没犯过的过错挨罚，为的是他干下的那些卑劣的小坏事。我母亲，还有她去世之后我的女家庭教师，都认为我解释时是在撒谎，认为我想逃避责任。后来我学会了不再解释；学会了倔强地领受惩罚，学会了在他试图占据上风时跟这另一个对抗。”

“那你打得了吗？”帕特问，嗓音明显发颤。“你斗得过他吗？”

“我更强一些；我能赢——通常是能赢。他化作任性的、卑劣的小小冲动，恶劣的心绪，无缘无故的仇恨诸如此类令人不快的东西，溜进我的意识。但我始终是强的那个：我学会了把他压到幕后去。”

“你说你‘过去’是强的那个，”她若有所思，“这是什么意思，尼克？”

“我一直都是强的那个；现在也是。可是近来，帕特——我想是从我爱上你之后——这场争斗变得势均力敌了。要么是我变弱了，要么是他变强了。我不得不时刻提防他；只要有一刻软弱，他就可能溜进来，就像上周我们坐车出去、差点出事的那回。还有星期六。”他把恳求的目光转向姑娘。“帕特，你相信我吗？”

===Original===
“I guess I’ll have to,” she said unhappily. “It﻿—makes things rather hopeless, doesn’t it?”

He nodded dejectedly. “Yes. I’ve always felt that sooner or later I’d win, and drive him away permanently. I’ve felt on the verge of complete victory more than once, but now﻿—” He shook his head doubtfully. “He had never dominated me so entirely until Saturday night﻿—Pat, you don’t know what Hell is like until you’re forced as I was to watch the violation of the being you worship, to stand helpless while a desecration is committed. I’d rather die than suffer it again!”

“Oh!” said the girl faintly. She was thinking of the sorry picture she must have presented as she reeled half-clothed through the alley. “Can you see what﻿—*he* sees?”

“Of course, and think his thoughts. But only when he’s dominant. I don’t know what evil he’s planning now, else I could forestall him, I would have warned you if I could have known.”

===Chinese===
“看来我只能信了，”她闷闷不乐地说，“这——让事情变得相当没有指望了，是吧？”

他沮丧地点点头。“是的。我一向觉得迟早会赢，把他永远赶走。不止一次，我都觉得自己已站在全胜的边缘，可现在——”他迟疑地摇了摇头。“直到星期六晚上之前，他还从不曾那样彻底地主宰过我——帕特，除非你也像我一样，被迫眼睁睁看着你所崇敬的人遭到玷污、在一场亵渎进行时无能为力地站在一旁，否则你不会知道地狱是什么样子。我宁可死，也不愿再受一遍那种罪！”

“哦！”姑娘轻呼了一声。她正想着自己当时衣衫不整、跌跌撞撞穿过小巷的那副狼狈相。“你能看见——他看见的东西吗？”

“当然，还能想他所想。不过只在他占据主导的时候。我不知道他眼下在谋划什么恶事，不然我就能先发制人；我要是能知道，早就警告你了。”

===Original===
“Where is he now?”

“Here,” said Nick somberly. “Here listening to us, knowing what I’m thinking and feeling, laughing at my unhappiness.”

“Oh!” gasped Pat again. She watched her companion doubtfully. Then the memory of Dr. Horker’s diagnosis came to her, and set her wondering. Was this story the figment of an unsettled mind? Was this irrational tale of a fiendish intruder merely evidence that the Doctor was right in his opinion? She was in a maze of uncertainty.

“Nick,” she said, “did you ever try medical help? Did you ever go to a doctor about it?”

“Of course, Pat! Two years ago I went to a famous psychiatrist in New York﻿—you’d know the name if I mentioned it﻿—and told him about the﻿—the case. And he studied me, and he treated me, and psychoanalyzed me, and the net result was just nothing. And finally he dismissed me with the opinion that ‘the whole thing is just a fixed delusion, fortunately harmless!’ Harmless! Bah! But it wasn’t I that did those things, Pat; I had to stand by in horror and watch. It was enough to *drive* me crazy, but it didn’t﻿—quite.”

===Chinese===
“他现在在哪儿？”

“就在这儿，”尼克阴沉地说，“在这儿听着我们说话，知道我在想什么、感受什么，还在嘲笑我的痛苦。”

“哦！”帕特又倒吸一口气。她将信将疑地打量着同伴。这时她想起了霍克医生的诊断，不由得犯了嘀咕。这番话会不会是一个精神失稳者的虚构？这个关于恶魔闯入者的荒诞故事，会不会恰恰证明医生的判断没错？她坠入了迷惘的迷宫。

“尼克，”她说，“你试过求医吗？有没有为这事看过医生？”

“当然试过，帕特！两年前我去纽约找过一位有名的精神病医生——我一提名字你就会知道——跟他讲了这桩——这个病例。他研究我，给我治疗，给我做精神分析，到头来一无所获。最后他打发我走时下了个结论：‘整件事不过是一种固定妄想，所幸无害！’无害！呸！可那些事不是我干的，帕特；我只能恐怖地站在一边看着。这足以把我逼疯——差一点，可还没有。”

===Original===
“But﻿—oh, Nick, what is it? What is this﻿—this outsider? Can’t we fight it somehow?”

“How can anyone except me fight it?”

“Oh, I don’t know!” she wailed miserably. “There must be a way. Doctors claim to know pretty nearly everything; there must be *something* to do.”

“But there isn’t,” he retorted gloomily. “I don’t know any more than you what that thing is, but it’s beyond your doctors. I’ve got to fight it out alone.”

“Nick﻿—” Her voice was suddenly tense. “Are you sure it isn’t some kind of madness? Something tangible like that could perhaps be treated.”

===Chinese===
“可是——哦，尼克，这到底是什么？这个——这个外来者是什么？我们难道没法子跟它斗一斗吗？”

“除了我，谁还能跟它斗？”

“哦，我不知道！”她悲苦地哀声道，“总该有个法子。医生们自称几乎无所不晓；总该有点什么可做的。”

“可就是没有，”他阴郁地反驳，“那东西是什么，我知道的并不比你多，但它不是你们的医生治得了的。我必须独自把这场仗打完。”

“尼克——”她的声音陡然绷紧了。“你敢肯定这不是某种疯狂吗？那样具体明确的病，或许还有得治。”

===Original===
“It’s no kind your doctors can treat, Pat. Did you ever hear of a madman who stood aside and rationally watched the working of his own insanity? And that’s what I’m forced to do. And yet﻿—this other isn’t insane either. Were its actions insane?”

Pat shuddered. “I﻿—don’t know,” she said in low tones. “I guess not.”

“No. Horrible, cruel, bestial, devilishly cunning, evil﻿—but not insane. I don’t know what it is, Pat. I know that the fight has to be made by me alone. There’s nothing, nobody in the world, that can help.”

“Nick!” she wailed.

===Chinese===
“那不是任何医生治得了的疯狂，帕特。你听说过哪个疯子会站在一旁，理智地旁观自己的疯狂发作吗？而这就是我被迫做的事。可是——这另一个也并不疯。它的所作所为像疯子干的吗？”

帕特打了个寒战。“我——不知道，”她低声说，“我想不像。”

“不像。可怕、残忍、兽性、恶毒地狡诈、邪恶——但不疯。我不知道它是什么，帕特。我只知道这场仗必须由我独自去打。世上没有任何东西、任何人帮得上忙。”

“尼克！”她哀声道。

===Original===
“I’m sorry, Pat dear. You understand now why I was so reluctant to fall in love with you. I was afraid to love you; now I know I was right.”

“Nick!” she cried, then paused hopelessly. After a moment she continued, “Yesterday I was determined to forget you, and now﻿—now I don’t care if this whole tale of yours is a mesh of fantastic lies, I love you! I’d love you even if your real self were that﻿—that other creature, and even if I knew that this was just a trap. I’d love you anyway.”

“Pat,” he said seriously, “don’t you believe me? Why should I offer to give you up if this were﻿—what you said? Wouldn’t I be pleading for another chance, making promises, finding excuses?”

“Oh, I believe you, Nick! It isn’t that; I was just thinking how strange it is that I could hate you so two nights past and love you so tonight.”

===Chinese===
“对不起，亲爱的帕特。你现在明白我为什么那么不敢爱上你了。我怕爱上你；现在我知道我没怕错。”

“尼克！”她喊道，随即又绝望地停住。过了一会儿她接着说：“昨天我还下定决心忘了你，可现在——现在我哪怕知道你这套话全是一团荒唐的谎言，我也不在乎，我爱你！哪怕你真正的自我就是那个——那个东西，哪怕我明知这是个圈套，我也爱你。无论如何我都爱你。”

“帕特，”他正色道，“难道你不信我？如果事情真是——你说的那样，我何必主动放弃你？我岂不是该恳求再给一次机会，又许愿又发誓，找种种借口？”

“哦，我信你，尼克！不是那个意思；我只是在想有多奇怪，前天晚上我还那么恨你，今晚却又这么爱你。”

===Original===
“Oh God, Pat! Even you can’t know how much I love you; and to win you and then be forced to give you up﻿—” He groaned.

The girl reached out her hand and covered his; it was the first time during the evening that she had touched him, and the feel of his flesh sent a tingle through her. She was miserably distraught.

“Honey,” she murmured brokenly. “Nick, Honey.”

He looked at her. “Do you suppose there’s a chance to beat the thing?” he asked. “I’d not ask you to wait, Pat, but if I only glimpsed a chance﻿—”

===Chinese===
“哦，上帝，帕特！连你也不知道我有多爱你；好不容易赢得你，却又不得不放弃你——”他呻吟了一声。

姑娘伸出手，覆在他的手上；这是今晚她第一次碰他，他肌肤的触感让她一阵战栗。她心乱如麻，凄楚难当。

“亲爱的，”她断断续续地喃喃道，“尼克，亲爱的。”

他望着她。“你觉得斗赢那东西还有一线希望吗？”他问，“我不会要你等的，帕特，可只要能让我瞥见一丝希望——”

===Original===
“I’ll wait. I don’t think I could do anything else but wait for you.”

“If I only knew what I had to fight!” he whispered. “If I only knew that!”

A sudden memory leaped into Pat’s mind. “Nick,” she said huskily, “I think I know.”

“What do you mean, Pat?”

===Chinese===
“我等。我想除了等你，我什么也做不了。”

“要是我知道要斗的是什么就好了！”他低语，“哪怕只知道这个也好！”

一段记忆蓦地跃入帕特脑海。“尼克，”她嗓音沙哑地说，“我想我知道。”

“你什么意思，帕特？”

===Original===
“It’s something Magda﻿—the cook﻿—said to me. It’s foolish, superstitious, but Nick, what else can it be?”

“Tell me!”

“Well, she was talking to me yesterday, and she said that when she was a child in the old country, she had seen a man once﻿—” she hesitated﻿—“a man who was possessed by a devil. Nick, I think you’re possessed by a devil!”

He stared at her. “Pat,” he said hoarsely, “that’s﻿—an impossibility!”

“I know, but what else can it be?”

===Chinese===
“是玛格达——我们家厨娘——跟我说过的话。很蠢，很迷信，可是尼克，除此之外还能是什么呢？”

“告诉我！”

“是这样的，她昨天跟我说话，讲起她小时候在故国老家见过一个男人——”她迟疑了一下——“一个被魔鬼附身的男人。尼克，我觉得你是被魔鬼附身了！”

他直勾勾地瞪着她。“帕特，”他声音发哑，“那——不可能！”

“我知道，可还能是别的什么？”

===Original===
“Out of the Dark Ages,” he muttered. “An echo of the Black Mass and witchcraft, but﻿—”

“What did they do,” asked the girl, “to people they thought were possessed?”

“Exorcism!” he whispered.

“And how did they﻿—exorcise?”

===Chinese===
“那是黑暗时代的老古董了，”他咕哝道，“黑弥撒和巫术的余响，可是——”

“那时候的人，”姑娘问，“对那些他们认为被附了身的人是怎么处置的？”

“驱魔！”他低声说。

“那他们是怎么——驱的呢？”

===Original===
“I don’t know,” he said in a low voice. “Pat, that’s an impossible idea, but﻿—I don’t know!” he ended.

“We’ll try,” she murmured, still covering his hand with her own. “What else can we do, Nick?”

“What’s done I’ll do alone, Pat.”

“But I want to help!”

“I’ll not let you, Dear. I won’t have you exposed to a repetition of those indignities, or perhaps worse!”

===Chinese===
“我不知道，”他低声说，“帕特，这是个不可能的念头，可是——我说不好！”末了他这样说。

“我们试试吧，”她喃喃道，手仍覆在他的手上，“除此之外还能怎么办，尼克？”

“该做的，我会一个人去做，帕特。”

“可我想帮忙！”

“我不能让你插手，亲爱的。我不会让你再遭受一回那样的凌辱，或者更糟的事！”

===Original===
“I’m not afraid.”

“Then I am, Pat! I won’t have it!”

“But what’ll you do?”

“I’ll go away. I’ll battle the thing through once for all, and I’ll either come back free of it or﻿—” He paused and the girl did not question him further, but sat staring at him with troubled eyes.

===Chinese===
“我不怕。”

“那我怕，帕特！我不许！”

“那你打算怎么做？”

“我会离开。我要跟那东西做个彻底了断，然后要么干干净净地回来，要么——”他停住了，姑娘没有再追问，只是坐在那儿，忧心忡忡地凝望着他。

===Original===
“I won’t write you, Pat,” he continued. “If you should receive a letter from me, burn it﻿—don’t read it. It might be from﻿—the other, a trap or a lure of some sort. Promise me! You’ll promise that, won’t you?”

She nodded; there was a glint of tears in her eyes.

“And I don’t want you to wait, Pat,” he proceeded. “I don’t want you to feel that you have any obligations to me﻿—God knows you’ve nothing to thank me for! When﻿—if I come back and you haven’t changed, then we’ll try again.”

“Nick,” she said in a small voice, “how do you know the﻿—the other won’t come back here? How can you promise for﻿—it?”

===Chinese===
“我不会给你写信的，帕特，”他接着说，“万一你收到我的信，烧掉它——别读。那可能是——另一个写来的，某种圈套或诱饵。答应我！你会答应的，对不对？”

她点了点头；眼里闪着泪光。

“还有，我不要你等，帕特，”他继续说，“我不要你觉得对我负有什么义务——上帝知道，你没什么该谢我的！等到——如果我回来时你还没有变心，那我们就再从头来过。”

“尼克，”她细声细气地说，“你怎么知道那——那另一个不会回到这里来？你怎么能替——它打包票？”

===Original===
“I’m still master!” he said grimly. “I won’t be dominated long enough at any time for that to happen. I’ll fight it down.”

“Then﻿—it’s goodbye?”

He nodded. “But not for always﻿—I hope.”

===Chinese===
“我还是主宰！”他狠声说，“任何一次都不会任他主宰太久，久到让他得逞。我会把他压下去。”

“那么——这就道别了？”

他点点头。“但不会是永别——我希望。”

===Original===
“Nick,” she murmured, “will you kiss me?” She felt a tear on her cheek. “I’ll stand losing you a little better if I can have a﻿—last kiss﻿—to remember.” Her voice was faltering.

His arms were about her. She yielded herself completely to his caress; the park, the crowd passing a few yards away, the people on nearby benches, were all forgotten, and once more she felt herself alone with Nicholas Devine in a vast empty cosmos.

An insistent voice penetrated her consciousness; she realized that it had been calling her name for some seconds.

“Miss Lane,” she heard, and again, “Miss Lane.” A hand tapped her shoulder; with a sudden start, she tore her lips away, and looked up into a face unrecognized for a moment. Then she placed it. It was the visage of Mueller, Dr. Horker’s companion on that disastrous Saturday night.

===Chinese===
“尼克，”她喃喃道，“吻吻我好吗？”她感到一滴泪落在脸颊上。“要是能留下一个——最后的吻——好让我记着，失去你我也能好受一点。”她的声音发颤。

他的双臂环住了她。她全然委身于他的爱抚；公园，几步之外往来的人流，附近长椅上的人们，全都被忘在了脑后，她再一次感到，自己与尼古拉斯·德文独处于浩瀚空旷的宇宙之中。

一个执拗的声音穿透了她的意识；她这才发觉，那声音唤她的名字已有好几秒了。

“莱恩小姐，”她听见，又是一声，“莱恩小姐。”一只手轻拍她的肩膀；她猛然一惊，双唇脱开，抬头对上一张一时认不出的脸。随即她认了出来。那是缪勒的脸——灾难性的那个星期六晚上，霍克医生的同伴。

## VI A Question of Science / 六 一个科学问题

===Original===
“Not that I’m displeased at this visit, Pat,” rumbled the Doctor, seating himself in one of the great chairs by the fireplace, “but I’m curious. I thought you were dating your ideal tonight, yet here you are, back alone a little after eleven. How come?”

“Oh,” said the girl nonchalantly, dropping crosswise in the other chair, “we decided we needed our beauty sleep.”

“Then why are you here, you young imp?”

“Thought you might be lonesome.”

===Chinese===
“倒不是不高兴你来看我，帕特，”医生嗡着嗓门说，在壁炉边一把大扶手椅里坐下，“只是我好奇。我以为你今晚跟你那位意中人约会去了，结果呢，十一点刚过你就一个人回来了。怎么回事？”

“哦，”姑娘漫不经心地说，横着陷进对面那把椅子里，“我们决定回去睡个美容觉。”

“那你还来这儿干什么，小捣蛋鬼？”

“想着你也许寂寞。”

===Original===
“I’ll bet you did! But seriously, Pat, what is it? Any trouble?”

“No-o,” she said dubiously. “No trouble. I just wanted to ask you a few hypothetical questions. About science.”

“Go to it, then, and quickly. I was ready to turn in.”

“Well,” said Pat, “about Nick’s father. He was a doctor, you said, and supposed to be cracked. Was he really?”

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛！说正经的，帕特，到底什么事？有麻烦？”

“没——有，”她迟疑地说，“没麻烦。我只想问你几个假设性的问题。关于科学的。”

“那就问吧，快点。我都准备睡了。”

“是这样，”帕特说，“关于尼克的父亲。你说过他是个医生，还据说脑子不正常。真的吗？”

===Original===
“Humph! That’s curious. I just looked up a brochure of his tonight in the *American Medical Journal*, after our conversation of this afternoon. Why do you ask that?”

“Because I’m interested, of course.”

“Well, here’s what I remember about him, Pat. He was an M.D., all right, but I see by his paper there﻿—the one I was reading﻿—that he was on the staff of Northern U. He did some work at the Cook County Asylum, some research work, and there was a bit of talk about his maltreating the patients. Then, on top of that, he published a paper that medical men considered crazy, and that started talk of his sanity. That’s all I know.”

“Then Nick﻿—”

===Chinese===
“哼！巧了。今天下午咱们聊过之后，我今晚正好在《美国医学杂志》上查了他的一本小册子。你怎么问起这个？”

“当然是因为我感兴趣。”

“好吧，我记得的情况是这样，帕特。他确实是个医学博士，不过从他那篇论文——就是我今晚读的那篇——看得出，他在北方大学任教。他在库克县疯人院做过些工作，研究性质的，当时有点关于他虐待病人的闲话。再加上后来他发表了一篇医学界公认为疯狂的论文，这才起了对他神智的议论。我知道的就这些。”

“那么尼克——”

===Original===
“I thought so! So it’s come to the point where you’re investigating his antecedents, eh? With an eye to marriage, or what?”

“Or what!” snapped Pat. “I was curious to know, naturally.”

“Naturally.” The Doctor gave her a keen glance from his shrewd eyes. “Did you think you detected incipient dementia in your ideal?”

“No,” said the girl thoughtfully. “Dr. Carl, is there any sort of craziness that could take an ordinarily shy person and make a passionate devil of him? I don’t mean passionate, either,” she added. “Rather cold, ruthless, domineering.”

===Chinese===
“我猜就是！都发展到调查起他的家底了，嗯？是冲着结婚去的，还是怎么？”

“还是怎么！”帕特没好气地说，“我只是好奇，自然想知道。”

“自然。”医生用精明的眼睛敏锐地扫了她一眼。“你是不是觉得，从你的意中人身上察觉了早期痴呆的苗头？”

“不，”姑娘若有所思地说，“卡尔医生，有没有哪种疯病，能把一个平素羞怯的人变成一个狂热的魔鬼？我说的也不是狂热，”她补充道，“更像是冷酷、无情、霸道。”

===Original===
“None that I know of,” said Horker, watching her closely. “Did this Nick of yours have one of his masterful moments?”

“Worse than that,” admitted Pat reluctantly. “We had a near accident, and it startled both of us, and then suddenly, he was looking at me like a devil, and then﻿—” She paused. “It frightened me a little.”

“What’d he do?” demanded Horker sharply.

“Nothing.” She lied with no hesitation.

===Chinese===
“据我所知没有，”霍克说着，紧盯着她，“你那位尼克莫非大发了一回男人的威风？”

“比那还糟，”帕特不情愿地承认，“我们差点出了车祸，两个人都吓着了，随后突然间，他看我的眼神就像个魔鬼，再然后——”她顿了顿。“我有点被吓着了。”

“他干什么了？”霍克厉声追问。

“没干什么。”她撒起谎来毫不迟疑。

===Original===
“Were there any signs of Satyromania?”

“I don’t know. I never heard of that.”

“I mean, in plain Americanese, did he make a pass at you?”

“He﻿—no, he didn’t.”

===Chinese===
“有没有男子淫狂症的迹象？”

“我不知道。我从没听说过这病。”

“我是说，用大白话讲，他对你动手动脚了没有？”

“他——没有，他没有。”

===Original===
“Well, what *did* he do?”

“He just looked at me.” Somehow a feeling of disloyalty was rising in her; she felt a reluctance to betray Nick further.

“What did he say, then? And don’t lie this time.”

“He just said﻿—He just looked at my legs and said something about their being beautiful, and that was all. After that, the look on his face faded into the old Nick.”

===Chinese===
“那么他到底干什么了？”

“他只是看着我。”不知怎么，一种不忠的感觉在她心里升起；她不愿再进一步出卖尼克了。

“那他说什么了？这回可别撒谎。”

“他只是说——他只是看了看我的腿，说了句它们很美之类的话，就这些。过后，他脸上的那种神情就褪回了原来的尼克。”

===Original===
“Old Nick is right﻿—the impudent scoundrel!” Horker’s voice rumbled angrily.

“Well, they’re nice legs,” said Pat defiantly, swinging them as evidence. “You’ve said it yourself. Why shouldn’t *he* say it? What’s to keep him from it?”

“The code of a gentleman, for one thing!”

“Oh, who cares for your Victorian codes! Anyway, I came here for information, not to be cross-examined. I want to ask the questions myself.”

===Chinese===
“原来的尼克才对——好个厚颜无耻的流氓！”霍克的嗓门气得嗡嗡响。

“哼，这双腿是漂亮，”帕特不服气地说，还把腿晃了晃当作证据，“你自己就说过的。他凭什么不能说？谁拦得住他？”

“就说一条：绅士的规矩！”

“哦，谁稀罕你那套维多利亚时代的规矩！反正我是来讨教知识的，不是来受盘问的。问题得由我来问。”

===Original===
“Pat, you’re a reckless little spitfire, and you’re going to get burned some day, and deserve it,” the Doctor rumbled ominously. “Ask your fool questions, and then I’ll ask mine.”

“All right,” said the girl, still defiant. “I don’t guarantee to answer yours, however.”

“Well, ask yours, you imp!”

“First, then﻿—is that Satyro-stuff you mentioned intermittent or continuous?”

===Chinese===
“帕特，你是个不知天高地厚的火爆丫头片子，早晚有一天要吃苦头，而且是活该，”医生凶巴巴地嗡声道，“问你的傻问题吧，问完该我问了。”

“行，”姑娘说，嘴上仍不服软，“不过你的问题我可保证不了回答。”

“那就问吧，小捣蛋鬼！”

“那第一个——你说的那个什么淫狂症，是间歇性的还是持续性的？”

===Original===
“It’s necessarily intermittent, you numb-skull! The male organism can’t function continuously!”

“I mean, does the mania lie dormant for weeks or months, and then flare up?”

“Not at all. It’s a permanent mania, like any other psychopathic sex condition.”

“Oh,” said Pat thoughtfully, with a sense of relief.

===Chinese===
“必然是间歇的，你这木头脑袋！男性的肌体没法连续运转！”

“我是说，这种狂症会不会潜伏几个星期或几个月，然后突然发作？”

“完全不会。它是持续性的狂症，跟其他任何病理性性心理状态一样。”

“哦，”帕特若有所思地说，松了口气。

===Original===
“Well, go on. What next?”

“What are these dual personalities you read about in the papers?”

“They’re aphasias. An individual forgets his name, and he picks, or is given, another, if he happens to wander among strangers. He forgets much of his past experience; the second personality is merely what’s left of the first﻿—sort of a vestige of his normal character. There isn’t any such thing as a dual personality in the sense of two distinct characters living in one body.”

“Isn’t there?” queried the girl musingly. “Could the second personality have qualities that the first one lacked?”

===Chinese===
“嗯，接着说。下一个呢？”

“报上常登的那种双重人格是怎么回事？”

“那是失语症一类的毛病。一个人忘了自己的名字，如果他恰好流落到陌生人中间，便会自己挑一个、或被人给起一个新名字。他忘掉了过去的许多经历；所谓第二人格不过是第一人格残存的部分——是他正常人格的一种残余。世上并不存在什么双重人格，不是两个截然不同的人格合住在一个身体里那种意义。”

“不存在吗？”姑娘出神地问，“那第二人格会不会具备第一人格所没有的特质？”

===Original===
“Not any more than it could have an extra finger! The second is merely a split off the first, a forgetfulness, a loss of memory. It couldn’t have *more* qualities than the whole, or normal, character; it *must* have fewer.”

“Isn’t that just too interesting!” said Pat in a bantering tone. “All right, Dr. Carl. It’s your turn.”

“Then what’s the reason for all this curiosity about perversions and aphasias? What’s happened to your genius now?”

“Oh, I’m thinking of taking up the study of psychiatry,” replied the girl cheerfully.

===Chinese===
“就像人不可能多长一根手指一样不可能！第二人格只是从第一人格上剥落的一块，是一种遗忘，一种记忆的丧失。它不可能比完整的、正常的人格具备更多的特质；只可能更少。”

“这不是太有意思了嘛！”帕特用打趣的口气说，“好了，卡尔医生。该你了。”

“那你对性倒错和失语症刨根问底，是为了什么？你那位天才这回又怎么了？”

“哦，我在考虑开始研究精神病学呢，”姑娘轻快地答道。

===Original===
“Aren’t you going to answer me seriously?”

“No.”

“Then what’s the use of my asking questions?”

“I know the right answer to that one. None!”

===Chinese===
“你不打算正经回答我吗？”

“不打算。”

“那我提问还有什么用？”

“这题我知道正确答案。没用！”

===Original===
“Pat,” said Horker in a low voice, “you’re an impudent little hoyden, and too clever for your own good, but you and your mother are very precious to me. You know that.”

“Of course I do, Dr. Carl,” said the girl, relenting. “You’re a dear, and I’m crazy about you, and you know that, too.”

“What I’m trying to say,” proceeded the other, “is simply that I’m trying to help you. I want to help you, if you need help. Do you?”

“I guess I don’t, Dr. Carl, but you’re sweet.”

===Chinese===
“帕特，”霍克放低声音说，“你是个没大没小的野丫头，聪明得过了头，可你和你母亲对我来说非常珍贵。这你是知道的。”

“我当然知道，卡尔医生，”姑娘软了下来，“你是个可亲的人，我也喜欢你喜欢得不得了，这你也知道。”

“我想说的，”对方接着说，“无非是我在设法帮你。如果你需要帮助，我愿意帮。你需要吗？”

“我看不需要，卡尔医生，不过您真好。”

===Original===
“Are you in love with this Nicholas Devine?”

“I think perhaps I am,” she admitted softly.

“And is he in love with you?”

“Frankly, could he help being?”

===Chinese===
“你爱这个尼古拉斯·德文吗？”

“我想也许是的，”她轻声承认。

“那他爱你吗？”

“坦白说，他能不爱吗？”

===Original===
“Then there’s something about him that worries you. That’s it, isn’t it?”

“I thought there was, Dr. Carl. I was a little startled by the change in him right after we had that narrow escape, but I’m sure it was nothing﻿—just imagination. Honestly, that’s all that troubled me.”

“I believe you, Pat,” said the Doctor, his eyes fixed on hers. “But guard yourself, my dear. Be sure he’s what you think he is; be sure you know him rightly.”

“He’s clean and fine,” murmured the girl. “I *am* sure.”

===Chinese===
“那么就是他身上有什么东西让你担心。是这么回事，对不对？”

“我原以为有，卡尔医生。死里逃生之后他立刻变了个人似的，那让我有点吃惊，不过我肯定那什么都不是——只是想象罢了。说真的，让我烦心的就这么多。”

“我相信你，帕特，”医生说，目光紧盯着她的眼睛，“可要保护好你自己，亲爱的。要确定他就是你以为的那个人；要确定你真正了解他。”

“他纯洁又优秀，”姑娘喃喃道，“我确定。”

===Original===
“But this puzzling yourself about his character, Pat﻿—I don’t like it. Make doubly sure before you permit your feelings to become too deeply involved. That’s only common sense, child, not psychiatry or magic.”

“I’m sure,” repeated Pat. “I’m not puzzled or troubled any more. And thanks, Dr. Carl. You run along to bed and I’ll do likewise.”

He rose, accompanying her to the door, his face unusually grave.

“Patricia,” he said, “I want you to think over what I’ve said. Be sure, be doubly sure, before you expose yourself to the possibility of suffering. Remember that, won’t you?”

===Chinese===
“可你这样为他的性子犯嘀咕，帕特——我不喜欢。在让你的感情陷得太深之前，务必看个清楚明白。这只是常识，孩子，不是精神病学，也不是法术。”

“我确定，”帕特重复道，“我不再困惑也不再烦恼了。谢谢您，卡尔医生。您快去睡吧，我也去睡。”

他站起身，陪她走到门口，脸色异乎寻常地凝重。

“帕特里夏，”他说，“我要你把我的话好好想一想。在让自己有受苦的可能之前，要确定，要千万确定。记住这一点，好吗？”

===Original===
“I’ll try to. Don’t fret yourself about it, Dr. Carl; I’m a hard-boiled young modern, and it takes a diamond to even scratch me.”

“I hope so,” he said soberly. “Run along; I’ll watch until you’re inside.”

Pat darted across the strip of grass, turned at her door to blow a goodnight kiss to the Doctor, and slipped in. She tiptoed quietly to her room, slipped off her dress, and surveyed her long, slim legs in the mirror.

“Why shouldn’t he say they were beautiful?” she queried of the image. “I can’t see any reason to get excited over a simple compliment like that.”

===Chinese===
“我尽量。您别为这个操心，卡尔医生；我是个心肠硬邦邦的现代青年，得用钻石才划得伤我。”

“但愿如此，”他正色道，“去吧；我看着你进门。”

帕特一溜烟跑过那窄条草地，在自家门口回身给医生抛了个晚安的飞吻，闪身进屋。她蹑手蹑脚回到房间，褪下衣裙，对着镜子端详自己那双修长纤细的腿。

“他凭什么不能说它们美？”她冲着镜中影像问，“这么一句简简单单的恭维话有什么可大惊小怪的，我看不出。”

===Original===
She made a face over her shoulder at the green Buddha above the fireplace.

“And as for you, fat boy,” she murmured, “I expect to see you wink at me tonight. And every night hereafter!”

She prepared herself for slumber, slipped into the great bed. She had hardly closed her lids before the image of a leering face with terrible bloody eyes flamed out of memory and set her trembling and shuddering.

===Chinese===
她回头朝壁炉架上那尊绿玉佛像做了个鬼脸。

“还有你，胖家伙，”她喃喃道，“我等着今晚看您朝我眨眼睛呢。往后每一晚都等着！”

她做好入睡的准备，钻进大床。眼皮刚合上，一张讪笑的脸、一双血红的可怕眼睛便从记忆里熊熊烧出，惊得她浑身发颤、抖个不停。

## XXI A Question of Synapses / 二十一 突触的问题

===Original===
Pat almost ran the few blocks to her home. She hastened along in a near panic, regardless of the glances of pedestrians she chanced to pass. With the disappearance of the immediate urge, the composure for which she had struggled had deserted her, and she felt shaken, terrified, and weak. Her arm ached miserably, and her wrenched shoulder pained at each movement. It was not until she attained her own doorstep that she paused, panting and quivering, to consider the events of the evening.

“I can’t stand any more of this!” she muttered wretchedly to herself. “I’ll just have to give up, I guess; I can’t pit myself another time against﻿—that thing.”

She leaned wearily against the railing of the porch, rubbing her injured arm.

“Dr. Carl was right,” she thought. “Nick was right; it’s dangerous. There was a moment there at the end when he﻿—or it﻿—almost had me. I’m frightened,” she admitted. “Lord only knows what might have happened had I been a little weaker. If the Lord *does* know,” she added.

===Chinese===
那几个街区，帕特几乎是一路跑回家的。她近乎惊慌地匆匆疾行，全然不顾擦肩而过的行人投来的目光。眼下那股迫在眉睫的冲动一消失，她苦苦撑起的镇定也随之瓦解，只觉得浑身发颤、惊恐、虚弱。她的手臂痛得厉害，扭伤的肩膀一动就疼。直到踏上前门的台阶，她才停下脚步，喘息着、颤抖着，回想今晚的种种。

“这种日子我再也受不了啦！”她凄惨地自言自语，“看来只好放弃了；我不能再拿自己去跟——那东西拼一回。”

她疲惫地倚着门廊的栏杆，揉着受伤的手臂。

“卡尔医生说得对，”她想，“尼克说得也对；这太危险了。最后有一刻，他——或者说它——差点就得手了。我害怕，”她承认，“天知道我要是再软弱一点会发生什么。如果天当真知道的话，”她又补了一句。

===Original===
She found her latchkey and entered the house. Only a dim light burned in the hall; her mother, of course, was at the Club, and the maid and Magda were far away in their chambers on the third floor. She tossed her wrap on a chair, switched on a brighter light, and examined the painful spot on her arm, a red mark already beginning to turn a nasty blue, with two tiny specks of drying blood. She shuddered, and trudged wearily up the stairs to her room.

The empty silence of the house oppressed her. She wanted human companionship﻿—safe, trustworthy, friendly company, anyone to distract her thoughts from the eerie, disturbing direction they were taking. She was still in somewhat of a panic, and suppressed with difficulty a desire to peep fearfully under the bed.

“Coward!” she chided herself. “You knew what to expect.”

Suddenly the recollection of her parting words recurred to her. She had told Nick﻿—if Nick had indeed heard﻿—to come to the house, to come at once, tonight, if he could. A tremor of apprehension ran through her. Suppose he came; suppose he came as her own Nick, and she admitted him, and then﻿—or suppose that other came, and managed by some trick to enter, or suppose that unholy fascination of his prevailed on her﻿—she shivered, and brushed her hand distractedly across her eyes.

===Chinese===
她摸出门锁钥匙，进了屋。门厅里只亮着一盏昏灯；母亲当然在俱乐部，女仆和玛格达都在三楼她们自己的房间里，离得远远的。她把披肩往椅子上一丢，拧亮一盏更亮的灯，检查手臂上作痛的地方——一块红痕已经开始转为骇人的青紫色，上面还有两个干涸的小血点。她打了个寒战，拖着疲惫的步子上楼回房。

空屋子的沉寂压得她喘不过气。她想要人做伴——安全、可靠、友善的伴，随便什么人，好把她的思绪从正滑向的那个阴森不安的方向上引开。她仍有些惊魂未定，好不容易才按住往床底下偷看一眼的冲动。

“胆小鬼！”她数落自己，“你早知道会是什么样的。”

突然，她想起了临别时说的那几句话。她告诉过尼克——倘若那真是尼克听见的——要他到家里来，要是他办得到，今晚就来，立刻来。一阵忧惧的战栗传遍她全身。万一他来了呢；万一他来时还是她的尼克的样子，她放他进了门，然后——又万一来的是那另一个，施了什么诡计混进来，再万一他那种邪异的魔力迷住了她的心窍——她哆嗦了一下，心烦意乱地用手抹了抹眼睛。

===Original===
“I can’t stand it!” she moaned. “I’ll have to give up, even if it means never seeing Nick again. I’ll have to!” She shook her head miserably as if to deny the picture that had risen in her mind of herself and that horror alone in the house.

“I won’t stay here!” she decided. She peeped out of the west windows at the Doctor’s residence, and felt a surge of relief at the sight of his iron-gray hair framed in the library window below. He was reading; she could see the book on his knees. There was her refuge; she ran hastily down the stairs and out of the door.

With an apprehensive glance along the street she crossed to his door and rang the bell. She waited nervously for his coming, and, with a sudden impulse, pulled her vanity-case from her bag and dabbed a film of powder over the mark on her arm. Then his ponderous footsteps sounded and the door opened.

“Hello,” he said genially. “These late evening visits of yours are becoming quite customary﻿—and see if I care!”

===Chinese===
“我受不了啦！”她呻吟道，“我必须放弃，哪怕从此再见不到尼克。我必须放弃！”她痛苦地摇着头，像是要驱走脑中浮现的画面——她独自和那个恐怖之物同处一室。

“我不能待在这儿！”她拿定了主意。她从西窗偷眼望向医生家，看见他铁灰色的头发正映在楼下书房的窗框里，顿时松了一大口气。他在看书；她能看见摊在他膝上的书。那儿就是她的避难所；她急匆匆跑下楼，出了门。

她朝街两头提心吊胆地张了一眼，穿过去按响他家的门铃。她紧张地等他来开门，一时冲动，从手袋里掏出粉盒，往手臂上的痕迹扑了薄薄一层粉。接着便响起他沉重的脚步声，门开了。

“你好啊，”他和蔼地说，“你这种深夜来访都快成惯例了——看我介不介意！”

===Original===
“May I come in a while?” asked Pat meekly.

“Have I ever turned you away?” He followed her into the library, pushed a chair forward for her, and dropped quickly into his own with an air of having snatched it from her just in time.

“I didn’t want your old armchair,” she remarked, occupying the other.

“And what’s the trouble tonight?” he queried.

===Chinese===
“我能进来待一会儿吗？”帕特乖乖地问。

“我几时撵过你？”他跟着她进了书房，推过一把椅子给她，自己一屁股坐进那把旧椅子，那副架势活像赶在她前头一把抢了回来。

“谁稀罕你那把破扶手椅，”她说着，坐进了另一把。

“今晚又出什么岔子了？”他问。

===Original===
“I﻿—well, I was just nervous. I didn’t want to stay in the house alone.”

“You?” His tone was skeptical. “You were nervous? That hardly sounds reasonable, coming from an independent little spitfire like you.”

“I was, though. I was scared.”

“And of what﻿—or whom?”

===Chinese===
“我——嗯，我就是心里发慌。不想一个人待在屋里。”

“你？”他的口气将信将疑。“你会发慌？这话出自你这么个天不怕地不怕的野丫头之口，可不大像话。”

“可我就是慌了。我害怕了。”

“怕什么——还是怕谁？”

===Original===
“Of haunts and devils.”

“Oh.” He nodded. “I see you’ve had results from your letter-writing.”

“Well, sort of.”

“I’m used to your circumlocutions, Pat. Suppose you come directly to the point for once. What happened?”

===Chinese===
“怕鬼怪，怕魔鬼。”

“哦。”他点点头。“看来你那封信写出效果来了。”

“嗯，算是吧。”

“你那些弯弯绕我早领教惯了，帕特。这回不如干脆直奔主题。出什么事了？”

===Original===
“Why, I wrote Nick to get in touch with me, and I got a reply. He said to meet him in the park at a place we knew. This evening.”

“And you did, of course.”

“Yes, but before that, this afternoon, he called up and told me not to, but I insisted and we did.”

“Told you not to, eh? And was his warning justified?”

===Chinese===
“是这样，我写信让尼克跟我联系，还真收到了回音。他约我在公园里一个我们熟地方见面。就是今晚。”

“你自然就去了。”

“去了，不过在这之前，今天下午他打来电话，叫我别去，可我非要去，我们就去了。”

“叫你别去，嗯？那他的警告应验了没有？”

===Original===
“Yes. Oh, yes! When I came to the place, it was﻿—the other.”

“So! Well, he could hardly manhandle you in a public park.”

Pat thought of her wrenched shoulder and bruised arm. She shuddered.

“He’s horrible!” she said. “Inhuman! He kept referring to Saturday night, and he threatened that if I moved or made a disturbance he’d let Nick suffer the consequences. So I kept still while he insulted me.”

===Chinese===
“应验了。哦，太应验了！我到了那地方，在那儿的是——另一个。”

“瞧瞧！哼，在公园这种公共场所，他总不好对你动粗吧。”

帕特想起自己扭伤的肩膀和淤青的手臂，不由得打了个寒战。

“他可怕极了！”她说，“没人性！他三句话不离星期六晚上，还威胁说，我要是动一下、闹出动静，就让尼克承担后果。所以他羞辱我的时候，我只有忍着。”

===Original===
“You nitwit!” There was more than a trace of anger in the Doctor’s voice. “I want to see that pup of yours! We’ll soon find out what this thing is﻿—a mania or simply lack of a good licking!”

“What it is?” echoed Pat. “Oh﻿—it told me! Dr. Carl, what’s a synopsis?”

“A synopsis! You know perfectly well.”

“I mean applied to physiology or psychology or something. It﻿—he told me he was a question of synopsis.”

===Chinese===
“你这糊涂虫！”医生的嗓音里不止带一丝火气。“我倒要见见你那位小哥！咱们很快就能弄明白这算什么东西——是狂症，还是纯粹欠一顿好揍！”

“是什么？”帕特应声道，“哦——它跟我说了！卡尔医生，什么叫‘概要’（synopsis）？”

“概要！你不是门儿清嘛。”

“我是说用在生理学、心理学或者那一类东西上是什么意思。它——他跟我说，他是一个概要的问题。”

===Original===
“This devil of yours said that?”

“Yes.”

“Hum!” The Doctor’s voice was musing. He frowned perplexedly, then looked up abruptly. “Was it﻿—did he by any chance say synapses? Not synopsis﻿—synapses?”

“That’s it!” exclaimed the girl. “He said he was a question of synapses. Does that explain him? Do you know what he is?”

===Chinese===
“你这位魔鬼说的这个？”

“是的。”

“嗯！”医生的语气若有所思。他困惑地皱起眉，随即猛地抬头。“那是——他说的会不会是突触（synapses）？不是概要——是突触？”

“就是这个！”姑娘失声道，“他说他是一个突触的问题。这能解释他吗？您知道他是什么东西吗？”

===Original===
“Doesn’t explain a damn thing!” snapped Horker. “A synapse is a juncture, or the meeting of two nerves. It’s why you can develop automatic motions and habits, like playing piano, or dancing. When you form a habit, the synapses of the nerves involved are sort of worn thin, so the nerves themselves are, in a sense, short-circuited. You go through motions without the need of your brain intervening, which is all a habit amounts to. Understand?”

“Not very well,” confessed Pat.

“Humph! It doesn’t matter anyway. I can’t see that it helps to analyze your devil.”

“I don’t care if it’s never analyzed,” said Pat with a return of despondency. “Dr. Carl, I can’t face that evil thing again. I can’t do it, not even if it means never seeing Nick!”

===Chinese===
“什么也解释不了！”霍克没好气地说，“突触是一种连接处，也就是两条神经的交汇点。人之所以能养成自动化的动作和习惯，比如弹钢琴、跳舞，靠的就是它。习惯养成时，相关神经的突触会变薄，于是那些神经本身，从某种意义上说，就被短路了。你做动作不必经过大脑介入——习惯的全部奥妙就在于此。明白了吗？”

“不很明白，”帕特坦白道。

“哼！反正无关紧要。我看这对你那魔鬼的剖析帮不上什么忙。”

“永远剖析不明白我也不在乎了，”帕特说着，又沮丧起来，“卡尔医生，我没办法再面对那个邪恶的东西了。我做不到，哪怕代价是再也见不到尼克！”

===Original===
“Sensible,” said the Doctor approvingly. “I’d like to have a chance at him, but not enough to keep you in this state of jitters. Although,” he added, “a lot of this mystery is the product of your own harum-scarum mind. You can be sure of that, Honey.”

“You *would* say so,” responded the girl wearily. “You’ve never seen that﻿—change. If it’s my imagination, then I’m the one that needs your treatments, not Nick.”

“It isn’t *all* imagination, most likely,” said Horker defensively. “I know these introverted types with their hysterias, megalomanias, and defense mechanisms! They’ve paraded through my office there for a good many years, Pat; they’ve provided the lion’s share of my practice. But this young psychopathic of yours seems to have it bad﻿—abnormally so, and that’s why I’m so interested, apart from helping you, of course.”

“I don’t care,” said Pat apathetically, repressing a desire to rub her injured arm. “I’m through. I’m scared out of the affair. Another week like this last one and I *would* be one of your patients.”

===Chinese===
“明智，”医生赞许地说，“我是想有机会会一会他，可还不至于为此让你老这么提心吊胆。不过，”他补充道，“这桩谜案有一大半是你自己那颗毛躁脑袋的产物。这一点你可以打包票，孩子。”

“你当然要这么说，”姑娘疲惫地应道，“你又没见过那种——变化。如果那真是我的想象，那需要治疗的就该是我，不是尼克。”

“多半不全是想象，”霍克辩解似的说，“这类内向型的人我是懂的，还有他们的癔症、自大狂和种种心理防御机制！这些人这些年在我诊所里排着队走马灯似的来，帕特；我的病人一多半是他们供的。不过你这位年轻的精神变态患者病得可不轻——反常地重，所以我才这么上心——当然，除了帮你这个缘故之外。”

“我不在乎，”帕特无精打采地说，一面按捺着去揉受伤手臂的念头，“我到此为止了。这件事把我吓破了胆。再来一个像上星期那样的星期，我就真要成为你的病人了。”

===Original===
“Best drop it, then,” said Horker, eyeing her seriously. “Nothing’s worth upsetting yourself like this, Pat.”

“Nick’s worth it,” she murmured. “He’s worth it﻿—only I just haven’t the strength. I haven’t the courage. I can’t do it!”

“Never mind, Honey,” the Doctor muttered, regarding her with an expression of concern. “You’re probably well out of the mess. I know damn well you haven’t told me everything about this affair﻿—notably, how you acquired that ugly mark on your arm that’s so carefully powdered over. So, all in all, I guess you’re well out of it.”

“I suppose I am.” Her voice was still weary. Suddenly the glare of headlights drew her attention to the window; a car was stopping before her home. “There’s Mother,” she said. “I’ll go on back now, Dr. Carl, and thanks for entertaining a lonesome and depressed lady.”

She rose with a casual glance through the window, then halted in frozen astonishment and a trace of terror.

===Chinese===
“那就趁早撒手，”霍克说着，郑重地打量她，“什么事都不值得让你把自己折腾成这样，帕特。”

“尼克值得，”她喃喃道，“他值得——只是我没有那个力气了。我没有那个勇气。我做不到！”

“别往心里去，孩子，”医生低声道，满脸关切地望着她，“跳出这摊浑水，八成是好事。我心里明镜似的，这件事你并没全告诉我——尤其是，你手臂上那块被你仔仔细细扑了粉盖住的难看痕迹是怎么来的。所以，总而言之，我看你抽身抽得对。”

“我也这么想。”她的声音仍透着倦意。忽然，一道车头灯的强光把她的目光引向窗外；一辆汽车正停在她家门口。“是母亲回来了，”她说，“我这就回去，卡尔医生，谢谢您款待一位寂寞又沮丧的小姐。”

她站起身，漫不经心地朝窗外扫了一眼，随即僵在原地，惊愕万分，还带着一丝恐怖。

===Original===
“Oh!” she gasped. The car was the modest coupe of Nicholas Devine.

She peered through the window; the Doctor rose and stared over her shoulder. “I told him to come,” she whispered. “I told him to come when he was able. He heard me, he or﻿—the other.”

A figure alighted from the vehicle. Even in the dusk she could perceive the exhaustion, the weariness in its movements. She pressed her face to the pane, surveying the form with fascinated intentness. It turned, supporting itself against the car and gazing steadily at her own door. With the movement the radiance of a streetlight illuminated its features.

“It’s Nick!” she cried with such eagerness that the Doctor was startled. “It’s *my* Nick!”

===Chinese===
“哦！”她倒吸一口气。那辆车是尼古拉斯·德文那辆不起眼的双门小轿车。

她贴着窗口细看；医生站起身，从她肩后望出去。“是我让他来的，”她低声说，“我让他办得到的时候就过来。他听见了，他——或者那另一个。”

一个身影下了车。即使隔着暮色，她也能察觉那动作里的衰竭与疲惫。她把脸贴在窗玻璃上，全神贯注又心惊胆战地打量着那个身影。那人转过身，倚着车，直勾勾地望着她家的门。随着这个动作，一盏路灯的光照亮了他的五官。

“是尼克！”她喊得那样急切，把医生吓了一跳。“是我的尼克！”

## XIV Bizarre Explanation / 十四 离奇的解释

===Original===
“I won’t be bullied!” Pat told herself, examining her features in the mirror. The two day interval had faded the discoloration of her cheek to negligible proportions, and all that remained as evidence of the violence of Saturday night was the diminishing mark on her chin. Of course, her knees﻿—but they were covered; most of the time, at least. She gave herself a final inspection, and somewhere below a clock boomed.

“Eight o’clock,” she remarked to her image; “Time to be leaving, and it serves Dr. Carl right for his high-handed actions last night. I won’t be bullied by anybody.” She checked herself as her mind had almost added, “Except Nick.” True or not, she didn’t relish the thought; the recent recollections it roused were too disturbing.

She tossed a stray wisp of black hair from her forehead and turned to the door. She heard her mother’s voice as she descended the stairs.

“Are you going out, Patricia? Do you think it wise?”

===Chinese===
“我才不受人要挟呢！”帕特对着镜子端详自己的容貌，暗自说道。两天的工夫已使她脸颊上的淤色淡得几乎看不见，星期六晚上那场暴行的证据，只剩下下巴上一块日渐消退的痕迹。当然，还有她的膝盖——不过那有衣裳遮着；至少大多数时候遮着。她给了自己最后一遍检查，楼下什么地方，一座钟嗡嗡地敲响了。

“八点了，”她对镜中人说，“该出发了。卡尔医生昨晚那么专横，正好让他着着急。谁也别想胁迫我。”话音未落，她的脑子差点自动补上一句“尼克除外”。她赶紧煞住。不管真假，这个念头都让她不痛快；它勾起的近期回忆太叫人烦乱了。

她把飘到额前的一缕黑发甩开，转身朝门口走去。下楼时，她听见母亲的声音。

“你要出去吗，帕特里夏？你觉得妥当吗？”

===Original===
“I am perfectly all right. I want to go for a walk.”

“I know, Dear; it was largely your appearance I meant.” She surveyed the girl with a critical eye. “Nice enough, except for that little spot on your chin, and will you never learn to keep your hair away from that side of your forehead? One can never do a bob right; why don’t you let it grow out like the other girls?”

“Makes me individual,” replied Pat, moving toward the outer door. “I won’t be late at all,” she added.

On the porch she cast a cautious glance at Dr. Horker’s windows, but his great figure was nowhere evident. Only a light burning in the library evinced his presence. She gave a sigh of relief, and tiptoed down the steps to the sidewalk, and moved hastily away from the range of his watchful eyes.

===Chinese===
“我好得很。我想出去散散步。”

“我知道，亲爱的；我说的主要是你这副模样。”她用挑剔的目光把姑娘打量了一番。“挺俊的，就是下巴上还有块小印子。还有，你什么时候才能学会别让头发搭在额头那一侧？齐耳短发怎么弄都不像样；为什么不像别的姑娘那样留长了呢？”

“这样才有个性，”帕特答着，朝大门走去。“我不会晚回来的，”她补了一句。

在门廊上，她提防地朝霍克医生家的窗户瞥了一眼，却哪儿也不见他魁梧的身影。只有书房里亮着的灯表明他在家。她宽慰地舒了口气，踮着脚下台阶走到人行道上，匆匆走出了他警惕目光所及的范围。

===Original===
No sooner had she sighted the park than doubts began to torment her. Suppose this were some trick of Nicholas Devine’s, to trap her into some such situation as that of Saturday night. Even suppose that she found him the sweet personality that she had loved, might that also be a trick? Mightn’t he be trusting to his ability to win her over, to the charm she had confessed to him that he held for her? Couldn’t he be putting his faith in his own amorous skill, planning some specious explanation to win her forgiveness only to use her once more as the material for some horrible experiment? And if he were, would she be able to prevent herself from yielding?

“Forewarned is forearmed,” she told herself. “I’ll not put up such a feeble resistance this time, knowing what I now know. And it’s only fair of me to listen to his explanation, if he really has one.”

She was reassured by the sight of the crowded park; groups strolled along the walks, and an endless procession of car-headlights marked the course of the roadway. Nothing could happen in such an environment; they’d be fortunate even to have an opportunity for confidential talk. She waited for the traffic lights, straining her eyes to locate Nicholas Devine; at the click of the signal she darted across the street.

She moved toward the lake; here was the spot, she was sure. She glanced about with eagerness unexpected even to herself, peering through the shadow-shot dusk. He wasn’t there, she concluded, with a curious sense of disappointment; her failure to appear last night had disheartened him; he had abandoned his attempt.

===Chinese===
公园刚一映入眼帘，疑虑便开始折磨她。万一这是尼古拉斯·德文的什么圈套，要诱她再陷入星期六晚上那样的境地呢？就算她见到的还是她爱过的那个温柔人儿，那会不会也是圈套？他会不会正仗着自己哄得动她、仗着她亲口向他招认过的那份魅力，笃信自己那一套求爱的手段，编出一套冠冕堂皇的解释博得她的原谅，只为再把她当一回某种可怕实验的材料？果真如此，她挡得住自己不屈服吗？

“凡事预则立，”她告诉自己，“如今我知己知彼，这回决不会再抵抗得那么软弱无力了。再说，他要真有一番解释，让我听听也是天公地道。”

公园里人来人往，这让她放宽了心；小径上一群群人悠然漫步，公路上车灯汇成无穷无尽的长龙。在这种地方出不了什么事；想觅个说体己话的机会都算是难得。她等着红绿灯，极力张望寻找尼古拉斯·德文的身影；信号灯咔哒一响，她便飞快穿过街去。

她朝湖边走去；就是这地方，她敢肯定。她四下张望，那份急切连她自己都感到意外，一双眼睛在暮色斑驳的阴影里搜寻。他不在，她断定，心里生出一种说不清的失望；昨晚她的爽约让他灰了心；他放弃尝试了。

===Original===
Then she saw him. He sat on a bench isolated from the rest in a treeless area overlooking the lake. She saw his disconsolate figure, his chin on his hand, staring moodily over the waters. A tremor ran through her, she halted deliberately, waiting until every trace of emotion had vanished, then she advanced, standing coolly beside him.

For a moment he was unaware of her presence; he sat maintaining his dejected attitude without glancing at her. Suddenly some slight movement, the flutter of her skirt, drew his attention; he turned sharply, gazing directly into her face.

“Pat!” He sprang to his feet. “Pat! is it you﻿—truly you? Or are you one of these visions that have been plaguing me for hours?”

“I’m real,” she said, returning his gaze with a studied coolness in her face. She made no other move; her cold composure disconcerted him, and he winced, flushed, and moved nervously aside as she seated herself. He dropped beside her; he made no attempt to touch her, but sat watching her in silence for so long a time that she felt her composure ebbing. There was a hungry, defeated look about him; there was a wistfulness, a frustration, in his eyes that seemed about to tug tears from her own eyes. Abruptly she dropped her gaze from his face.

===Chinese===
然后她看见了他。他坐在一条与别的长椅隔开的椅子上，那儿没有树，俯瞰着湖水。她望见他郁郁寡欢的身影，一手托着下巴，忧郁地凝望水面。一阵战栗掠过她全身；她刻意停住脚步，等到情绪的痕迹消失殆尽，才走上前去，冷冷地站到他身旁。

有那么一刻，他没有察觉她的到来；他保持着那副颓丧的姿态坐着，看也不看她。忽然，一个小小的动静，她裙裾的拂动，引起了他的注意；他猛地转过脸，直直地望进她的眼睛。

“帕特！”他霍地跳起来。“帕特！是你吗——真的是你？还是又是一个这几个钟头一直折磨我的幻影？”

“我是真的，”她说，脸上带着刻意练出的冷淡迎住他的目光。她没有别的动作；她冷漠的镇定让他狼狈起来，他瑟缩了一下，涨红了脸，在她落座时紧张地往旁边挪了挪。他在她身边坐下；他丝毫没有碰她的意思，只是默默地凝视她，凝视得那么久，久得她觉得自己的镇定正在流失。他一副饥渴而挫败的神情；他眼里那种怅惘、那种郁结，几乎要把她自己眼里的泪都牵引出来。她猛地把目光从他脸上移开。

===Original===
“Well?” she said finally in a small voice, and as he made no reply, “I’m here.”

“Are you really, Pat? Are you truly here?” he murmured, still watching her avidly. “I﻿—I still don’t believe it. I waited here for hours and hours last night, and I’d given up hope for tonight, or any night. But I would have come again and again.”

She started as he bent suddenly toward her, but he was merely examining her face. She saw the gleam of horror in his expression as his eyes surveyed the faintly visible bruise on her cheek, the red mark on her chin.

“Oh my God, Pat!” His words were barely audible. “Oh my God!” he repeated, drawing away from her and resuming the attitude of desolation in which her arrival had found him. “I’ve hoped it wasn’t true!”

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”她终于细声开口，见他仍不作声，又道：“我来了。”

“你真来了，帕特？你真的在这儿？”他喃喃道，仍贪婪地望着她。“我——我还是不敢信。昨晚我在这儿等了好久好久，今晚本来已经不抱指望了，哪一晚都不抱了。可我还是会一次又一次地来。”

他突然朝她俯过身来，她一惊，但他只是在细看她的脸。她看见他的目光扫过她脸颊上隐约可见的淤痕、下巴上的红印时，表情里掠过一丝惊骇。

“哦，我的上帝，帕特！”他的话轻得几乎听不见。“哦，我的上帝！”他又说了一遍，直起身避开她，重新摆回她到来时那副凄惶的姿态。“我一直盼着那不是真的！”

===Original===
“What wasn’t?” She was keeping her voice carefully casual; this miserable contrition of Nick’s was tugging at her rather too powerfully for complete safety.

“What I remembered. What I saw just now.”

“You hoped it wasn’t true?” she queried in surprise. “But you did it.”

“*I* did it, Pat? Do you think *I* could have done it?”

===Chinese===
“什么不是真的？”她小心地让语气听上去若无其事；尼克这副痛悔交加的模样对她的牵动实在太大，大得不够安全。

“我记得的那些。还有我刚才看见的。”

“你盼着那不是真的？”她诧异地问，“可那是你干的。”

“是我干的，帕特？你觉得我会干得出那种事？”

===Original===
“But you did!” Her voice had taken on a chill inflection; the memory of those indignities came to steel her against him.

“Pat, do you think I could assault your daintiness, or maltreat the beauty I worship? Didn’t anything occur to you? Didn’t anything seem queer about﻿—about that ghastly evening?”

“Queer!” she echoed. “That’s certainly a mild word to use, isn’t it?”

“But I mean﻿—hadn’t you any idea of what had happened? Didn’t you think anything of it except that I had suddenly gone mad? Or that I’d grown to hate you?”

===Chinese===
“可就是你干的！”她的声音添了一丝寒意；那些受辱的记忆涌上来，替她铸起对付他的铁石心肠。

“帕特，你以为我舍得侵犯你的娇贵，虐待我崇拜的美人？你就没起过一点疑心？就没觉出那——那个可怕的晚上有什么蹊跷？”

“蹊跷！”她应声道，“这词儿说得可真轻巧，是不是？”

“可我是说——你就一点儿没想到出了什么事？你就只想到我突然疯了？或者想到我恨上你了？”

===Original===
“What was I to think?” she countered, trying to control the tremor that had crept into her voice.

“But did you think that?”

“No,” the girl confessed after a pause. “At first, when you started with that drink, I thought you were looking for material for your work. That’s what you said﻿—an experiment. Didn’t you?”

“I guess so,” he groaned.

===Chinese===
“不然让我怎么想？”她反问，竭力控制住爬进嗓音里的颤抖。

“那你当时真是这么想的吗？”

“不，”姑娘顿了顿，坦白道，“起初，你端出那杯饮料的时候，我以为你在为自己的研究找素材。你自己就是这么说的——一次实验。是不是？”

“大概是吧，”他呻吟道。

===Original===
“But after that, after I’d swallowed that horrible stuff, but before everything went hazy, I﻿—thought differently.”

“But what, Pat? What did you think?”

“Why, then I realized that it wasn’t you﻿—not the real you. I could feel the﻿—well, the presence of the person I knew; this presence that was tormenting me was another person, a terrible, cold, inhuman stranger.”

“Pat!” There was a note almost of relief in his voice. “Did you really feel that?”

===Chinese===
“可后来，等我咽下那可怕的东西之后、还没等眼前一切都变得模糊之前，我——改了想法。”

“改成什么了，帕特？你想的是什么？”

“于是我就明白了，那不是你——不是真正的你。我能感觉到——怎么说呢，能感觉到我认识的那个人还在；而折磨我的这个存在是另一个人，一个可怕的、冷酷的、不似人类的陌生人。”

“帕特！”他的声音里几乎带着解脱的调子。“你真有那种感觉？”

===Original===
“Yes. Does it help matters, my sensing that? I can’t see how.”

His eyes, which had been fixed on hers, dropped suddenly. “No,” he muttered, all the relief gone out of his tones, “no, it doesn’t help, does it? Except that it’s a meager consolation to me to know that you felt it.”

Pat struggled to suppress an impulse to reach out her hand, to stroke his hair. She caught herself sharply; this was the very danger against which she had warned herself﻿—this was the very attitude she had anticipated in Nicholas Devine, the lure which might bait a trap. Yet he looked so forlorn, so wistful! It was an effort to forbear from touching him; her fingers fairly ached to brush his cheek.

“Only a fool walks twice into the same trap,” she told herself. Aloud she said, “You promised me an explanation. If you’ve any excuse, I’d like to hear it.” Her voice had resumed its coolness.

===Chinese===
“有。我有那种感觉，事情就能好些吗？我看不出怎么会。”

他那一直紧盯着她的目光忽然垂了下去。“不，”他喃喃道，方才那点解脱从语气里褪得干干净净，“不，帮不上，是吧？顶多让我知道你有过那种感觉，算是给我一点可怜的安慰。”

帕特拼命按住一股冲动，想伸手过去，抚一抚他的头发。她猛地醒过神来；这正是她警告过自己要提防的危险——这正是她预料中尼古拉斯·德文会摆出的姿态，是可能藏着陷阱的诱饵。可他看上去那么孤单，那么惹人怜惜！忍住不碰他几乎用尽了她全身的力气；她的手指都发痒，想拂一拂他的脸颊。

“只有傻瓜才会两次踏进同一个陷阱，”她告诫自己。嘴上却说：“你答应过给我一个解释。你要是有任何借口，我愿意听听。”她的声音恢复了冷淡。

===Original===
“I haven’t any excuse,” he responded gloomily, “and the explanation is perhaps too bizarre, too fantastic for belief. *I* don’t believe it entirely; I suppose *you* couldn’t believe it at all.”

“You promised,” she repeated. The carefully assumed composure of her voice threatened to crack; this wistfulness of his was a powerful weapon against her defense.

“Oh, I’ll give you the explanation,” he said miserably. “I just wanted to warn you you’d not believe me.” He gave her a despondent glance. “Pat, as I love you I swear that what I tell you is the truth. Do you think you can believe me?”

“Yes,” she murmured. The tremor had reappeared in her voice despite her efforts.

Nicholas Devine turned his eyes toward the lake and began to speak.

===Chinese===
“我没有任何借口，”他阴郁地答道，“而这番解释恐怕太离奇、太荒诞，叫人没法相信。连我自己都不全信；我想你只怕根本不会信。”

“你答应过的，”她重复道。她刻意装出的平静眼看就要绷不住；他这份怅惘是攻破她防线的一件利器。

“哦，我会解释的，”他凄然说，“我只是想先提醒你，你是不会信我的。”他灰心丧气地看了她一眼。“帕特，我以我对你的爱起誓，我下面说的句句是真话。你觉得你能相信我吗？”

“能，”她喃喃道。尽管她百般克制，嗓音里的颤抖还是冒了出来。

尼古拉斯·德文把目光转向湖面，开口说了起来。

## IX Descent Into Avernus / 九 堕入阿维尔努斯

===Original===
Pat slammed the empty tumbler down on the checked tablecloth and buried her face in her hands, choking and gasping from the effects of the fiery liquor. Her throat burned, her mouth was parched by the acrid taste, and a conflagration seemed to be raging somewhere within her. Then she steadied, raised her eyes, and stared straight into the strange eyes of Nicholas Devine.

“Well?” she said fiercely. “Is that enough?”

He was watching her coldly as an image or a painting; the intensity of his gaze was more catlike than human. She moved her head aside; his eyes, without apparent shift, were still on hers, like the eyes of a pictured face. A resurgence of anger shook her at his immobility; his aloofness seemed to imply that nothing she could do would disturb him.

“Wasn’t it enough?” she screamed. “Wasn’t it? Then look!”

===Chinese===
帕特把空酒杯砰地砸在格子桌布上，双手捂住脸，被烈酒的劲儿呛得直喘。她喉咙火烧火燎，嘴里被辛辣的味道烧得发干，体内什么地方仿佛燃起了一场大火。随后她稳住自己，抬起眼，直直望进尼古拉斯·德文那双古怪的眼睛。

“怎么样？”她凶狠地说，“够了吗？”

他冷冷地注视着她，像看一尊塑像或一幅画；那目光之凝注，与其说像人，不如说像猫。她把头偏向一边；他的眼睛不见明显的转动，却依旧盯在她眼睛上，就像画中人的眼睛。他这份纹丝不动重新勾起她的怒火；他那种冷眼旁观的架势似乎在说，她做什么都搅不动他分毫。

“这还不够吗？”她尖叫道，“还不够吗？那你看好了！”

===Original===
She seized the bottle, poured another stream of the oily liquid into her glass, and raised it to her lips. Again the burning fluid excoriated her tongue and throat, and then suddenly, the tumbler was struck from her hand, spilling the rest of its contents on the table.

“That is enough,” said the icy voice of her companion.

“Oh, it is? We’ll see!” She snatched at the bottle, still more than half full. The thin hand of Nicholas Devine wrenched it violently away.

“Give me that!” she cried. “You wanted what you’re getting!” The warmth within her had reached the surface now; she felt flushed, excited, reckless, and desperately angry.

===Chinese===
她抓过酒瓶，往杯里又倒出一股油亮的酒液，举到唇边。灼热的酒液又一次磨得她舌头喉咙生疼，突然间，酒杯被人从她手里打飞，剩下的酒泼了一桌。

“够了。”同伴的嗓音冷若冰霜。

“哦，够了？走着瞧！”她伸手去抢那还剩大半瓶的酒瓶。尼古拉斯·德文瘦削的手一把将它猛地夺开。

“给我！”她喊道，“这是你自己找的！”此刻体内的燥热已经烧到了表面；她只觉得面红耳赤、亢奋、无所顾忌，还有豁出去的愤怒。

===Original===
The other set the bottle deliberately on the floor; he rose, circled the table, and stood glaring down at her with that same inexplicable expression. Suddenly he raised his hand; twisting her black hair in his fist, he dealt her a stinging blow across the lips half-opened to scream, then flung her away so violently that she nearly sprawled from her chair.

The scream died in her throat; dazed by the blow, she dropped her head to the table, while sobs of pain and fear shook her. Coherent thought had departed, and she knew only that her lips stung, that her clear, active little mind was caught in a mesh of befuddlement. She couldn’t think; she could only sob in the haze of dizziness that encompassed her. After a long interval, she raised her head, opened her eyes upon a swaying, unsteady world, and faced her companion, who had silently resumed his seat.

“Nicholas Devine,” she said slowly, speaking as if each word were an effort, “I hate you!”

“Ah!” he said and was again silent.

===Chinese===
对方不慌不忙地把酒瓶搁到地上；他站起身，绕过桌子，带着那副说不清道不明的神情居高临下地瞪着她。突然他扬起手，攥住她的黑发缠在拳上，朝着她刚张开要尖叫的嘴唇狠狠抽了一记，又猛地把她掼开，差点把她从椅子上摔下去。

尖叫卡死在喉咙里；她被那一巴掌打得晕头转向，一头伏在桌上，痛苦和恐惧的抽泣震撼着她。条理的思绪已经远遁，她只知道嘴唇火辣辣地疼，只知道她那颗清明活跃的小脑袋陷进了一张混沌的网。她想不了事情；只能在包围着她的眩晕迷雾里呜咽。过了好久，她才抬起头，睁开眼面对一个摇摇晃晃、站不稳的世界，面对已悄然坐回原位的同伴。

“尼古拉斯·德文，”她缓缓地说，每个字都像要费尽力气，“我恨你！”

“啊！”他说了一声，又归于沉默。

===Original===
She forced her eyes to focus on his face, while his features danced vaguely as if smoke flowed between the two of them. It was as if there were smoke in her mind as well; she made a great effort to rise above the clouds that bemused her thoughts.

“Take me home,” she said. “Nicholas, I want to go home.”

“Why should I?” he asked impassively. “The experiment is hardly begun.”

“Experiment?” she echoed dully. “Oh, yes﻿—experiment. I’m an experiment.”

===Chinese===
她强逼自己的眼睛对准他的脸，可他的五官虚虚晃晃，仿佛两人之间淌过一缕青烟。她脑子里也像罩了烟；她费了大力气，想从那团迷惑神思的云雾里升上来。

“带我回家，”她说，“尼古拉斯，我要回家。”

“凭什么？”他无动于衷地问，“实验才刚刚开始。”

“实验？”她木然地应道，“哦，对——实验。我是个实验品。”

===Original===
“An experiment in evil,” he said.

“Yes﻿—in evil. And I hate you! That’s evil enough, isn’t it?”

He reached down, lifted the bottle to the table, and methodically poured himself a drink of the liquor. He raised it, watching the oily swirls in the light, then tipped the fluid to his lips while the girl gazed at him with a sullen set to her own lips. A tiny crimson spot had appeared in the corner of her mouth; at its sting, she raised her hand and brushed it away. She stared as if in unbelief at the small red smear it left on her fingers.

“Nicholas,” she said pleadingly, “won’t you take me home? Please, Nicholas, I want to leave here.”

===Chinese===
“一个关于恶的实验。”他说。

“对——关于恶。而我恨你！这够恶的了，不是吗？”

他俯身把酒瓶提到桌上，不紧不慢地给自己斟了一杯。他举杯对着光，端详酒液油亮的旋纹，然后仰头倒进嘴里；姑娘绷着嘴闷闷地望着他。她嘴角已渗出一点殷红；被那刺痛一激，她抬手抹了一把，盯着指尖留下的一小抹红痕，简直不敢相信似的。

“尼古拉斯，”她哀求道，“送我回家好不好？求你了，尼古拉斯，我想离开这儿。”

===Original===
“Do you hate me?” he asked, a queer twisting smile appearing on his lips.

“If you’ll take me home I won’t,” said Pat, snatching through the rising clouds of dizziness at a straw of logic. “You’re going to take me home, aren’t you?”

“Let me hear you say you hate me!” he demanded, rising again. The girl cringed away with a little whimper as he approached. “You hate me, don’t you?”

He twisted his hand again in her ebony hair, drawing her face back so that he stared down at it.

===Chinese===
“你恨我吗？”他问，唇边浮起一个古怪的、扭曲的笑。

“你送我回家我就不恨，”帕特说，在高涨的眩晕迷雾中拼命抓住一星半点的逻辑，“你会送我回家的，对不对？”

“让我听见你说你恨我！”他喝令道，又站了起来。他一走近，姑娘便呜咽一声缩避开去。“你恨我，不是吗？”

他的手又缠进她乌黑的头发，把她的脸向后扯起，好让自己俯视着它。

===Original===
“There’s blood on your lips,” he said as if gloating. “Blood on your lips!”

He clutched her hair more tightly; abruptly he bent over her, pressing his mouth to hers. Her bruised lips burned with pain at the fierce pressure of his; she felt a sharp anguish at the impingement of his teeth. Yet the cloudy pall of dizziness about her was unbroken; she was too frightened and bewildered for resistance.

“Blood on your lips!” he repeated exultingly. “Now is the beauty of evil!”

“Nicholas,” she said wearily, clinging desperately to a remnant of logic, “what do you want of me? Tell me what you want and then let me go home.”

===Chinese===
“你唇上有血，”他说得像是心花怒放，“你唇上有血！”

他把她的头发攥得更紧；蓦地俯下身，把嘴压上她的嘴。她撞伤的双唇被他狠狠压得灼痛；他的牙齿磕上来，她感到一阵尖锐的苦痛。然而裹着她的那层眩晕的昏霾并未破开；她惊骇又茫然，全然无力抵抗。

“你唇上有血！”他得意扬扬地又说了一遍，“这便是恶之美！”

“尼古拉斯，”她疲惫地说，死死抱住残存的一丝逻辑，“你要我怎么样？告诉我要我做什么，然后放我回家。”

===Original===
“I want to show you the face of evil,” he said. “I want you to know the glory of evil, the loveliness of supreme evil!”

He dragged his chair around the table, placing it beside her. Seated, he drew her into his arms, where she lay passive, too limp and befuddled to resist. With a sudden movement, he turned her so that her back rested across his knees, her face gazing up into his. He stared intently down at her, and the light, shining at an angle into his eyes, suddenly struck out the red glow that lingered in them.

“I want you to know the power of evil,” he murmured. “The irresistible, incomprehensible fascination of it, and the unspeakable pleasures of indulgence in it.”

Pat scarcely heard him; she was struggling now in vain against the overwhelming fumes of the alcohol she had consumed. The room was wavering around her, and behind her despair and terror, a curious elation was thrusting itself into her consciousness.

===Chinese===
“我要让你看看恶的面目，”他说，“我要你认识恶的荣光，至恶的可爱！”

他把椅子拖过桌子那边，摆到她身旁。坐定后，他把她拉进怀里；她软瘫瘫、昏沉沉，任由摆布，无力抗拒。他猛一动，把她转得仰面横躺在他膝上，脸仰望着他。他目不转睛地俯视她；灯光斜射进他的眼睛，蓦地点亮了潜伏其中的红光。

“我要你认识恶的力量，”他喃喃道，“认识它那不可抗拒、不可思议的魔力，认识沉溺其中那难以言说的快感。”

帕特几乎听不见他说话；此刻她在徒劳地对抗着喝下的烈酒那席卷一切的酒气。房间在她四周荡漾摇晃；在绝望与恐惧的后面，一种奇异的亢奋正挤进她的意识。

===Original===
“Evil,” she echoed vaguely.

“Blood on your lips!” he muttered, peering down at her. “Taste the unutterable pleasure of kisses on bloody lips; drain the sweet anguish of pain, the fierce delight of suffering!”

He bent down; again his lips pressed upon hers, but this time she felt herself responding. Some still sane portion of her brain rebelled, but the intoxication of sense and alcohol was dominant. Suddenly she was clinging to him, returning his kisses, glorying in the pain of her lacerated lips. A red mist suffused her; she had no consciousness of anything save the exquisite pain of the kiss, that somehow contrived to transform itself into an ecstasy of delight. She lay gasping as the other withdrew his lips.

“You see!” he gloated. “You understand! Evil is open to us, and all the unutterable pleasures of the damned, who cry out in transports of joy at the bite of the flames of Hell. Do you see?”

===Chinese===
“恶。”她恍恍惚惚地应了一声。

“你唇上有血！”他低头凝视着她，喃喃道，“尝尝带血的吻那难以言传的快感；饮尽痛苦那甜美的煎熬、受难那酣畅的欢愉！”

他俯下身；他的唇再度压上她的唇，而这一回，她发觉自己在回应。她脑中尚存清明的那一小块在反抗，可感官与酒精的迷醉占了上风。突然间她攀住了他，回吻着他，在唇伤的疼痛里陶然欲醉。一层红雾漫过她全身；她什么都意识不到了，只剩下那个吻的妙不可言的痛楚——它竟不知怎么化成了一种狂喜。对方撤回嘴唇时，她躺在那儿直喘。

“你瞧见了吧！”他幸灾乐祸，“你懂了！恶向我们敞开，还有那些被诅咒者难以言传的种种快乐——地狱之火噬咬上来，他们竟狂喜地放声欢叫。你瞧见了吗？”

===Original===
The girl made no answer, sobbing in a chaotic mingling of pain and excruciating pleasure. She was incapable of speech or connected thought; the alcohol beat against her brain with a persistence that defied resistance. After a moment, she stirred, struggling erect to a sitting posture.

“Evil!” she said dizzily. “Evil and good﻿—what’s difference? All in a lifetime!”

She felt a surge of tipsy elation, and then the muffled music of the mechanical piano, drifting through the closed door, penetrated her befuddled consciousness.

“I want to dance!” she cried. “I’m drunk and I want to dance! Am I drunk?” she appealed to her companion.

===Chinese===
姑娘没有作答，在痛苦与刺骨快意杂糅的一片混沌中抽泣着。她说不出话，也串不起一个完整的念头；酒精一下又一下捶打着她的大脑，不由分说。过了一会儿，她动了动，挣扎着撑起身子坐直。

“恶！”她晕晕乎乎地说，“恶啊善啊——有什么两样？一辈子的事罢了！”

一阵醺醺然的快活涌上她心头；接着，自动钢琴那闷闷的乐声透过关着的门飘进来，钻进她迷乱的意识。

“我要跳舞！”她嚷道，“我醉了，我要跳舞！我醉了吗？”她向同伴求助似的问。

===Original===
“Yes,” he said.

“I am not! I just want to dance, only it’s hot in here. Dance with me, Nicholas﻿—show me an evil dance! I want to dance with the Devil, and I will! You’re the Devil, name and all! I want to dance with Old Nick himself!”

She rose unsteadily from her chair; instantly the room reeled crazily about her and she fell sprawling. She felt the grasp of arms beneath her shoulders, raising her erect; she leaned against the wall and heard herself laughing wildly.

“Funny room!” she said. “Evil room﻿—on pivots!”

===Chinese===
“醉了。”他说。

“我才没有！我就是想跳舞，只是这屋里太热了。陪我跳，尼古拉斯——给我跳一支恶之舞！我要同魔鬼跳舞，我说到做到！你就是魔鬼，连名字都一样！我要同老尼克本尊跳舞！”

她摇摇晃晃地从椅子上站起来；霎时间房间围着她疯狂打转，她扑通栽倒。她感到两条手臂在她腋下把她架起；她靠在墙上，听见自己在放声狂笑。

“有意思的屋子！”她说，“邪恶的屋子——装了转轴！”

===Original===
“You’re still to learn,” came the toneless voice of Nicholas Devine. “Do you want to see the face of evil?”

“Sure!” she said. “Got a good memory for faces!”

She realized that he was fumbling with the catch of her dress on her left shoulder; again some remnant, some vestige of sanity deep in her brain warned her.

“Mustn’t,” she said vaguely.

===Chinese===
“你还什么都不懂，”尼古拉斯·德文那毫无起伏的嗓音响起，“你想看看恶的面目吗？”

“当然想！”她说，“我记性可好呢，过目不忘！”

她意识到他正在摸她左肩上裙子的挂钩；脑子深处残存的、最后一点清明又一次向她报警。

“不可以。”她含含糊糊地说。

===Original===
Then suddenly the catch was open; the dress dropped away around her, crumpling to a shapeless blob of cloth about her diminutive feet. She covered her face with her hands, fighting to hold that last, vanishing vestige of sobriety, while she stood swaying drunkenly against the wall.

Then Nicholas Devine’s arms were about her again; she felt the sharp sting of his kisses on her throat. He swung her about, bent her backwards across the low table; she was conscious of a bewildered sensation of helplessness and of little else.

“Now the supreme glory of evil!” he was muttering in her ear. She felt his hands on her bare shoulders as he pressed her backward.

Then, abruptly, he paused, releasing her. She sat dizzily erect, following the direction of his gaze. In the half open door stood the nondescript bartender leering in at them.

===Chinese===
然后挂钩忽然开了；裙子从她身上褪下，在她纤小的脚边皱成一团没个形状的布堆。她双手捂住脸，死命攥住那最后一丝正在消散的清醒，整个人醉醺醺地倚着墙摇晃。

接着尼古拉斯·德文的手臂又环住了她；她感到他的吻扎痛她的颈项。他把她一转，让她向后仰倒在低桌上；她只觉一阵无能为力的迷乱，此外便什么都不知道了。

“现在，是至恶的极乐！”他对着她耳朵喃喃。她感到他的双手按上她裸露的双肩，把她往后压。

接着他突然顿住，松开了她。她晕乎乎地坐直，顺着他的目光望去。半开的门口站着那个说不上长相的酒保，正阴笑着往里张望。

## XXII Doctor and Devil / 二十二 医生与魔鬼

===Original===
Pat rushed to the door, out upon the porch, and down to the street. Dr. Horker followed her to the entrance and stood watching her as she darted toward the dejected figure beside the car.

“Nick!” she cried. “I’m here, Honey. You heard me, didn’t you?”

She flung herself into his arms; he held her eagerly, pressing a hasty, tender kiss on her lips.

“You heard me!” she murmured.

===Chinese===
帕特冲到门口，奔出门廊，一路跑到街上。霍克医生跟到门口，站着目送她飞奔向车旁那个垂头丧气的身影。

“尼克！”她喊道，“我在这儿，亲爱的。你听见我了，对不对？”

她扑进他怀里；他急切地抱住她，在她唇上匆匆印下一个温柔的吻。

“你听见我了！”她喃喃道。

===Original===
“Yes.” His voice was husky, strained. “What is it, Pat? Tell me quickly﻿—God knows how much time we have!”

“It’s Dr. Carl. He’ll help us, Nick.”

“Help us! No one can help us, dear. No one!”

“He’ll try. It can’t do any harm, Honey. Come in with me. Now!”

===Chinese===
“听见了。”他的嗓音沙哑、紧绷。“怎么回事，帕特？快告诉我——天知道我们还有多少时间！”

“是卡尔医生。他会帮我们的，尼克。”

“帮我们！没人帮得了我们，亲爱的。没有人！”

“他愿意试试。反正不会有坏处，亲爱的。跟我进来。现在就来！”

===Original===
“It’s useless, I tell you!”

“But come,” she pleaded. “Come anyway!”

“Pat, I tell you this battle has to be fought out by me alone. I’m the only one who can do anything at all and,” he lowered his voice, “Pat, I’m losing!”

“Nick!”

===Chinese===
“没用的，我告诉你！”

“可你还是来吧，”她恳求道，“不管怎样来一趟！”

“帕特，我告诉你，这场仗必须由我一个人打到底。只有我还勉强能做点什么，而且，”他压低声音，“帕特，我快输了！”

“尼克！”

===Original===
“That’s why I came tonight. I was too cowardly to make our last meeting﻿—Monday evening in the park﻿—a definite farewell. I wanted to, but I weakened. So tonight, Pat, it’s a final goodbye, and you thank Heaven for it!”

“Oh, Nick dear!”

“It was touch and go whether I came at all tonight. It was a struggle, Pat; *he* is as strong as I am now. Or stronger.”

The girl gazed searchingly into his worn, weary face. He looked miserably ill, she thought; he seemed as exhausted as one who had been engaged in a physical battle.

===Chinese===
“所以我今晚才来。上回见面——星期一晚上在公园——我太懦弱了，没能把那变成正式的诀别。我本想那样，可我软了心肠。所以今晚，帕特，就是最后的道别了，你该谢天谢地才是！”

“哦，亲爱的尼克！”

“今晚我到底来不来，本身就在毫厘之间。那是一场搏斗，帕特；他现在跟我一样强了。或者更强。”

姑娘细细端详他那张憔悴疲惫的脸。他看上去病得厉害，她想；他疲惫得像刚经历过一场肉搏的人。

===Original===
“Nick,” she said insistently, “I don’t care what you say, you’re coming in with me. Only for a little while.”

She tugged at his hand, dragging him reluctantly after her. He followed her to the porch where the open door still framed the great figure of the Doctor.

“You know Dr. Carl,” she said.

“Come inside,” growled Horker. Pat noticed the gruffness of his voice, his lack of any cordiality, but she said nothing as she pulled her reluctant companion through the door and into the library.

===Chinese===
“尼克，”她执拗地说，“不管你说什么，你都得跟我进来。就一小会儿。”

她拽着他的手，硬把不情愿的他拖在身后。他跟着她走到门廊，敞开的门里仍框着医生魁梧的身影。

“你认识卡尔医生的。”她说。

“进来吧。”霍克瓮声瓮气地说。帕特注意到他语气生硬，不见半点热络，但她什么也没说，只把不情愿的同伴拉进门，带进书房。

===Original===
The Doctor drew up another chair, and Pat, more accustomed to his devices, observed that he placed it in such position that the lamp cast a stream of radiance on Nick’s face. She sank into her own chair and waited silently for developments.

“Well,” said Horker, turning his shrewd old eyes on Nick’s countenance, “let’s get down to cases. Pat’s told me what she knows; we can take that much for granted. Is there anything more you might want to tell?”

“No, sir,” responded the youth wearily. “I’ve told Pat all I know.”

“Humph! Maybe I can ask some leading questions, then. Will you answer them?”

===Chinese===
医生又拖过来一把椅子；帕特对他的花样再熟悉不过，一眼就看出他摆的位置恰好让灯光直泻在尼克脸上。她坐进自己的椅子里，静待事态发展。

“那么，”霍克把那双精明的老眼转向尼克的脸，“咱们就开门见山。帕特把她知道的都告诉我了；这部分我们就算定下了。你还有什么要补充的吗？”

“没有了，先生，”青年疲惫地答道，“我知道的都告诉帕特了。”

“哼！那也许我可以问些诱导性的问题。你愿意回答吗？”

===Original===
“Of course, any that I can.”

“All right. Now,” the Doctor’s voice took on a cool professional edge, “you’ve had these﻿—uh﻿—attacks as long as you can remember. Is that right?”

“Yes.”

“But they’ve been more severe of late?”

===Chinese===
“当然，凡是答得上的我都答。”

“好。那么，”医生的嗓音带上了职业性的冷静锋芒，“这种——呃——发作，从你有记忆起就有。对吗？”

“对。”

“但近来发作得更凶了？”

===Original===
“Much worse, sir!”

“Since when?”

“Since﻿—about as long as I’ve known Pat. Four or five weeks.”

“M﻿—m,” droned the Doctor. “You’ve no idea of the cause for this increase in the malignancy of the attacks?”

===Chinese===
“凶得多，先生！”

“从什么时候开始的？”

“从——大概是我认识帕特那会儿起。四五个星期吧。”

“唔——嗯，”医生拖长了声调，“发作越发恶毒，是什么缘故，你一点头绪也没有？”

===Original===
“No sir,” said Nick, after a barely perceptible hesitation.

“You don’t think the cause could be in any way connected with, let us say, the emotional disturbances attending your acquaintance with Pat here?”

“No, sir,” said the youth flatly.

“All right,” said Horker. “Let that angle go for the present. Are there any aftereffects from these spells?”

===Chinese===
“没有，先生。”尼克说，之前有一丝几乎察觉不到的迟疑。

“你不认为这病因，比方说，跟你认识这位帕特引发的情绪波动有什么关联？”

“不关联，先生。”青年干巴巴地说。

“好吧，”霍克说，“这条线索先放一放。这些发作有没有什么后遗症？”

===Original===
“Yes. There’s always a splitting headache.” He closed his eyes. “I have one of them now.”

“Localized?”

“Sir?”

“Is the pain in any particular region? Forehead, temples, eyes, or so forth?”

===Chinese===
“有。每次都头痛欲裂。”他闭上眼，“我现在就疼着呢。”

“疼在固定部位吗？”

“先生？”

“疼是不是集中在某一处？前额、太阳穴、眼睛，之类的？”

===Original===
“No. Just a nasty headache.”

“But no other aftereffects?”

“I can’t think of any others. Except, perhaps, a feeling of exhaustion after I’ve gone through what I’ve just finished.” He closed his eyes as if to shut out the recollection.

“Well,” mused the Doctor, “we’ll forget the physical symptoms. What happens to your individuality, your own consciousness, while you’re suffering an attack?”

===Chinese===
