“不是。就是疼得凶。”

“此外没有别的后遗症了？”

“想不出别的了。大概除了——每次熬完我刚熬过的这种事之后，有一种精疲力竭的感觉。”他闭上眼，仿佛要把那记忆关在外面。

“嗯，”医生沉吟道，“身体症状我们先不管。发作的时候，你的人格、你自己的意识，怎么样了？”

===Original===
“Nothing happens to it,” said Nick with a suppressed shudder. “I watch and hear, but what *he* does is beyond my control. It’s terrifying﻿—horrible!” he burst out suddenly.

“Doubtless,” responded Horker smoothly. “What about the other? Does that one stand by while you’re in the saddle?”

“I don’t know,” muttered Nick dully. “Of course he does!” he added abruptly. “I can feel his presence at all times﻿—even now. He’s always lurking, waiting to spring forth, as soon as I relax!”

“Humph!” ejaculated the Doctor. “How do you manage to sleep?”

===Chinese===
“它毫发无损，”尼克说着，压抑住一阵战栗，“我看得到、听得到，可他干的事全不由我做主。那太吓人了——太可怕了！”他突然脱口喊道。

“想必是的，”霍克不紧不慢地应道，“那另一个呢？你执掌缰绳的时候，他就在一边待着？”

“我不知道，”尼克木然咕哝，“他当然在！”他蓦地又补上一句，“我随时都能感到他在——连此刻都是。他总在暗处潜伏着，只等我松懈，就扑出来！”

“哼！”医生猛地哼了一声。“那你怎么睡得着觉？”

===Original===
“By waiting for exhaustion,” said Nick wearily. “By waiting until I can stay awake no longer.”

“And can you bring this other personality into dominance? Can you change controls, so to speak, at will?”

“Why﻿—yes,” the youth answered, hesitating as if puzzled. “Yes, I suppose I could.”

“Let’s see you, then.”

===Chinese===
“等着熬到筋疲力尽，”尼克疲惫地说，“等到再也撑不住不睡为止。”

“那你能让另一个人格掌权吗？打个比方，你能随意切换操控吗？”

“呃——能，”青年答道，迟疑着，像是自己也拿不准，“能吧，我想可以。”

“那就让我们看看。”

===Original===
“But﻿—” Horror was in his voice.

“No, Dr. Carl!” Pat interjected in fright. “I won’t let him!”

“I thought you declared yourself out of this,” said Horker with a shrewd glance at the girl.

“Then I’m back in it! I won’t let him do what you want﻿—anyway, not that!”

===Chinese===
“可是——”他的声音里满是惊恐。

“不行，卡尔医生！”帕特惊惶地插话，“我不许他这么做！”

“我记得你宣布过退出这件事了。”霍克说着，精明地瞥了姑娘一眼。

“那我现在又回来了！你想让他做的事我不许——反正不许做那个！”

===Original===
“Pat,” said the Doctor with an air of patience, “you want me to treat this affliction, don’t you? Isn’t that what both of you want?”

The girl murmured a scarcely audible assent.

“Very well, then,” he proceeded. “Do you expect me to treat the thing blindly﻿—in the dark? Do you think I can guess at the cause without observing the effect?”

“No,” said Pat faintly.

===Chinese===
“帕特，”医生摆出耐心十足的样子说，“你要我治这个毛病，对不对？这不正是你们两个都想要的吗？”

姑娘应了一声，轻得几乎听不见。

“那好，”他接着说，“你指望我瞎治——摸黑治吗？你以为不看效果就能猜出病因？”

“不，”帕特有气无力地说。

===Original===
“So! Now then,” he turned to Nick, “Let’s see this transformation.”

“Must I?” asked the youth reluctantly.

“If you want my help.”

“All right,” he agreed with another tremor. He sat passively staring at the Doctor; a moment passed. Horker heard Pat’s nervous breathing; other than that, the room was in silence. Nicholas Devine closed his eyes, brushed his hand across his forehead. A moment more and he opened them to gaze perplexedly at the Doctor.

===Chinese===
“这就对了！那么现在，”他转向尼克，“让我们看看这场变身吧。”

“非得这样吗？”青年不情愿地问。

“你要是想让我帮忙，就得这样。”

“好吧。”他又打了个寒战，应承下来。他一动不动地坐着，凝视医生；片刻过去。霍克听得见帕特紧张的呼吸声；除此之外，房间里一片寂静。尼古拉斯·德文合上眼，抬手抹了抹前额。又过了片刻，他睁开眼，一脸困惑地望着医生。

===Original===
“He won’t!” he muttered in astonishment. “He won’t do it!”

“Humph!” snapped Horker, ignoring Pat’s murmur of relief. “Finicky devil, isn’t he? Likes to pick company he can bully!”

“I don’t understand it!” Nick’s face was blank. “He’s been tormenting me until just now!” He looked at the Doctor. “You don’t think I’m lying about it, do you, Dr. Horker?”

“Not consciously,” replied the other coolly. “If I thought you were responsible for a few of the indignities perpetrated on Pat here, I’d waste no time in questions, young man. I’d be relieving myself of certain violent impulses instead.”

===Chinese===
“他不肯！”他愕然喃喃道，“他不肯出来！”

“哼！”霍克厉声道，全不理会帕特如释重负的低呼，“还挺挑理的魔鬼，不是吗？专挑欺负得了的人作伴！”

“我搞不懂！”尼克一脸茫然，“他直到刚才还在折磨我！”他看向医生。“您不会以为我在这事上撒谎吧，霍克医生？”

“至少不是有意的，”对方冷冷地答道，“年轻人，要是我认为强加在这位帕特身上的那几桩辱行该由你负责，我就不会浪费时间问东问西了。我会去好好发泄一下心里的某些暴力冲动。”

===Original===
“I *couldn’t* harm Pat!”

“You gave a passable imitation of it, then! However, that’s beside the point; as I say, I don’t hold you responsible for aberrations which I believe are beyond your control. The main thing is a diagnosis.”

“Do you know what it is?” cut in Pat eagerly.

“Not yet﻿—at least, not for certain. There’s only one real method available; these questions will get us nowhere. We’ll have to psychoanalyze you, young man.”

===Chinese===
“我是绝不可能伤害帕特的！”

“那你模仿得可够像样的！不过这是题外话；正如我所说，这些失常我相信不由你控制，我不追究你。要紧的是诊断。”

“您知道那是什么吗？”帕特急切地插嘴。

“还不知道——至少还不确定。真正管用的办法只有一个；这样问下去问不出名堂。我们得给你做精神分析，年轻人。”

===Original===
“I don’t care what you do, if you can offer any hope!” he declared vehemently. “Let’s get it over!”

“Not as easy as all that!” rumbled Horker. “It takes time; and besides, it can’t be successful with the subject in a hectic mood such as yours.” He glanced at his watch. “Moreover, it’s after midnight.”

He turned to Nicholas Devine. “We’ll make it Saturday evening,” he said. “Meanwhile, young man, you’re not to see Pat. Not at all﻿—understand? You can see her here when you come.”

“That’s infinitely more than I’d planned for myself,” said the youth in a low voice. “I’d abandoned the hope of seeing her.”

===Chinese===
“只要您能给出一线希望，你们怎么做都行！”他激烈地宣告，“干脆现在就做吧！”

“哪有那么容易！”霍克嗡声道，“那需要时间；再说，受试者情绪像你这样激荡，是做不成的。”他看了看表。“况且，已经过了午夜。”

他转向尼古拉斯·德文。“就定星期六晚上吧，”他说，“在此期间，年轻人，你不得见帕特。一律不见——明白吗？你来的时候，可以在这儿见到她。”

“这已经比我给自己安排的好出无数倍了，”青年低声说，“我本来已经放弃见她的指望了。”

===Original===
He rose and moved toward the door, and the others followed. At the entrance he paused; he leaned down to plant a brief, tender kiss on the girl’s lips, and moved wordlessly out of the door. Pat watched him enter his car, and followed the vehicle with her eyes until it disappeared. Then she turned to Horker.

“Do you really know anything about it?” she queried. “Have you any theory at all?”

“He’s not lying,” said the Doctor thoughtfully. “I watched him closely; he believes he’s telling the truth.”

“He is. I know what I saw!”

===Chinese===
他起身朝门口走去，其余两人跟着。在门口他停了停；他俯身在姑娘唇上匆匆印下一个温柔的吻，一言不发地出了门。帕特望着他上车，目光追着那辆车，直到它消失。然后她转向霍克。

“您真的对此有底吗？”她问，“您到底有没有一点看法？”

“他没撒谎，”医生若有所思地说，“我盯着他看得很紧；他自己相信句句是实。”

“他本来就没撒谎。我亲眼见过的！”

===Original===
“He hasn’t the signs of praecox or depressive,” mused the Doctor. “It’s puzzling; it’s one of those functional aberrations, or a fixed delusion of some kind. We’ll find out just what it is.”

“It’s the devil,” declared Pat positively. “I don’t care what sort of scientific tag you give it﻿—that’s what it is. You doctors can hide a lot of ignorance under a long name.”

Horker paid no attention to her remarks. “We’ll see what the psychoanalysis brings out,” he said. “I shouldn’t be surprised if the whole thing were the result of a defense mechanism erected by a timid child in an effort to evade responsibility. That’s what it sounds like.”

“It’s a devil!” reiterated Pat.

===Chinese===
“他没有早发性痴呆或抑郁症的迹象，”医生沉吟道，“真是费解；这是那种官能性失常之一，要么就是某种固定妄想。我们总会查个水落石出。”

“那就是魔鬼，”帕特斩钉截铁地说，“你们给它贴什么科学标签我都不管——它就是魔鬼。你们这些医生啊，一个长长的名词底下能藏多少无知。”

霍克没理会她的话。“且看精神分析能挖出什么来，”他说，“就算整件事不过是一个胆小孩子为逃避责任而筑起的防御机制所致，我也不会惊讶。听起来就是这么回事。”

“是魔鬼！”帕特重申。

===Original===
“Well,” said the Doctor, “if it is, it has one thing in common with every spook or devil I ever heard of.”

“What’s that?”

“It refuses to appear under any conditions where one has a chance to examine it. It’s like one of these temperamental mediums trying to perform under a spotlight.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，”医生说，“它要是魔鬼，那它倒有一处跟我听说过的所有鬼怪魔鬼一模一样。”

“哪一处？”

“凡是让人有机会查验的场合，它一概拒绝现身。活像那种娇气的灵媒，一到聚光灯下就撒泼罢演。”

## VIII Gateway to Evil / 八 通向邪恶之门
===Original===
Pat stared curiously at the apparition but made no move to alight from the vehicle. She was conscious of no fear, only a sense of wonder and perplexity. After all, this was merely Nick, her own harmless, adoring Nick, in some sort of mysterious masquerade, and she felt full confidence in her ability to handle him under any circumstances.
“Where’s here?” she said, remaining motionless in her place.
“A place to dance,” came the toneless reply.
Pat eyed him; a street car rumbled past, and the brief glow from its lighted windows swept over his face. Suddenly the visage was that of Nick; the crimson glare of the eyes was imperceptible, and the features were the well-known appurtenances of Nicholas Devine, but queerly tensed and strained.

===Chinese===
帕特好奇地盯着那个幽灵般的身影，却没有动身下车。她并不觉得害怕，只有一种惊奇与困惑交织的感觉。说到底，这不过是尼克，她那个无害的、痴恋着她的尼克，在玩某种神秘的化装把戏；她完全相信自己无论如何都应付得了他。
“这是哪儿？”她说，原地纹丝不动。
“跳舞的地方。”回答的嗓音平淡无调。
帕特端详着他；一辆有轨电车隆隆驶过，车窗里的灯火短暂地扫亮他的脸。那张脸霎时成了尼克的脸；眼中猩红的凶光不见踪影，眉眼也是尼古拉斯·德文那副熟悉的样子，只是出奇地紧绷僵硬。

===Original===
“A trick of the light,” she thought, as the street car lumbered away, and again a faint gleam of crimson appeared. She gazed curiously at the youth, who stood impassively returning her survey as he held the door of the car. But the face was the face of Nick, she perceived, probably in one of his grim moods.
She transferred her glance to the building opposite which they had stopped. The strains of the mechanical piano had ceased; blank, shaded windows faced them, around whose edges glowed a subdued light from within. A drab, battered, paintless shack, she thought, dismal and unpleasant; while she gazed, the sound of the discordant music recommenced, adding, it seemed, the last unprepossessing item.
“It doesn’t look very attractive, Nick,” she observed dubiously.
“I find it so, however.”

===Chinese===
“光线耍的把戏，”她想，随着电车轰隆隆驶远，眼底又泛起一抹淡淡的红。她好奇地凝视着这年轻人；他扶着车门，木然任她打量。可那张脸终究是尼克的脸，她看出来了，多半是又犯了他那股阴沉的性子。
她把目光移向他们停靠处对面的那座房子。自动钢琴的乐声已经停歇；一扇扇挂了帘的暗窗正对着他们，窗沿四周渗出屋内幽幽的微光。一所灰扑扑、破破烂烂、连漆都不上的棚屋，她想，又阴惨又不痛快；她正望着，那阵刺耳的音乐重新响起，像是给这副败象凑上了最后一样。
“这地方看着可不怎么诱人，尼克。”她迟疑地说。
“我却觉得挺诱人。”

===Original===
“Then you’ve been here?”
“Yes.”
“But I thought you said you didn’t know any place to go.”
“This one hadn’t occurred to me﻿—then.”

===Chinese===
“那你来过这儿？”
“来过。”
“可我记得你说过，你不知道有什么地方可去。”
“当时——没想到这个地方。”

===Original===
“Well,” she said crisply, “I could have done as well as this with my eyes closed. It doesn’t appeal to me at all, Nick.”
“Nevertheless, here’s where we’ll go. You’re apt to find it﻿—interesting.”
“Look here, Nicholas Devine!” Pat snapped, “What makes you think you can bully me? No one has ever succeeded yet!”
“I said you’d find it interesting.” His voice was unchanged; she stared at him in complete bafflement.

===Chinese===
“哼，”她干脆利落地说，“我闭着眼睛也能找到这么个地方。这地方一点儿也不合我的意，尼克。”
“话虽如此，我们要去的就是这儿。你多半会觉得它——有意思。”
“听着，尼古拉斯·德文！”帕特厉声道，“你凭什么觉得能欺负我？到现在还没有人得逞过！”
“我说了你会觉得有意思。”他的嗓音毫无变化；她彻底茫然地盯着他。

===Original===
“Oh, Nick!” she exclaimed in suddenly softer tones. “What difference does it make? Didn’t I say anywhere would do, so we went together?” She smiled at him. “This will do if you wish, though really, Honey, I’d prefer not.”
“I do wish it,” the other said.
“All right, Honey,” said Pat the faintest trace of reluctance in her voice as she slipped from the car. “I stick to my bargains.”
She winced at the intensity of his grip as he took her arm to assist her. His fingers were like taut wires biting into her flesh.

===Chinese===
“哦，尼克！”她忽然放软了声调叫道，“那有什么要紧？我不是说了吗，只要咱们一起去，哪儿都行？”她朝他一笑。“你要愿意，这儿也行；不过说真的，亲爱的，我还是宁可不来。”
“我就是要来这儿。”对方说。
“好吧，亲爱的，”帕特一面溜下车，一面带着一丝若有若无的勉强说，“我说话算话。”
他搀她下车、握住她手臂时，那手劲大得让她直皱眉。他的手指像绷紧的铁丝，掐进她的肉里。

===Original===
“Nick!” she cried. “You’re hurting me! You’re bruising my arm!”
He released her; she rubbed the spot ruefully, then followed him to the door of the mysterious establishment. The unharmonious jangle of the piano dinned abruptly louder as he swung the door open. Pat entered and glanced around her at the room revealed.
Dull, smoky, dismal﻿—not the least exciting or interesting as yet, she thought. A short bar paralleled one wall, behind which lounged a little, thin, nondescript individual with a small mustache. Half a dozen tables filled the remainder of the room; four or five occupied by the clientele of the place, as unsavory a group as the girl could recall having encountered on the hither side of the motion picture screen. Two women tittered as Nick entered; then with one accord, the eyes of the entire group fixed on Pat, where she stood drawing her wrap more closely about her, standing uncomfortably behind her escort. And the piano tinkled its discords in the far corner.

===Chinese===
“尼克！”她叫道，“你弄疼我了！我胳膊都要给你捏坏了！”
他松开她；她懊恼地揉了揉那处地方，跟着他朝那家神秘场所的门走去。他一把推开门，钢琴那阵不谐和的叮当声陡然轰响起来。帕特走进去，四下打量这间显露在眼前的屋子。
阴暗、烟雾腾腾、愁惨——眼下还谈不上半点刺激或有趣，她想。一面墙边平行横着一张短酒吧台，台后懒洋洋倚着个瘦小不起眼、留着两撇小胡子的男人。屋里其余地方摆着六张桌子；其中四五张坐着这儿的座客，这样不堪的一伙人，姑娘回想起来，在电影银幕的这一边还真没遇见过。尼克进门时，两个女人吃吃地笑；随即，全屋人的目光不约而同地钉在帕特身上，她站在那儿把披肩往身上拢得更紧，局促不安地躲在男伴背后。远处的角落里，钢琴还在叮叮咚咚地弹着它的刺耳噪音。

===Original===
“Same place,” said Nick shortly to the bartender, ignoring the glances of the others. Pat followed him across the room to a door, into a hall, thence into a smaller room furnished merely with a table and four chairs. The nondescript man stood waiting in the doorway as Nick took her wrap and seated her in one of the chairs.
“Quart,” he said laconically, and the bartender disappeared.
Pat stared intently, studiously, into the face of her companion. Nick’s face, certainly; here in full light there was no trace of the red-eyed horror she had fancied out there in the semidarkness of the street. Or was there? Now﻿—when he turned, when the light struck his eyes at an angle, was that a glint of crimson? Still, the features were Nick’s, only a certain grim intensity foreign to him lurked about the set of his mouth, the narrowed eyelids.
“Well!” she said. “So this is Paris! What are you trying to do﻿—teach me capital L﻿—life? And where do we dance?”

===Chinese===
“老位子。”尼克简短地对酒保说，不理会旁人的目光。帕特跟着他穿过屋子到一扇门前，进了门厅，再从那儿进一间小些的屋子，屋里只摆着一张桌子、四把椅子。尼克接过她的披肩，让她在一张椅子上坐下，那个不起眼的男人立在门口候着。
“一夸脱。”他言简意赅地说，酒保便不见了。
帕特一心一意、仔仔细细地盯着同伴的脸。尼克的脸，没错；这儿灯火通明，外面街上半暗里她疑心瞧见的红眼怪物，在这儿没有一丝痕迹。还是说有？这一刻——他一转身，灯光斜斜打在他眼睛上，那是不是一点猩红的微光？不过，五官终归是尼克的，只是他嘴角的绷紧、眯起的眼睑四周，潜着一种他本没有的阴沉的狠劲。
“好嘛！”她说，“原来这就是巴黎！你想干什么——教我见识大写的‘生活’？那我们在哪儿跳舞？”

===Original===
“In here.”
“And what kind of quart was that you ordered? You know how little I drink, and I’m darned particular about even that little.”
“You’ll like this.”
“I doubt it.”

===Chinese===
“就在这儿。”
“还有，你叫的是哪一种夸脱？你知道我酒量多小，就连那一小口我还挑三拣四呢。”
“你会喜欢这个的。”
“我可不觉得。”

===Original===
“I said you’ll like it,” he reiterated in flat tones.
“I heard you say it.” She regarded him with a puzzled frown. “Nick,” she said suddenly, “I’ve decided I like you better in your gentle pose; this masterful attitude isn’t becoming, and you can forget what I said about wishing you’d display it oftener.”
“You’ll like that, too.”
“Again I doubt it. Nick, dear, don’t spoil another evening like that last one!”

===Chinese===
“我说了你会喜欢。”他用平板的声调重申。
“我听见了。”她皱着眉、一脸困惑地打量他。“尼克，”她忽然说，“我拿定了主意，我还是喜欢你那副温文的做派；这副专断的架势跟你很不相称，我说过盼你多摆出几回，那话你只管忘了。”
“那个你也会喜欢的。”
“我又不觉得了。尼克，亲爱的，别再毁一个晚上，跟上回那样！”

===Original===
“This one won’t be like the last one!”
“But Honey﻿—” she paused at the entrance of the bartender bearing a tray, an opened bottle of ginger ale, two glasses of ice, and a flask of oily amber liquid. He deposited the assortment on the red-checked tablecloth.
“Two dollars,” he said, pocketed the money and silently retired.
“Nicholas,” said the girl tartly, “there’s enough of that poison for a regiment.”

===Chinese===
“这一晚跟上回那晚不一样！”
“可是亲爱的——”酒保托着托盘进来，她顿住了；盘里是一瓶开了盖的姜汁汽水、两杯冰，还有一瓶油亮亮的琥珀色酒液。他把这一摊东西搁在红格桌布上。
“两块。”他说，收了钱，一声不响地退了出去。
“尼古拉斯，”姑娘刻薄地说，“这毒药够灌一个团的了。”

===Original===
“I don’t think so.”
“Well, I won’t drink it, and I won’t let you drink it! So now what?”
“I think you’ll do both.”
“I don’t!” she snapped. “And I don’t like this, Nick﻿—the place, or the liquor, or your attitude, or anything. We’re going to leave!”

===Chinese===
“我看不够。”
“哼，我不喝，也不许你喝！现在怎么办？”
“我看你两样都会做。”
“我才不呢！”她厉声道，“我也不喜欢这个，尼克——这地方、这酒、你这副做派，什么都不喜欢。我们要走了！”

===Original===
Instead of answering, he pulled the cork from the bottle, pouring a quantity of the amber fluid into each of the tumblers. To one he added an equal quantity of ginger ale, and set it deliberately squarely in front of Pat. She frowned at it distastefully, and shook her head.
“No,” she said. “Not I. I’m leaving.”
She made no move, however; her eyes met those of her companion, gazing at her with a cold intentness in their curious amber depths. And again﻿—was that a flash of red? Impulsively she reached out her hand, touched his.
“Oh, Nick!” she said in soft, almost pleading tones. “Please, Honey﻿—I don’t understand you. Don’t you know I love you, Nick? You can hear me say it: I love you. Don’t you believe that?”

===Chinese===
他不答话，拔出瓶塞，往两只平底大杯里各倒了些琥珀色的酒。又在其中一杯里兑了等量的姜汁汽水，不慌不忙、端端正正地放在帕特面前。她厌恶地对着酒杯皱眉，摇了摇头。
“不，”她说，“我不喝。我走了。”
可她并没有动；她的眼睛遇上同伴的眼睛，那双眼睛奇异的琥珀色深处正冷冷地紧盯着她。而且又是——那是不是一闪红光？她一时冲动，伸手碰了碰他的手。
“哦，尼克！”她柔声说，几乎是恳求的调子，“求求你，亲爱的——我不明白你了。你不知道我爱你吗，尼克？你听得见我说：我爱你。你信不信？”

===Original===
He continued his cold, intense stare; the grim set of his mouth was as unrelaxing as marble. Pat felt a shiver of apprehension run through her, and an almost hypnotic desire to yield herself to the demands of the inexplicable eyes. She tore her glance away, looking down at the red checks of the tablecloth.
“Nick, dear,” she said. “I can’t understand this. Will you tell me what you﻿—will you tell me why we’re here?”
“It is out of your grasp.”
“But﻿—I know it has something to do with Wednesday night, something to do with that reluctance of yours, the thing you said you didn’t understand. Hasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
他继续那冷冷的逼视；嘴上那副冷酷的绷紧，像大理石一样纹丝不松。帕特觉得一阵忧惧窜过全身，还有一种近乎催眠的愿望，想把自己交付给那双费解的眼睛的索求。她猛地扯开目光，低头看着桌布上的红格子。
“尼克，亲爱的，”她说，“这事我弄不懂。你肯告诉我你——你肯告诉我，我们为什么上这儿来吗？”
“这超出了你的理解范围。”
“可是——我知道这事跟星期三晚上有关，跟你那阵踌躇有关，跟你说过你自己也不明白的那件事有关。是不是？”

===Original===
“Do you think so?”
“Yes,” she said. “I do! And Nick, Honey﻿—didn’t I tell you I could forgive you anything? I don’t care what’s happened in the past; all I care for is now, now and the future. Don’t you understand me? I’ve told you I loved you, Honey! Don’t you love me?”
“Yes,” said the other, staring at her with no change in the fixity of his gaze.
“Then how can you﻿—act like this to me?”

===Chinese===
“你这么想？”
“是的，”她说，“我确实这么想！还有，尼克，亲爱的——我不是告诉过你，你做什么我都能原谅吗？过去发生过什么我不在乎；我在乎的只有现在，现在和将来。你还不明白我的意思吗？我说过我爱你了，亲爱的！你不爱我吗？”
“爱。”对方说，凝视的目光纹丝不变。
“那你怎么能——这样对我？”

===Original===
“This is my conception of love.”
“I don’t understand!” the girl said helplessly. “I’m completely puzzled﻿—it’s all topsy-turvy.”
“Yes,” he said in impassive agreement.
“But what is this, Nick? Please, please﻿—what is this? Are you mad?” She had almost added, “Like your father.”

===Chinese===
“这就是我对爱的理解。”
“我不明白！”姑娘无助地说，“我彻底糊涂了——全乱了套了。”
“是啊。”他无动于衷地表示同意。
“可这是什么呀，尼克？求你，求你——这是什么呀？你疯了吗？”她差一点儿补上一句：“就像你父亲那样。”

===Original===
“No,” he said, still in those cold tones. “This is an experiment.”
“An experiment!”
“Yes. An experiment in evil.”
“I don’t understand,” she repeated.

===Chinese===
“没有，”他说，仍是那种冷冰冰的调子，“这是一次实验。”
“实验！”
“是的。一次关于恶的实验。”
“我不明白。”她又说了一遍。

===Original===
“I said you wouldn’t.”
“Do you mean,” she asked, struck by a sudden thought, “that discussion of ours about pure horror? What you said that night last week?”
“That!” His voice was icy and contemptuous. “That was the drivel of a weakling. No; I mean evil, not horror﻿—the living evil that can be so beautiful that one walks deliberately, with open eyes, into Hell only to prevent its loss. That is the experiment.”
“Oh,” said Pat, her own voice suddenly cool. “Is that what you wish to do﻿—experiment on me?”

===Chinese===
“我说过你不会明白。”
“你是说，”她问，猛然想到一桩事，“我们那回关于纯粹恐怖的讨论？上星期那天晚上你说的那些话？”
“那个！”他的嗓音冰冷而轻蔑。“那是一个懦夫的呓语。不；我说的是恶，不是恐怖——那种活的恶，它能美到这般地步：人会为了不让它失去，睁大眼睛、存心故意地走进地狱。这才是那个实验。”
“哦，”帕特说，她自己的声音也突然冷了下来。“你想做的就是——拿我做实验？”

===Original===
“Yes.”
“And what am I supposed to do?”
“First you are to drink with me.”
“I see,” she said slowly. “I see﻿—dimly. I am a subject, a reagent, a guinea pig, to provide you material for your writing. You propose to use me in this experiment of yours﻿—this experiment in evil. All right!” She picked up the tumbler; impulsively she drained it. The liquor, diluted as it was, was raw and strong enough to bring tears smarting to her eyes. Or *was* it the liquor?

===Chinese===
“是的。”
“那我该做什么呢？”
“首先，你要陪我喝。”
“我明白了，”她缓缓地说，“我明白了——隐隐约约地明白。我是个受试者，一种试剂，一只豚鼠，给你提供写作的材料。你打算把我用在你的这场实验里——这场关于恶的实验。好吧！”她端起平底杯，一冲动灌了下去。那酒虽经稀释，仍然又辛辣又凶烈，呛得她眼泪直冒。可那*究竟*是不是酒呛的？

===Original===
“All right!” she cried. “I’ll drink it all﻿—the whole bottle!” She seized the flask, filling her tumbler to the brim, while her companion watched her with impassive gaze. “You’ll have your experiment! And then, Nicholas Devine, we’re through! Do you hear me? Through!”
She caught up the tumbler, raised it to her lips, and drained the searing liquid until she could see her companion’s cold eyes regarding her through the glass of its bottom.

===Chinese===
“好啊！”她喊道，“我全喝了——一整瓶！”她抓过酒瓶，把平底杯斟到齐口，同伴只冷眼瞧着。“你会得到你的实验！然后呢，尼古拉斯·德文，我们就完了！听见没有？完了！”
她抓起平底杯，举到唇边，把那灼人的液体一饮而尽，直到能透过杯底的玻璃，看见同伴那双冷冷的眼睛正端详着她。

## XIII Indecision / 十三 犹豫不决
===Original===
Pat turned the envelope dubiously in her hands, while a maze of chaotic thoughts assailed her. She felt almost a sensation of guilt as if she were in some manner violating the promise given to Dr. Horker; she felt a tinge of indignation that Nicholas Devine should dare communicate with her at all, and she felt too that queer exultation, an inexplicable pleasure, a feeling of secret triumph. She slipped the letter in the pocket of her robe and padded quietly up the stairs to her own room.

Strangely, her loneliness had vanished. The great house, empty now save for herself and Magda in the distant kitchen, was no longer a place of solitude; the discovery of the letter, whatever its contents, had changed the deserted rooms into chambers teeming with her own excitements, trepidations, doubts, and hopes. Even hopes, she admitted to herself, though hopes of what nature she was quite unable to say. What *could* Nick write that had the power to change things? Apologies? Pleas? Promises? None of these could alter the naked, horrible facts of the predicament.

Nevertheless, she was almost a-tremble with expectation as she skipped hastily into her own room, carefully closed the door, and settled herself by the west windows. She drew the letter from her pocket, and then, with a tightening of her throat, tore open the envelope, slipping out the several pages of scrawled paper. Avidly she began to read.

===Chinese===
帕特把那只信封在手里将信将疑地翻来覆去，一团乱麻般的思绪向她袭来。她几乎有种做贼似的负疚感，仿佛自己在某种意义上违背了对霍克医生的承诺；她又感到一丝愤懑，恼火尼古拉斯·德文居然还敢跟她通信；可与此同时，她心头又泛起那种古怪的雀跃，一种说不清道不明的欢喜，一种偷偷的得意。她把信塞进晨衣口袋，蹑手蹑脚地悄悄上楼，进了自己的房间。

说来奇怪，她的孤独感竟消失了。偌大的宅子里如今只剩下她和远处厨房里的玛格达，却再也不是一块荒寂之地；这封信的出现，不管里面写了什么，已经让那些空落的房间重新充盈起她自己的激动、惶恐、疑虑和希望。连希望都有，她对自己承认，尽管是怎样的希望，她全然说不上来。尼克*到底*能写出什么来改变局面？道歉？恳求？许诺？这些统统改变不了这赤裸裸、可怕的困境事实。

尽管如此，当她匆匆溜进自己的房间，仔细关上门，在西窗旁坐定时，她几乎因为期待而微微发颤。她从口袋里掏出信来，随即喉咙一阵发紧，撕开了信封，抽出那几页字迹潦草的信纸。她如饥似渴地读了起来。

===Original===
> “I don’t know whether you’ll ever see this”﻿—the missive began without salutation﻿—“and I’ll not blame you, Pat dear, if you do return it unopened. There’s nothing you can do that wouldn’t be justified, nor can you think worse of me than I do of myself. And that’s a statement so meaningless that even as I wrote it, I could anticipate its effect on you.
> “Pat﻿—How am I going to convince you that I’m sincere? Will you believe me when I write that I love you? Can you believe that I love you tenderly, worshipfully﻿—reverently?
> “You can’t; I know you can’t after that catastrophe of last night. But it’s true, Pat, though the logic of a Spinoza might fail to convince you of it.
> “I don’t know how to write you this. I don’t know whether you want to hear what I could say, but I know that I must try to say it. Not apologies, Pat﻿—I shouldn’t dare approach you for so poor a reason as that﻿—but a sort of explanation. You more than anyone in the world are entitled to that explanation, if you want to hear it.
> “I can’t write it to you, Pat; it’s something I can only make you believe by telling you﻿—something dark and rather terrible. But please, Dear, believe that I mean you no harm, and that I plan no subterfuge, when I suggest that you see me. It will be, I think, for the last time.
> “Tonight, and tomorrow night, and as many nights to follow as I can, I’ll sit on a bench in the park near the place where I kissed you that first time. There will be people passing there, and cars driving by; you need fear nothing from me. I choose the place to bridle my own actions, Pat; nothing can happen while we sit there in the view of the world.
> “To write you more than this is futile. If you come, I’ll be there; if you don’t, I’ll understand.
> “I love you.”

===Chinese===
> “我不知道你会不会看到这封信”——信一开头便没有称呼——“帕特，亲爱的，就算你把它原封退回，我也不会怪你。你无论做什么都不为过，你再怎么往坏里想我，也想不过我对自己的看法。而这话实在空洞得很，我一落笔，就已料到它会在你心里激起什么。”
> “帕特——我要怎样才能让你相信我是真心的？当我写下我爱你的时候，你会相信吗？你能相信我爱你爱得温柔、爱得近乎崇拜——近乎虔敬吗？”
> “你不能；经过昨晚那场大祸，我知道你不能。可这是真的，帕特，尽管只怕拿斯宾诺莎的逻辑来论证，也未必能让你信服。”
> “我不知道该怎么把这写给你。我不知道你想不想听我要说的话，可我知道我必须试着说。不是道歉，帕特——我才不敢拿这么可怜的理由去接近你——而是一种解释。你比世上任何人都更有资格听这个解释，只要你还想听。”
> “我没法把它写给你，帕特；这件事，只有当面告诉你才能让你相信——一件黑暗而相当可怕的事。可是求你，亲爱的，相信我无意伤你，也并非设下什么圈套，才请你见我。我想，那将是最后一次。”
> “今晚，明晚，还有往后的每一个夜晚，只要我做得到，我都会坐在公园的那条长椅上，就在我头一回吻你的地方近旁。那里会有人来来往往，有汽车驶过；你不必怕我。我挑那个地方是为了约束我自己，帕特；我们坐在世人眼目之下，就出不了任何事情。”
> “再多写也是徒劳。你来，我就在那里；你不来，我也明白。”
> “我爱你。”

===Original===
The letter was signed merely “Nick.” She stared at the signature with feelings so confused that she forebore any attempt to analyze them.

“But I can’t go,” she mused soberly. “I’ve promised Dr. Carl. Or at least, I can’t go without telling him.”

That last thought, she realized, was a concession. Heretofore she hadn’t let herself consider the possibility of seeing Nicholas Devine again, and now suddenly she was weakening, arguing with herself about the ethics of seeing him. She shook her head decisively.

“Won’t do, Patricia Lane!” she told herself. “Next thing, you’ll be slipping away without a word to anybody, and coming home with two black eyes and a broken nose. Won’t do at all!”

===Chinese===
信的落款只有“尼克”二字。她盯着那个签名，心绪乱得她索性放弃了一切想去分辨的尝试。

“可我不能去，”她正色思忖道，“我答应过卡尔医生。至少，不经他知道，我不能去。”

她意识到，后面这念头已是一步退让。在此之前，她从不许自己考虑再见尼古拉斯·德文的可能，此刻却突然软了下来，竟跟自己争辩起见他合不合情理来。她断然摇了摇头。

“不行，帕特里夏·莱恩！”她告诫自己，“再下一步，你就要一声不吭地溜出去，谁也不告诉，回来时顶着两只乌青眼、一个断鼻梁了。万万不行！”

===Original===
She dropped her eyes to the letter. “Explanations,” she reflected. “I guess Dr. Carl would give up a hole-in-one to hear that explanation. And I’d give more than that.” She shook her head regretfully. “Nothing to do about it, though. I promised.”

The sun was slanting through the west windows; she sat watching the shadows lengthen in the room, and tried to turn her thoughts into more profitable channels. This was the first Sunday in many months that she had spent alone in the house; it was a custom for herself and her mother to spend the afternoon at the club. The evening too, as a rule; there was invariably bridge for Mrs. Lane, and Pat was always the center of a circle of the younger members. She wondered dreamily what the crowd thought of her nonappearance, reflecting that her mother had doubtless enlarged on Dr. Carl’s story of an accident. Dr. Carl wouldn’t say much, simply that he’d ordered her to stay at home. But sooner or later, Nick would hear the accident story; she wondered what he’d think of it.

She caught herself up sharply. “My ideas wander in circles,” she thought petulantly. “No matter where I start, they curve around back to Nick. It won’t do; I’ve got to stop it.”

===Chinese===
她垂眼看了看信。“解释，”她思忖道，“我看卡尔医生宁肯拿一次一杆进洞来换这个解释听。我舍得比那更多。”她遗憾地摇了摇头。“可是没有办法。我答应过的。”

阳光斜斜地穿过西窗照进来；她坐在那里，望着屋里的影子渐渐拉长，努力把心思引上更有益的轨道。多少个月来，这是她头一个独自在家度过的星期天；按惯例，她和母亲要在俱乐部消磨一下午。通常晚上也去；莱恩太太那里总免不了有牌局，帕特呢，也总是年轻人那一圈的中心。她恍恍惚惚地想，那伙人会怎么议论她没露面，又想到母亲准是把卡尔医生那套出了意外的说法添油加醋了一番。卡尔医生不会多说什么，只说是他吩咐她待在家里的。可是迟早，尼克总会听到那个意外的说法；她不知道他会怎么想。

她猛地收住心神。“我的念头净在兜圈子，”她烦躁地想，“不管从哪儿起头，一转弯就又绕回尼克身上。这样不行；我得打住。”

===Original===
Nearly time for the evening meal, she mused, watching the sun as it dropped behind Dr. Horker’s house. She didn’t feel much like eating; there was still a remnant of the exhausted, dragged-out sensation, though the headache that had accompanied her awakening this morning had disappeared.

“I know what the morning after feels like, anyway,” she reflected with a wry little smile. “Everybody ought to experience it once, I suppose. I wonder how Nick﻿—”

She broke off abruptly, with a shrug of disgust. She slipped the letter back into its envelope, rose and deposited it in the drawer of the night-table. She glanced at the clock ticking on its shiny top.

===Chinese===
快到晚饭时候了，她望着落到霍克医生家屋顶后面的太阳，心里思忖。她不怎么想吃东西；那种精疲力竭、浑身被抽干似的余感还没散尽，虽然今早醒来时伴随她的头痛已经消失了。

“不管怎么说，宿醉次日早上的滋味我算是尝过了，”她带着一丝苦笑想道，“依我看，人人都该经历一回。不知道尼克——”

她蓦地打住，厌烦地耸了耸肩。她把信塞回信封，起身放进床头柜的抽屉里。她瞥了一眼在那光亮的柜面上滴答作响的钟。

===Original===
“Six o’clock,” she murmured. Nick would be sitting in the park in another two hours or so. She had a twinge of sympathy at the thought of his lone vigil; she could visualize the harried expression on his face when the hours passed without her arrival.

“Can’t be helped,” she told herself. “He’s no right to ask for anything of me after last night. He knows that; he said so in his letter.”

She suppressed an impulse to reread that letter, and trotted deliberately out of the room and down the stairs. Magda had set the table in the breakfast room; it was far cozier than the great dining room, especially without her mother’s company. And the maid was away; the breakfast room simplified serving, as well.

===Chinese===
“六点了。”她喃喃道。再过两个来钟头，尼克就要在公园里坐下了。想到他那孤零零的守候，她心里一阵刺痛；她能想象出，时辰一个个过去、她始终不来时，他脸上那副被煎熬的神情。

“没法子，”她对自己说，“经过昨晚，他没资格再向我要求什么。他自己知道；他信里也这么说了。”

她压下重读那封信的冲动，故意快步走出房间，下了楼。玛格达已在早餐室摆好餐桌；那里比大饭厅要温馨得多，尤其是缺了母亲作伴的时候。再说女仆也出去了；用早餐室，伺候起来也简便些。

===Original===
She tried valorously to eat what Magda supplied, but the food failed to tempt her. It wasn’t so much her physical condition, either; it was﻿—She clenched her jaws firmly; was the memory of Nicholas Devine to haunt her forever?

“Pat Lane,” she said in admonition, “you’re a crackbrained fool! Just because a man kicks you all over the place is no reason to let him become an obsession.”

She drank her coffee, feeling the sting of its heat on her injured lips. She left the table, tramped firmly to her room, and began defiantly to read. The effort was useless; half a dozen times she forced her attention to the page only to find herself staring vaguely into space a moment or two later. She closed the book finally with an irritable bang, and vented her restlessness in pacing back and forth.

===Chinese===
她奋勇地想咽下玛格达端上的饭菜，可食物就是引不起她的胃口。这也不全是身上没力气的缘故；而是——她狠狠咬紧牙关；难道尼古拉斯·德文的影子要缠她一辈子？

“帕特·莱恩，”她告诫自己道，“你是个犯糊涂的傻瓜！总不能因为一个男人把你踢打得团团转，就让他变成你的执念。”

她喝了咖啡，热气蜇得她受伤的嘴唇生疼。她离了餐桌，坚定地大步回房，赌气似的读起书来。这番努力全无用处；五六回她强逼自己盯住书页，不消一两分钟，便发觉自己又茫然望向了半空。末了她烦躁地啪一声合上书，靠来回踱步发泄那股坐立不安。

===Original===
“This house is unbearable!” she snapped. “I’m not going to stay shut up here like a jailbird in solitary confinement. A walk in the open is what I need, and that’s what I’ll have.”

She glanced at the clock; seven-thirty. She tore off her robe pettishly, flung out of her pajamas, and began to dress with angry determination. She refused to think of a lonely figure that might even now be sitting disconsolately on a bench in the nearby park.

===Chinese===
“这宅子没法待人！”她厉声道，“我才不要像个关独间牢房的囚犯似的锁在这儿。我需要到露天去走一走，而且我非去不可。”

她看了一眼钟；七点半。她赌气地扯下晨衣，甩脱睡衣，带着一股怒气开始穿衣。她拒绝去想那个孤单的身影——此刻他说不定正怅然坐在附近公园的长椅上。

===Original===
She disguised her bruised cheek as best she could, dabbed a little powder on the abrasion on her chin, and tramped militantly down the stairs. She caught up her wrap, still lying where the Doctor had tossed it last night, and moved toward the door, opening it and nearly colliding with the massive figure of Dr. Horker!

“Well!” boomed the Doctor as she started back in surprise. “You’re pretty spry for a patient. Think you were going out?”

“Yes,” said Pat defiantly.

“Not tonight, child! I left the Club early to take a look at you.”

===Chinese===
她尽量把乌青的脸颊遮掩了一番，在下巴的擦伤上扑了点粉，雄赳赳地大步下了楼。她抓起披肩——还扔在昨晚医生甩下的地方——朝门口走去，一开门，差点撞上霍克医生那魁伟的身躯！

“嚯！”见她吃惊地直往后缩，医生瓮声说道。“你这病人倒挺利索。琢磨着要出去？”

“是啊。”帕特不服气地说。

“今晚不行，孩子！我早早离开俱乐部，就是来看看你的。”

===Original===
“I am perfectly all right. I want to go for a walk.”

“No walk. Doctor’s orders.”

“I’m of legal age!” she snapped. “I want to go for a walk. Do I go?”

“You do not.” The Doctor placed his great form squarely in the doorway. “Not unless you can lick me, my girl, and I’m pretty tough. I put you to bed last night, and I can do as much tonight. Shall I?”

===Chinese===
“我好得很。我想出去散散步。”

“不许散步。医嘱。”

“我已经成年了！”她厉声道，“我要出去散步。到底让不让？”

“不让。”医生把他那高大的身躯端端正正堵在门口。“除非你打得过我，小姑娘，而我这身板硬朗得很。昨晚是我把你弄上床的，今晚我照样能。要不要？”

===Original===
Pat backed into the hall. “You don’t have to,” she said sullenly. “I’m going there myself.” She flung her wrap angrily to a chair and stalked up the stairs.

“Good night, spitfire,” he called after her. “I’ll read down here until your mother comes home.”

===Chinese===
帕特退进门厅里。“用不着你动手，”她闷闷地说，“我自己会上床去睡。”她怒冲冲地把披肩摔进一把椅子，昂首阔步上了楼。

“晚安，火爆脾气，”他在她背后喊道，“我就在楼下看书，等你母亲回来。”

===Original===
The girl stormed into her room in anger that she knew to be illogical.

“I won’t be watched like a problem child!” she told herself viciously. “I know damn well what he thought﻿—and I wasn’t going to meet Nick! I wasn’t at all!”

She calmed suddenly, sat on the edge of her bed and kicked off her pumps. It had occurred to her that Nick had written his intention to wait for her in the park tomorrow night as well, and Dr. Horker’s interference had confirmed her in a determination to meet him.

===Chinese===
姑娘冲进自己的房间，满腔怒火，连她自己都知道这火发得没道理。

“我才不要像个问题儿童似的被人看管着！”她狠狠地对自己说，“他打的什么算盘我一清二楚——可我根本不是去见尼克！压根儿就不是！”

她忽然平静下来，坐到床沿上，踢掉高跟鞋。她想起尼克信里写过，明晚他也要在公园里等她；而霍克医生这一番拦阻，反倒把她去见他的决心给定下来了。

## XII Letter from Lucifer / 十二 路西法的来信
===Original===
“I’m glad!” Pat told herself. “I’m glad it’s over, and I’m glad I promised Dr. Carl﻿—I guess I was mighty close to the brink of disaster that time.”
She examined the injuries on her face, carefully powdered to conceal the worst effects from her mother. The trick had worked, too; Mrs. Lane had delivered herself of an excited lecture on the dangers of the gasoline age, and then thanked Heaven it was no worse. Well, Pat reflected, she had good old Dr. Carl to thank for the success of the subterfuge; he had broken the news very skillfully, set the stage for her appearance, and calmed her mother’s apprehensions of scars. And Pat, surveying her image in the glass above her dressing-table, could see for herself the minor nature of the hurts.
“Scars﻿—pooh!” she observed. “A bruised cheek, a split lip, a skinned chin. All I need is a black eye, and I guess I’d have had that in five minutes more, and perhaps a cauliflower ear into the bargain.”

===Chinese===
“我真高兴！”帕特对自己说，“高兴一切都过去了，也高兴我答应了卡尔医生——我看那一回，我离灾难的边缘只差一线了。”
她端详着脸上的伤，已仔细扑过粉，把最难看的几处遮起来瞒过母亲。这一招也奏效了；莱恩太太就汽油时代的种种危险慷慨激昂地训了一通，又谢天谢地，庆幸没闹得更糟。唉，帕特想，这套障眼法能成功，多亏了老好人卡尔医生；是他极其得体地透了消息，替她露面搭好了台口，又打消了母亲怕留疤的忧心。而帕特对着梳妆台上方的镜子里自己的模样，也亲眼看清了这些伤有多么轻微。
“疤——呸！”她评了一句，“一副青肿的脸颊，一张裂开的嘴唇，一个擦破的下巴。就差一只乌青眼了，我看再过五分钟连那个也有了，说不定还饶上一只菜花耳。”

===Original===
But her mood was anything but flippant; she was fighting off the time when her thoughts had of necessity to face the unpleasant, disturbing facts of the affair. She didn’t want to think of the thing at all; she wanted to laugh it off and forget it, yet she knew that for an impossibility. The very desire to forget she recognized as a coward’s wish, and she resented the idea that she was cowardly.
“Forget the wisecracks,” she advised her image. “Face the thing and argue it out; that’s the only way to be satisfied.”
She rose with a little grimace of pain at the twinge from her bruised knees, and crossed to the chaise lounge beside the far window. She settled herself in it and resumed her cogitations. She was feeling more or less herself again; the headache of the morning had nearly vanished, and aside from the various aches and a listless fagged-out sensation, she approximated her normal self. Physically, that is; the shadow of that other catastrophe, the one she hesitated to face, was another matter.

===Chinese===
可她的心情怎么也谈不上轻松；她在推延那个时刻——到那时，她的思绪将不得不去面对这件事里那些不愉快、扰人的事实。她压根不愿去想这件事；她只想一笑置之、把它忘掉，然而她知道那不可能。她把这份想忘掉的欲望认作懦夫的心思，而一想到自己懦弱，她就恼火。
“别耍嘴皮子了，”她劝镜中的自己，“正视这件事，把它论个明白；只有这样，心里才能踏实。”
她站起身，擦破的膝盖一阵刺痛，疼得她微微一咧嘴，随后走到远端窗边的躺椅前。她安顿下来，继续寻思。她觉得自己多少恢复了常态；早晨的头疼差不多消散了，除了周身的几处酸痛和一种懒洋洋的疲惫感，她已接近平日的自己。身体上是如此；至于另一场灾祸的阴影——她迟迟不敢面对的那场——就是另一回事了。

===Original===
“I’m lucky to get off this easily,” she assured herself, “after going on a bust like that one, like a lumberjack with his pay in his pocket.” She shook her head in mournful amazement. “And I’m Patricia Lane, the girl whom Billy dubbed ‘Pat the Impeccable’! Impeccable! Wandering through alleys in step-ins and a tablecloth﻿—getting beaten up in a drunken brawl﻿—passing out on rotgut liquor﻿—being carried home and put to bed! Not impeccable; incapable’s the word! I belong to Dr. Carl’s parade of incompetents.”

===Chinese===
“那么一场狂饮胡闹，居然这么轻易就脱了身，算我走运，”她宽慰自己，“活像兜里揣着工钱的伐木工。”她哀伤而又惊诧地摇了摇头。“可我还是帕特里夏·莱恩呢，是比利封的‘无可挑剔的帕特’！无可挑剔！穿着内裤、裹着桌布在巷子里乱逛——在醉汉斗殴里挨揍——灌劣质烧酒灌得不省人事——被人抬回家、放上床！不是无可挑剔；是无能为力！我该归入卡尔医生的无能者游行队伍里去。”

===Original===
She continued her rueful reflections. “Well, item one is, I don’t love Nick any more. I couldn’t now!” she flung at the smiling green buddha on the mantel. “That’s over; I’ve promised.”
Somehow there was not satisfaction in the memory of that promise. It was logical, of course; there wasn’t anything else to do now, but still﻿—
“That *wasn’t* Nick!” she told herself. “That wasn’t *my* Nick. I guess Dr. Carl is right, and he’s a depressed whatever-it-was; but if he’s crazy, so am I! He had me convinced last night; I understood what he meant, and I felt what he wanted me to feel. If he’s crazy, I am too; a fine couple we are!”

===Chinese===
她继续着这些怅然的思索。“好吧，头一条是，我不再爱尼克了。如今我做不到了！”她把这句话甩给壁炉架上那尊笑吟吟的绿玉佛。“那段情了结了；我许过诺了。”
不知怎么，回想起那个承诺，心里并无快意。这当然合乎逻辑；如今也没有别的路可走，可是——
“那*不是*尼克！”她告诉自己，“那不是*我的*尼克。我看卡尔医生说得对，他是个抑郁的什么劳什子；可他要是疯了，我也疯了！昨晚他把我说服了；我懂了他的意思，也感到了他要我感到的东西。他要是疯了，我也疯了；咱们真是天造地设的一对！”

===Original===
She continued. “But it wasn’t Nick! I saw his face when we drove off, and it had changed again, and that was Nick’s face, not the other. And he was sorry; I could see he was sorry, and the other could never have regretted it﻿—not ever! The other isn’t﻿—quite human, but Nick is.”
She paused, considering the idea. “Of course,” she resumed, “I might have imagined that change at the end. I was hazy and quavery, and it’s the last thing I *do* remember; that must have been just before I passed out.”
And then, replying to her own objection, “But I *didn’t* imagine it! I saw it happen once before, that other night when﻿—Well, what difference does it make, anyway? It’s over, and I’ve given my promise.”

===Chinese===
她继续说：“可那不是尼克！我们开车走的时候，我看见了他的脸，它又变了回来，那是尼克的脸，不是另一个的。而且他很难过；我看得出他难过，而另一个绝不会懊悔——永远不会！那另一个不是——十足的人，尼克才是。”
她顿了顿，掂量着这个念头。“当然，”她重新开口，“末了那个变化，也可能是我想象出来的。我当时迷迷糊糊、浑身发颤，而那是我*确实*记得的最后一件事；准是在我昏过去之前。”
接着，她回答自己的异议：“可我*并没有*想象！那个变化我早先亲眼见过一回，就在另一天晚上，当时——唉，反正那又有什么要紧？都结束了，我也许下了诺。”

===Original===
But she was unable to dismiss the matter as easily as that. There was some uncanny, elusive element in it that fascinated her. Cruel, terrible, demoniac, he might have been; he had also been kind, lovable, and gentle. Yet Dr. Carl had told her that split personalities could contain no characteristics that were not present in the original, normal character. Was cruelty, then, a part of kindness? Was cruelty merely the lack of kindness, or, cynical thought, was kindness but the lack of cruelty? Which qualities were positive in the antagonistic phases of Nicholas Devine’s individuality, and which negative? Was the gentle, lovable, but indubitably weaker character the split, and the demon of last evening his normal self? Or vice-versa? Or were both of these fragmentary entities, portions of some greater personality as yet unapparent to her?
The whole matter was a mystery; she shrugged in helpless perplexity.
“I don’t think Dr. Carl knows as much about it as he says,” she mused. “I don’t think psychiatry or any other science knows that much about the human soul. Dr. Carl doesn’t even believe in a soul; how could he know anything about it, then?” She frowned in puzzlement and gave up the attempt to solve the mystery.

===Chinese===
可她没法把这件事就这么轻易撂开。这里面有种离奇而捉摸不定的成分，牢牢地吸引着她。他也许残忍、可怕、恶魔一般；他也曾善良、可爱、温柔。然而卡尔医生告诉过她，分裂出来的人格不可能含有原本正常人格中所没有的特性。那么，残忍是善良的一部分么？残忍仅仅是善良的缺失，还是——愤世的想法——善良不过是残忍的缺失？在尼古拉斯·德文人格相互敌对的两个方面中，哪些品质是正的，哪些是负的？是那个温柔可爱却无疑更孱弱的人格属于分裂，而昨晚的恶魔才是他的本相？还是恰恰相反？又或者，这两者都是残缺的实体，是某个她尚未得见的更伟大人格的碎片？
整件事是个谜；她一筹莫展地困惑着，耸了耸肩。
“我看卡尔医生对这事懂得没有他说的那么多，”她沉思道，“我看精神病学也好，别的什么科学也好，对人类灵魂都懂得没有那么多。卡尔医生压根不信有灵魂；那他又怎么可能懂呢？”她困惑地皱起眉头，放弃了破解这个谜的尝试。

===Original===
The hours she had spent in her room, at her mother’s insistence, began to pall; she didn’t feel particularly ill﻿—it was more of a languor, a depressed, worn-out feeling. Her mother, of course, was out somewhere; she felt a desire for human companionship, and wondered if the Doctor might by some chance drop in. It seemed improbable; he had his regular Sunday afternoon routine of golf at the Club, and it took a real catastrophe to keep him away from that. She sighed, stretched her legs, rose from her position on the chaise lounge, and wandered toward the kitchen where Magda was doubtless to be found.
It was in the dusk of the rear hall that the first sense of her loss came over her. Heretofore her renunciation of Nicholas Devine was a rational thing, a promise given but not felt; but now it was suddenly a poignant reality. Nick was gone, she realized; he was out of her world, irrevocably sundered from her. She paused at the top of the rear flight of stairs, considering the matter.

===Chinese===
她拗不过母亲，在屋里捱过的这几个钟头开始变得乏味；她并不觉得怎么难受——那更像一种倦怠，一种消沉、耗尽的感觉。母亲自然是出门到哪儿去了；她渴望有人作伴，寻思医生会不会碰巧过来。看来不大可能；星期天下午去俱乐部打高尔夫是他雷打不动的惯例，没有真正的天灾人祸，离不开那个。她叹了口气，伸了伸腿，从躺椅上起身，朝厨房溜达过去，玛格达八成在那儿。
正是在后门厅的暮色里，丧失的最初感觉向她袭来。在此之前，她对尼古拉斯·德文的舍弃是一件理性的事，一个许下却不曾痛感过的承诺；可此刻，它骤然成了锥心的现实。尼克走了，她这才明白；他已出了她的世界，同她不可挽回地分离了。她在后楼梯顶上停住脚，思量着这件事。

===Original===
“He’s gone! I won’t see him ever again.” The thought was appalling; she felt already a premonition of loneliness to come, of an emptiness in her world, a lack that nothing could replace.
“I shouldn’t have promised Dr. Carl,” she mused, knowing that even without that promise her course must still have been the same. “I shouldn’t have, not until I’d talked to Nick﻿—my own Nick.”
And still, she reflected forlornly, what difference did it make? She had to give him up; she couldn’t continue to see him not knowing at what instant that terrible caricature of him might appear to torment her. But he might have explained, she argued miserably, answering her own objection at once﻿—he’s said he couldn’t explain, didn’t understand. The thing was at an impasse.

===Chinese===
“他走了！我再也见不到他了。”这个念头骇人；她已经预感到日后的孤独，感到她的世界里将出现一片空虚，一处任何东西都填补不了的缺憾。
“我不该答应卡尔医生的，”她思忖道，心里明白即便没有那个承诺，她的路也还是这一条。“不该答应的，总该先跟尼克谈谈——跟我自己的尼克。”
然而，她凄然想道，那又有什么分别？她只得舍弃他；她没法再见他，却不知道他那副可怕的讽刺画，会在哪一瞬间冒出来折磨她。可他本可以解释的呀，她苦楚地辩道，随即又自己作了答——他说过他解释不了，他自己也不明白。这件事陷入了僵局。

===Original===
She shook her shining black head despondently, and descended the dusky well of the stairs to the kitchen. Magda was there clattering among her pots and pans; Pat entered quietly and perched on the high stool by the long table. Old Magda, who had warmed her babyhood milk and measured out her formula, gave her a single glance and continued her work.
“Sorry about the accident, I was,” she said without looking up.
“Thanks,” responded the girl. “I’m all right again.”
“You don’t look it.”
“I feel all right.”
She watched the mysterious, alchemistic mixing of a pastry, and thought of the vast array of them that had come from Magda’s hands. As far back as she could remember she had perched on this stool observing the same mystic culinary rites.

===Chinese===
她黯然地摇了摇那颗乌亮的脑袋，顺着楼梯昏暗的井道下到厨房。玛格达正在那儿锅盆瓢勺地丁当作响；帕特悄没声地进去，在高脚凳上坐定，挨着那张长桌。老玛格达——她温过帕特襁褓时的牛奶，给她量配过奶粉——只瞥了她一眼，便继续干活。
“出了那桩意外，我心里难受来着。”她头也不抬地说。
“多谢，”姑娘答道，“我又没事了。”
“你瞧着可不像。”
“我觉得没事。”
她望着一份点心里那种神秘的、炼金术般的调配，想起从玛格达手里出来过的那一长列糕点。打她记事起，她就蹲在这张凳子上，观礼同一套神秘的烹饪仪式。

===Original===
Suddenly another memory rose out of the grave of forgetfulness and went gibbering across her world. She remembered the stories Magda used to tell her, frightening stories of witchcraft and the evil eye, tales out of an older region and a more credulous age.
“Magda,” she asked, “did you ever see a devil?”
“Not I, but I’ve talked with them that had.”
“Didn’t you ever see one?”

===Chinese===
突然，另一段记忆从遗忘的坟茔里爬起，疯疯癫癫地窜过她的世界。她想起玛格达从前常给她讲的故事，那些吓人的巫术和邪眼的故事，出自一片更古老的乡土、一个更轻信的年代的传说。
“玛格达，”她问，“你见过魔鬼吗？”
“我没见过，可我跟见过的人搭过话。”
“你从来一个也没见过？”

===Original===
“No.” The woman slid a pan into the oven. “I saw a man once, when I was a tot, possessed by a devil.”
“You did? How did he look?”
“He screamed terrible, then he said queer things. Then he fell down and foam came out of his mouth.”

===Chinese===
“没有。”女人把一只烤盘送进烤炉。“我倒见过一个人，那会儿我还是个小不点儿，他被魔鬼附了体。”
“真见过？他什么样儿？”
“他叫得吓人，跟着说了些怪话。后来他倒在地上，嘴里冒白沫。”

===Original===
“Like a fit?”
“The Priest, he said it was a devil. He came and prayed over him, and after a while he was real quiet, and then he was all right.”
“Possessed by a devil,” said Pat thoughtfully. “What happened to him?”

===Chinese===
“像发痫风？”
“神父说，那是魔鬼。他来给他祷告，过了一阵他就安安静静的，后来就好了。”
“被魔鬼附了体，”帕特若有所思地说，“他后来怎么样了？”

===Original===
“Dunno.”
“What queer things did he say?”
“Wicked things, the Priest said. I couldn’t tell! I was a tot.”

===Chinese===
“不知道。”
“他都说些什么怪话？”
“邪性话，神父说的。我可听不明白！那时我是个小不点儿。”

===Original===
“Possessed by a devil!” Pat repeated musingly. She sat immersed in thoughts on the high stool while Magda clattered busily about. The woman paused finally, turning her face to the girl.
“What you so quiet about, Miss Pat?”
“I was just thinking.”
“You get your letter?”

===Chinese===
“被魔鬼附体！”帕特喃喃地重复。她坐在高脚凳上，沉浸在思绪里，玛格达在旁边丁丁当当地忙碌。末了，女人停下来，把脸转向姑娘。
“你怎么这么闷声不响的，帕特小姐？”
“我在想事儿呢。”
“你的信拿到了吧？”

===Original===
“Letter? What letter? Today’s Sunday.”
“Special delivery. The girl, she put it in the hall.”
“I didn’t know anything about it. Who’d write me a special?”

===Chinese===
“信？什么信？今天是星期天。”
“限时专送。那姑娘把它搁在门厅里了。”
“我怎么一点儿都不知道。谁会给我写限时专送？”

===Original===
She slipped off the high stool and proceeded to the front hall. The letter was there, solitary on the salver that always held the mail. She picked it up, examining the envelope in sudden startled amazement and more than a trace of illogical exultation.
For the letter, postmarked that same morning, was addressed in the irregular script of Nicholas Devine!

===Chinese===
她溜下高脚凳，往前厅走去。信就在那儿，孤零零地躺在那只常年盛放邮件的托盘上。她拾起信，端详着信封，心头猛然又惊又愕，还带着不止一丝说不通道理的狂喜。
因为这封当天早晨付邮的信，信封上写地址的笔迹，正是尼古拉斯·德文那笔歪斜不整的手书！

## XXVIII Lunar Omen / 二十八 月兆
===Original===
After a considerable interval, during which Nick held the girl tightly and silently in his arms, he released her, sat with his head resting on his cupped palms in an attitude of deep study. Pat, beside him, fell mechanically to repinning the throat of her frock, which had opened during the moments of the embrace. He rose to his feet, pacing nervously before her.
“It isn’t a thing to do on the impulse of a moment, Pat,” he muttered, pausing at her side. “You must see that.”
“It isn’t the impulse of a moment.”
“But one doesn’t abandon everything, the whole world, so easily, Honey. One doesn’t cast away a last hope, however forlorn a hope it may be!”
“Is there a hope, Nick?” she asked gently. “Is there a chance left to us?”

===Chinese===
过了好一阵——这期间尼克把姑娘紧紧地、默默地搂在怀里——他才松开手，双手捧着头坐下，一副深思的姿态。帕特在他身旁，机械地重新别好连衣裙的领口——方才拥抱时它敞开了。他站起身，在她面前焦躁地踱来踱去。
“这不是能凭一时冲动去做的事，帕特，”他在她身边停下，喃喃道。“你一定明白。”
“这不是一时冲动。”
“可人不会那么轻易抛下一切、抛下整个世界，亲爱的。哪怕最后一线希望再渺茫，人也不会把它扔掉！”
“还有希望吗，尼克？”她轻声问。“我们还剩下机会吗？”

===Original===
“I don’t know!” His voice held an increasing tenseness. “Before God﻿—I﻿—don’t know!”
“If there’s a chance, the very slightest shadow of the specter of a chance, we’ll take it, won’t we? Because the other way is always open to us, Nick.”
“Yes. It’s always open.”
“But we won’t take that chance,” she continued defiantly, “if it involves my losing you, Honey. I meant what I said, Nick: I don’t want to live without you!”
“What chance have we?” he queried somberly. “Those are our alternatives﻿—life apart, death together.”
“Then you know my choice!” she cried desperately. “Nick, Honey﻿—don’t let’s draw it out in futile talking! I can’t stand it!”

===Chinese===
“我不知道！”他的声音越来越紧绷。“上帝在上——我——不知道！”
“只要还有机会，哪怕只是机会的幽灵投下的一丝影踪，我们也要抓住它，对不对？因为另一条路，永远向我们敞开着，尼克。”
“是的。它永远敞开。”
“但我们不会选那条路，”她倔强地接着说，“如果那意味着失去你，亲爱的。我说的是真心话，尼克：没有你我活不下去！”
“我们还有什么机会？”他阴沉地问。“摆在我们面前的只有两条路——生而分离，或者死在一处。”
“那你知道我的选择了！”她绝望地喊道。“尼克，亲爱的——别再用无谓的谈话拖下去了！我受不了啦！”

===Original===
He moved his hand in a gesture of bewilderment and frustration, and turned away, striding nervously toward the window whose blind she had raised. He leaned his hands on the table, peering dejectedly out upon the street below.
“What time,” he asked irrelevantly in a queer voice, “did the Doctor say the moon rose? Do you remember?”
“No,” she said tensely. “Oh, Honey! Please﻿—don’t stand there with your back to me now, when I’m half crazy!”
“I’m thinking,” he responded. “It rises a little earlier each night﻿—or is it later? No matter; come here, Pat.”
She rose wearily and joined him; he slipped his arm about her, and drew her against him.
“Look there,” he said, indicating the night-dark vista beyond the window.

===Chinese===
他茫然又沮丧地挥了挥手，转身走开，大步走向那扇她已拉起百叶的窗。他双手撑在桌上，黯然凝望着楼下的街道。
“医生说过，”他用一种古怪的声调没头没脑地问，“月亮什么时候升起？你还记得吗？”
“不记得，”她紧绷地说。“哦，亲爱的！求求你——我都快疯了，别在这个时候背对着我站着！”
“我在想事情，”他答道。“它每晚都升起得早一点——还是晚一点？无所谓；过来，帕特。”
她疲惫地起身走过去；他揽过手臂，把她拉进怀里。
“看那儿，”他说着，指向窗外夜色沉沉的景象。

===Original===
She looked out upon a dim-lit street or court, at the blind end of which the house was apparently situated. Far off at the open end, across a distant highway where even at this hour passed a constant stream of traffic, flashed a narrow strip of lake; and above it, rising gigantic from the coruscating moon-path, lifted the satellite. She watched the remote flickering of the waves as they tossed back the broken bits of the light strewn along the path. Then she turned puzzled eyes on her companion.
“That’s Heaven,” he said pointing a finger at the great flowing lunar disk. “There’s a world that never caught the planet-cancer called Life, or if it ever suffered, it’s cured. It’s clean﻿—burned clean by the sun and scoured clean by the airless zero of space. A dead world, and therefore not an unhappy one.”
The girl stared at him without comprehension. She murmured, “I don’t understand, Nick.”
“Don’t you, Pat?” He pointed again at the moon. “That’s Heaven, the dead world, and this is Hell, the living one. Heaven and Hell swinging forever about their common center!” He gestured toward the sparkling moon-path on the water. “Look, Pat! The dead world strews flowers on the grave of the living one!”
Some of his bitter ecstasy caught the girl; she felt his somber mood of exaltation.
“I love you, Nick!” she whispered, pressing closely to him.

===Chinese===
她望向外面一条灯光昏暗的街道，或者说是院子，这幢房子显然就坐落在它的死端上。在远远的敞口那头，隔着一条车流不断——即便此刻也依然奔涌不息——的远处的公路，闪现出一带湖水；湖面之上，那颗卫星正从粼粼闪动的月光之路上巍然升起。她望着波浪把洒在那条光路上的细碎光片翻搅着荡回，远处波光闪烁。随后她带着困惑的目光转向同伴。
“那就是天堂，”他伸手指着那轮流泻着光华的巨大月盘说。“那是一个从未染上名为‘生命’的行星癌的世界；即便它曾受过这病，如今也已痊愈。它是洁净的——被太阳灼烧得洁净，被无空气的太空奇寒涤荡得洁净。一个死寂的世界，因而也不是个不幸福的世界。”
姑娘茫然地盯着他。她喃喃道：“我不明白，尼克。”
“你不明白吗，帕特？”他又指向月亮。“那是天堂，死的世界；这是地狱，活的世界。天堂与地狱永远绕着它们共同的中心回旋！”他朝水面上闪烁的月光之路一挥手。“看啊，帕特！死的世界在活世界的坟墓上撒着鲜花！”
他那苦涩的狂喜感染了姑娘；她体会到了他那种阴郁的亢奋。
“我爱你，尼克！”她低声说，紧紧依偎着他。

===Original===
“What difference does it make﻿—our actions?” he queried. “There’s the omen, that lifeless globe in the sky. Where we go, all humanity now living will follow before a century, and in a million years, the human race as well! What if we go a year or a million years before the rest? Will it make any difference in the end?” He looked down at her. “All we’ve been valuing here is hope. To the devil with hope! Let’s have peace instead!”
“I’m not afraid, Nick.”
“Nor I. And if we go, *he* goes, and he’s mortally afraid of death!”
“Can he﻿—prevent you?”
“Not now! I’m the stronger now. For this time, I’m master.”
He turned again to stare at the glowing satellite as it rose imperceptibly from the horizon. “There’s nothing to regret,” he murmured, “except one thing﻿—the loss of beauty. Beauty like that﻿—and like you, Pat. That’s bitterly hard to foreswear!” He leaned forward toward the remote disk of the moon; he spoke as if addressing it, in tones so low that the girl, pressed close to him, had to quiet the sound of her own breath to listen. He said:

===Chinese===
“我们的所作所为——又有什么要紧？”他问。“天上那颗无生命的球体就是预兆。我们去的地方，如今活着的全人类不出一个世纪都会跟来；再过一百万年，整个人类也一样！我们不过比别人早走一年，或者早走一百万年，又能怎样？到头来有什么分别？”他低头看她。“我们在此地珍视的一切，不过是希望。让希望见鬼去吧！我们宁可要安宁！”
“我不怕，尼克。”
“我也不怕。而且我们若是走了，*他*也得跟着走，而他最怕的就是死！”
“他——能拦住你吗？”
“现在不能！如今我更强了。这一次，做主的是我。”
他又转向那轮发光的卫星，望着它从地平线上不易察觉地缓缓升起。“没什么可遗憾的，”他喃喃道，“除了一样——美的失落。那样的美——还有你这样的美，帕特。要舍弃它，真是难如登天！”他朝遥远的月盘俯身过去；他说话的样子仿佛在对着它倾诉，声音低得紧偎着他的姑娘不得不屏住自己的呼吸去聆听。他说：

===Original===
> “Long miles above cloud-bank and blast,
 

 And many miles above the sea,
 

 I watch you rise majestically
 

 Feeling your chilly light at last﻿—
 

 Cold beauty in the way you cast
 

 Split silver fragments on the waves,
 

 As if this planet’s life were past,
 

 And all men peaceful in their graves.”

===Chinese===
> “高出云海与罡风迢迢万里，
 

 更在茫茫沧海之上，
 

 我望你庄严升起，
 

 终于触到你清冷的光——
 

 寒冽的美，在你将
 

 碎银片片抛上波涛，
 

 仿佛这行星的生命已逝，
 

 众生皆安然长眠于坟墓。”

===Original===
Pat was silent for a moment as he paused, then she murmured a low phrase. “Oh, I love you, Nick!” she said.
“And I you, dear,” he responded. “Have we decided anything? Are we﻿—going through with it?”
“I’ve not faltered,” she said soberly. “I meant it, Nick. Without you, life would be as empty as that airless void you speak of. I’m not afraid. What’s there to be afraid of?”
“Only the transition, Pat. That and the unknown﻿—but no situation could possibly be more terrible than our present one. It *couldn’t* be! Oblivion, annihilation﻿—they’re preferable, aren’t they?”
“Oh, yes! Nothing I can imagine could be other than a change for the better.”
“Then let’s face it!” His voice took on a note of determination. “I’ve thought to face it a dozen times before this, and each time I’ve hesitated. The hesitation of a coward, Pat.”

===Chinese===
他停下来后，帕特沉默了片刻，然后低低地呢喃了一句。“哦，我爱你，尼克！”她说。
“我也爱你，亲爱的，”他应道。“我们拿定主意了吗？我们——真要做下去？”
“我没有动摇，”她正色道。“我是认真的，尼克。没有你，人生就会像你说的那种无气的虚空一样空洞。我不害怕。有什么可怕的？”
“怕的只是那个过渡，帕特。还有未知——但再没有哪种境况能比我们眼下的处境更可怕了。*不可能*有！遗忘、湮灭——它们反倒可取，不是吗？”
“哦，是的！我想象不出还有什么不是变得更好。”
“那我们就面对它！”他的声音带上了决然的意味。“在这之前，我已十几次想要面对它，每一次都犹豫了。懦夫式的犹豫，帕特。”

===Original===
“You’re no coward, dear. It was that illusion of hope; that always weakens one. No one’s strong who hasn’t given up hope.”
“Then,” he repeated, “let’s face it!”
“How, Nick?”
“My father has left us the means. There in the cabinet are a hundred deaths﻿—swift ones, lingering ones, painful, and easy! I don’t know one from the other; our choice must be blind.” He strode over to the case, sending slivers of glass from the shattered front glistening along the floor. “I’d choose an easy one, Dear, if I knew, for your sake. Euthanasia!”
He stared hesitantly at the files of mysterious drugs with their incomprehensible labels.
Suddenly the scene appeared humorous to the girl, queerly funny, in some unnatural horrible fashion. Her nerves, overstrained for hours, were on the verge of breaking; without realization of it, she had come to the border of hysteria.

===Chinese===
“你不是懦夫，亲爱的。那是希望的幻象在作祟；它总能削弱一个人。不曾放弃希望的人，谈不上坚强。”
“那么，”他重复道，“我们就面对它！”
“怎么面对，尼克？”
“我父亲给我们留下了手段。那口柜子里有一百种死法——干脆的、拖延的、痛苦的、安详的！我分不清哪个是哪个；我们的选择只能是盲选。”他大步走向柜子，破碎柜门上的玻璃碴被踩得沿地板闪闪滑落。“亲爱的，要是我认得出来，看在你的份上，我会挑一种安详的。安乐死！”
他迟疑地盯着那一排排神秘药剂和上头叫人看不懂的标签。
在姑娘眼里，这场面突然变得好笑了，古怪地滑稽，带着某种不自然的可怕味道。她紧绷了几个小时的神经已濒临断裂；她自己都没意识到，已走到了歇斯底里的边缘。

===Original===
“Shopping for death!” she choked, trying to suppress the wild laughter that beat in her throat. “Which one’s most suitable? Which one’s most becoming? Which one”﻿—a hysterical laughing sob shook her﻿—“will wear the longest?”
He turned, gazing at her with an illogical concern in his face.
“What’s the difference?” she cried wildly. “I don’t care﻿—painful or pleasant, it all ends in the same grave! Close your eyes and choose!”
Suddenly he was holding her in his arms again, and she was sobbing, clinging to him frantically. She was miserably unstrung; her body shook under the impact of her gasping breath. Then gradually, she quieted, and was silent against him.
“We’ve been mad!” he murmured. “It’s been an insane idea﻿—for me to inflict this on you, Pat. Do you think I could consider the destruction of your beauty, Dear? I’ve been lying to myself, stifling my judgment with poetic imagery, when all the while it was just that I’m afraid to face the thing alone!”
“No,” she murmured, burying her face against his shoulder. “I’m the coward, Nick. I’m the one that’s frightened, and I’m the one that broke down! It’s just been﻿—too much, this evening; I’m all right now.”

===Chinese===
“置办死亡！”她哽咽着，竭力压住喉头翻涌的狂笑。“哪一种最合适？哪一种最衬我？哪一种”——一声歇斯底里的带笑呜咽震得她发抖——“最经穿？”
他转过身，脸上带着一种不合时宜的关切望着她。
“有什么分别？”她狂乱地喊道。“我不在乎——痛苦也好，痛快也罢，最后都进同一座坟墓！闭上眼睛挑吧！”
突然他又把她搂进了怀里，她抽泣着，发疯似的紧攀着他。她垮得一塌糊涂；喘息的冲击震得她全身发抖。渐渐地，她平静下来，偎着他沉默了。
“我们疯了！”他喃喃道。“让我把这强加给你，这想法本就疯狂，帕特。你以为我真能容忍毁灭你的美貌吗，亲爱的？我一直在自欺，用诗意的意象扼杀自己的判断，其实说到底，我只是不敢独自面对这件事！”
“不，”她把脸埋进他的肩头喃喃道。“懦夫是我，尼克。害怕的是我，崩溃的也是我！只是今晚——太难受了；我现在好了。”

===Original===
“But we’ll not go through with *this*, Pat!”
“But we will! It’s better than life without you, Dear. We’ve argued and argued, and at last forgotten the one truth, the one thing I’ll never retract: I can’t face living without you, Nick! I can’t!”
He brushed his hand wearily before his eyes. “Back at the starting point,” he muttered. “All right, Honey. So be it!”
He strode again to the cabinet. “Corrosive sublimate,” he murmured. “Cyanide of Potassium. They’re both deadly, but I think the second is rapid, and therefore less painful. Cyanide let it be!”
He extracted two small beakers from the glassware on the shelf. He filled them with water from a carafe on the table, and, while the girl watched him with fascinated eyes, he deliberately tilted a spoonful or so of white crystals into each of them. The mixture swirled a moment, then settled clear and colorless, and the crystals began to shrink as they passed swiftly into solution.
“There it is,” he announced grimly. “There’s peace, oblivion, forgetfulness, and annihilation for you, for me, and﻿—for *him*! Beyond all doubt, the logical course for us, isn’t it? Do we take it?”

===Chinese===
“可我们不能把*这件事*做下去，帕特！”
“我们就是要做！这总好过没有你的人生，亲爱的。我们争了又争，到头来却忘了那唯一的真相，那句我绝不收回的话：没有你，我无法面对生活，尼克！我做不到！”
他疲惫地抬手抹了把脸。“又回到原点了，”他咕哝道。“好吧，亲爱的。就这样吧！”
他再次大步走向柜子。“升汞，”他喃喃念道。“氰化钾。两个都致命，不过我想第二种快，因而痛苦也少些。就用氰化钾吧！”
他从架上的玻璃器皿里取出两只小烧杯，用桌上的水瓶倒了水，在姑娘出神的注视下，从容地往每只杯里倾入一勺上下的白色晶体。混合液打了个旋，随即澄清无色，晶体迅速溶解，渐渐缩小。
“就在这儿了，”他冷峻地宣布。“这里有安宁、忘却、遗忘与寂灭，给你，给我，也给——*他*！毫无疑问，这是合乎逻辑的路，不是吗？我们喝吗？”

===Original===
“Please,” she said faintly. “Kiss me first, Honey. Isn’t that the proper course for lovers in this situation?” She felt a faint touch of astonishment at her own irony; the circumstances had ceased to have any reality to her, and had become merely a dramatic sequence like the happenings in a play.
He gathered her again into his arms and pressed his lips to hers. It was a long, tender, wistful kiss; when at last it ended, Pat found her eyes again filled with tears, but not this time the tears of hysteria.
“Nick!” she murmured. “Nick, darling!”
He gave her a deep, somber, but very tender smile, and reached for one of the deadly beakers, “To another meeting!” he said as his fingers closed on it.
Suddenly, amazingly, the strident ring of a doorbell sounded, the more surprising since they had all but forgotten the existence of a world about them. Interruption! It meant only the going through once more of all that they had just passed.
“Drink it!” exclaimed Pat impulsively, seizing the remaining beaker.

===Chinese===
“求你，”她虚弱地说。“先吻我，亲爱的。这种处境里的恋人，不都该这么做吗？”她对自己的讥诮微感诧异；这境况于她已失去一切真实感，只剩下戏台上那一幕幕的戏剧进程。
他再次把她揽进怀里，将唇贴上她的唇。那是一个悠长、温柔而怅惘的吻；它终于结束时，帕特发觉自己眼中又盈满了泪水，但这一次，不再是歇斯底里的泪。
“尼克！”她喃喃着。“尼克，亲爱的！”
他朝她深深一笑，阴郁却极尽温柔，然后伸手去取一只夺命的烧杯。“为了再会！”他的手指扣上杯身时说道。
突然，难以置信地，一阵刺耳的门铃声响起，愈发令人错愕——他们几乎忘了周遭还有一个世界的存在。打扰！这只意味着，方才经历的一切都得从头再来一遍。
“喝掉它！”帕特冲动地喊道，一把抓起剩下的那只烧杯。

## XIX Man or Monster? / 十九 人，还是怪物？
===Original===
Pat suffered Wednesday through somehow, knowing that any such early response to her letter was impossible. Still, that impossibility did not deter her from starting at the sound of the telephone, and sorting through the mail with an eagerness that drew a casual attention from her mother.
“Good Heavens, Patricia! You’re like a child watching for an answer to his note to Santa Claus!”
“That’s what I am, I guess,” responded the girl ruefully. “Maybe I expect too much from Santa Claus.”
Late in the afternoon she drifted over to Dr. Horker’s residence, to be informed that he was out. For distraction, she went in anyway, and spent a while browsing among the books in the library. She blundered into Kraft-Ebing, and read a few pages in growing indignation.

===Chinese===
帕特勉勉强强熬过了星期三，心里明白她的信不可能这么快就有回音。然而这个不可能，并没有阻止她一听见电话铃就惊跳起来，也没有阻止她满怀热切地翻拣邮件——那股热切劲儿，引得母亲随口投来注意。
“天哪，帕特里夏！你活像个巴望圣诞老人回信的孩子！”
“我看我就是，”姑娘苦笑着答道。“也许我对圣诞老人指望得太多了。”
傍晚时分，她信步溜达到霍克医生的宅邸，却被告知他外出了。为了散心，她还是进去了，在书房里翻了一会儿书。她一头撞上了克拉夫特-埃宾，越读越愤慨地看了几页。

===Original===
“I’m ashamed to be human!” she muttered disgustedly to herself, slamming shut the *Psychopathia Sexualis*. “I wouldn’t be a doctor, or have a child of mine become one, if I were positively certain he’d turn into Lord Lister himself! Nick was right when he said doctors live on people’s troubles.”
She wondered how Dr. Horker could remain so human, so kindly and understanding, when as he said himself his world was a parade of misfits, incompetents, and all the nastiness of mortals. *He* was nice; she felt no embarrassment in confiding in him even when she might hesitate to bare her feelings to her own mother. Or was it simply the natural thing to do to tell one’s troubles to a doctor?
Not, of course, that the situation reflected any discredit on her mother. Mrs. Lane was a very precious sort of parent, she mused, young as Pat in spirit, appreciative and enthusiastically fond of her daughter. That she trusted Pat, that she permitted her to do entirely as she pleased, was exactly as the girl would have it; it argued no lack of affection that each of them had their separate interests, and if the girl occasionally found herself in unpleasantness such as this, that too was her own fault.
And yet, she reflected, it was a bitter thing to have no one to whom to turn. If it weren’t for Dr. Carl and his jovial willingness to commit any sin up to malpractice to help her, she might have felt differently. But there always *was* Dr. Carl, and that, she concluded, was that.

===Chinese===
“我耻于做个人类！”她厌恶地自言自语，砰地合上那本《性精神病态》。“就算我能百分百确定我儿子会成为李斯特勋爵本人，我也不愿当医生，也不愿让我的孩子去当！尼克说得对，医生就是靠世人的烦恼过活的。”
她不明白，霍克医生怎么能保持这么有人情味、这么和善体贴——他自己都说过，他的世界就是一场不合时宜者、无能者和人类一切污秽的大游行。*他*是可亲的；向母亲袒露心迹都可能犹豫的事，向他倾诉却毫不难为情。抑或，把自己的烦恼讲给医生听，本就是天经地义的事？
当然，这并不是说这种处境有损她母亲的名声。莱恩太太是位极为难得的母亲，她暗想，心境和帕特一样年轻，赏识女儿、疼爱女儿至极。她信任帕特，任由她完全自行其是，这正合姑娘的心意；母女各人有各人的兴趣，这并不说明缺少亲情；而姑娘偶尔陷入眼下这类麻烦，那也是她自己之过。
然而，她想，身边没有一个可以投靠的人，终究是件苦事。若不是卡尔医生乐呵呵地甘愿为她犯下任何罪——直至医疗事故——她也许会另有感受。但卡尔医生*总是*在的，如此而已，她得出结论。

===Original===
She wandered back to her own side of the hedge, missing for the first time in many weeks the companionship of the old crowd. There hadn’t been many idle afternoons heretofore during the summer; there’d always been some of the collegiate vacationing in town, and Pat had never needed other lure than her own piquant vivacity to assure herself of ample attention. Now, of course, it was different; she had so definitely tagged herself with the same Nicholas Devine that even the most ardent of the group had taken the warning.
“And I don’t regret it either!” she told herself as she entered the house. “Trouble, mystery, suffering and all﻿—I don’t regret it! I’ve had my compensations too.”
She sighed and trudged upstairs to prepare for dinner.
---
Morning found Pat in a fair frenzy of trepidation. She kept repeating to herself that two days wasn’t enough, that more time might be required, that even had Nicholas Devine received her letter, he might not have answered at once. Yet she was quivering as she darted into the hall to examine the mail.

===Chinese===
她缓步走回篱笆自己家这一边，许多个星期以来头一回怀念起老伙伴们的陪伴。这个夏天先前倒没多少清闲的下午；城里总有些放假的大学生，帕特从来不需要别的诱饵，单凭自己那份俏皮的活泼，就足以让她成为众人瞩目的中心。如今当然不同了；她已被明确地贴上了“尼古拉斯·德文的人”这个标签，连那伙人里最热烈的几位也识趣地退开了。
“而且我也不后悔！”她走进家门时对自己说。“烦恼、神秘、苦难，全都算上——我不后悔！我也得到了我的补偿。”
她叹了口气，拖着步子上楼去梳洗，准备晚餐。
---
清晨来临，帕特已处在相当程度的惶恐狂乱之中。她不断对自己重复：两天不够，也许还需要更多时间，就算尼古拉斯·德文收到了她的信，也未必立刻作答。可她冲进门厅查看邮件时，仍旧浑身发颤。

===Original===
It was there! She spied a fragment of the irregular handwriting and seized the envelope from beneath a clutter of notes, bills, and advertisements. She glanced at the postmark. Chicago! He hadn’t left the city, trusting perhaps to the anonymity conferred by its colossal swarm of humanity. Indeed, she thought as she stared at the missive, he might have moved around the corner, and save for the chance of a fortuitous meeting she’d never know it.
She tore open the envelope and scanned the several scrawled lines.
No heading, no salutation, not even a signature. Just, “Thursday evening at our place in the park.” No more; she studied the few words intently, as if she could read into their bald phrasing the moods and hidden emotions of the writer.
A single phrase, but sufficient. The day was suddenly brighter, and the hope which had glowed so dimly yesterday was abruptly almost more than a hope﻿—a certainty. All her doubts of Dr. Horker’s abilities were forgotten; already the solution of this uncanny mystery seemed assured, and the restoration of romance imminent. She carried the letter to her own room and tucked it carefully by the other in the drawer of the night-table.
Thursday evening﻿—this evening! Many hours intervened between now and a reasonable time for the meeting, but they loomed no longer drab, dull, and hopeless. She lay on her bed and dreamed.

===Chinese===
信在那儿！她瞥见一角那不规则的潦草字迹，从一堆便笺、账单和广告底下抽出那只信封。她看了一眼邮戳。芝加哥！他没有离开这座城市，大概是仗着那数百万熙攘人海所赋予的匿名之便。的确，她盯着那封信想，他说不定就搬到街角去了，若不是碰巧撞见，她永远无从知晓。
她撕开信封，扫视那几行涂鸦般的字。
没有抬头，没有称呼，连签名都没有。只有一句：“星期四晚上，公园里我们的老地方。”再无其他；她细细琢磨这几个字，仿佛能从那光秃秃的措辞里读出写信人的心绪与隐情。
寥寥一语，却足够了。这一天陡然明亮起来，昨天还那么黯淡的希望，骤然间几乎已不止是希望——而是确凿无疑。她对霍克医生本领的一切怀疑统统忘光了；这桩离奇之谜的解答似乎已十拿九稳，爱情的复归近在眼前。她把信拿回自己房里，小心地与另一封并排收进床头柜的抽屉。
星期四晚上——就是今晚！从现在到赴约的合适时辰，中间还隔着好几个钟头，可它们不再显得阴郁、沉闷、无望。她躺在床上做梦。

===Original===
She could meet Nick as early as possible; perhaps at eight-thirty, and bring him directly to the Doctor’s residence. No use wasting a moment, she mused; the sooner some light could be thrown on the affliction, the sooner they could lay the devil﻿—exorcise it. Demon, fixed idea, mental aberration, or whatever Dr. Carl chose to call it, it had to be met and vanquished once and forever. And it *could* be vanquished; in her present mood she didn’t doubt it. Then﻿—after that﻿—there was the prospect of her own Nick regained, and the sweet vistas opened by that reflection.
She lunched in an abstracted manner. In the afternoon, when the phone rang, she jumped in a startled manner, then relaxed with a shrug.
But this time it *was* for her. She darted into the hall to take the call on the lower phone; she was hardly surprised but thoroughly excited to recognize the voice of Nicholas Devine.
“Pat?”
“Nick! Oh, Nick, Honey! What is it?”
“My note to you.” Even across the wire she sensed the strain in his tense tones. “You’ve read it?”

===Chinese===
她要尽早去见尼克；也许八点半就动身，直接带他去医生的宅邸。一刻也不能浪费，她想；这病灶早一天照出光亮，他们就能早一天降服那魔鬼——把它驱走。恶魔也好，执念也好，精神失常也好，随卡尔医生爱怎么叫它，它都必须被迎头击溃，一劳永逸。而且它*必定*能被击溃；以她此刻的心境，她对此毫不怀疑。然后——在那之后——便是夺回她自己的尼克的前景，是这念头所敞开的甜美远景。
她心不在焉地吃了午饭。下午电话铃响时，她吃惊地跳起来，随即耸耸肩放松下来。
可这一次*真是*找她的。她冲进门厅，拿起楼下的话筒；听出尼古拉斯·德文的声音，她几乎不觉得意外，却彻底兴奋起来。
“帕特？”
“尼克！哦，尼克，亲爱的！怎么了？”
“我给你的便条。”隔着电话线她也觉察得出他紧绷语调里的张力。“你看了吗？”

===Original===
“Of course, Nick! I’ll be there.”
“No.” His voice was trembling. “You won’t come, Pat. Promise you won’t!”
“But why? Why not, Nick? Oh, it’s terribly important that I see you!”
“You’re not to come, Pat!”
“But﻿—” An idea was struggling to her consciousness. “Nick, was it﻿—?”
“Yes. You know now.”

===Chinese===
“当然，尼克！我会去的。”
“不。”他的声音在发抖。“你不要来，帕特。答应我你不来！”
“可是为什么？为什么不能来，尼克？哦，见到你对我太重要了！”
“你不许来，帕特！”
“可是——”一个念头正挣扎着浮上她的意识。“尼克，是不是——？”
“是的。你现在知道了。”

===Original===
“But, Honey, what difference does it make? *You* come. You must, Nick!”
“I won’t meet you, I tell you!” She could hear his voice rising excitedly in pitch, she could feel the intensity of the struggle across unknown miles of lifeless copper wire.
“Nick,” she said, “I’m going to be there, and you’re going to meet me.”
There was silence at the other end.
“Nick!” she cried anxiously. “Do you hear me? I’ll be there. Will you?”
His voice sounded again, now flat and toneless.

===Chinese===
“可是，亲爱的，那又有什么关系？*你*来呀。你必须来，尼克！”
“我不见你，我告诉你！”她听得见他激动的嗓音陡然拔高，她能感受到那场挣扎的剧烈，正隔着不知多少英里毫无生气的铜线传来。
“尼克，”她说，“我会到那儿，你也会来见我。”
电话那头一片沉默。
“尼克！”她焦急地喊。“你听见了吗？我会到那儿。你呢？”
他的声音再度响起，此刻平淡而喑哑。

===Original===
“Yes,” he said. “I’ll be there.”
The receiver clicked at the far end of the wire; there was only a futile buzzing in Pat’s ears. She replaced the instrument and sat staring dubiously at it.
Had that been Nick, really her Nick, or﻿—? Suppose she went to that meeting and found﻿—the other? Was she willing to face another evening of indignities and terrors like those still fresh in her memory?
Still, she argued, what harm could come to her on that bench, exposed as it was to the gaze of thousands who wandered through the park on summer evenings? Suppose it *were* the other who met her; there was no way to force her into a situation such as that of Saturday night. Nick himself had chosen that very spot for their other meeting, and for that very reason.
“There’s no risk in it,” she told herself, “Nothing can possibly happen. I’ll simply go there and bring Nick back to Dr. Carl’s, along a lighted, busy street, the whole two blocks. What’s there to be afraid of?”
Nothing at all, she answered herself. But suppose﻿—She shuddered and deliberately abandoned her chain of thought as she rose and rejoined her mother.

===Chinese===
“好吧，”他说。“我会到那儿。”
听筒在电话线远端咔哒一声挂断；帕特耳中只剩徒劳的嗡嗡声。她放下话筒，坐着满腹狐疑地盯着它。
刚才是尼克吗，真正属于她的尼克，还是——？假如她赴约时发现来的是——另一个？那些屈辱与恐怖记忆犹新，她还愿意再面对这样一个夜晚吗？
不过，她辩解道，在那条长椅上又能出什么伤害？夏日的傍晚，成千上万的人在公园里漫步，长椅正暴露在众目睽睽之下。就算来见她的是*另一个*；也没有办法把她逼进星期六晚上那样的境地。尼克本人正是为了这个缘故，才为他们的另一次会面选中了那个地点。
“这没有什么风险，”她对自己说。“什么都不会发生。我只要到那儿，带着尼克沿一条灯火通明、人来人往的大街回卡尔医生家，统共两个街区。有什么可怕的？”
什么都没有，她自答。可是万一——她打了个寒战，刻意掐断了这条思路，起身回到母亲身边。

## XXXI “Not Humanly Possible” / 三十一 “非人力所能”
===Original===
Pat opened weary eyes and gazed at a blank, uninformative ceiling. It was some moments before she realized that she was lying on the couch in the room of Nicholas Devine. Somebody had placed her there, presumably, since she was quite unaware of the circumstances of her awakening. Then recollection began to form﻿—Dr. Carl, the *other*, the roar of a shot. After that, nothing save a turmoil ending in blankness.
A sound of movement beside her drew her attention. She turned her head and perceived Dr. Horker kneeling over a form on the floor, fingering a white bandage about the head of the figure. Her recollections took instant form; she remembered the catastrophes of the evening﻿—last night, rather, since dawn glowed dully in the window. She had shot Nick! She gave a little moan and pushed herself to a sitting position.
The Doctor glanced at her with a sick, shaky smile. “Hello,” he said. “Come to, have you? Sorry I couldn’t give you any attention.” He gave the bandage a final touch. “Here’s a job I had no heart for,” he muttered. “Better for everyone to let things happen without interference.”
The girl, returning to full awareness, noticed now that the bandage consisted of strips of the Doctor’s shirt. She glanced fearfully at the still features of Nicholas Devine; she saw pale cheeks and closed eyes, but indubitably not the grim mien of the demon.
“Dr. Carl!” she whispered. “He isn’t﻿—he isn’t﻿—”
“Not yet.”

===Chinese===
帕特睁开疲惫的双眼，望着一片空白、什么也告诉不了她的天花板。过了好一会儿她才意识到，自己正躺在尼古拉斯·德文房间的沙发上。大概是有人把她安置在那儿的，因为对于自己如何醒来，她全无印象。随后记忆开始成形——卡尔医生，那个*另一个*，一声枪响的轰鸣。再往后，就只剩一片混乱，归于空白。
身旁一阵响动引开了她的注意。她转过头，看见霍克医生跪在地板上的一具人形旁，正摆弄着那人头上的白色绷带。她的回忆霎时成形；她记起了那个晚上的灾祸——不如说是昨夜，因为窗里已泛起暗淡的曙光。她开枪打了尼克！她低哼一声，撑着坐了起来。
医生瞥了她一眼，脸上是一个虚弱发颤的笑。“哈罗，”他说。“醒过来了？抱歉没能照看你。”他在绷带上最后按了按。“这种活儿我实在下不了手，”他咕哝道。“让事情不受干扰地自行发生，对谁都好。”
姑娘完全清醒过来，这才注意到绷带是用医生的衬衫撕成的布条。她战战兢兢地望向尼古拉斯·德文那张一动不动的脸；她看见苍白的双颊和闭着的眼，但那分明不是恶魔的狰狞面容。
“卡尔医生！”她低唤。“他不是——他不是——”
“还没有。”

===Original===
“But will he﻿—?”
“I don’t know. That’s a bad spot, a wound in the base of the brain. You’d best know it now, Pat, but also realize that nothing can happen to you. I’ll see to that!”
“To me!” she said dully. “What difference does that make? It’s Nick I want saved.”
“I’ll do my best for you, Honey,” said Horker with almost a hint of reluctance. “I’ve phoned Briggs General for an ambulance. Your faint lasted a full quarter hour,” he added.
“What can we tell them?” asked the girl. “What can we say?”
“Don’t you say anything, Pat. I’m not on the board for nothing.” He rose from his knees, glancing out of the window into the cool dawn. “Queer neighborhood!” he said. “All that yelling and a shot, and still no sign of interest from the neighbors. That’s Chicago, though,” he mused. “Lucky for us, Pat; we can handle the thing quietly now.”

===Chinese===
“可他会不会——？”
“我不知道。那位置很凶险，是颅底的伤。你现在最好心里有数，帕特，但也要明白，你不会出任何事。这点我来保证！”
“我！”她木然说。“那有什么要紧？我要救的是尼克。”
“我会为你尽力的，亲爱的，”霍克说，语气里几乎带一丝勉强。“我已经给布里格斯综合医院打过电话叫救护车了。你晕了足足一刻钟，”他补了一句。
“我们怎么跟他们说？”姑娘问。“我们能说什么？”
“你什么也不用说，帕特。我不是白当医院董事的。”他从地上站起身，望向窗外清凉的黎明。“这街坊真怪！”他说。“又喊又叫还开了枪，邻居们竟没有一丝关注的迹象。不过这就是芝加哥，”他沉吟道。“咱们走运，帕特；这事现在能悄悄了结。”

===Original===
But the girl was staring dully at the still figure on the floor. “Oh God!” she said huskily. “Help him, Dr. Carl!”
“I’ll do my best,” responded Horker gloomily. “I was a good surgeon before I specialized in psychiatry. Brain surgery, too; it led right into my present field.”
Pat said nothing, but dropped her head on her hands and stared vacantly before her.
“Better for you, and for him too, if I fail,” muttered the Doctor.
His words brought a reply. “You won’t fail,” she said tensely. “You won’t!”
“Not voluntarily, I’m afraid,” he growled morosely. “I’ve still a little respect for medical ethics, but if ever a case﻿—” His voice trailed into silence as from somewhere in the dawn sounded the wail of a siren. “There’s the ambulance,” he finished.

===Chinese===
可姑娘只是木然盯着地上那不动的人影。“哦，上帝！”她声音沙哑地说。“救救他，卡尔医生！”
“我会尽力，”霍克阴郁地应道。“在专攻精神病学之前，我是个不错的外科医生。脑外科也做过；正是它把我领进了如今这一行。”
帕特没说话，只把头垂到手上，失神地望着前方。
“我要是失手了，对你、对他反倒都好，”医生喃喃道。
他的话引来了回答。“你不会失手的，”她绷紧了声音说。“你不会！”
“只怕不是出于本心，”他阴沉地咕哝。“我对医道还有几分敬畏，可这样的病例——”他的声音渐弱无声，黎明中的某处响起了救护车的哀鸣。“救护车来了，”他说完。

===Original===
Pat sat unmoving as the sounds from outdoors detailed the stopping of the vehicle before the house. She heard the Doctor descending the steps, and the creak of the door. Though it took place before her eyes, she scarcely saw the white-coated youths as they lifted the form of Nicholas Devine and bore it from the room on a stretcher, treading with carefully broken steps to prevent the swaying of the support. Dr. Horker’s order to follow made no impression on her; she sat dully on the couch as the chamber emptied.
Why, she wondered, had the thought of Nick’s death disturbed her so? Wasn’t it but a short time since they had both contemplated it? What had occurred to alter that determination? Nick was dying, she thought mournfully; all that remained was for her to follow. There on the floor lay the revolver, and on the table, glistening in the wan light, reposed the untouched lethal draft. That was the preferable way, she mused, staring fixedly at its glowing contour.
But suppose Nick weren’t to die﻿—she’d have abandoned him to his terrible doom, left him to face a situation far more ominous than any unknown terrors beyond death. She shook her head distractedly, and looked up to meet the eyes of Dr. Horker, who was watching her gravely in the doorway.
“Come on, Pat,” he said gently.
She rose, followed him down the stairs and out into the morning light. The driver of the ambulance stared curiously at her dishevelled, bedraggled figure, but she was so weary and forlorn that even the effort of brushing away the black strands of hair that clouded her smoke-dark eyes was beyond her. She slumped into the seat of the Doctor’s car and sighed in utter exhaustion.
“Rush it!” Horker called to the driver ahead. “I’ll follow you.”

===Chinese===
屋外的声响一一诉说着救护车在房前停下的过程，帕特坐着没动。她听见医生走下台阶，听见门轴的吱呀。虽然一切都发生在眼前，她却几乎没看见那些白大褂青年抬起尼古拉斯·德文的身体、用担架抬出房去，碎着步子小心走着以免担架晃动。霍克医生让她跟上的吩咐，她也没听进去；房间空了，她还呆坐在沙发上。
为什么，她纳闷，尼克死的念头让她如此不安？不久之前，他们俩不还一同盘算过此事吗？是什么改变了那个决心？尼克快要死了，她悲苦地想；剩下的只是随他而去。地板上躺着那把左轮枪，桌上，在惨淡的光线里闪烁着的，是那杯原封未动的致命药液。那才是更好的方式，她凝视着它微光的轮廓，暗自思忖。
可万一尼克死不成呢——那她就等于弃他于可怕的宿命，任他去面对一种比死后任何未知的恐怖都凶险得多的境地。她心烦意乱地摇摇头，抬起头来，正遇上霍克医生的目光，他正在门口严肃地注视着她。
“走吧，帕特，”他温和地说。
她起身跟着他下楼，走进晨光里。救护车司机好奇地打量她衣衫不整、狼狈不堪的样子，可她实在太疲惫、太凄惶，连拂开那几缕遮住她暗色眸子的黑发的力气都没有了。她瘫进医生汽车的座椅，精疲力竭地叹了口气。
“开快点！”霍克朝前面的司机喊。“我跟着你。”

===Original===
The car swept into motion, and the swift cool morning air beating against her face from the open window restored some clarity to her mind. She fixed her eyes on the rear of the speeding vehicle they followed.
“Is there any hope at all?” she queried despondently.
“I don’t know, Pat. I can’t tell yet. When you closed your eyes, he half turned, dodged; the bullet entered his skull near the base, near the cerebellum. If it had pierced the cerebellum, his heart and breathing must have stopped instantly. They didn’t, however, and that’s a mildly hopeful sign. Very mildly hopeful, though.”
“Do you know now what that devil﻿—what the attack was?”
“No, Pat,” Horker admitted. “I don’t. Call it a devil if you like; I can’t name it any better.” His voice changed to a tone of wonder. “Pat, I can’t understand that paralyzing fascination the thing exerted. I﻿—any medical man﻿—would say that mental dominance of that sort doesn’t exist.”
“Hypnotism,” the girl suggested.

===Chinese===
汽车疾驰而去，敞开的车窗外迅疾清凉的晨风扑在她脸上，让她头脑恢复了几分清明。她目不转睛地盯着前头那辆飞驰的车子的尾部。
“到底还有没有希望？”她沮丧地问。
“我不知道，帕特。现在还说不好。你闭上眼睛的时候，他半侧过身，躲了一下；子弹从他靠近颅底、靠近小脑的地方射进了颅骨。要是穿进了小脑，他的心跳和呼吸必定当场就停。可它们没有，这是个勉强算得上希望的迹象。不过也只是很勉强的希望。”
“你现在知道那个魔鬼——那次发作是怎么回事了吗？”
“不知道，帕特，”霍克承认。“我不明白。你愿意叫它魔鬼就叫吧；我也起不出更好的名字。”他的声音转成了困惑的调子。“帕特，我无法理解那东西施加的那种令人瘫痪的魅惑。我——任何一个医学人士——都会说，那种精神支配根本不存在。”
“催眠术，”姑娘提了一句。

===Original===
“Bah! Every psychiatrist uses hypnotism in his business; it’s part of some treatments. There’s nothing of fascination about it; no dominance of one will over another, despite the popular view. That’s natural and understandable; this was like﻿—well, like the exploded claims of Mesmerism. I tell you, it’s not humanly possible﻿—and yet I felt it!”
“Not *humanly* possible,” murmured Pat. “That’s the answer, then, Dr. Carl. Maybe now you’ll believe in my devil.”
“I’m tempted to.”
“You’ll have to! Can’t you see it, Dr. Carl? Even his name, Nick﻿—that’s a colloquialism for the devil, isn’t it?”
“And Devine, I suppose,” said Horker, “refers to his angelic ancestry. Devils are only fallen angels, aren’t they?”
“All right,” said Pat wearily. “Make fun of it. You’ll see!”

===Chinese===
“呸！哪个精神科医生行医不用催眠术；那是某些疗法的组成部分。它根本谈不上魅惑；没有一种意志对另一种意志的支配，尽管大众都那么看。那种东西自然又可以理解；而这个像——嗯，像麦斯麦术那些早已被戳穿的把戏。我跟你说，这不是人力所能——可我亲身感受到了！”
“不是*人力*所能，”帕特喃喃道。“那么答案就在这儿了，卡尔医生。也许这下你肯信我的魔鬼了。”
“我有点动心了。”
“你非信不可！你还不明白吗，卡尔医生？连他的名字，尼克——那在俗语里就是魔鬼的意思，不是吗？”
“那我猜，德文这个姓，”霍克说，“指的是他的天使血统。魔鬼不过是堕落的天使，不是吗？”
“行，”帕特疲惫地说。“拿它开玩笑吧。有你瞧的！”

===Original===
“I’m not making fun of your theory, Honey. I can’t offer a better one myself. I never saw nor heard of anything similar, and I’m not in position to ridicule any theory.”
“But you don’t believe me.”
“Of course I don’t, Pat. You’re weaving an intricate fairy tale about a pathological condition and a fortuitous suggestiveness in names. Whatever the condition is﻿—and I confess I don’t understand it﻿—it’s something rational, and those things can be treated.”
“Treated by exorcism,” said the girl. “That’s the only way anyone ever succeeded in casting out a devil.”
The Doctor made no answer. The wailing vehicle ahead of them swung rapidly out of sight into an alley, and Horker halted his car before the gray façade of Briggs General.
“Come in here,” he said, helping Pat to alight. “You’ll want to wait, won’t you?”

===Chinese===
“我不是在笑你的理论，亲爱的。我自己也拿不出更好的。我从未见过也没听说过类似的事，我没资格嘲笑任何理论。”
“可你不相信我。”
“我当然不信，帕特。你是在围绕一种病理状态和名字上的巧合暗示，编织一个精巧的童话。不管那病症是什么——我承认我不懂——它总归是种合乎理性的东西，而那类东西是可以治的。”
“用驱魔术来治，”姑娘说。“那是人类驱走魔鬼唯一成功过的方式。”
医生没有作答。前头那辆哀鸣的车子一拐弯迅速消失在一条巷子里，霍克把车停在布里格斯综合医院灰色的大楼前。
“进来吧，”他说，扶帕特下车。“你要等的，对吧？”

===Original===
