“Nick!” she called. Somewhere ahead a light flashed on; she saw the well of a staircase, and heard his footsteps descending. She followed in frantic haste, gaining the top step just as the pounding below ceased. She heard the click of the door, and paused suddenly at the sound of a familiar voice.
“Where’s Pat?” The words drifted up in low, rumbling, ominous tones.
“Dr. Carl!” she shrieked. She ran swiftly down the stairs to Nick’s side, where he stood facing the great figure of the Doctor. “Dr. Carl! How’d you find me?”

===Chinese===
“又是那个希望的幻象，”她低语。“它就这点本事，尼克——而这意味着现在一切都得从头再来一遍！整件事，从起点重来——而我们刚才已经那么接近——终点！”
“我知道，”他凄然说。“这些我全知道，可是——”楼下的喧嚣加倍执着起来，他停了停。“我去应门，”他最后说。
他离开她，穿过这房间唯一的门消失了。她一动不动地望着他离去，可他刚从视野里消失，一种莫名的恐惧便压上她心头。她甩开麻木与倦怠，一阵风似的追进门厅的黑暗。
“尼克！”她喊。前方某处灯光乍亮；她看见楼梯的井道，听见他的脚步向下。她疯了似的追下去，刚到顶阶，楼下的擂门声停了。她听见门扣一声轻响，随后一个熟悉的声音让她蓦地止步。
“帕特在哪儿？”这句话低沉、滚雷一般、带着不祥，飘上楼来。
“卡尔医生！”她尖声叫。她飞奔下楼，奔到尼克身旁——他正面对着医生那高大的身影。“卡尔医生！你怎么找到我的？”

===Original===
The newcomer gave her a long, narrow-eyed, speculative survey. “I spent nearly the whole night doing it,” he growled at last. “It took me hours to locate Mueller and get this address from him.” He stepped forward, taking the girl’s arm. “Come on!” he said gruffly, without a glance at Nick standing silently beside her. “I’m taking you home!”
She held back. “But why?”
“Why? Because I don’t like the company you keep. Is that reason enough?”
She still resisted his insistent tug. “Nick hasn’t done anything,” she said defiantly, with a side glance at the youth’s flushed, unhappy features.
“He hasn’t? Look at yourself, girl! Look at your clothes, and your forehead! What’s more, I saw enough from my window; I saw him bundle you into that car!” His eyes were flashing angrily, and his grip on her arm tightened, while his free hand clenched into an enormous fist.
“That wasn’t Nick!”

===Chinese===
来客眯起眼，上上下下打量了她许久。“我花了几乎一整夜，”他终于咆哮道。“光是找到缪勒、再从他嘴里问出这个地址，就耗了我几个钟头。”他跨步上前，攥住姑娘的臂膀。“走！”他粗声说，看也不看默默立在她身旁的尼克。“我送你回家！”
她往后挣。“可是为什么？”
“为什么？我不喜欢你结交的伴儿。这理由够不够？”
她仍在抗拒他执拗的拉拽。“尼克什么也没做，”她倔强地说，侧目瞥了瞥年轻人涨红而不快的脸。
“什么也没做？照照你自己吧，姑娘！看看你的衣裳，看看你的额头！再说，从我窗口我看了个够；我亲眼见他把你塞进那辆车！”他双眼冒火，攥着她手臂的手越收越紧，另一只手则捏成一只硕大的拳头。
“那不是尼克！”

===Original===
“No. It was your devil, I suppose!” said Horker sarcastically. “Anyway, Pat, you’re coming with me before I do violence to what remains of your devil!”
Nick spoke for the first time since the Doctor’s entrance. “Please do, Pat,” he said softly. “Please go with him.”
“I won’t!” she snapped. The sudden shifts of situation during the long hours of that terrible evening were irritating her. She had alternated so rapidly between horror and hope and despair that her frayed nerves had seized now at the same reality of anger. Her mind, so long overstrained, was now deliberately forgetting her swing from the pit of terror to the verge of death. “You come up like a hero to the rescue!” she taunted the doctor. “Hairbreadth Horker!”
“You little fool!” growled the Doctor. “A fine reception, after losing a night’s sleep! I’ll drag you home, if I have to!” He moved ponderously toward the door; she gave a violent wrench and freed her arm from his grasp.
“If you can, you mean!” she jeered. She looked at his exasperated face, and suddenly, with one of her abrupt changes of mood, she softened. “Dr. Carl, Honey,” she said in apologetic tones, “I’m sorry. You’re very sweet, and I’m really grateful, but I can’t leave Nick now.” Her eyes turned troubled. “Not now.”
“Why, Pat?” Mollified by the change in her mien, his voice rumbled in sympathetic notes.

===Chinese===
“对。我猜是你的魔鬼！”霍克讥讽道。“反正，帕特，趁我还没对你那魔鬼剩下的一半动手，你先跟我走！”
自医生进门后，尼克头一回开口。“听他的吧，帕特，”他轻声说。“请跟他走吧。”
“我不！”她厉声道。那个可怕夜晚的漫长时辰里，局面一次次陡转，搅得她心头火起。她在恐怖、希望与绝望之间转变得太快，磨秃了的神经这回一把抓住了愤怒这同一种真实。她的头脑紧绷太久，此刻索性刻意忘掉自己怎样从恐怖的深渊荡到死亡的边缘。“你倒像英雄似的赶来救驾了！”她挖苦医生。“千钧一发霍克！”
“你这个小傻瓜！”医生咆哮。“害我一夜没睡，就落得这番款待！逼急了我拖也把你拖回家！”他迈着沉重的步子往门口去；她猛一挣，把手臂从他手里夺了回来。
“你是说，只要你拖得动！”她嘲道。她看看他那气急败坏的脸，忽然又犯了情绪急转的老毛病，软了下来。“卡尔医生，亲爱的，”她用抱歉的口气说，“对不起。你待我真好，我也真心感激，可我现在不能丢下尼克。”她的眼神转为忧烦。“现在不行。”
“为什么，帕特？”她神色的转变让他消了气，嗓音里滚出同情的调子。

===Original===
“I can’t,” she repeated. “It’s﻿—it’s getting worse.”
“Bah!”
“So it’s ‘Bah’!” she flared. “Well, if you’re so contemptuous of the thing, why don’t you cure it? What good did your psychoanalysis do? You don’t even know what it is!”
“What do you expect?” roared the Doctor. “Can I diagnose it by absent treatment? I haven’t had a chance to see the condition active yet!”
“All right!” said Pat, her strained nerves driving her to impatience. “You’re here and Nick’s here! Go on with your diagnosis; get it over with, and let’s see what you can do. *You* ought at least to be able to name the condition﻿—the outstanding authority in the Middle West on neural and mental pathology!” Her tone was sardonic.
“Listen, Pat,” said Horker with exaggerated patience, in the manner of one addressing a stupid child, “I’ve explained before that I can’t get at the root of a mental aberration when the subject’s as unstrung as your young man here seems to be. Psychoanalysis just won’t work unless the subject is calm, composed, and not in a nervous state. Can you comprehend that?”

===Chinese===
“我不行，”她重复道。“它——它越来越糟了。”
“呸！”
“好啊，一个‘呸’字！”她发作了。“行，你要是这么瞧不起这毛病，干吗不把它治好？你的精神分析顶了什么用？你连它是什么都不知道！”
“你还想怎样？”医生吼道。“难道我能隔着千里遥诊？我至今还没机会见过它发作时的样子！”
“好哇！”帕特说，紧绷的神经逼出了她的急躁。“你在这儿，尼克也在这儿！接着诊你的断吧；快些弄完，倒要看看你有什么本事。*你*总该至少叫得出这病症的名字——中西部神经与精神病理学的头号权威！”她的腔调带着讥诮。
“听着，帕特，”霍克拿出一种夸张的耐心，像在对一个笨小孩讲话，“我早解释过，对象像你这位年轻人眼下这样神思涣散，我就没法追到精神失常的根子。对象不冷静、不沉着、神经紧张，精神分析根本行不通。你能明白吗？”

===Original===
“Just dimly!” she snapped. “You ought to know another way﻿—you, the outstanding authority﻿—”
“Be still!” he interrupted gruffly. “Of course I know another way, if I wanted to drag all of us back to my office, where I have the equipment!﻿—which I won’t do tonight,” he finished grimly.
“Then do it here.”
“I haven’t what I need.”
“There’s everything upstairs,” said Pat. “It’s all there, all Nick’s father’s equipment.”
“Not tonight! That’s final.”

===Chinese===
“勉强明白！”她抢白。“你该另有一手——你，堂堂头号权威——”
“住口！”他粗暴地打断。“我当然另有一手，只要我乐意把咱们仨全拖回我诊所去，设备都在那儿！——今晚我可不乐意，”他冷冷收尾。
“那就在这儿做。”
“我缺家伙什。”
“楼上有的是，”帕特说。“全在那儿，尼克父亲的那些家当，一应俱全。”
“今晚不行！就这样定了。”

===Original===
The girl’s manner changed again. She turned troubled, imploring eyes on Horker. “Dr. Carl,” she said plaintively, “I can’t leave Nick now.” She seized the arm of the silent, dejected youth, who had been standing passively by. “I can’t leave him, really. I’d not be sure of seeing him again, ever. Please, Dr. Carl!”
“If these frenzies of yours,” rumbled Horker, “are so violent and malicious, you ought to be confined. Do you know that, young man?”
“Yes, sir,” mumbled Nick wretchedly.
“And I’ve thought of it,” continued the Doctor. “I’ve thought of it!”
“Please!” cried Pat imploringly. “Won’t you try, Dr. Carl?”
“The devil!” he growled. “All right, then.”

===Chinese===
姑娘的态度又变了。她把忧烦哀恳的目光投向霍克。“卡尔医生，”她凄声道，“我现在不能离开尼克。”她抓住那沉默沮丧、一直木然站在旁边的年轻人的手臂。“我不能离开他，真的。我拿不准还能不能再见到他，永远拿不准。求你了，卡尔医生！”
“要是你的这些狂性发作，”霍克瓮声说，“当真这般凶暴歹毒，你该被关起来。你可知道这点，年轻人？”
“知道，先生，”尼克可怜巴巴地含糊应道。
“我也想过这一层，”医生接着说。“我想过的！”
“求你了！”帕特哀求地喊。“你就不能试一试吗，卡尔医生？”
“见鬼！”他咕哝。“好吧，那就试。”

===Original===
He followed the girl up the stairs, while Nick trailed disconsolately behind. She led him back into the chamber they had quitted, where a curious odor of peach pits seemed to scent the air. Horker sniffed suspiciously, then seized the remaining beaker, raising it cautiously to his nostrils.
“Damnation!” he exploded. “Prussic acid﻿—or cyanide! What in﻿—” He caught sight of Pat’s tragic eyes, and suddenly replaced the container. “Pat!” he groaned. “Pat, Honey!” He drew her into the circle of his great arm. “I’ll help you, dear! All I can, with all my heart, since it means that much to you!” He groaned again under his breath. “Oh, my God!”
He held her a moment, patting her tousled black head with his massive, delicate fingered hand. Then he released her, turning to Nick.
“This the stuff?” he asked, brusquely, indicating the cabinet of bottles, with its splintered front.
Nick nodded. Pat sank to the chair beside the table and watched Horker as he scanned the array of containers. He pulled out a tiny wooden case and snapped it open to reveal a number of steel needles that glinted brightly in the yellow light. He grunted in satisfaction and continued his inspection.
“Atropine,” he muttered, reading the labeled boxes. “Cocaine, daturine, hyoscine, hyoscyamine﻿—won’t do!”

===Chinese===
他跟着姑娘上楼，尼克垂头丧气地缀在后面。她把他领回他们方才离开的那间屋——空气里似乎浮着一股桃核似的怪味。霍克狐疑地嗅了嗅，随即抓起剩下的那只烧杯，小心翼翼举到鼻端。
“该死！”他炸了。“氢氰酸——还是氰化物！这到底是——”他撞见帕特那双悲怆的眼睛，猛地把容器放回原处。“帕特！”他呻吟。“帕特，亲爱的！”他把她拢进自己粗大的臂弯。“我会帮你的，孩子！竭尽所能，全心全意，既然这事对你重到这个地步！”他又压着嗓子呻吟了一声。“哦，我的上帝！”
他搂了她片刻，用那只大手——指头却很灵巧——轻拍她蓬乱的黑发。随后他放开她，转向尼克。
“就是这些货色？”他指着那口塞满瓶子的柜子问道，语气生硬，柜门上的碎玻璃还没清。
尼克点头。帕特颓坐进桌旁的椅子，看着霍克检视那一排容器。他抽出一只小木盒，啪地打开，露出若干钢针，在黄澄澄的灯光下亮闪闪的。他满意地哼了一声，继续清点。
“阿托品，”他念着盒上的标签咕哝。“可卡因、曼陀罗碱、东莨菪碱、莨菪碱——不顶事！”

===Original===
“What do you need?” the girl queried faintly.
“A mild hypnotic,” said the Doctor abstractedly, still searching. “Pretty good substitutes for psychoanalysis﻿—certain drugs. Dulls the conscious mind, but not to complete unconsciousness. Good means of getting at the subconscious. See?”
“Sort of,” said Pat. “If it only works!”
“Oh, it’ll work if we can find﻿—ah!” He seized a tiny cardboard box. “Scopolamine! This’ll do the work.”
He extracted a tiny glassy something from one or other of the boxes he held, and frowned down at it. He seized the carafe of water, plunged something pointed and shiny into it.
“Antiseptic,” he muttered thoughtfully. He seized a brown bottle from the case, held it toward the light, and shook it. “Peroxide’s gone flat,” he growled. “Nothing but water.”

===Chinese===
“你要找什么？”姑娘有气无力地问。
“一种温和的催眠剂，”医生心不在焉地说，手上还在翻。“精神分析的蛮好替身——某些药物。能把显意识钝化，又不至于全然昏死。探入潜意识的好路子。明白？”
“算明白吧，”帕特说。“只要管用就行！”
“哦，只要找得到就管用——啊！”他抓过一只小纸盒。“东莨菪碱！这就成了。”
他从手里这几个盒子中的某一个里拈出一件小小的玻璃似的物事，皱眉端详。他抓过水瓶，把一件尖而发亮的东西插了进去。
“消个毒，”他若有所思地咕哝。他从木盒里抓起一只棕色瓶子，对着光晃了晃。“双氧水跑气了，”他抱怨。“跟白水没两样。”

===Original===
He pulled a silver cigar-lighter from his pocket and snapped a yellow flame to it. He passed the point of the hypodermic rapidly back and forth through the little spear of fire. Finally he turned to Nick.
“Take off your coat,” he ordered. “Roll up your shirt sleeve﻿—the left one. And sit over there.” He indicated the couch along the wall.
The youth obeyed without a word. The only indication of emotion was a long, miserable, wistful look at Pat as he seated himself impassively on the spot that the girl had so recently occupied.
“Now!” said the Doctor briskly, approaching the youth. “This will make you drowsy, sleepy. That’s all it’ll do. Don’t fight the effect. Just relax, let the thing take its course, and I’ll see what I can get out of you.”
Pat gasped and Nick winced as he drove the needle into the bared arm.
“So!” he said. “Now relax. Lean back and close your eyes.”

===Chinese===
他从口袋里摸出一支银质雪茄打火机，啪地打出一簇黄火苗。他把注射器的针尖在那杆小小的火矛上迅速来回炙过。最后他转向尼克。
“脱掉上衣，”他命令道。“把衬衫袖子卷起来——左边的。然后坐到那边去。”他指了指沿墙的躺椅。
年轻人一声不吭地照办。唯一的情绪表示，是他漠然坐上姑娘片刻之前还躺着的位置时，朝帕特投去的悠长、凄苦而眷恋的一瞥。
“好了！”医生干脆利落地说，走近年轻人。“这针会让你发困、想睡。它的本事就这些。别抗拒药力。只管放松，由它自行发作，我看看能从你嘴里问出点什么。”
针头扎进裸露的手臂，帕特倒抽一口气，尼克瑟缩了一下。
“好了！”他说。“现在放松。往后靠，闭上眼睛。”

===Original===
He stepped to the door, dragged in a battered chair from the hall, and occupied it. He sat beside Pat, watching the pale features of the youth, who sat quietly with closed eyes, breathing slowly, heavily.
“Long enough,” muttered Horker. He raised his voice. “Can you hear me?” he called to the motionless figure on the couch. There was no response, but Pat fancied she saw a slight change in Nick’s expression.
“Can you hear me?” repeated Horker in louder tones.
“Yes, I can hear you,” came in icy tones from the figure on the couch. Pat started violently as the voice sounded. The eyes opened, and she saw in sudden terror the ruddy orbs of the demon!

===Chinese===
他走到门口，从门厅拖进一把破椅子，坐了下来。他坐在帕特身旁，注视着年轻人苍白的面容——后者安静地闭目坐着，呼吸缓慢而沉重。
“时间差不多了，”霍克喃喃道。他提高嗓门。“听得见我吗？”他朝躺椅上纹丝不动的人影喊。没有回应，但帕特仿佛看见尼克的神情起了一丝变化。
“听得见我吗？”霍克放大声音再问。
“听得见。”躺椅上的人影用冰冷的声调答道。那声音一响，帕特猛地一颤。眼睛睁开了，她在一阵突如其来的恐怖中，看见了恶魔那双血红的眼睛！

## XX The Assignation / 二十 幽会
===Original===
Pat was by no means as buoyant as she had been in the morning. She approached the appointed meeting place with a feeling of trepidation that all her arguments could not subdue.
She surveyed the crowded walks of the park with relief; she felt confirmed in her assumption that nothing unpleasant could occur with so many onlookers. So she approached the bench with somewhat greater self-assurance than when she had left the house.
She saw the seat with its lone occupant, and hastened her steps. Nicholas Devine was sitting exactly as he had on that other occasion, chin cupped on his hands, eyes turned moodily toward the vast lake that coruscated now with the reflection of stars and many lights. As before, she moved close to his side before he looked up, but here the similarity of the two occasions vanished. Her fears were realized; she was looking into the red-gleaming eyes and expressionless features of his other self﻿—the demon of Saturday evening!
“Sit down!” he said as a sardonic half-smile twisted his lips. “Aren’t you pleased? Aren’t you thrilled to the very core of your being?”

===Chinese===
帕特再也不是早上那副轻松劲儿了。她朝约定的会面地点走去，心头满是忐忑，任凭她怎么给自己讲道理也压不下去。
她环顾公园里熙熙攘攘的小径，松了口气；有这么多人旁观，总不至于发生什么不愉快的事——她这一假定似乎得到了印证。于是她朝那张长椅走去，比出门时多了几分自信。
她望见长椅上孤零零坐着一个人，便加快了脚步。尼古拉斯·德文坐着的姿势与上回一模一样，双手托着下巴，忧郁的目光投向浩渺的湖面——此刻湖水正映着星光和万家灯火，波光闪烁。和上次一样，她走到他身边他才抬起头来，但两回的相似之处到此为止。她担心的事终究发生了：她看到的，是那个人格红色发光的双眼和毫无表情的面孔——星期六晚上的那个恶魔！
“坐下！”他开口道，嘴角扭出一丝讥诮的浅笑。“你不高兴吗？你难道没有从骨子里感到一阵兴奋吗？”

===Original===
Pat stood irresolute; she controlled an impulse to break into sudden, abandoned flight. The imminence of the crowded walks again reassured her, and she seated herself gingerly on the extreme edge of the bench, staring at her companion with coolly inimical eyes. He returned her gaze with features as immobile as carven stone; only his red eyes gave evidence of the obscene, uncanny life behind the mask.
“Well?” said Pat in as frigid a voice as she could muster.
“Yes,” said the other surveying her. “You are quite as I recalled you. Very pretty, almost beautiful, save for a certain irregularity in your features. Not unpleasant, however.” His eyes traveled over her body; automatically she drew back, shrinking away from him. “You have a seductive body,” he continued. “A most seductive body; I regret that circumstances prevented our full enjoyment of it. But that will come. Yes, that will come!”
“Oh!” said Pat faintly. It took all her determination to remain seated by the side of the horror.

===Chinese===
帕特站在原地犹豫不决；她强压住一阵想要拔腿狂奔的冲动。近在咫尺的喧闹小径又一次让她安下心来，她小心翼翼地在长椅最边上坐下，用冷冷的敌意目光盯着她的同伴。他也回望着她，面孔纹丝不动，如同石刻；只有那双红眼睛，透露出面具背后那卑秽而诡异的生命的存在。
“有事吗？”帕特用她所能摆出的最冷冰冰的语气说。
“是啊，”那人打量着她说，“你跟我记忆里的完全一样。很漂亮，几乎算得上美丽，只可惜五官略有瑕疵。不过并不讨厌。”他的目光在她身上游走；她不由自主地往后缩，躲开他。“你有一副诱人的身体，”他接着说，“非常诱人的身体；遗憾的是当时的情形不容我们充分消受。不过来日方长。是的，来日方长！”
“哦！”帕特虚弱地应了一声。要下多大的决心，她才能继续坐在这个怪物身边。

===Original===
“You were extremely attractive as I attired you Saturday,” the other proceeded. His lips took on a curious sensual leer. “I could have done better with more time; I would have stripped you somewhat more completely. Everything, I think, except your legs; I am pleased by the sight of long, straight, silk-clad legs, and should perhaps have received some pleasure by running these hands along them﻿—scratching at proper intervals for the aesthetic effect of blood. But that too will come.”
The girl sprang erect, gasping and speechless in outraged anger. She turned abruptly; nothing remained of her determination now. She felt only an urge to escape from the sneering tormentor who had lost in her mind all connection with her own Nicholas Devine. She took a sudden step.
“Sit down!” She heard the tones of the entity behind her, flat, unchanged. “Sit down, else I’ll drag you here!”
She paused in sheer surprise, turning a startled face on the other.

===Chinese===
“星期六我给你打扮的时候，你迷人极了，”那人继续说。他的嘴唇浮出一丝古怪而淫邪的狞笑。“要是时间再多些，我本可以做得更称心；我会把你剥得更彻底些。我想，除了那双腿以外全部剥光；我喜欢看修长笔直、裹着丝袜的腿，或许还能让这双手顺着摸下去得到些乐趣——隔三差五地挠上几道，好添些血的美感。不过这也会来的。”
姑娘猛地跳起身来，气得说不出话，直喘粗气。她猛一转身；此刻她的决心已荡然无存，只觉得一股要逃开的冲动，要逃离这个讥笑折磨她的人——在她心里，这人已同她自己的尼古拉斯·德文毫无干系。她突然迈出一步。
“坐下！”她听见身后那个存在的语调，平板依旧，毫无起伏。“坐下，不然我把你拖回来！”
她纯粹出于惊讶停住脚步，一张惊惶的脸转向那人。

===Original===
“You wouldn’t dare!” she said, amazed at the bald effrontery of the threat. “You don’t dare touch me here!”
The other laughed. “Don’t I? What have I to risk? *He*’ll suffer for any deed of mine! You’ll call for aid against me and only loose the hounds on *him*.”
Pat stared blankly at the evil face. She had no answer; for once her ready tongue found no retort.
“Sit down!” reiterated the other, and she dropped dazedly to her position on the bench. She turned dark questioning eyes on him.

===Chinese===
“你不敢！”她说，对这赤裸裸恫吓的无耻感到震惊。“在这里你不敢碰我！”
那人笑了。“我不敢？我有什么可担心的？我干的任何事，都得*他*来受罪！你嚷嚷着叫人来对付我，不过是放狗去咬*他*。”
帕特怔怔地盯着那张邪恶的面孔。她无言以对；她那条素来伶俐的舌头，这一回竟找不出一句反驳的话。
“坐下！”那人又说了一遍，她迷迷糊糊地跌坐回长椅上原来的位置，用一双幽暗的、满含疑问的眼睛望着他。

===Original===
“Do you see,” he sneered, “how weakening an influence is this love of yours? To protect him you are obeying me; this is my authority over you﻿—this body I share with him!”
She made no reply; she was making a desperate effort to lash her mind into activity, to formulate some means of combating the being who tortured her.
“It has weakened him, too,” the other proceeded. “This disturbed love of his has taken away the mastery which birth gave him, and his enfeeblement has given that mastery to me. He knows now the reason for his weakness; I tell it to him too late to harm me.”
Pat struggled for composure. The very presence of the cold demon tore at the roots of her self-control, and she suppressed a fierce desire to break into hysterical laughter. Ridiculous, hopeless, incomprehensible situation! She forced her quivering throat to husky speech.

===Chinese===
“你瞧见没有，”他讥讽道，“你这所谓的爱情是多么叫人软弱的东西？为了护着他，你就得听我的；这就是我辖制你的权柄——这具我和他共用的身体！”
她没有作声；她在拼命鞭策自己的头脑转动起来，想找出某种办法来对付这个折磨她的东西。
“它也把他削弱了，”那人继续说，“他这场心神不宁的恋爱，夺走了他与生俱来的支配权，而他的衰弱又把这份支配权交到了我手里。他如今知道了自己软弱的原因；我告诉他的时机，已经晚得伤不到我了。”
帕特竭力保持镇定。这个冷酷的恶魔光是坐在那里，就在撼动她自制的根基；她强压住一阵歇斯底里大笑的冲动。荒唐、无望、不可理喻的处境！她迫使颤抖的喉咙发出沙哑的声音。

===Original===
“What﻿—what are you?” she stammered.
“Synapse! I’m a question of synapses,” jeered the other. “Simple! Very simple! Ask your friend the Doctor!”
“I think,” said the girl, a measure of control returning to her voice, “that you’re a devil. You’re some sort of a fiend that has managed to attach itself to Nick, and you’re not human. That’s what I think!”
“Think what you please,” said the other. “We’re wasting time here,” he said abruptly. “Come.”

===Chinese===
“你——你到底是什么？”她结结巴巴地问。
“突触！我是个突触问题，”那人嘲弄道，“简单！非常简单！去问问你那位医生朋友吧！”
“我看，”姑娘开口说，声音里恢复了几分自持，“你是个魔鬼。你是某种设法缠上尼克的妖邪，你不是人。我就是这么看的！”
“随你怎么想，”那人说。“我们在这儿浪费时间了，”他突然转口道，“走吧。”

===Original===
“Where?” Pat was startled; she felt a recurrence of fright.
“No matter where. Come.”
“I won’t! Why do you want me?”
“To complete the business of Saturday night,” he said. “Your lips have healed; they bleed no longer, but that is easy to remedy. Come.”

===Chinese===
“去哪儿？”帕特吃了一惊；恐惧又涌了上来。
“去哪儿无所谓。走。”
“我不去！你要我干什么？”
“把星期六晚上没做完的事做完，”他说，“你的嘴唇已经养好了；不再出血了，不过那容易补救。走。”

===Original===
“I won’t!” exclaimed the girl in sudden panic. “I won’t!” She moved as if to rise.
“You forget,” intoned the being beside her. “You forget the authority vested in me by virtue of this love of yours. Let me convince you.” He stretched forth a thin hand. “Move and you condemn your sweetheart to the punishment you threaten me.”
He seized her arm, pinching the flesh brutally, his nails breaking the smooth skin. Pat felt her face turn ashy pale; she closed her eyes and bit her nearly-healed lips at the excruciating pain, but she made not the slightest sound nor the faintest movement. She simply sat and suffered.
“You see!” sneered the other, releasing her. “Thank my kindly nature that I marked your arm instead of your face. Shall we go?”

===Chinese===
“我不去！”姑娘突然惊慌起来，叫道，“我不去！”她作势要起身。
“你忘了，”她身边的那个存在拖长了声调说，“你忘了凭着你这份爱情授予我的权柄。让我来说服你。”他伸出一只瘦手。“你再动一动，就是把你心上人推向你要用来吓唬我的那番惩罚。”
他抓住她的手臂，狠命掐着那肉，指甲刺破了光滑的皮肤。帕特觉得自己脸色刷地变得灰白；剧痛之下，她闭上眼睛，咬住快要愈合的嘴唇，却没有发出一点声音，也没有一丝动作。她只是坐在那里忍受。
“瞧见了吧！”那人松开手，讥诮道，“多亏我心地仁慈，只在你胳膊上留了记号，没在你脸上动。我们走？”

===Original===
A scarcely audible whimper of pain came from the girl’s lips. She sat palled and unmoving, with her eyes still closed.
“No,” she murmured faintly at last. “No. I won’t go with you.”
“Shall I drag you?”
“Yes. Drag me if you dare.”
His hand closed on her wrist; she felt herself jerked violently to her feet, so roughly that it wrenched her shoulder. A startled, frightened little cry broke from her lips, and then she closed them firmly at the sight of several by-passers turning curious eyes on them.

===Chinese===
姑娘嘴里逸出一声几乎听不见的痛楚呜咽。她脸色发白，一动不动地坐着，双眼依然闭着。
“不，”她终于微弱地喃喃道，“不。我不跟你去。”
“要我来拖你吗？”
“好啊。有本事你就拖。”
他的手扣住她的手腕；她感到自己被猛地拽起来，动作粗暴得几乎扭了她的肩膀。她脱口发出一声又惊又怕的轻呼，随即瞥见几个路人好奇地朝他们张望，便紧紧闭上了嘴。

===Original===
“I’ll come,” she murmured. The glimmering of an idea had risen in her chaotic mind.
She followed him in grim, bitter silence across the clipped turf to the limit of the park. She recognized Nick’s modest automobile standing in the line of cars along the street; her companion, or captor, moved directly towards it, opened the door and clambered in without a single backward glance. He turned about and watched her as she paused with one diminutive foot on the running board, and rubbed her hand over her aching arm.
“Get in!” he ordered coldly.
She made no move. “I want to know where you intend to take me.”

===Chinese===
“我这就去，”她低声说。一个念头在她纷乱的脑海里初露端倪。
她怀着阴沉苦涩的沉默，跟在他身后穿过修剪齐整的草坪，走到公园尽头。她认出尼克的那辆不起眼的汽车正停在沿街的车列里；她的同伴，或者说掳她的人，径直朝那车走去，拉开车门钻了进去，头也不回一下。他转过身来看着她，见她一只小小的脚踩在踏板上停住，正用手揉着作痛的手臂。
“上车！”他冷冷地命令。
她不动。“我想知道你打算把我带到哪儿去。”

===Original===
“It doesn’t matter. To a place where we can complete that unfinished experiment of ours. Aren’t you happy at the prospect?”
“Do you think,” she said unsteadily, “that I’d consent to that even to save Nick from disgrace and punishment? Do you think I’m fool enough for that?”
“We’ll soon see.” He extended his hand. “Scream﻿—fight﻿—struggle!” he jeered. “Call them down on your sweetheart!”
He had closed his hand on her wrist; she jerked it convulsively from his grasp.

===Chinese===
“无所谓。去一个能完成我们那桩未竟实验的地方。一想到这个，你不高兴吗？”
“你以为，”她声音发颤地说，“就算是为了让尼克免遭羞辱和惩处，我也会答应那种事？你以为我蠢到那个地步？”
“很快就会见分晓。”他伸出手来。“尖叫啊——挣扎啊——搏斗啊！”他嘲弄道，“把他们都招来收拾你的心上人吧！”
他的手已经扣上她的手腕；她猛地抽搐般把手从他掌握里挣脱出来。

===Original===
“I’ll bargain with you!” she gasped. She needed a moment’s respite to clarify a thought that had been growing in her mind.
“Bargain? What have you to offer?”
“As much as you!”
“Ah, but I have a threat﻿—the threat to your sweetheart! And I’m offering too the lure of that evil whose face so charmed you recently. Have you forgotten how nearly I won you to the worship of that principle? Have you forgotten the ecstasy of that pain?”

===Chinese===
“我跟你做个交易！”她喘着气说。她需要喘息片刻，把脑子里一个渐渐成形的念头理清楚。
“交易？你有什么可拿来交易的？”
“不比你少！”
“啊，可我手里有个威胁——拿你的心上人作要挟！我还奉上那种邪恶的诱惑，前些日子它的嘴脸不是把你迷得神魂颠倒么。你忘了差一点我就让你皈依了那一套么？你忘了那种痛楚带来的狂喜么？”

===Original===
His terrible, bloodshot eyes were approaching her face; and strangely, the girl felt a curious recurrence of that illogical desire to yield that had swept over her on that disastrous night of Saturday. There *had* been an ecstasy; there *had* been a wild, ungodly, unhallowed pleasure in his blows, in the searing pain of his kisses on her lacerated lips. She realized vaguely that she was staring blankly, dazedly, into the red eyes, and that somewhere within her, some insane brain-cells were urging her to clamber to the seat beside him.
She tore her eyes away. She rubbed her bruised shoulder, and the pain of her own touch restored her vanishing logical faculties. She returned her gaze to the face of the other, meeting his gaze now coolly.
“Nick!” she said earnestly, as if calling him from a distance. “Nick!”

===Chinese===
他那双可怕的、布满血丝的眼睛正向她的脸凑近；奇怪的是，姑娘感到那股不合逻辑的屈从欲望又冒了上来——那个倒霉的星期六晚上，这股欲望曾将她整个吞没。那*确实*是一种狂喜；在他的殴打里，在他烙在她破裂嘴唇上的灼痛亲吻里，*确实*有过一种狂野的、渎神的、邪恶的快感。她迷迷糊糊地意识到，自己正失魂落魄地盯着那双红眼睛，而在她身体里的某个地方，某些疯狂的脑细胞正怂恿她爬上他身旁的座位。
她硬把目光挪开。她揉了揉瘀伤的肩膀，自己触到的痛楚把她那正在消散的理智拉了回来。她把目光转回那人的脸上，此刻冷静地迎着他的注视。
“尼克！”她恳切地唤道，像是从远处呼唤他。“尼克！”

===Original===
There was, she fancied, the faintest gleam of concern apparent in the features opposite her. She continued.
“Nick!” she repeated. “You can hear me, Honey. Come to the house as soon as you are able. Come tonight, or any time; I’ll wait until you do. You’ll come, Honey; you must!”
She backed away from the car; the other made no move to halt her. She circled the vehicle and dashed recklessly across the street. From the safety of the opposite walk she glanced back; the red-eyed visage was regarding her steadily through the glass of the window.

===Chinese===
她仿佛觉得，对面那张面孔上掠过了一丝近乎难以察觉的关切。她继续说下去。
“尼克！”她又说了一遍。“你听得见我的话，亲爱的。一有可能就到我家来。今晚来，随时来；我会一直等你来。你会来的，亲爱的；你一定要来！”
她倒退着离开汽车；那人没有动手拦她。她绕过车身，不管不顾地飞奔过街。到了对面人行道的安全地带，她回头望了一眼；那张红眼睛的面孔正隔着车窗玻璃，一动不动地注视着她。

## XXIV The Dark Other / 二十四 黑暗的他人
===Original===
It was early in the evening, not yet eight o’clock, when Pat saw the car of Nicholas Devine draw up before the house. She had already been watching half an hour, sitting cross-legged in the deep window seat, like her jade Buddha. That equivocal poem of his had disturbed her, lent an added strength to the moods and doubts already implanted by Magda’s mystical tale, and it was with a feeling of trepidation that she watched him emerge wearily from his vehicle and stare in indecision first at her window and then at the Horker residence. The waning daylight was still sufficient to delineate his worn features; she could see them, pale, harried, but indubitably the mild features of her own Nick.
While he hesitated, she darted to the door and out upon the porch. He gave her a wan smile of greeting, advanced to the foot of the steps, and halted there.
“The Doctor’s not home yet,” she called to him. He stood motionless below her.
“Come up on the porch,” she invited, as he made no move. She uttered the words with a curious feeling of apprehension; for even as she ached for his presence, the uncertain state of affairs was frightening. She thought fearfully that what had happened before might happen again. Still, there on the open porch, in practically full daylight, and for so brief a time﻿—Dr. Carl would be coming very shortly, she reasoned.

===Chinese===
夜色尚早，还不到八点钟，帕特就看见尼古拉斯·德文的汽车停到了屋前。她已经守候了半个钟头，盘腿坐在深深的窗座上，活像她那尊玉佛。他那首语意隐晦的诗搅得她心神不定，给玛格达那神秘故事早已种下的疑虑和不安又添了几分力气；她怀着忐忑看他疲惫地从车里出来，先是望望她的窗口，又望望霍克家的宅子，犹豫不决。渐暗的天光还足以勾出他憔悴的面容；她看得清那张脸，苍白、困顿，却分明是她自己那个尼克温和的脸。
趁他犹豫的当口，她一溜烟跑到门口，出了门廊。他朝她苍白地一笑算打招呼，走到台阶下，便停在那里。
“医生还没回来，”她朝他喊道。他一动不动地站在她下面。
“上来门廊坐吧，”见他不动，她邀请道。她说这话时心里有种说不出的惶恐；因为她一面渴望他在身边，一面又害怕这悬而未决的局面。她心惊胆战地想，先前发生过的事可能重演。不过——就在敞开的门廊上，天光几乎还大亮着，又只这么一小会儿——卡尔医生马上就回来了，她这样劝自己。

===Original===
“I can’t,” said Nick, staring wistfully at her. “You know I can’t.”
“Why not?”
“I promised. You remember﻿—I promised Dr. Horker I’d not see you except in his presence.”
“So you did,” said Pat doubtfully. The promise offered escape from a distressing situation, she thought, and yet﻿—somehow, seeing Nick standing pathetically there, she couldn’t imagine anything harmful emanating from him. There had been many and many evenings in his company that had passed delightfully, enjoyably, safely. She felt a wave of pity for him; after all, the affliction was his, most of the suffering was his.
“We needn’t take it so literally,” she said almost reluctantly. “He’ll be home very soon now.”

===Chinese===
“我不能，”尼克说，恋恋地望着她，“你知道我不能。”
“为什么不能？”
“我答应过的。你记得——我答应过霍克医生，除非他在场，我不见你。”
“你确实答应过，”帕特将信将疑地说。这个承诺可以让人躲开一个难堪的局面，她想，可是——不知怎么，看着尼克可怜巴巴地站在那儿，她实在想不出他能有什么害处。和他一起度过的一个又一个夜晚，都是那么愉快、惬意、平安无事。她心里涌起一阵怜惜；毕竟，遭罪的是他，受苦最多的也是他。
“咱们不必把话说得那么死，”她几乎是勉勉强强地说，“他马上就回来了。”

===Original===
“I know,” said Nick soberly, “but it was a promise, and besides, I’m afraid.”
“Never mind, Honey,” she said, after a momentary hesitation. “Come up and sit here on the steps, then﻿—here beside me. We can talk just as well as there on the settee.”
He climbed the steps and seated himself, watching Pat with longing eyes. He made no move to touch her, nor did she suggest a kiss.
“I read your poem, Honey,” she said finally. “It worried me.”

===Chinese===
“我知道，”尼克正色道，“可那是个承诺，再说，我也怕。”
“别管那么多，亲爱的，”她迟疑了片刻，说，“那就上来坐在台阶上——就这儿，我旁边。在这儿说话和在那边长椅上一样方便。”
他上了台阶坐下，用渴望的目光望着帕特。他没有伸手碰她，她也没有示意要亲吻。
“我读了你的诗，亲爱的，”她终于开口，“它让我担心。”

===Original===
“I’m sorry, Pat. I couldn’t sleep. I kept wandering around the house, and at last I wrote it and took it out and mailed it. It was a vent, a relief from the things I’d been thinking.”
“What things, Honey?”
“A way, mostly,” he answered gloomily, “of removing myself from your life. A permanent way.”
“Nick!”

===Chinese===
“对不起，帕特。我睡不着。我在屋里转来转去，最后写下了它，拿出去寄了。那是个出口，把心里想的那些东西排解一下。”
“哪些东西，亲爱的？”
“主要是一个办法，”他阴郁地答道，“把自己从你生活里除去的办法。一个一劳永逸的办法。”
“尼克！”

===Original===
“I didn’t, as you see, Pat. I was too cowardly, I suppose. Or perhaps it was because of this forlorn hope of ours. There’s always hope, Pat; even the condemned man with his foot on the step to the gallows feels it.”
“Nick dear!” she cried, her voice quavering in pity. “Nick, you mustn’t think of those things! It might weaken you﻿—make it easier for *him*!”
“It can’t. If it frightens *him*, I’m glad.”
“Honey,” she said soothingly, “we’ll give Dr. Carl a chance. Promise me you’ll let him try, won’t you?”
“Of course I will. Is there anything I’d refuse to promise you, Pat? Even,” he added bitterly, “when reason tells me it’s a futile promise.”

===Chinese===
“我没那么做，你也看见了，帕特。大概是太怯懦了吧。也可能是因为咱们这点渺茫的希望。希望总是有的，帕特；就连一只脚已踏上绞架台阶的死囚，心里也还存着它。”
“亲爱的尼克！”她叫道，声音因怜惜而发颤，“尼克，你不许想那些事！那会削弱你——让*他*得逞！”
“削弱不了。要是那能吓着*他*，我倒高兴。”
“亲爱的，”她柔声劝道，“咱们给卡尔医生一个机会。答应我让他试试，好不好？”
“我当然答应。有什么事是你要求了我还会拒绝的，帕特？哪怕是，”他苦涩地补了一句，“理智明明告诉我这是个空头的承诺。”

===Original===
“Don’t say it!” she urged fiercely. “We’ve got to help him. We’ve got to believe﻿—There he comes!” she finished with sudden relief.
The Doctor’s car turned up the driveway beyond his residence. Pat saw his face regarding them as he disappeared behind the building.
“Come on, Honey,” she said. “Let’s get at the business.”
They moved slowly over to the Doctor’s door, waiting there until his ponderous footsteps sounded. A light flashed in the hall, and his broad shadow filled the door for a moment before it opened.

===Chinese===
“别说这种话！”她激烈地催促道，“我们得帮他。我们得相信——他回来了！”她如释重负地收了尾。
医生的汽车拐上了他家宅子旁的车道。帕特看见他消失在楼房后面之前，朝他们望了一眼。
“来吧，亲爱的，”她说，“咱们谈正事吧。”
他们慢慢走到医生的门前，站在那儿等，直到响起他那沉重的脚步声。门厅里灯光一闪，他宽大的身影在门上映了片刻，门开了。

===Original===
“Come in,” he rumbled jovially. “Fine evening we’re spoiling, isn’t it?”
“It could be,” said Pat as they followed him into the library, “only it’ll probably rain some more.”
“Hah!” snorted the Doctor, frowning at the mention of rain. “The course was soft. Couldn’t get any distance, and it added six strokes to my score. At least six!”
Pat chuckled commiseratingly. “You ought to lay out a course in Greenland,” she suggested. “They say anyone can drive a ball a quarter of a mile on smooth ice.”

===Chinese===
“进来吧，”他兴致勃勃地嗡声说，“多好的晚上，全让咱们给糟蹋了，是不是？”
“好倒是好，”帕特跟着他进了书房，说，“只怕还要再下场雨。”
“哈！”医生一听见雨就皱眉，哼了一声，“球场软得要命。球打不远，一局多花了我六杆。至少六杆！”
帕特体恤地轻笑。“你该去格陵兰修个球场，”她提议道，“据说在平滑的冰面上，谁都能把球打出四分之一英里远。”

===Original===
“Humph!” The Doctor waved toward a great, low chair. “Suppose you sit over there, young man, and we’ll get about our business. And don’t look so woebegone about it.”
Nick settled himself nervously in the designated chair; the Doctor seated himself at a little distance to the side, and Pat sat tensely in her usual place beside the hearth. She waited in strained impatience for the black magic of psychoanalysis to commence.
“Now,” said Horker, “I want you to keep quiet, Pat﻿—if possible. And you, young man, are to relax, compose yourself, get yourself into as passive a state as possible. Do you understand?”
“Yes, sir,” The youth leaned back in the great chair, closing his eyes.
“So! Now, think back to your childhood, your earliest memories. Let your thoughts wander at random, and speak whatever comes to your mind.”

===Chinese===
“哼！”医生朝一把宽大的矮椅挥挥手。“你坐那边吧，年轻人，咱们办正事。别一副愁眉苦脸的样子。”
尼克紧张地在指定的椅子上坐定；医生在侧边不远的地方落座，帕特则紧绷着坐在壁炉边她惯常的位置上。她焦灼不安地等着精神分析这场黑魔法开场。
“好了，”霍克说，“我要你保持安静，帕特——尽可能做到。至于你，年轻人，要放松，定下神来，让自己进入尽可能被动的状态。明白吗？”
“明白了，先生。”青年在大椅子里向后一靠，合上了眼睛。
“就这样！现在，回想你的童年，你最早的记忆。让思绪随意飘，想到什么就说什么。”

===Original===
Nick sat a moment in silence. “That’s hard to do, sir,” he said finally.
“Yes. It will take practice, weeks of it, perhaps. You’ll have to acquire the knack of it, but to do that, we’ll have to start.”
“Yes, sir.” He sat with closed eyes. “My mother,” he murmured, “was kind. I remember her a little, just a little. She was very gentle, not apt to blame me. She could understand. Made excuses to my father. He was hard, not cruel﻿—strict. Couldn’t understand. Blamed me when I wasn’t to blame. Other did it. I wasn’t mischievous, but got the blame. Couldn’t explain, he wouldn’t believe me.” He paused uncertainly.
“Go on,” said Horker quietly, while Pat strained her ears to listen.

===Chinese===
尼克默默坐了片刻。“这很难做到，先生，”他终于说。
“是的。这需要练习，也许得好几个星期。你得练出这个窍门来，而要练出来，咱们就得先开始。”
“是，先生。”他闭目坐着。“我母亲，”他喃喃道，“很慈爱。我记得她一点，就一点点。她非常温柔，不轻易怪我。她能理解。替我在父亲面前开脱。他严厉，倒不凶——是苛刻。理解不了。不该怪我的时候怪我。是那一个干的。我并不淘气，却总背黑锅。没法解释，他不肯信我。”他迟疑地停了下来。
“接着说，”霍克轻声道，帕特则竖起耳朵听着。

===Original===
“Mrs. Stevens,” he continued. “Governess after Mother died. Strict like Father, got punished when I wasn’t to blame. Just as bad after Father died. Always blamed. Couldn’t explain, nobody believed me. Other threw cat in window, I had to go to bed. Put salt in bird seed, broke leg of chair to make it fall. Punished﻿—I couldn’t explain.” His voice droned into silence; he opened his eyes. “That all,” he said nervously.
“Good enough for the first time,” said the Doctor briskly. “Wait a few weeks; we’ll have your life’s history out of you. It takes practice.”
“Is that all?” queried Pat in astonishment.
“All for the first time. Later we’ll let him talk half an hour at a stretch, but it takes practice, as I’ve mentioned. You run along home now,” he said to Nick.

===Chinese===
“史蒂文斯太太，”他继续说，“母亲去世后的家庭女教师。跟父亲一样苛刻，我不该受罚也挨罚。父亲死后还是一样。总是背黑锅。没法解释，谁也不信我。那一个把猫扔进窗子，我却得上床睡觉。往鸟食里掺盐，弄折椅子腿让它摔倒。挨罚——我说不清。”他的声音低低地拖下去，没了声息；他睁开眼。“完了。”他局促地说。
“头一回能做到这样不错了，”医生干脆地说，“再过几个星期，我们会把你一辈子的经历都挖出来。这需要练习。”
“就这些？”帕特惊讶地问。
“头一回就这些。往后我们会让他说上半个钟头不停，但这要练，我说过了。你现在回家去吧，”他对尼克说。

===Original===
“But it’s early!” objected Pat.
“Early or not,” said the Doctor, “I’m tired, and you two aren’t to see each other except here. You remember that.”
Nick rose from his seat in the depths of the great chair. “Thank you, sir,” he said. “I don’t know why, but I feel easier in your presence. The﻿—the struggle disappears while I’m here.”
“Well,” said Horker with a smile, “I like patients with confidence in me. Good night.”

===Chinese===
“可天还早呢！”帕特反对。
“早不早的，”医生说，“我累了，你们两个除了在这儿不许见面。你记得的。”
尼克从大椅子深处站起身。“谢谢您，先生，”他说，“不知道为什么，在您跟前我觉得松快些。那场——那场搏斗，在您这儿就不见了。”
“好，”霍克笑着说，“我喜欢对我有信心的病人。晚安。”

===Original===
At the door Nick paused, turning wistful eyes on Pat. “Good night,” he said, leaning to give her a light kiss. A rush of some emotion twisted his features; he stared strangely at the girl. “I’d better go,” he said abruptly, and vanished through the door.
“Well?” said Pat questioningly, turning to the Doctor. “Did you learn anything from that?”
“Not much,” the other admitted, yawning. “However, the results bear out my theory.”
“How?”

===Chinese===
在门口尼克停了停，恋恋不舍地看着帕特。“晚安，”他说着，俯身轻轻吻了她一下。一阵说不清的情绪扭歪了他的面容；他古怪地盯着姑娘。“我还是走吧，”他突然说，随即消失在门外。
“怎么样？”帕特转过身，带着疑问对医生说，“您从刚才听出什么了吗？”
“不多，”对方打着哈欠承认，“不过，结果印证了我的理论。”
“怎么讲？”

===Original===
“Did you notice how he harped on the undeserved punishment theme? He was punished for another’s mischief?”
“Yes. What of that?”
“Well, picture him as a timid, sensitive child, rather afraid of being punished. Afraid, say, of being locked up in a dark closet. Now, when he inadvertently commits a mischief, as all children do, he tries desperately to divert the blame from himself. But there’s no one else to blame! So what does he do?”

===Chinese===
“你注意到他怎么反复念叨无端受罚这个调子吗？他替别人的捣蛋挨了罚？”
“注意到了。那又怎样？”
“这么说吧，把他想成一个胆小、敏感的孩子，相当怕受罚。比如说，怕被关进黑橱柜里。那么，当他像所有孩子一样不小心闯了祸，他就拼命想把罪责从自己身上挪开。可又没有别人可以指！于是他怎么办？”

===Original===
“What?”
“He invents this *other*, the mischievous one, and blames him. And now the other has grown to the proportions of a delusion, haunting him, driving him to commit acts apart from his normal inclinations. Understand? Because I’m off to bed whether you do or not.”
“I understand all right,” murmured Pat uncertainly as she moved to the door. “But somehow, it doesn’t sound reasonable.”

===Chinese===
“怎么办？”
“他凭空造出这个*那一个*，那个捣蛋的，把罪推给他。如今那一个已经长成了一个妄念，缠着他不放，驱使他做出种种背离他本性的行为。明白了吗？因为不管你明不明白，我都睡觉去了。”
“我明白是明白了，”帕特朝门口走去，迟疑地喃喃道，“可不知怎么，听着不大对劲。”

===Original===
“It will,” said the Doctor. “Good night.”
Pat wandered slowly down the steps and through the break in the hedge, musing over Doctor Horker’s expression of opinion. Then, according to him, the devil was nothing more than an invention of Nick’s mind, the trick of a cowardly child to evade just punishment. She shook her head; it didn’t sound like Nick at all. For all his gentleness and sensitivity, he wasn’t the one to hide behind a fabrication. He wasn’t a coward; she was certain of that. And she was as sure as she could ever be that he hated, feared, loathed this personality that afflicted him; he *couldn’t* have created it.
She sighed, mounted the steps, and fumbled for her key. The sound of a movement behind her brought a faint gasp of astonishment. She turned to see a figure materializing from the shadows of the porch. The light from the hall fell across its features, and she drew back as she recognized Nicholas Devine﻿—not the being she had just kissed good night, but in the guise of her tormentor, the red-eyed demon!

===Chinese===
“会对劲的，”医生说，“晚安。”
帕特慢慢走下台阶，穿过树篱的缺口，一路琢磨着霍克医生的高见。照他这么说，那魔鬼不过是尼克心里造出来的东西，是一个胆小孩子逃避应得惩罚的把戏。她摇了摇头；这完全不像尼克。他固然温柔敏感，却不是那种躲在谎话背后的人。他不是懦夫；这一点她可以肯定。而且她无比确信，他憎恨、惧怕、厌弃这个折磨他的人格；这东西*不可能*是他造出来的。
她叹了口气，登上台阶，摸索钥匙。身后一声响动让她轻呼了一声，她转过身，看见一条人影正从门廊的暗处显形。门厅的灯光落在那张脸上，她认出是尼古拉斯·德文，不由得倒退一步——那不是她刚刚吻过道晚安的那个人，而是以折磨她的那个化身出现：红眼睛的恶魔！

## XXX The Demon Free / 三十 挣脱的恶魔
===Original===
Pat emitted a small, startled shriek, and heard it echoed by a surprised grunt from Dr. Horker.
“Queer!” he muttered. “The stuff must be mislabeled. Scopolamine doesn’t act like this; it’s a narcotic.”
“He’s﻿—the other!” gasped Pat, while the being on the couch grinned sardonically.
“Eh? An attack? Can’t be!” The Doctor shook his head emphatically.

===Chinese===
帕特发出一声小小的惊叫，紧接着听见霍克医生也诧异地哼了一声。
“怪了！”他咕哝道，“这药八成贴错了标签。东莨菪碱不会是这个反应，它是麻醉性的。”
“他变成了——那一个！”帕特喘着气说，长椅上那个存在则讥诮地咧嘴一笑。
“呃？发作了？不可能！”医生斩钉截铁地摇头。

===Original===
“It’s not Nick!” cried the girl in panic. “You’re not, are you?” she appealed to the grim entity.
“Not your sweetheart?” queried the creature, still with his mocking leer. “A few hours ago you were lying here all but naked, confessing you were mine. Have you forgotten?”
She shuddered at the reference, and shrank back in her chair. She heard the Doctor’s ominous, angry rumble, and the evil tittering chuckle of the other.
“Pathological or not,” snapped Horker, “I can resent your remarks! I’ve considered several times varying my treatment with another solid cut to the jaw!” He rose from his chair, stamping viciously toward the other.

===Chinese===
“这不是尼克！”姑娘惊慌地叫道。“你不是他，对不对？”她向那个狰狞的存在哀告。
“不是你的心上人？”那东西问道，脸上仍是那副嘲弄的淫笑。“几个钟头以前你还几乎光着身子躺在这儿，承认你是我的人。你忘了？”
她一听这话就打了个寒噤，缩回椅子里。她听见医生一声不祥的怒吼，还有那人邪恶的嘻嘻窃笑。
“管你有没有病，”霍克厉声道，“你说这种话我可咽不下！我好几次都琢磨着想换换疗法，照你下巴再结结实实来一拳！”他从椅子里站起来，怒冲冲地朝那人跺着脚走去。

===Original===
“A moment,” said Nicholas Devine. “Do you know what you’ve done? Have you any idea what you’ve done?” He turned cool, mocking, red-glinting eyes on the Doctor.
“Huh?” Horker paused as if puzzled. “What *I’ve* done? What do you mean?”
“You don’t know, then.” The other gave a satyric smile. “You’re stupid; I gave you the clue, yet you hadn’t the intelligence to follow it. Do you know what I am?” He leaned forward, his eyes leering evilly into the Doctor’s. “I’ll tell you. I’m a question of synapses. That’s all﻿—merely a question of synapses!” He tittered again, horribly. “It still means nothing to you, doesn’t it, Doctor?”
“I’ll show you what it means!” Horker clenched a massive fist and strode toward the figure, whose eyes stared, steadily, unwinkingly into his own.

===Chinese===
“且慢，”尼古拉斯·德文说，“你知道自己干了什么吗？你有没有想过自己干了什么？”他把冷静、讥诮、闪着红光的眼睛转向医生。
“嗯？”霍克一怔，似乎糊涂了。“*我*干了什么？你什么意思？”
“这么说你不知道。”那人浮出一个淫邪的笑。“你蠢；我把线索都给你了，你却没那个脑子顺着查下去。你知道我是什么吗？”他朝前探身，眼睛邪恶地睨着医生的眼睛。“我告诉你。我是个突触问题。就这么回事——不过是个突触问题！”他又发出那种可怕的嘻嘻声。“对你来说这话还是一无所指，对不对，医生？”
“我让你知道知道它的意思！”霍克攥起一只蒲扇大的拳头，大步朝那条身影走去；那双眼睛一动不动、连眨也不眨地盯着他的眼睛。

===Original===
“Back!” the being snapped as the great form bent over him. The Doctor paused as if struck rigid, his arm and heavy fist drawn back like the conventional fighting pose of a boxer. “Go back!” repeated the other, rising. Pat whimpered in abject terror as she heard Horker’s surprised grunt, and saw him recede slowly, and finally sink into his chair. His bewildered eyes were still fixed on those of Nicholas Devine.
“I’ll tell you what you’ve done!” said the strange being. “You’ve freed me! There was nothing wrong with your scopolamine. It worked!” He chuckled. “You drugged *him* and freed me!”
Horker managed a questioning grunt.
“I’m free!” exulted the other. “For the first time I haven’t *him* to fight! He’s here, but helpless to oppose me﻿—he’s feeble﻿—feeble!” He gave again the horrible tittering chuckle. “See how weak the two of you are against my unopposed powers!” he jeered. “Weaklings﻿—food for my pleasures!”
He turned his eyes, luminous and avid, on Pat. “This time,” he said, “there’ll be no interruptions. A witness to our experiment will add a delicate touch of pleasure﻿—”

===Chinese===
“退后！”那伟大身躯俯下来时，那存在厉声喝道。医生猛地顿住，像被冻僵了似的，一条胳膊和那只重拳收在半空，摆出拳击手开打的架势。“退回去！”那人站起身又喝了一遍。帕特听见霍克惊愕的哼声，看见他慢慢退回去，最后一屁股坐进椅子里，吓得低声呜咽起来。他惶惑的眼睛仍旧死死盯着尼古拉斯·德文的眼睛。
“我来告诉你你干了什么！”那奇怪的存在说。“你放了我！你的东莨菪碱一点毛病没有。它起效了！”他咯咯直笑。“你麻翻了*他*，放出了我！”
霍克勉强发出一声疑问的哼声。
“我自由了！”那人大喜若狂。“头一回，我不用再跟*他*斗了！他就在这儿，却无力阻拦我——他虚弱——虚弱得很！”他又发出那阵可怕的窃笑。“看看你们两个在我毫无阻拦的威力面前多么不堪一击！”他讥讽道，“废物——供我取乐的料！”
他把那双放光而贪婪的眼睛转向帕特。“这一次，”他说，“不会再有人打扰了。有一位看客见证我们的实验，倒能添一分妙趣——”

===Original===
He broke off at the Doctor’s sudden movement. Horker had snatched a glistening blue revolver from his pocket, held it leveled at the lust-filled eyes.
“Huh!” growled the Doctor triumphantly. “Do you think I come trailing a maniac without some protection? Especially a vicious one like you?”
Nicholas Devine turned his eyes on his opponent. He stared long and intently.
“Drop it!” he commanded at length. Pat felt a surge of chaotic terror as the weapon clattered to the floor. She turned a frightened glance on Horker’s face, and her fright redoubled at the sight of his straining jaw, the perspiration-beaded forehead, and his bewildered eyes. The demon kicked the gun carelessly aside.

===Chinese===
医生突然一动，他的话戛然而止。霍克从口袋里掏出一把亮闪闪的蓝钢左轮手枪，对准那双欲火灼灼的眼睛。
“哼！”医生得意地吼道，“你以为我会毫无防备地盯梢一个疯子？尤其是你这种歹毒的货色？”
尼古拉斯·德文把眼睛转向他的对手。他久久地、目不转睛地盯着。
“放下！”他终于下令。手枪啪嗒一声掉在地板上，帕特只觉一阵乱麻似的恐怖涌上来。她惊惶地瞥了一眼霍克的脸，看见他咬紧的牙关、沁满汗珠的额头和茫然的两眼，恐惧又添了一倍。那恶魔随手一脚把手枪踢到旁边。

===Original===
“Puerile!” he said contemptuously. He backed away from them, reseating himself on the couch whence he had risen. He surveyed the pair in sardonic mirth.
“Pat!” muttered the Doctor huskily. “Get out of here, Honey! He’s got some hellish trick of fascination that’s paralyzed me. Get out and get help!”
The girl moved as if to rise. Nicholas Devine shifted his eyes for the barest instant to her face; she felt the strength drain out of her body, and she sank weakly to her chair.
“It’s useless,” she murmured hopelessly to the Doctor. “He’s﻿—he’s just what I told you﻿—a devil!”

===Chinese===
“幼稚！”他轻蔑地说。他从两人面前退开，重新坐回方才起身的那张长椅，用讥诮的快意打量这一对。
“帕特！”医生沙哑地低呼，“快离开这儿，姑娘！他有种邪门的摄魂术，把我制住了。出去叫人来！”
姑娘作势要起身。尼古拉斯·德文把目光朝她脸上掠了一瞬；她便觉得浑身的力气都被抽走了，软软地瘫回椅子里。
“没用的，”她绝望地低声对医生说，“他——他就是我说的那个东西——一个魔鬼！”

===Original===
“I guess you were right,” mumbled Horker dazedly.
There was a burst of demonic mirth from the being on the couch. “Merely a matter of synapses,” he rasped, chuckling. His face changed, took on the familiar coldness, the stony expression Pat had observed there before. “This palls!” he snapped. “I’ve better amusement﻿—after we’ve rendered your friend merely an interested onlooker.” He narrowed his red eyes as if in thought. “Take off a stocking,” he ordered. “Tie his hands to the back of the chair.”
“I won’t!” said the girl. The eyes shifted to her face. “I won’t!” she repeated tremulously as she kicked off a diminutive pump. She shuddered at the gleam in the evil eyes as she stripped the long silken sheath from a white, rounded limb. She slipped a bare foot into the pump and moved reluctantly behind the chair that held the groaning Horker. She took one of the clenched, straining hands, and drew it back, fumbling with shaking fingers as she twisted the strip of thin chiffon. The demon moved closer, standing over her.
“Loose knots!” he snarled abruptly. He knocked her violently away with a stinging slap across her cheek, and seized the strip in his own hands. He drew the binding tight, twisting it about the lowest rung of the chair’s ladder back. Horker was forced to lean awkwardly to the rear; in this unbalanced position it was quite impossible to rise.

===Chinese===
“看来你说对了，”霍克晕头晕脑地喃喃道。
长椅上的那个存在爆发出一阵魔鬼般的大笑。“不过是个突触问题罢了。”他哑着嗓子咯咯笑道。他的脸变了，摆出那股帕特见过的熟悉的冷酷，那副石头的表情。“这没意思了！”他呵斥道，“我有更好的乐子——等咱们把你朋友变成一个有兴趣的旁观者之后。”他眯起红眼睛，似乎在想什么。“脱下一只长袜，”他命令道，“把他的手绑在椅子背上。”
“我不干！”姑娘说。那双眼睛转到她脸上。“我不干！”她一边发着抖重复，一边踢掉一只小巧的浅口鞋。当她从一条白皙圆润的腿上褪下那截长长的丝袜套时，邪眼里闪过的光让她不寒而栗。她把光脚塞进鞋里，不情愿地挪到捆着呻吟的霍克的椅子后面。她扳过一只攥紧绷直的手拉到背后，抖着手指笨拙地绞着那条薄纱的窄带。恶魔凑了过来，立在她头顶。
“松垮垮的！”他突然咆哮起来。他一巴掌狠狠抽在她脸颊上，把她打得踉跄跌开，自己一把抓住那条袜带。他把绑缚收紧，缠在梯式椅背最下面的一根横档上。霍克被迫别扭地向后仰着；这么个失衡的姿势，是万万起不了身的。

===Original===
Nicholas Devine turned away from the straining, perspiring Doctor, and advanced toward Pat, who cowered against the shattered cabinet.
“Now!” he muttered. “The experiment!” He chuckled raspingly. “What delicacy of degradation! Your lover and your guardian angel﻿—both helpless watchers! Excellent! Oh, very excellent!”
He grasped her wrist, drawing her after him to the center of the room, into the full view of the horrified, staring eyes of Horker.
“Always before,” continued her tormentor, “these hands have prepared you for the rites﻿—the ceremony that failed on two other occasions to transpire. Would it add a poignancy to the torture if I made you strip this body of yours with your own hands? Or will they suffer more watching me? Which do you think?”

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯·德文从那个拼命挣扎、汗如雨下的医生身边转过身，朝帕特走去；她正缩在打碎的柜子旁。
“现在！”他低声道，“做实验！”他哑着嗓子咯咯笑。“多么精致的屈辱！你的情人和你的守护天使——两个都成了束手无策的看客！妙极了！哦，妙极了！”
他攥住她的手腕，把她拖到房间中央，正落在霍克那双惊恐圆睁的眼睛的视线里。
“从前每一次，”折磨她的人继续说，“都是这双手替你为仪式作准备——那两回都没能举行的仪式。要是我让你亲手把这具身子剥光，这刑罚会不会更辛辣些？还是他们看着我动手更受罪些？你说哪个好？”

===Original===
Pat closed her eyes in helpless resignation to her fate. “Nick!” she moaned. “Oh, Nick dearest!”
“Not this time!” sneered the other. “Your friend and protector, the Doctor, has thoughtfully eliminated your sweetheart as a factor. He struggles too feebly for me to feel.”
“Nick!” she murmured again. “Dr. Carl!”
But the Doctor, now pulling painfully at his bonds, could only groan in distraction, and curse the unsuspected strength of sheer chiffon. He writhed miserably at the chafing of his wrists; his strange paralysis had departed, but he was quite helpless to assist Pat.

===Chinese===
帕特闭上眼睛，听天由命地认了。“尼克！”她呻吟道，“哦，最亲爱的尼克！”
“这回他出不来了！”那人讥笑道，“你的朋友兼保护人医生，想得周到，把你心上人这个变数排除掉了。他挣扎得太轻，我都觉不出来。”
“尼克！”她又低唤，“卡尔医生！”
可医生此刻正痛楚地拽着绑绳，只能心乱如麻地呻吟，咒骂薄薄一层纱竟有这般料想不到的韧劲。手腕勒得生疼，他痛苦地扭动着；那古怪的麻痹已经退去，但他完全无力搭救帕特。

===Original===
“I think,” said the cold tones of Nicholas Devine, “that the more delicate torture lies in your willingness. Let us see.”
He drew her into his arms. He twisted a hand in her hair, jerked her head violently backward, and pressed avid lips to hers. She struggled a little, but hopelessly, automatically. At last she lay quite passive, quite motionless, supported by his arms, and making not the slightest response to his kiss.
“Are you mine?” he queried fiercely, releasing her lips. “Are you mine now?”
She shook her head without opening her eyes. “No,” she said dully. “Not now, or ever.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”尼古拉斯·德文用冰冷的声调说，“更精妙的刑罚在于你的甘愿。咱们试试看。”
他把她拉进怀里。他一手绞住她的头发，猛地把她的头向后一扳，把贪婪的嘴唇压上她的唇。她稍稍挣扎了一下，却是无望的、下意识的。最后她一动不动地瘫着，全靠他的手臂撑住，对他的吻没有半点回应。
“你是我的吗？”他松开她的唇，狠狠地问，“现在你是我的了吗？”
她没有睁眼，摇了摇头。“不，”她木然地说，“现在不是，永远也不是。”

===Original===
Again he crushed her, while the Doctor looked on in helpless, bewildered, voiceless anger. This time his kiss was painful, burning, searing. Again that unholy fascination and unnatural delight in her own pain stirred her, and it took what little effort she was able to make to keep from responding. After a long interval, his lips again withdrew.
“Are you mine?” he repeated. She made no answer; she was gasping, and tears glistened under her closed eyelids, from the pain of her crushed lips. Again he kissed her, and again the wild abandonment to evil suffused her. She was suddenly responding to his agonizing caress; she was clinging fiercely to his torturing lips, feeling an unholy exaltation in the pain of his tearing fingers in the flesh of her back.
“Yours!” she murmured in response to his query. She heard her voice repeat madly, “Yours! Yours! Yours!”
“Do you yield willingly?” came the icy tones of the demon.

===Chinese===
他再次把她紧紧箍住，医生在一旁看着，满腔无助、惶惑、无声的愤怒。这一次他的吻带着痛楚，灼人，如火烙。那股邪异的蛊惑、那种对自己疼痛的反常快感又一次在她心里骚动起来，她费尽仅剩的一点力气才克制住不去回应。过了许久，他的唇才又移开。
“你是我的吗？”他又问了一遍。她没有作答；她喘着气，被压伤的双唇疼得闭着的眼睑下泪光闪闪。他又吻了她，那股放纵投向邪恶的狂潮又一次浸透了她。她突然回应起他那折磨人的爱抚；她死死贴着他那折磨人的唇，感受着他抠进她背上的手指带来的痛楚，心里升起一种渎神的亢奋。
“我是你的！”她应着他的逼问喃喃道。她听见自己的声音疯狂地重复着，“你的！你的！你的！”
“你是甘愿降服的吗？”恶魔的声音冰冷地传来。

===Original===
“Yes﻿—yes﻿—yes! Willingly!”
“Take off your clothes!” sounded the terrible, overpowering voice. He thrust her from him, so that she staggered dizzily backward. She stood swaying; the voice repeated its command.
The girl’s eyes widened wildly; she had the appearance of one in an ecstasy, a religious fervor. She raised her hand with a jerky impulsive gesture to the neck of her frock, still pinned together in the makeshift repairs of the evening.
There came a strange interruption. The Doctor, helpless onlooker, had at length evolved an idea out of the bewilderment in his mind. He opened his mouth and emitted a tremendous, deep, ear-shattering bellow!

===Chinese===
“是——是——是！甘愿！”
“把衣服脱了！”那可怕而不可抗拒的声音响起。他把她猛地推开，她晕头转向地踉跄后退。她摇摇晃晃地站着；那声音又把命令重复了一遍。
姑娘的双眼狂乱地睁大；她那样子像入了迷狂，像怀着宗教式的热忱。她手一抽一抽地、身不由己地举向连衣裙的领口，那里还别着今晚仓促缝补的别针。
就在这时来了个古怪的岔子。医生这个束手无策的旁观者，总算从满脑子的慌乱里想出了一个主意。他张开嘴，发出一声惊天动地、震耳欲聋的大吼！

===Original===
Nicholas Devine sent the girl spinning to the floor with a vicious shove, and turned his blazing eyes on Horker, who was drawing in his breath for a repetition of his roar. “Quiet!” he rasped, his red orbs boring down at the other. “Quiet, or I’ll muffle you!” Closing his eyes, the Doctor repeated his mighty shout.
The demon snatched the blanket from the couch, tossing it over the figure of the Doctor, where it became a billowing, writhing heap of brown wool. He turned his gaze on Pat, who was just struggling to her feet, and moved as if to advance toward her.
He paused. She had retrieved the Doctor’s revolver from the floor, and now faced him with the madness gone out of her eyes, supporting the weapon with both hands, the muzzle wavering toward his face.
“Drop it!” he commanded. She felt a recurrence of fascination, and an impulse to obey. Out of the corner of her eye, she saw the Doctor’s head emerging from the blanket as he shook it off.

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯·德文狠狠一掌把姑娘推得原地打转摔倒在地，转过那双冒火的眼睛盯着霍克，后者正吸足了气准备再吼一嗓子。“住口！”他哑声喝道，红色的眼珠灼灼地逼视着对方，“住口，不然我堵上你的嘴！”医生闭上眼睛，又发出那声震天的大吼。
恶魔一把扯过长椅上的毯子，兜头罩在医生身上，毯子顿时成了褐色羊毛的一堆翻腾扭动。他把目光转向刚刚挣扎着爬起来的帕特，作势要朝她走去。
他停住了。她已从地板上拾起医生的左轮手枪，此刻正面对着他，眼中的疯癫已经褪去，双手托着枪，枪口晃晃悠悠地指着他的脸。
“放下！”他命令道。她又感到那股蛊惑卷土重来，一阵想要服从的冲动。她用眼角看见医生正甩开毯子，脑袋从里面钻了出来。

===Original===
“Drop it!” repeated Nicholas Devine.
She closed her eyes, shutting out the vision of his dominant visage. With a surge of terror, she squeezed the trigger, staggering back to the couch at the roar and the recoil.
She opened her eyes. Nicholas Devine lay in the center of the room on his face; a crimson spot was matting the hair on the back of his head. She saw the Doctor raise a free hand; he was working clear of his bonds.
“Pat!” he said softly. He looked at her pale, sickened features. “Honey,” he said, “sit down till I get free. Sit down, Pat; you look faint.”
“Never faint!” murmured the girl, and pitched backward to the couch, with one clad and one bare leg hanging in curious limpness over the edge.

===Chinese===
“放下！”尼古拉斯·德文又说了一遍。
她闭上眼睛，把那张威凌的面孔关在视野之外。她心一横，恐怖之中扣下了扳机，在轰鸣和后坐力里踉跄着跌回长椅。
她睁开眼。尼古拉斯·德文脸朝下躺在房间中央；后脑的头发上一滩殷红正洇开。她看见医生举起一只挣脱出来的手；他正在从绑缚里脱身。
“帕特！”他轻声唤道。他望着她苍白、欲呕的脸色。“孩子，”他说，“坐下，等我脱开身。坐下，帕特；你脸色不对。”
“我才不晕呢！”姑娘喃喃说着，向后一头栽倒在长椅上，一条穿着袜、一条光着的腿软塌塌地悬在椅边，那姿势说不出的怪。

## XXV The Demon Lover / 二十五 恶魔情人
===Original===
Pat drew back, leaning against the door, and her key tinkled on the concrete of the porch. She was startled, shocked, but not as completely terrified as she might have expected. After all, she thought rapidly, they were standing in full view of a public street, and Dr. Carl’s residence was but a few feet distant. She could summon his help by screaming.
“Well!” she exclaimed, eyeing the figure inimically. “Your appearances and disappearances are beginning to remind me of the Cheshire Cat.”
“Except for the grin,” said the other in his cold tones.
“What do you want?” snapped Pat.

===Chinese===
帕特向后一缩，背抵住门，钥匙在门廊的水泥地上叮当一响。她又惊又震，却不像自己预想的那样吓得魂飞魄散。毕竟，她飞快地想，他们正站在大庭广众的街面上，卡尔医生的住处不过几步之遥。她一喊就能唤他来救。
“好啊！”她瞪着那条人影，叫道，“你这忽隐忽现的做派，越来越像柴郡猫了。”
“只差那副龇牙咧嘴的笑。”那人用他那冷冰冰的声调说。
“你想干什么？”帕特厉声问。

===Original===
“You know what I want.”
“You’ll not get it,” said the girl angrily. “You﻿—you’re doomed to extinction, anyway! Go away!”
“Suppose,” said the other with a strange, cold, twisted smile, “it were *he* that’s doomed to extinction﻿—what then?”
“It isn’t!” cried Pat. “It isn’t!” she repeated, while a quiver of uncertainty shook her. “He’s the stronger,” she said defiantly.

===Chinese===
“你知道我要什么。”
“你休想得到，”姑娘怒道，“你——反正你注定要完蛋！走开！”
“假如，”那人说着，脸上浮出一个古怪、冰冷、扭曲的笑，“注定完蛋的是*他*呢——那又如何？”
“才不是！”帕特叫道。“才不是！”她重复着，一阵动摇的颤栗却掠过心头。“他才是强者，”她不服地说。

===Original===
“Then where is he now?”
“Dr. Carl will help us!”
“Doctor!” sneered the other. “He and his clever theory! Am I an illusion?” he queried sardonically, thrusting his red-glinting eyes toward her. “Am I the product of his puerile, vacillating nature? Bah! I gave you the clue, and your Doctor hasn’t the intelligence to follow it!”
“Go away!” murmured Pat faintly. The approach of his face had unnerved her, and she felt terror beginning to stir within her. “Go away!” she said again. “Why do you have to torment me? Anyone would serve your purpose﻿—any woman!”
“You have an aesthetic appeal, as I’ve told you before,” replied the other in that toneless voice of his. “There is a pleasure in the defacement of black hair and pale skin, and your body is seductive, most seductive. Another might afford me less enjoyment, and besides, you hate me. Don’t you hate me?” He peered evilly at her.

===Chinese===
“那他这会儿在哪儿？”
“卡尔医生会帮我们！”
“医生！”那人讥笑，“他和他那套聪明理论！我是幻觉吗？”他把红光闪闪的眼睛逼向她，挖苦地问，“我是他那幼稚、摇摆的性情生出来的产物吗？呸！线索我给你了，可你们那位医生没那个脑子追下去！”
“走开！”帕特虚弱地喃喃道。他那张脸逼过来，让她心惊胆战，恐惧开始在她心里翻涌。“走开！”她又说了一遍，“你为什么偏要折磨我？随便谁都合你的用——随便哪个女人！”
“你有种审美的吸引力，这我早说过了，”那人用他那没有抑扬的声音回答，“毁坏黑发白肤自有一种乐趣，何况你这身子诱人，诱人得很。换一个或许给我的乐趣要少些；再说，你恨我。你不是恨我吗？”他邪恶地盯着她。

===Original===
“Oh, God﻿—yes!” The girl was shuddering.
“Say it, then! Say you hate me!”
“I hate you!” the girl cried vehemently. “Will you go away now?”
“With you!”

===Chinese===
“哦，天哪——恨！”姑娘浑身发抖。
“那就说出来！说你恨我！”
“我恨你！”姑娘激烈地喊道，“现在你可以走了吧？”
“带着你走！”

===Original===
“I’ll scream if you come any closer. You don’t dare touch me; I’ll call Dr. Horker.”
“You’ll only damage *him*﻿—your lover.”
“Then I’ll do it! He’ll understand.”
“Yes,” said the other reflectively. “He’s fool enough to forgive you. He’ll forgive you anything﻿—the weakling!”
“Go away! Get away from here!”

===Chinese===
“你再过来一步我就喊了。你不敢碰我；我要叫霍克医生了。”
“你只会害了*他*——你的情人。”
“那我也喊！他会明白的。”
“是啊，”那人若有所思地说，“他蠢得会原谅你。你干什么他都会原谅——那个软骨头！”
“走开！离这儿远点！”

===Original===
The other stared at her out of bloodshot eyes. “Very well,” he said in his flat tones. “This time the victory is yours.”
He backed slowly toward the steps. Pat watched him as he moved, feeling a surge of profound relief. As his shadow shifted, her key gleamed silver at her feet, and she stooped to retrieve it.
There was a rush of motion as her eyes left the form of her antagonist. A hand was clamped violently over her mouth, an arm passed with steel-like rigidity about her body. Nicholas Devine was dragging her toward the steps; she was halfway down before she recovered her wits enough to struggle.
She writhed and twisted in his grasp. She drove her elbow into his body with all her power, and kicked with the strength of desperation at his legs. She bit into the palm across her mouth﻿—and suddenly, with a subdued grunt of pain, he released her so abruptly that her own struggles sent her spinning blindly into the bushes of the hedge.

===Chinese===
那人布满血丝的眼睛盯着她。“很好，”他用平板的声调说，“这一局算你赢。”
他慢慢朝台阶退去。帕特望着他移动，心头涌起一阵如释重负。他的影子挪开时，她的钥匙在脚边闪出银光，她弯腰去捡。
她的目光一离开对手的身形，一阵疾风般的动作就扑了过来。一只手死死捂住她的嘴，一条胳膊钢铁般僵硬地箍住她的身子。尼古拉斯·德文正把她往台阶那儿拖；她下到一半才回过神来挣扎。
她在他掌握里扭来扭去。她用尽全力把胳膊肘顶进他身子，又拼死踢他的腿。她一口咬住捂嘴的手掌——他闷哼一声，猛地松了手，她自己的挣扎反倒把她甩得晕头转向，跌进树篱的灌木丛里。

===Original===
She turned gasping, unable for the moment to summon sufficient breath to scream. The other stood facing her with his eyes gleaming terribly into her own; then they ranged slowly from her diminutive feet to the rumpled ebony of her hair that she was brushing back with her hands from her pallid, frightened face.
“Obstinate,” he observed, rubbing his injured palm.
“Obstinate and unbroken﻿—but worth the trouble. Well worth it!” He reached out a swift hand, seizing her wrist as she backed against the bushes.
Pat twisted around, gazing frantically at Doctor Horker’s house, where a light had only now flashed on in the upper windows. Her breath flowed back into her lungs with a strengthening rush.
“Dr. Carl!” she screamed. “Dr. Carl! Help me!”

===Chinese===
她转过身直喘，一时间透不出够喊一嗓子的气。那人站在她面前，眼睛可怕地灼灼盯进她的眼睛；随后那目光又缓缓地从她一双小巧的脚，移到她那头揉乱的乌黑头发——她正用双手把头发从苍白惊惶的脸上向后拢。
“倔得很，”他一边揉着受伤的手掌一边评价。
“又倔又不服——倒值得费这番事。很值得！”他手一伸，在她退到灌木上时一把攥住了她的手腕。
帕特扭过身子，狂乱地望着霍克医生的宅子——楼上的窗子刚刚亮起灯。一股劲儿随着呼吸涌回她的胸腔。
“卡尔医生！”她尖叫，“卡尔医生！救命！”

===Original===
The other spun her violently about. She had a momentary glimpse of a horribly evil countenance, then he drew back his arm and shot a clenched fist to her chin.
The world reeled into a blaze of spinning lights that faded quickly to darkness. She felt her knees buckling beneath her, and realized that she was crumpling forward toward the figure before her. Then for a moment she was aware of nothing.
She didn’t quite lose consciousness, or at least for no more than a moment. She was suddenly aware that she was gazing down at a moving pavement, at her own arms dangling helplessly toward it. She perceived that she was lying limply across Nicholas Devine’s shoulder with his arms clenched about her knees. And then, still unable to make the slightest resistance, she was bundled roughly into the seat of his coupe; he was beside her, and the car was purring into motion.
She summoned what remained of her strength. She drew herself erect, fumbling at the handle of the door with a frantic idea of casting herself out of the car to the street. The creature beside her jerked her violently back; as she reeled into the seat, he struck her again with the side of his fist. It was a random blow, delivered with scarcely a glance at her; it caught her on the forehead, snapping her head with an audible thump against the wall of the vehicle. She swayed for a moment with closing eyes, then collapsed limply against him, this time in complete unconsciousness.

===Chinese===
那人把她猛地拧过来。她刹那间瞥见一张狰狞邪恶的面孔，接着他收回手臂，一记攥紧的拳头直捣她的下巴。
世界晃成一片旋转的火光，随即迅速暗下去。她觉得双膝一软，意识到自己正朝着面前那条身影栽倒下去。接着有一瞬间，她什么都不知道了。
她并没有完全昏过去，或者至多只昏了一瞬。她忽然发觉自己正朝下望着移动的路面，望着自己两条无力的胳膊朝路面晃荡。她明白了：她正软软地趴在尼古拉斯·德文的肩上，他的双臂箍着她的膝弯。接着，仍然连半点反抗都做不出，她被粗暴地塞进他那辆双门轿车的座位；他坐到她身边，汽车轻吼着开动起来。
她唤起仅存的力气。她直起身，胡乱去摸车门把手，一门心思要把自己扔出车外摔到街上。身边那东西把她猛地拽了回去；她跌回座位时，他又用拳侧打了她一下。那是随手的一击，打时几乎看都没看她；正落在她额头上，她的头咚的一声磕在车壁上。她晃了晃，眼睛渐渐合上，随即瘫软在他身上，这一回彻底昏了过去。

===Original===
That lapse too must have been brief. She opened dazed eyes on a vista of moving street lights; they were still in the car, passing now along some unrecognized thoroughfare lined with dark old homes. She lay for some moments uncomprehending; she was completely unaware of her situation.
It dawned on her slowly. She moaned, struggled away from the shoulder against which she had been leaning, and huddled miserably in the far corner of the seat. Nicholas Devine gave her a single glance with his unpleasant eyes, and turned them again on the street.
The girl was helpless, unable to put forth the strength even for another attempt to open the door. She was still only half aware of her position, and realized only that something appalling was occurring to her. She lay in passive misery against the cushions of the seat as the other turned suddenly up a dark driveway and into the open door of a small garage. He snapped off the engine, extinguished the headlights, and left them in a horrible, smothering, silent darkness.
She heard him open the door on his side; after an apparently interminable interval, she heard the creak of the hinges on her own side. She huddled terrified, voiceless, and immobile.

===Chinese===
那阵昏沉想必也不长。她睁开迷惘的眼睛，看见一串移动的路灯掠过；他们还在车里，正驶过一条认不出来的大街，两旁是黑沉沉的老房子。她躺了好一会儿，懵懵懂懂；全然不明白自己的处境。
她慢慢明白过来。她呻吟一声，从靠着的那副肩膀上挣开，缩到座位最里面的角落里。尼古拉斯·德文那双讨厌的眼睛瞥了她一下，又转回街上。
姑娘已经无力了，连再试一次开车门的力气都拿不出。她还是只半明白自己的处境，只意识到一件骇人的事正落到她头上。她认命地、凄凄地靠在座垫上，任那人猛地拐上一条黑漆漆的车道，驶进一间小车库敞开的门里。他啪地熄了火，关掉车灯，把他们俩撂在一团可怕、窒闷、死寂的黑暗里。
她听见他打开他那一侧的车门；仿佛过了没完没了的一阵，她才听见自己这一侧铰链的吱呀声。她吓得缩成一团，发不出声，动弹不得。

===Original===
He reached in, fumbling against her in the darkness. He found her arm, and dragged her from the car. Again, as on that other occasion, she found herself reeling helplessly behind him through the dark as he tugged at her wrist. He paused at a door in the building adjacent to the garage, searching in his pocket with his free hand.
“I won’t go in there!” she muttered dazedly. The other made no reply, but inserted a key in the lock, turned it, and swung open the door.
He stepped through it, dragging her after him. With a sudden access of desperate strength, she caught the frame of the door, jerked violently on her prisoned wrist, and was unexpectedly free. She reeled away, turned toward the street, and took a few faltering steps down the driveway.
Almost instantly her tormentor was upon her, and his hand closed again on her arm. Pat had no further strength; she sank to the pavement and crouched there, disregarding the insistent tugging on her arm.

===Chinese===
他伸手进来，在黑暗里朝她身上摸索。他摸到她的胳膊，把她从车里拖出去。和上回一样，她又发现自己被他拽着手腕，身不由己地跌跌撞撞跟在后面，穿过黑暗。他在车库旁边那栋楼的一扇门前停下，腾出的那只手在口袋里掏着。
“我不进去！”她晕晕乎乎地喃喃道。那人不答话，只把钥匙插进锁孔一拧，推开了门。
他跨进门，把她往里拖。她忽然爆出一股拼命的力气，抓住门框，被攥住的手腕猛力一挣，竟出乎意料地挣脱了。她踉跄着退开，转向街上，沿着车道跌跌撞撞地跑出几步。
几乎是转眼间，折磨她的人已扑到她身上，手又扣住了她的胳膊。帕特再没有力气了；她瘫倒在路面上，蜷缩在那里，任凭那只手一个劲儿地拽她的胳膊。

===Original===
“Come on,” he growled. “You only delay the inevitable. Must I drag you?”
She made no reply. He tugged violently at her wrist, dragging her a few inches along the pavement. Then he stooped over her, raised her in his arms, and bore her toward the dark opening of the door. He crowded her roughly through it, disregarding the painful bumping of her shoulders and knees. She heard the slam of the door as he kicked it closed, and she realized that they were mounting a flight of stairs, moving somewhere into the oppressive threatening darkness.
Then they were moving along a level floor, and her arm was bruised against another door. There was a moment of stillness, and then she was released, dropped indifferently to the surface of a bed or couch. A moment later a light flashed on.

===Chinese===
“起来，”他咆哮道，“你不过是拖拖躲不掉的事。非要我拖你不可？”
她不答。他狠狠拽她的手腕，把她在路面上拖出几寸。然后他俯下身，把她抱起来，朝那扇门黑洞洞的门口走去。他把她的肩膀和膝盖磕得生疼，也全然不管，硬把她塞进门去。她听见他一脚把门踢上的一声闷响，接着意识到他们正在登上一段楼梯，走进那压抑逼人的黑暗深处。
随后他们走在平坦的地面上，她的胳膊又撞在另一扇门上，撞得生疼。静了片刻，她被松开，被漠然地丢在一张床还是长椅的东西上。过了一会儿，灯一亮。

===Original===
The girl was conscious at first only of the gaze of the red eyes. They held her own in a fascinating, unbreakable, trance-like spell. Then, in a wave of dizziness, she closed her own eyes.
“Where are we?” she murmured. “In Hell?”
“You should call it Heaven,” came the sardonic voice. “It’s the home of your sweetheart. His home﻿—and mine!”

===Chinese===
起初姑娘意识到的只有那双红眼睛的凝视。它们摄住她的目光，像一道无法挣脱的迷咒。随后一阵眩晕袭来，她闭上了眼睛。
“这是哪儿？”她喃喃地问，“是地狱吗？”
“你该叫它天堂才对，”那讥诮的声音答道，“这是你心上人的家。他的家——也是我的！”

## XXVI The Depths / 二十六 深渊
===Original===
“Heaven and Hell always were the same place,” said Nicholas Devine, his red eyes glaring down at the girl. “We’ll demonstrate the fact.”
Pat shifted wearily, and sat erect, passing her hand dazedly across her face. She brushed the tangled strands of black hair from before her eyes, and stared dully at the room in which she found herself.
It had some of the aspects of a study, and some of a laboratory, or perhaps a doctor’s office. There was a case of dusty books on the wall opposite, and another crystal-fronted cabinet containing glassware, bottles, little round boxes suggestive of drugs or pharmaceuticals. There was a paper-littered table too; she gave a convulsive shudder at the sight of a bald, varnished death’s head, its lower jar articulated, that reposed on a pile of papers and grinned at her.
“Where﻿—” she began faintly.

===Chinese===
“天堂和地狱从来就是同一个地方，”尼古拉斯·德文说，红眼睛俯视着姑娘，“咱们这就来验证这个事实。”
帕特疲乏地挪动了一下，坐直身子，恍恍惚惚地用手抹了把脸。她把纠缠的黑发从眼前拨开，怔怔地打量她所处的这间屋子。
它有点像书房，又有点像实验室，或者像间诊室。对面墙上一柜落满灰尘的书，另一只玻璃门的柜子里摆着玻璃器皿、瓶瓶罐罐，还有一只只小圆盒，看着像药剂药粉。还有一张堆满纸张的桌子；桌上纸堆里搁着一颗上了清漆的秃脑袋骷髅，下颌还装着关节，正朝她咧着嘴笑，她见了猛地一阵抽搐般的战栗。
“这是——”她虚弱地开了口。

===Original===
“This was the room of your sweetheart’s father,” said the other. “His and my mutual father. He was an experimenter, a researcher, and so, in another sense, am I!” He leered evilly at her. “He used this chamber to further his experiments, and I for mine﻿—the carrying on of a noble family tradition!”
The girl scarcely heard his words; the expressionless tone carried no meaning to the chaos which was her mind. She felt only an inchoate horror and a vague but all-encompassing fear, and her head was aching from the blows he had dealt her.
“What do you want?” she asked dully.
“Why, there is an unfinished experiment. You must remember our interrupted proceedings of a week ago! Have you already forgotten the early steps of our experiment in evil?”

===Chinese===
“这是你心上人父亲的房间，”那人说，“也就是我和他共同的父亲。他是个实验家，一个研究者，从另一种意义上说，我也是！”他邪恶地睨着她。“他用这间屋子推进他的实验，我用它推进我的——承继一个高贵家族的传统！”
姑娘几乎没听见他的话；那没有抑扬的语调，传不进她那乱成一团的头脑。她只感到一阵说不成形的恐怖和一种模糊却无所不在的恐惧，他的几下殴打还打得她头疼。
“你要干什么？”她木然问。
“嘿，还有个没做完的实验呢。一星期前咱们那场被打断的仪式，你总该记得吧！这么快就忘了我们这项邪恶实验的入门步骤？”

===Original===
Pat cringed at the cold, sardonic tones of the other. “Let me go,” she whimpered. “Please!” she appealed. “Let me go!”
“In due time,” he responded. “You lack gratitude,” he continued. “Last time, out of the kindness that is my soul, I permitted you to dull your senses with alcohol, but you failed, apparently, to appreciate my indulgence. But this time”﻿—His eyes lit up queerly﻿—“this time you approach the consummation of our experiment with undimmed mind!”
He approached her. She drew her knees up, huddling back on the couch, and summoned the final vestiges of her strength.
“I’ll kick you!” she muttered desperately. “Keep back from me!”

===Chinese===
那人冰冷讥诮的语调让帕特缩成一团。“放我走，”她呜咽道，“求求你！”她哀告着，“放我走！”
“到时自会放，”他答道，“你不知好歹，”他接着说，“上一次，出于我灵魂里那份仁慈，我容许你用酒精麻痹自己的感官，可你显然没领会我的宽纵。但这回——”他的眼睛怪异地亮起来——“这回你将带着全然清醒的头脑，来迎接我们实验的圆满！”
他朝她走近。她缩起双膝，蜷在长椅深处，唤起最后一点残存的力气。
“我要踢你了！”她拼命地低声道，“别过来！”

===Original===
He paused just beyond her reach. “I had hoped,” he said ironically, “if not for your cooperation, at least for no further active resistance. It’s quite useless; I told you days ago that this time would come.”
He advanced cautiously; Pat thrust out her foot, driving it with all her power. Instantly he drew back, catching her ankle in his hand. He jerked her leg sharply upwards, and she was precipitated violently to the couch. Again he advanced.
The girl writhed away from him. She slipped from the foot of the couch and darted in a circle around him, turning in an attempt to gain the room’s single exit﻿—the door by which they had entered. He moved quickly to intercept her; he closed the door as she backed despairingly away, retreating to the far end of the room. Once more he faced her, his malicious eyes gleaming, and moved deliberately toward her.
She drew back until the table halted her; she pressed herself against it as if to force her way still further. The other moved at unaltered pace. Suddenly her hand pressed over some smooth, round, hard object; she grasped it and flung the grinning skull at the more terrible face that approached her. He dodged; there was a crash of glass as the gruesome missile shattered the pane of the cabinet of drugs. And inexorably, Nicholas Devine approached once more.

===Chinese===
他在她够不着的地方停住。“我本来指望，”他讥讽地说，“纵然得不到你的合作，至少也别再积极抵抗。那毫无用处；几天前我就说过，这一天会来的。”
他小心地逼近；帕特伸出脚，用尽全力蹬出去。他倏地退开，手却抓住了她的脚踝。他猛地把她的腿向上一掀，她便重重跌回长椅上。他又逼了上来。
姑娘扭身躲开他。她从长椅脚边溜下来，绕着他兜了个圈子，转身想抢到屋里唯一的出口——他们进来的那扇门。他迅速抢上封堵；她绝望地步步后退，退到房间尽头时，他把门关上了。他再度面对她，恶意的双眼灼灼放光，从容不迫地朝她走来。
她退到桌子挡住去路才停下；她抵着桌子，仿佛还想挤过去。那人脚步丝毫未变。忽然她的手按到一个光滑、浑圆、坚硬的东西；她一把抓起那颗龇牙咧嘴的骷髅，朝逼近的这张更可怕的脸砸过去。他一偏头躲过；那件可怖的暗器砸碎了药柜的玻璃，哗啦一声脆响。而尼古拉斯·德文又一次无情地逼了上来。

===Original===
She moved along the edge of the table, squeezed herself between it and the wall. Behind her was one of the room’s two windows, curtainless, with drawn shades. She found the cord, jerked it, and let the blind coil upward with an abrupt snap.
“I’ll throw myself through the window!” she announced with a sort of desperate calm. “Don’t dare move a step closer!”
The demon paused once more in his deliberate advance. “You will, of course,” he said as if considering. “Given the opportunity. Your body torn and broken, spotted with blood﻿—that might be a pleasure second only to that I plan.”
“You’ll suffer for it!” said the girl hysterically. “I’ll be glad to do it, knowing you’ll suffer!”

===Chinese===
她沿着桌边挪动，把自己挤进桌子和墙壁之间。她身后是屋里两扇窗之一，没挂窗帘，遮光帘却拉着。她摸到拉绳，猛地一扯，帘子哗啦一声卷了上去。
“我就从这窗户跳出去！”她带着一种绝望的平静宣布，“你敢再迈一步试试！”
恶魔从容的脚步又停了。“你当然跳得出来，”他像是思量着说，“只要有这个机会。你的身子摔得血肉模糊、血点斑斑——那份乐趣，也许仅次于我计划中的那桩。”
“你要为此受罪的！”姑娘歇斯底里地说，“知道你会受罪，我跳得心甘情愿！”

===Original===
“Not I﻿—your sweetheart.”
“I don’t care! I can’t stand it!”
The other smiled his demoniac smile, and resumed his advance. She watched him in terror that had now reached the ultimate degree; her mind could bear no more. She turned suddenly, raised her arm, and beat her fist against the pane of the window.
With the surprising resistance glass sometimes displays, it shook at her blow but did not shatter. She drew back for a second attempt, and her upraised arm was caught in a rigid grip, and she was dragged backward to the center of the room, thrown heavily to the floor. She sat dazedly looking up at the form standing over her.

===Chinese===
“受罪的不是我——是你的心上人。”
“我不管了！我受不了啦！”
那人浮起他那魔鬼的笑，继续进逼。她望着他，恐怖已达极点；她的心神再也承受不住了。她猛地转身，抬起手臂，一拳砸向窗玻璃。
玻璃有时会显出那种出人意料的韧性，被她一击只晃了晃，并未碎裂。她缩回手准备再砸，高举的手臂却被一只铁硬的手攥住，她整个人被拖回房间中央，重重摔在地板上。她晕晕地坐在那里，仰望着立在头顶的那条身影。

===Original===
“Must I render you helpless again?” queried the flat voice of the other. “Are you not yet broken, convinced of the uselessness of this struggle?”
She made no answer, staring dully at his immobile features.
“Are you going to fight me further?” As she was still silent, he repeated, “Are you?”
She shook her head vaguely. “No,” she muttered. She had reached the point of utter indifference; nothing at all was important enough now to struggle for.

===Chinese===
“非要我再把你打得动弹不得？”那人平板的嗓音问道，“你还没有垮么？还不明白这场挣扎毫无用处么？”
她没有回答，木然盯着他那张纹丝不动的脸。
“还要再跟我斗么？”见她仍不作声，他又问了一遍，“斗不斗？”
她茫然摇了摇头。“不斗了，”她咕哝道。她已到了全然麻木的地步；如今什么都无所谓了，犯不上再挣扎。

===Original===
“Stand up!” ordered the being above her.
She pulled herself wearily to her feet, leaning against the wall. She closed her eyes for a moment, then opened them dully as the other moved.
“What﻿—are you﻿—are you going to do?” she murmured.
“First,” said the demon coldly, “I shall disrobe you somewhat more completely than on our other occasion. Thereafter we will proceed to the consummation of our experiment.”

===Chinese===
“站起来！”立在她头顶的那个存在下令。
她疲惫地撑起身，靠着墙站住。她闭了一会儿眼，等那人一动，又木然睁开。
“你——要——要干什么？”她喃喃道。
“首先，”恶魔冷冷地说，“我要把你剥得比上回更彻底些。然后，我们就进行实验的最后一环。”

===Original===
She watched him indifferently, uncomprehendingly, as he crooked a thin finger in the neck of her frock. She felt the pressure as he pulled, heard the rip of the fabric, and the pop of buttons, but she was conscious of no particular sensation as the garment cascaded into a black and red pool at her feet. She stood passive as he hooked his finger in the strap of her vest, and that too joined the little mound of cloth. She shivered slightly as she stood bared to the waist, but gave no other sign.
Again the thin hand moved toward her; from somewhere in her tormented spirit a final shred of resistance arose, and she pushed the questing member feebly to one side. She heard a low, sardonic laugh from her oppressor.
“Look at me!” he commanded.
She raised her eyes wearily; she drew her arm about her in a forlorn gesture of concealment. Her eyes met the strange orbs of the other, and a faint thrill of horror stirred; other than this, she felt nothing. Then his eyes were approaching her; she was conscious of the illusion that they were expanding, filling all the space in front of her. Their weird glow filled the world, dominated everything.

===Chinese===
她漠然地、懵懂地看着他把一根瘦手指勾进她连衣裙的领口。她感到他一扯的力道，听见布料撕裂、纽扣迸落的声音，可那件衣服在她脚边堆成一摊黑红相间的碎片时，她心里并无特别的感觉。他手指又勾住她内衣的肩带，那件也加入了那小堆衣料，她仍是顺从地站着。上身赤裸时她微微一颤，此外再无表示。
那只瘦手又朝她伸来；在她饱受折磨的心底，最后一丝反抗冒了上来，她无力地把那只摸索的手推到一边。她听见压迫她的人低低一声讥笑。
“看着我！”他命令道。
她疲惫地抬起眼；她把一条胳膊横抱在身前，作个凄凉的遮掩姿势。她的目光遇上那人那双怪眼，一丝恐怖的悸栗掠过；除此之外，她毫无感觉。随后他的眼睛向她逼近；她产生了一种错觉，仿佛那双眼在胀大，塞满了她面前的整个空间。那诡异的光华充盈了天地，宰制了一切。

===Original===
“Will you yield?” he queried.
The eyes commanded. “Yes,” she said dully.
She felt his hands icy cold on her bare shoulders. They traveled like a shudder about her body, and suddenly she was pressed close to him.
“Are you mine?” he demanded. For the first time there was a tinge of expression in the toneless voice, a trace of eagerness. She made no answer; her eyes, held by his, stared like the eyes of a person in a trance, unwinking, fascinated.
“Are you mine?” he repeated, his breath hissing on her cheek.

===Chinese===
“你降不降？”他问。
那双眼睛在发号施令。“降。”她木然说。
她感到他的双手落上她裸露的肩膀，冰冷彻骨。那双手像一阵寒噤游遍她的身子，突然间她被紧紧按进他的怀里。
“你是我的吗？”他逼问。那没有抑扬的嗓音里头一回有了一丝表情，一丝急切。她没有回答；她的眼睛被他摄住，像梦游者的眼睛一样一眨不眨，失魂地直视着。
“你是我的吗？”他又问，气息咝咝地喷在她脸颊上。

===Original===
“Yes.” She heard her own voice in automatic reply to his question.
“Mine﻿—for the delights of evil?”
“Yours!” she murmured. The eyes had blotted out everything.
“And do you hate me?”
“No.”

===Chinese===
“是。”她听见自己的声音机械地应着他的问话。
“我的人——共尝邪恶之乐？”
“你的！”她喃喃道。那双眼睛已抹去了世间的一切。
“你恨我么？”
“不恨。”

===Original===
The arms about her tightened into crushing bands. The pressure stopped her breath; her very bones seemed to give under their fierce compression.
“Do you hate me?” he muttered.
“Yes!” she gasped. “Yes! I hate you!”
“Ah!” He twisted his hand in her black hair, wrenching it roughly back. “Are you ready now for the consummation? To look upon the face of evil?”
She made no reply. Her eyes, as glassy as those of a sleepwalker, stared into his.

===Chinese===
箍着她的双臂收紧，成了碾人的铁箍。那压力让她透不过气；她的骨头仿佛都要在那狠命的一箍下碎裂。
“你恨我么？”他低声道。
“恨！”她喘着气喊，“恨！我恨你！”
“啊！”他把手绞进她的黑发，狠狠向后一拽。“现在可以功德圆满了么？敢不敢直面邪恶的嘴脸？”
她没有回答。她那双梦游者般失神的眼睛，直直望进他的眼睛。

===Original===
“Are you ready?”
“Yes,” she said.
He pressed his mouth to hers. The fierceness of the kiss bruised her lips, the pull of his hand in her hair was a searing pain, the pressure of his arm about her body was a suffocation. Yet﻿—somehow﻿—there was again the dawning of that unholy pleasure﻿—the same degraded delight that had risen in her on that other occasion, in the room of the red-checked tablecloth. Through some hellish alchemy, the leaden pain was transmuting itself into the garish gold of a horrible, abnormal pleasure. She found her crushed lips attempting a feeble, painful response.
At her movement, she felt herself swung abruptly from her feet. With his lips still crushing hers, he raised her in his arms; she felt herself borne across the room. He paused; there was a sudden release, and she crashed to the hard surface of the couch, whose rough covering scratched the bare flesh of her back. Nicholas Devine bent over her; she saw his hand stretch toward her single remaining garment. And again, from somewhere in her harassed soul, a spark of resistance flashed.

===Chinese===
“准备好了么？”
“好了。”她说。
他把嘴压上她的嘴。那吻的狠劲捣得她嘴唇生疼，他揪住头发的手带来灼烫的剧痛，箍在她身上的手臂让她窒息。然而——不知怎的——那种邪异的快感又一次萌动了——和上回一样，在那间铺着红格桌布的屋子里升起过的那种堕落的欢愉。经过某种地狱般的炼金术，铅一般沉的疼痛正把自己炼成一种骇人、反常的快感的俗艳金子。她那被捣伤的嘴唇，竟试着作出微弱而痛楚的回应。
她这一动，便觉得自己被猛地拦腰抱起。他的唇仍压着她的，他把她横抱在怀中；她感到自己被抱过了房间。他停住；骤然一松，她重重摔在硬邦邦的长椅上，粗糙的椅面磨着她背上裸露的肌肤。尼古拉斯·德文俯下身来；她看见他的手伸向她身上仅剩的那件衣物。而这时，从她备受凌迫的灵魂深处，又是一星反抗的火花闪起。

===Original===
“Nick!” she moaned. “Oh, Nick! Help me!”
“Call him!” said the other, a sneer on his face. “Call him! He hears; it adds to his torment!”
She covered her eyes with her hands. She felt his hand slip coldly between her skin and the elastic about her waist.
“Nick!” she moaned again. “Nick! Oh, my God! Nick!”

===Chinese===
“尼克！”她呜咽道，“哦，尼克！救救我！”
“叫啊！”那人说，脸上带着讥诮，“叫啊！他听得见；这能添他的苦！”
她双手捂住眼睛。她感到他的手冰冷地探进她的肌肤和腰间松紧带之间。
“尼克！”她又一次哀鸣，“尼克！哦，我的天！尼克！”

## VII The Red Eyes Return / 七 红眼归来
===Original===
“I suppose I really ought to meet your friends, Patricia,” said Mrs. Lane, peering out of the window, “but they all seem to call when I’m not at home.”
“I’ll have some of them call in February,” said Pat. “You’re not out as often in February.”
“Why do you say I’m not out as often in February?” demanded her mother. “I don’t see what earthly difference the month makes.”
“There are fewer days in February,” retorted Pat airily.

===Chinese===
“我看我真该见见你的朋友们了，帕特里夏，”莱恩太太望着窗外说，“可他们好像都挑我不在家的时候上门。”
“我让他们二月里来拜访，”帕特说，“二月里您出去得不那么勤。”
“为什么说我二月里出去得不勤？”她母亲质问道，“我不明白跟月份有什么相干。”
“二月的天数少呗。”帕特轻飘飘地回了一句。

===Original===
“Facetious brat!”
“So I’ve been told. You needn’t worry, though, Mother; I’m sober, steady, and reliable, and if I weren’t, Dr. Carl would see to it that my associates were.”
“Yes; Carl is a gem,” observed her mother. “By the way, who’s this Nicholas you’re so enthusiastic about?”
“He’s a boy I met.”

===Chinese===
“贫嘴的丫头！”
“都这么说。不过您放心，妈妈；我清醒、稳重、靠得住，就算我不靠谱，卡尔医生也会把关，让我来往的人靠谱。”
“是啊；卡尔是块宝，”她母亲评论道，“对了，你那么起劲儿念叨的这个尼古拉斯是谁？”
“我认识的一个小伙子。”

===Original===
“What’s he like?”
“Well, he speaks English and wears a hat.”
“Imp! Is he nice?”
“That means is his family acceptable, doesn’t it? He hasn’t any family.”

===Chinese===
“人怎么样？”
“嗯，他说英语，戴帽子。”
“捣蛋鬼！他人好吗？”
“您这是问他家里够不够体面，对吧？他没有家人。”

===Original===
Mrs. Lane shrugged her attractive shoulders. “You’re a self-reliant sort, Patricia, and cool as iced lettuce, like your father. I don’t doubt that you can manage your own affairs, and here comes Claude with the car.” She gave the girl a hasty kiss. “Goodbye, and have a good time, as I’m sure I shan’t with Bret Cutter in the game.”
Pat watched her mother’s trim, amazingly youthful figure as she entered the car. More like a companion than a parent, she mused; she liked the independence her mother’s attitude permitted her.
“Better than being watched like a prizewinning puppy,” she thought. “Maybe Dr. Carl as a father would have a detriment or two along with the advantages. He’s a dear, and I’m mad about him, but he does lean to the nineteenth century as far as parental duties are concerned.”
She saw Nick’s car draw to the curb; as he emerged she waved from the window and skipped into the hall. She caught up her wrap and bounded out to meet him just ascending the steps.

===Chinese===
莱恩太太耸了耸她那对好看的肩膀。“你是个自立的性子，帕特里夏，冷静得像冰镇生菜，随你父亲。你自己能打理自己的事，这我不怀疑——克劳德开车来了。”她匆匆吻了女儿一下。“再见，玩得开心，有布雷特·卡特凑局，我肯定玩不开心。”
帕特望着母亲那苗条得惊人的年轻身影上了车。与其说像位母亲，不如说像位伴侣，她想；她喜欢母亲这种态度给她带来的自由。
“总比像得奖的小狗一样被人盯着强，”她想，“卡尔医生当父亲，或许好处不少，毛病也有一两样。他是个宝贝，我迷他得紧，可论起做家长的职责，他确实偏十九世纪。”
她看见尼克的车靠上路缘；他一下车，她就从窗口挥挥手，蹦进门厅。她抓起披肩，飞奔出去迎他，他正踏上台阶。

===Original===
