“Let’s go!” she greeted him. She cast an apprehensive glance at his features, but there was nothing disturbing about him. He gave her a diffident smile, the shy, gentle smile that had taken her in that first moment of meeting. This was certainly no one but her own Nick, with no trace of the unsettling personality of their last encounter.
He helped her into the car, seating himself at her side. He leaned over her, kissing her very tenderly; suddenly she was clinging to him, her face against the thrilling warmth of his cheek.
“Nick!” she murmured. “Nick! You’re just safely you, aren’t you? I’ve been imagining things that I knew couldn’t be so!”
He slipped his arm caressingly about her, and the pressure of it was like the security of encircling battlements. The world was outside the circle of his arms; she was within, safe, inviolable. It was some moments before she stirred, lifting her pert face with tear-bright eyes from the obscurity of his shoulder.

===Chinese===
“走吧！”她招呼他。她忐忑地瞥了一眼他的脸，却看不出一丝不安的影子。他朝她腼腆地一笑，正是初见那一刻就把她俘获的那种羞怯温柔的笑。这千真万确是她自己的尼克，上次见面那个搅得人心神不宁的人格没有留下半点痕迹。
他扶她上车，在她身边坐下。他俯身过来，无比温柔地吻她；突然间她依偎进他怀里，脸贴着他脸颊上那令人心颤的暖意。
“尼克！”她喃喃道，“尼克！你完完全全是你自己，对不对？我一直在瞎想那些明知不可能的事！”
他爱怜地伸臂搂住她，那圈臂膀的力道像环绕的城堞一样安稳。世界在他手臂的圆圈之外；她在圈内，安然无恙，不可侵犯。过了好一会儿她才动了动，从他的肩头抬起她那张俏脸，眼中泪光晶亮。

===Original===
“So!” she exclaimed, patting the black glow of her hair into composure. “I feel better, Nick, and I hope you didn’t mind.”
“Mind!” he ejaculated. “If you mean that as a joke, Honey, it’s far too subtle for me.”
“Well, I didn’t think you’d mind,” said Pat demurely, settling herself beside him. “Let’s be moving, then; Dr. Carl is nearly popping his eyes out in the window there.”
The car hummed into motion; she waved a derisive arm at the Doctor’s window by way of indicating her knowledge of his surveillance. “Ought to teach him a lesson some time,” she thought. “One of these fine evenings I’ll give him a real shock.”

===Chinese===
“好啦！”她叫道，拍了拍一头乌亮的秀发，让它服帖。“我舒坦多了，尼克，希望你不介意。”
“介意！”他失声道，“你要是拿这个开玩笑，亲爱的，那也太深奥了，我参不透。”
“嗯，我知道你不介意，”帕特一本正经地说，在他身边坐稳，“那就走吧；卡尔医生在那边窗口眼睛都快瞪出来了。”
汽车嗡地开动；她朝医生的窗口嘲弄地挥了挥手，示意自己早知道他在监视。“哪天得教训教训他，”她想，“改天晚上非吓他一大跳不可。”

===Original===
“Where’ll we go?” queried Nick, veering skilfully into the swift traffic of Sheridan Road.
“Anywhere!” she said blithely. “Who cares as long as we go together?”
“Dancing?”
“Why not? Know a good place?”

===Chinese===
“咱们去哪儿？”尼克问，一边利落地拐进谢里丹路湍急的车流。
“随便哪儿！”她快活地说，“只要咱们一起，谁在乎去哪儿？”
“跳舞去？”
“怎么不？知道哪家好吗？”

===Original===
“No.” He frowned in thought. “I haven’t indulged much.”
“The Picador?” she suggested. “The music’s good, and it’s not too expensive. But it’s ’most across town, and besides, Saturday nights we’d be sure to run into some of the crowd.”
“What of it?”
“I want to dance with you, Nick﻿—all evening. I want to be without distractions.”

===Chinese===
“不知道。”他皱眉想着，“我很少玩这个。”
“皮卡多怎么样？”她提议，“音乐好，价钱也不贵。可它差不多在城另一头，再说星期六晚上，我们准会撞上那帮熟人。”
“撞上又怎样？”
“我要跟你跳，尼克——跳一晚上。我不想被人分心。”

===Original===
“Pat, dear! I could kiss you for that.”
“You will,” she murmured softly.
They moved aimlessly south with the traffic, pausing momentarily at the light-controlled intersections, then whirring again to rapid motion. The girl leaned against his arm silently, contentedly; block after block dropped behind.
“Why so pensive, Honey?” he asked after an interval. “I’ve never known you so quiet before.”

===Chinese===
“帕特，亲爱的！为这话我真想吻你。”
“你会的。”她柔声呢喃。
他们随车流漫无目的地向南开，在有信号灯的路口暂驻片刻，又嗡嗡地加速前行。姑娘静静地、心满意足地靠着他的手臂；街区块块向后退去。
“怎么这么沉静，亲爱的？”过了一会儿他问，“我从没见你这么安静过。”

===Original===
“I’m enjoying my happiness, Nick.”
“Aren’t you usually happy?”
“Of course, only these last two or three days, ever since our last date, I’ve been making myself miserable. I’ve been telling myself foolish things, impossible things, and it’s only now that I’ve thrown off the blues. I’m happy, Dear!”
“I’m glad you are,” he said. His voice was strangely husky, and he stared fixedly at the street rushing toward them. “I’m glad you are,” he repeated, a curious tensity in his tones.

===Chinese===
“我在享受我的幸福呢，尼克。”
“你平时不幸福吗？”
“当然幸福，只是前几天，自打咱们上次约会起，我一直让自己难受。我净跟自己念叨些蠢事、不可能的事，直到现在才甩掉那阵忧郁。我幸福着呢，亲爱的！”
“你幸福我就高兴，”他说。他的嗓音出奇地沙哑，直勾勾地盯着迎面扑来的街道。“你幸福我就高兴，”他又说了一遍，语调里有一种奇怪的紧绷。

===Original===
“So’m I.”
“I’ll never do anything to make you unhappy, Pat﻿—never. Not﻿—if I can help it.”
“You can help it, Nick. You’re the one making me happy; please keep doing it.”
“I﻿—hope to.” There was a queer catch in his voice. It was almost as if he feared something.

===Chinese===
“我也是。”
“我永远不会做让你伤心的事，帕特——永远不会。不会——只要我拦得住。”
“你拦得住的，尼克。让我幸福的就是你；请你一直这样做下去。”
“我——希望如此。”他的嗓音里有一处古怪的哽咽。几乎像是他在害怕什么。

===Original===
“Selah!” said Pat conclusively. She was thinking, “Wrong of me to refer to that accident. After all it was harmless; just a natural burst of passion. Might happen to anyone.”
“Where’ll we go?” asked Nick as they swung into the tree-shadowed road of Lincoln Park. “We haven’t decided that.”
“Anywhere,” said the girl dreamily. “Just drive; we’ll find a place.”
“You must know lots of them.”

===Chinese===
“就此打住！”帕特斩钉截铁地说。她心里想的是：“我不该提那桩意外。那到底无害；不过是一阵天然的激情爆发。谁都可能碰上。”
“咱们去哪儿？”他们拐上林肯公园绿荫覆盖的马路时，尼克问，“还没定呢。”
“随便哪儿，”姑娘梦呓般地说，“只管开；我们会找到一个地方的。”
“你肯定知道好多家。”

===Original===
“We’ll find a new place; we’ll discover it for ourselves. It’ll mean more, doing that, than if we just go to one of the old places where I’ve been with every boy that ever dated me. You don’t want me dancing with a crowd of memories, do you?”
“I shouldn’t mind as long as they stayed merely memories.”
“Well, I should! This evening’s to be ours﻿—exclusively ours.”
“As if it could ever be otherwise!”

===Chinese===
“我们要找个新的；自己去发现一个。那样才有意思，总比去那些老地方强——那些地方我跟每个约过我的小伙子都去过。你总不想让我踩着一群回忆跳舞吧？”
“只要它们仅仅是回忆，我倒不介意。”
“哼，我介意！今晚得归我们——只归我们俩。”
“好像还能有别的归法似的！”

===Original===
“Indeed?” said Pat. “And how do you know what memories I might choose to carry along? Are you capable of inspecting my mental baggage?”
“We’ll check it at the door. You’re traveling light tonight, aren’t you?”
“Pest!” she said, giving his cheek an impudent vicious pinch. “Nice, pleasurable pest!”
He made no answer. The car was idling rather slowly along Michigan Boulevard; half a block ahead glowed the green of a traffic light. Faster traffic flowed around them, passing them like water eddying about a slow floating branch.

===Chinese===
“是吗？”帕特说，“你怎么知道我兴许会捎上哪些回忆？你有本事检查我的精神行李吗？”
“咱们到门口把它寄存了。你今晚轻装出行，对不对？”
“讨厌鬼！”她说着，放肆地狠狠拧了一下他的脸颊。“讨人喜欢的、让人快活的讨厌鬼！”
他没有答话。车子沿着密歇根大道慢悠悠地滑行；前方半条街外亮着绿灯。更快的车流从四周涌过，像流水绕过一根缓缓漂浮的树枝那样超过他们。

===Original===
Suddenly the car lurched forward. The amber flame of the warning light had flared out; they flashed across the intersection a split second before the metallic click of the red light, and a scant few feet before the converging lines of traffic from the side street swept in with protesting horns.
“Nick!” the girl gasped. “You’ll rate yourself a traffic ticket! Why’d you cut the light like that?”
“To lose your guardian angel,” he muttered in tones so low she barely understood his words.
Pat glanced back; the lights of a dozen cars showed beyond the barrier of the red signal.
“Do you mean one of those cars was following us? What on earth makes you think that, and why should it, anyway?”

===Chinese===
车子猛地向前一蹿。警示灯的黄焰已经亮起；他们赶在红灯咔哒亮起前的一瞬冲过路口，比两旁街口汇拢来的、喇叭乱响的车流只抢出了几英尺。
“尼克！”姑娘倒吸一口气，“你想吃罚单啊！干吗这么闯灯？”
“甩掉你的守护天使。”他咕哝道，声音低得她勉强听清。
帕特回头望；红灯的屏障后面亮着十几辆车的车灯。
“你是说那些车里有车在跟踪咱们？你到底凭什么这么想？再说它图什么呀？”

===Original===
The other made no answer; he swerved the car abruptly off the avenue, into one of the nondescript side streets. He drove swiftly to the corner, turned south again, and turned again on some street Pat failed to identify﻿—South Superior or Grand, she thought. They were scarcely a block from the magnificence of Michigan Avenue and its skyscrapers, its brilliant lights, and its teeming night traffic, yet here they moved down a deserted dark thoroughfare, a street lined with ramshackle wooden houses intermingled with mean little shops.
“Nick!” Pat exclaimed. “Where are we going?”
The low voice sounded. “Dancing,” he said.

===Chinese===
那人不答话；他猛打方向盘拐出大道，钻进一条不起眼的侧街。他疾驰到街角，又向南一拐，再在帕特认不出的某条街上拐了个弯——她想不是南苏必利尔街就是格兰德街。这儿离密歇根大道的巍峨、摩天楼、璀璨灯火和鼎沸的夜车流不过一个街区，他们行驶的却是一条荒僻昏暗的大街，两旁是东倒西歪的木屋，夹着些寒碜的小铺子。
“尼克！”帕特叫道，“我们这是去哪儿？”
那低低的嗓音响了。“跳舞。”他说。

===Original===
He brought the car to the curb; in the silence as the motor died, the faint strains of a mechanical piano sounded. He opened the car door, stepped around to the sidewalk.
“We’re here,” he said.
Something metallic in his tone drew Pat’s eyes to his face. The eyes that returned her stare were the bloody orbs of the demon of last Wednesday night!

===Chinese===
他把车靠上路缘；马达熄灭的寂静里，传来自动钢琴隐约的乐声。他打开车门，绕到人行道上。
“到了。”他说。
他语调里某种金属般的东西让帕特抬眼去看他的脸。回望她的那双眼睛，正是上个星期三晚上那个恶魔的血红眼珠！

## IV The Transfiguration / 四 变容
===Original===
The car slid smoothly along a straight white road that stretched ahead into the darkness like an earthbound Milky Way. In the dim distance before them, red as Antares, glowed the taillight of some automobile; except for this lone evidence of humanity, reflected Pat, they might have been flashing through the cosmic depths of interstellar space, instead of following a highway in the very shadow of Chicago. The colossal city of the lake-shore was invisible behind them, and the clustering suburbs with it.
“Queer, isn’t it?” said Pat, after a silence, “how contented we can be with none of the purchased amusement people crave﻿—shows, movies, dancing, and all that.”
“It doesn’t seem queer to me,” answered Nick. “Not when I look at you here beside me.”
“Nice of you!” retorted Pat. “But it’s never happened to me before.” She paused, then continued, “How do you like the Doctor?”
“How does he like me? That’s considerably more to the point, isn’t it?”
“He thinks you’re nice, but﻿—let’s see﻿—introverted, repressed, and ill-adjusted to your environment. I think those were the points.”

===Chinese===
汽车平稳地滑行在一条笔直的白色公路上，那路伸进黑暗深处，像一条落在人间的银河。前方昏暗的远处，一点红光如心宿二般灼灼——那是某辆汽车的尾灯；帕特心想，若没有这一点仅有的人迹，他们简直是在星际空间的宇宙深渊里飞掠，而不是行驶在芝加哥近旁的公路上。湖畔那座巨城连同成片的郊区，都已被甩在身后，望不见了。
“怪不怪？”沉默了一阵后帕特说，“人们渴求的那些花钱买来的消遣——戏啊、电影啊、跳舞啊什么的，咱们一样没有，却也能这么心满意足。”
“我倒不觉得怪，”尼克答道，“只要看看坐在我身边的你，就不怪了。”
“嘴真甜！”帕特回敬道，“可这种事我从前没有过。”她顿了顿，又问，“你觉得医生怎么样？”
“他觉得我怎么样？这才是更要紧的，对吧？”
“他觉得你人不错，不过——我想想——内向、压抑、跟环境格格不入。我记得就是这几条。”

===Original===
“Well, *I* liked *him*, in spite of your maneuvers, and in spite of his being a doctor.”
“What’s wrong with being a doctor?”
“Did you ever read *Tristram Shandy*?” was Nick’s irrelevant response.
“No, but I read the newspapers!”
“What’s the connection, Pat?”
“Just as much connection as there is between the evils of being a doctor and reading *Tristram Shandy*. I know that much about the book, at least.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，*我*倒喜欢*他*，不管你耍了什么安排，也不管他是个医生。”
“当医生有什么不好？”
“你读过《项狄传》吗？”尼克答非所问。
“没读过，可我读报纸！”
“这有什么关系，帕特？”
“跟‘当医生的坏处’和‘读《项狄传》’之间的关系一样多。关于那本书，我至少知道这么多。”

===Original===
“You’re nearly right,” laughed Nick. “I was just referring to one of Tristram’s remarks on doctors and lawyers. It fits my attitude.”
“What’s the remark?”
“Well, he had the choice of professions, and it occurred to him that medicine and law were the vulture professions, since lawyers live by men’s quarrels and doctors by men’s misfortunes. So﻿—he became a writer.”
“And what do writers live by?” queried Pat mischievously. “By men’s stupidity!”
“You’re precious, Pat!” Nick chuckled delightedly. “If I’d created you to order, I couldn’t have planned you more to taste﻿—pepper, tabasco sauce, vinegar, spice, and honey!”
“And to be taken with a grain of salt,” retorted the girl, puckering her piquant, impish features. She edged closer to him, locking her arm through his where it rested on the steering wheel.

===Chinese===
“差不多说对了，”尼克笑道，“我只是想起项狄关于医生和律师的一句议论。正合我的看法。”
“什么议论？”
“是这样，他当时要选职业，想到医学和法律都是秃鹫行当：律师靠人的争讼过活，医生靠人的不幸过活。于是——他当了作家。”
“那作家靠什么过活？”帕特调皮地问，“靠人的愚蠢！”
“你真是个宝贝，帕特！”尼克乐得直笑，“就算我是照单定做的你，也调配不出更合我口味的——胡椒、辣汁、酸醋、香料，还有蜜！”
“还得就着一撮盐吃。”姑娘回嘴道，俏皮促狭的脸皱成一团。她朝他挨近些，把胳膊穿进他搭在方向盘上的臂弯里。

===Original===
“Nick,” she said, her tones suddenly gentle, “I think I’m pretty crazy about you. Heaven knows why I should be, but it’s a fact.”
“Pat, dear!”
“I’m crazy about you in this meek, sensitive pose of yours, and I’m fascinated by those masterful moments you flash occasionally. Really, Nick, I almost wish you flamed out oftener.”
“Don’t!” he said sharply.
“Why not?”
“Let’s not talk about me, Pat. It﻿—embarrasses me.”

===Chinese===
“尼克，”她忽然放缓了声音，“我想我是真让你迷住了。天知道为什么，可这是事实。”
“帕特，亲爱的！”
“你这一副温顺、敏感的模样叫我着迷，你偶尔闪露的那几分专断魄力更让我心荡神驰。真的，尼克，我几乎希望你更常露出锋芒来。”
“别说！”他厉声道。
“为什么不说？”
“咱们别谈我，帕特。这——让我难为情。”

===Original===
“All right, Mr. Modesty! Let’s talk about me, then. I’ll promise we won’t succeed in embarrassing me.”
“And it’s quite the most interesting subject in the world, Pat.”
“Well, then?”
“What?”
“Why don’t you start talking? The topic is all attention.”
He chuckled. “How many men have told you you were beautiful, Pat?”

===Chinese===
“好啊，谦虚先生！那就谈我吧。我保证，想让我难为情可没那么容易。”
“这可是天底下顶有意思的题目，帕特。”
“那还等什么？”
“等什么什么？”
“你怎么还不开讲？题目都竖起耳朵听着呢。”
他轻声笑了。“有多少男人跟你说过你美，帕特？”

===Original===
“I never kept account.”
“And in many different ways?”
“Why? Have you, perchance, discovered a new way, Nick?”
“Not at all. The oldest way of any, the way of Sappho and Pindar.”
“O-ooh!” She clapped her hands in mock delight. “Poetry!”
“The only medium that could possibly express how lovely you are,” said Nick.

===Chinese===
“我没记账。”
“说法也各式各样？”
“怎么？莫非你发现了一种新说法，尼克？”
“才不是。是最古老的一种，萨福和平达用的那种。”
“哦——！”她装出欢喜的样子拍起手来。“诗！”
“唯一可能表达你有多可爱的媒介。”尼克说。

===Original===
“Nicholas, have you gone and composed a poem to me?”
“Composed? No. It isn’t necessary, with you here beside me.”
“What’s that? Some very subtle compliment?”
“Not subtle, Pat. You’re the poem yourself; all I need do is look at you, listen to you, and translate.”
“Neat!” applauded the girl. “Do I hear the translation?”
“You certainly do.” He turned his odd amber-green eyes on her, then bent forward to the road. He began to speak in a low voice.

===Chinese===
“尼古拉斯，你该不会真给我写了一首诗吧？”
“写？没有。有你在身边，用不着写。”
“这话怎么讲？很微妙的一句恭维？”
“一点也不微妙，帕特。你自己就是那首诗；我只要看着你，听你说，然后翻译出来。”
“妙！”姑娘喝彩，“我能听听译文吗？”
“当然能。”他把那双奇特的琥珀绿眼睛转向她，随即俯身望着路。他低声开口了。

===Original===
> “In no far country’s silent ways
 

 Shall I forget one little thing﻿—
 

 The soft intentness of your gaze,
 

 The sweetness of your murmuring
 

 Your generously tender praise,
 

 The words just hinted by a breath﻿—
 

 In no far country’s silent way,
 

 Unless that country’s name be Death﻿—”

===Chinese===
> “纵使去到远方国度的寂静路上，
我亦不会忘怀那些细微——
你凝眸时温软的专注，
你絮语时甜蜜的声响，
你慷慨温柔的赞许，
你气息轻递、欲言又止的半句——
纵使去到远方国度的寂静路上，
除非那国度的名字叫做死亡——”

===Original===
He paused abruptly, and drove silently onward.
“Oh,” breathed Pat. “Why don’t you go on, Nick? Please.”
“No. It isn’t the mood for this night, Dear. Not this night, alone with you.”
“What is, then?”

===Chinese===
他蓦地收住，默默继续赶路。
“哦，”帕特轻叹，“怎么不念下去了，尼克？求你了。”
“不念了。今晚的情调不合这首诗，亲爱的。尤其是今晚，单独跟你在一起。”
“那什么才合？”

===Original===
“Nothing sentimental. Something lighter, something﻿—oh, Elizabethan. That’s it.”
“And what’s stopping you?”
“Lack of an available idea. Or﻿—wait. Listen a moment.” He began, this time in a tone of banter.

===Chinese===
“不来伤感的。来点轻快的，来点——哦，伊丽莎白时代的。就是它。”
“那还等什么？”
“一时没想出词儿。要么——等等。听好。”他又开了口，这次是打趣的调子。

===Original===
> “When mornings, you attire yourself
 

 For riding in the city,
 

 You’re such a lovely little elf,
 

 Extravagantly pretty!
 

 And when at noon you deign to wear
 

 The habit of the town,
 

 I cannot call to mind as fair
 

 A symphony in brown.
> “Then evenings, you blithely don
 

 A daintiness of white,
 

 To flash a very paragon
 

 Of lightsomeness﻿—and light!
 

 But when the rounds of pleasure cease,
 

 And you retire at night,
 

 The Godling on your mantelpiece
 

 Must know a fairer sight!”

===Chinese===
> “清晨你把自己穿戴停当，
骑马进城去，
你是个多可爱的小精灵，
俏得没道理！
正午你屈尊换上
进城的行头，
我想不起还有什么能比
这一袭棕色交响曲更秀。
> “入夜你轻快地披上
一身素雅的白，
闪亮出一个十足的典范——
轻盈，而且光彩！
可待欢场散尽，
你夜里卸妆安寝，
你壁炉架上的小神像
见到的才更是美景！”

===Original===
“Sweet!” laughed Pat. “But personal. And anyway, how do you know I’ve a godling on my mantel? Don’t you credit me with any modesty?”
“If you haven’t, you should have! The vision I mentioned ought to enliven even a statue.”
“Well,” said the girl, “I have one﻿—a jade Buddha, and with all the charms I flash before him nightly, he’s never batted an eyelash. Explain that!”
“Easily. He’s green with envy, and frozen with admiration, and struck dumb by wonder.”
“Heavens! I suppose I ought to be thankful you didn’t say he was petrified with fright!” Pat laughed. “Oh Nick,” she continued, in a voice gone suddenly dreamy, “this *is* marvelous, isn’t it? I mean our enjoying ourselves so completely, and our being satisfied to be so alone. Why, we’ve never even danced together.”
“So we haven’t. That’s a subterfuge we haven’t needed, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“甜！”帕特笑道，“就是太促狭。再说，你怎么知道我壁炉架上有座小神像？你就不许我有半点矜持？”
“没有也该有一座！我说的那副景象，连石像都能给它看活了。”
“嗯，”姑娘说，“我还真有一座——一尊玉佛；我天天晚上在他跟前卖弄万种风情，他连眼皮都没眨过一下。你解释解释！”
“好解释。他羡慕得发绿，倾倒得僵住，惊叹得说不出话。”
“天哪！想来我还该谢天谢地，你没说他是吓成了石头！”帕特笑起来。“哦，尼克，”她接着说，声音忽然变得梦一般，“这*真是*妙极了，是不是？我是说咱们这么快活，又这么安于两人独处。想想看，咱们连舞都没一起跳过。”
“可不是。那是咱们还用不上的借口，对吧？”

===Original===
“It is,” replied the girl, dropping her glossy gleaming black head against his shoulder. “And besides, it’s much more satisfactory to be held in your arms in private, instead of in the midst of a crowd, and sitting down, instead of standing up. But I should like to dance with you, Nick,” she concluded.
“We’ll go dancing, then, whenever you like.”
“You’re delightfully complaisant, Nick. But﻿—you’re puzzling.” She glanced up at him. “You’re so﻿—so reluctant. Here we’ve been driving an hour, and you haven’t tried to kiss me a single time, and yet I’m quite positive you care for me.”
“Lord, Pat!” he muttered. “You never need doubt that.”
“Then what is it? Are you so spiritual and ethereal, or is my attraction for you just sort of intellectual? Or﻿—are you afraid?” As he made no reply, she continued, “Or are those poems you spout about my physical charms just﻿—poetic license?”
“They’re not, and you know it!” he snapped. “You’ve a mirror, haven’t you? And other fellows than I have taken you around, haven’t they?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”姑娘答道，乌黑油亮的头靠上他的肩膀。“再说，让你搂在怀里，背着人比当着一大群人强，坐着比站着强，不知惬意多少。不过我还是愿意跟你跳一回舞，尼克。”她补了一句。
“那什么时候你想去，咱们就去跳。”
“你真是殷勤得可人，尼克。可是——你叫人捉摸不透。”她抬眼看他。“你怎么这么——这么放不开。咱们开了快一个钟头车，你一次也没想吻我，可我千真万确知道你喜欢我。”
“天哪，帕特！”他咕哝道，“这一点你永远不用怀疑。”
“那到底是怎么回事？你是太超凡、太空灵，还是我对你的吸引力只算得上精神层面的？还是——你害怕？”他没作声，她又接着说，“再不然，你那些吟咏我容貌的诗，只是——诗人的特许，随口夸张？”
“不是，这你自己清楚！”他厉声道，“你有镜子，不是吗？再说带你出去过的也不止我一个，对不对？”

===Original===
“Oh, I’ve been taken around! That’s what perplexes me about you, Nick. I’d think you were actually afraid of kissing me if it weren’t﻿—” Her voice trailed into silence, and she stared speculatively ahead at the ribbon of road that rolled steadily into the headlights’ glare.
She broke the interval of wordlessness. “What is it, Nick?” she resumed almost pleadingly. “You’ve hinted at something now and then. Please﻿—you don’t have to hesitate to tell me; I’m modern enough to forgive things past, entanglements, affairs, disgraces, or anything like that. Don’t you think I should know?”
“You’d know,” he said huskily, “if I could tell you.”
“Then there is something, Nick!” She pressed his arm against her. “Tell me, isn’t there?”
“I don’t know.” There was the suggestion of a groan in his voice.
“You don’t know! I can’t understand.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我被人带出去过的可多了！所以才叫我不懂，尼克。要不是——我准以为你真的怕吻我。”她的声音低了下去，没入沉默，她若有所思地望着前方，那条路像一条带子，源源不断地滚进车灯的光幕里。
她打破了这一阵无言。“到底是怎么回事，尼克？”她几乎是恳求地重新开口，“你时不时漏一点口风。求求你——不用吞吞吐吐的；我够新派，过去的事我都能原谅，纠葛也好、情史也好、丑闻也好，什么都行。你不觉得我该知道吗？”
“要是我能告诉你，”他沙哑地说，“你早就知道了。”
“那就是真有事，尼克！”她把他的手臂往自己身上按了按。“告诉我，是不是有？”
“我不知道。”他的声音里带着一丝呻吟似的东西。
“你不知道！我不明白。”

===Original===
“I can’t either. Please, Pat, let’s not spoil tonight; if I could tell you, I would. Why, Pat, I love you﻿—I’m terribly, deeply, solemnly in love with you.”
“And I with you, Nick.” She gazed ahead, where the road rose over the arch of a narrow bridge. The speeding car lifted to the rise like a zooming plane.
And suddenly, squarely in the center of the road, another car, until now concealed by the arch of the bridge, appeared almost upon them. There was a heart-stopping moment when a collision seemed inevitable, and Pat felt the arm against her tighten convulsively into a bar of steel. She heard her own sobbing gasp, and then, somehow, they had slipped unscathed between the other car and the rail of the bridge.
“Oh!” she gasped faintly, then with a return of breath, “That was nice, Nick!”
Beyond the bridge, the road widened once more; she felt the car slowing, edging toward the broad shoulder of the road.
“There was danger,” said her companion in tones as emotionless as the rasping of metal. “I came to save it.”

===Chinese===
“我也不明白。求你了，帕特，别让今晚扫兴；要是我能说，我一定说。帕特，你想想，我爱你——我狂热地、深深地、郑重地爱着你。”
“我也爱你，尼克。”她望着前方，公路在一座窄桥的拱背上隆起。疾驰的汽车跃上坡顶，像一架拉升的飞机。
突然，一辆汽车端端正正出现在路中央——它一直被桥拱挡着——眼看就要撞上。有那么一个令人心脏停跳的瞬间，相撞似乎已不可避免，帕特感到贴着她的那条手臂猛地收紧，硬成一根钢条。她听见自己抽泣般的一声喘息，然后，不知怎么的，他们已从那辆车和桥栏之间毫发无损地滑了过去。
“哦！”她虚弱地喘着，缓过气来后又说，“真带劲，尼克！”
过了桥，路又宽了起来；她感到车子在减速，朝路旁宽阔的路肩靠去。
“刚才有危险，”她的同伴用一种像金属刮擦般毫无感情的声调说，“我来救下它。”

===Original===
“Save what?” queried Pat as the car slid to a halt on the turf.
“Your body.” The tones were still cold, like grinding wheels. “The beauty of your body!”
He reached a thin hand toward her, suddenly seized her skirt and snatched it above the silken roundness of her knees. “There,” he rasped. “That is what I mean.”
“Nick!” Pat half-screamed in appalled astonishment. “How﻿—” She paused, shocked into abrupt silence, for the face turned toward her was but a remote, evil caricature of Nicholas Devine’s. It leered at her out of bloodshot eyes, as if behind the mask of Nick’s face peered a red-eyed demon.

===Chinese===
“救什么？”汽车滑进路边草地停住时，帕特问。
“你的身体。”那语调仍然冰冷，像砂轮打磨。“你这身体的美！”
他把一只瘦手伸向她，突然抓住她的裙子，猛地掀到她膝盖浑圆的丝袜以上。“瞧，”他哑声说，“我说的就是这个。”
“尼克！”帕特惊骇得半叫出来。“你怎么——”她顿住，震骇得戛然无声；转向她的那张脸，不过是尼古拉斯·德文面孔的一幅冷漠邪恶的漫画。那脸布满血丝的眼睛淫邪地睨着她，仿佛尼克的面具背后，正窥视着一个红眼的恶魔。

## XXVII Two in Hell / 二十七 同在地狱
===Original===
The cold hand against Pat was still; she felt it rigid and stiff on her flesh. She lay passive with closed eyes; having voiced her final appeal, she was through. The words torn from her misery represented the final iota of spirit remaining to her; and her bruised body and battered mind had nothing further to give.

The hand quivered and withdrew. For a moment more she lay motionless with her arms clutched about her, then she opened her eyes, gazing dully, hopelessly at the demon standing over her. He was watching her with a curious abstracted frown; as she stirred, the scowl intensified, and he drew back a step.

His face contorted suddenly in a spasm of some unguessable emotion. His fists clenched; a low unintelligible mutter broke from his lips. “Strange!” she heard him say, and after a moment, “I’m still master here!”

He *was* master; in a moment the emotion vanished, and he was again standing over her, his face the same impassive demoniac mask. She watched him in a dull stupor of despair that was too deep for even a whimper of pain as he wrenched at the elastic about her waist, and it cut into her flesh and parted. He tore the garment away, and the red eyes bored down with a wild elation in their depths.

===Chinese===
搭在帕特身上的那只冰冷的手不动了；她觉出它僵直僵硬地贴在自己的肌肤上。她闭着眼一动不动地躺着；最后的恳求已经出口，她再没有别的了。那几句从苦难里挤出来的话，耗尽了她残存的最后一丝心气；她那遍体鳞伤的身子和饱受摧折的心神，再也拿不出什么来了。

那只手颤了颤，缩了回去。她抱着双臂又僵卧了片刻，才睁开眼睛，怔怔地、绝望地望着俯身立于她之上的恶魔。他正蹙着眉出神地打量她，神情古怪；她一动，他的怒容更深了，退后了一步。

他的脸忽然扭曲起来，一阵猜不透什么情绪的痉挛掠过。他双拳紧握，唇间迸出一串含混的低语。“奇怪！”她听见他说，过了一会儿又说：“这里还是我说了算！”

他*确实*说了算；转眼间那阵情绪消失了，他又立在她上方，脸上仍是那副毫无表情的恶魔面具。他猛地去扯她腰间的松紧带，带子勒进她的皮肉，绷断开来，而她只在麻木的绝望昏沉里望着，那昏沉深得连一声痛哼都发不出来。他把那件衣物扯了下来，红色的眼睛俯逼下来，深处燃着狂乱的得意。

===Original===
“Mine!” the being muttered, a new hoarseness in his voice. “Are you mine?”

Pat made no answer; his voice croaked in more insistent tones. “Are you mine?”

She could not reply. She felt his fingers bite into the flesh of her shoulder. She was shaken roughly, violently, and the question came again, fiercely. The eyes flamed in command, and she felt through her languor and weakness, the stirring of that strange and unholy fascination that he held over her.

“Answer!” he croaked. “Are you mine?”

===Chinese===
“我的！”那生灵喃喃道，嗓音里多了一层新的沙哑。“你是我的吗？”

帕特没有回答；他的嗓音沙哑地、更加咄咄逼人地又问：“你是我的吗？”

她答不上来。她觉出他的手指抠进她肩头的皮肉。她被粗暴地、狠狠地摇晃着，那问题又凶狠地逼来。那双眼睛喷着威令的火光，透过自己的绵软无力，她感到那种他加诸于她的古怪而不圣洁的魅惑又在蠢蠢而动。

“回答！”他沙声道，“你是我的吗？”

===Original===
The torture of his searing grip on her shoulder wrung an answer from her.

“Yes,” she murmured faintly. “Yours.”

She closed her eyes again in helpless resignation. She felt the hand withdrawn, and she lay passive, waiting, on the verge of unconsciousness, numb, spirit-broken, and beaten.

===Chinese===
他扣住她肩膀的那只手灼痛如火，这份酷刑从她身上拧出了一个回答。

“是。”她微弱地喃喃道，“是你的。”

她又闭上眼睛，无可奈何地听天由命。她觉出那只手缩了回去，便一动不动地躺着，等待着，在昏迷的边缘，麻木了，心碎了，屈服了。

===Original===
Nothing happened. After a long interval she opened her eyes, and saw the other standing again with clenched fists and contorted countenance. His features were writhing in the intensity of his struggle; a strange low snarl came from his lips. He backed away from her, step by step; he leaned against the bookshelves, and beads of perspiration formed on his scowling face.

He was no longer master! She saw the change; imperceptibly the evil vanished from his features, and suddenly they were no longer his, but the weary, horror-stricken visage of her Nick! The red eyes were no longer satanic, but only the bloodshot, troubled, gentle eyes of her sweetheart, and the lips had lost their grimness, and gasped and quivered and trembled. He reeled against the wall, staggered to the chair at the table, and sank weakly into it.

Pat was far too exhausted, far too dazed, to feel anything but the faintest sensation of relief. She realized only dimly that tears were welling from her eyes, and that sharp sobs were shaking her. She was for the moment unable to stir, and it was not long until the being at the table turned stricken eyes on her that she moved. Then she drew her knees up before her, as if to hide her body behind their slim, chiffon-clad grace.

===Chinese===
什么也没有发生。过了很久，她睁开眼睛，看见那人又站在那里，双拳紧握，面容扭曲。他的五官在剧烈的搏斗中抽搐着；唇间发出一阵古怪的低吼。他一步一步地向后退；退到书架旁靠住，紧锁的眉头下渗出了汗珠。

他不再说了算了！她看见了这变化；那股邪气不知不觉地从他脸上消散，突然间，那张脸不再是他的了，而是她的尼克的、疲惫而惊骇的面容！那双红眼睛不再是撒旦式的，只是她的心上人那双充血的、忧惶的、温柔的眼睛；那两片嘴唇也卸去了狞厉，喘息着、哆嗦着、颤抖着。他踉跄着靠上墙，又跌跌撞撞地挪到桌边的椅子前，虚脱似的瘫坐了下去。

帕特已是筋疲力尽、神志恍惚，除了最微弱的一丝宽慰，什么也觉不出来了。她只是恍恍惚惚地意识到泪水正从眼里涌出，一阵阵抽泣震撼着她。她一时动弹不得，直到桌边那人把痛楚的目光转向她，她才动了动。随后她把双膝收到身前，仿佛想借那两条裹着雪纺绸的纤细秀腿，把自己的身子遮住。

===Original===
Nick rose from the table, approaching her with weary, hesitant tread. He seized a cover of some sort that was folded over the foot of the couch, shook it out and cast it over her. She clutched it about her body, sat erect and leaned back against the wall in utter exhaustion. Many minutes passed with no word from either of the occupants of the unholy chamber. It was Nick who broke the long silence.

“Pat,” he murmured in low tones. “Pat﻿—Dear. Are you﻿—all right?”

She stared at him dazedly without answer.

“Honey!” he said. “Honey! Tell me you’re all right!”

===Chinese===
尼克从桌边起身，迈着疲惫而迟疑的脚步走向她。他抓起叠在长沙发脚头的什么盖布，抖开，罩在她身上。她把它裹在身上，坐直了，精疲力竭地向后靠在墙上。好几分钟过去，这间邪祟屋子里的两个人谁也没有说话。是尼克先打破了这长长的沉默。

“帕特，”他低低地喃喃道，“帕特——亲爱的。你——还好吗？”

她怔怔地盯着他，没有回答。

“亲爱的！”他说，“亲爱的！告诉我你没事！”

===Original===
“All right?” she repeated uncomprehendingly. “Yes. I guess I’m all right.”

“Then go, Pat! Get away from here before he﻿—before anything happens! Put your clothes on and hurry away!”

“I can’t!” she said, faintly. “I﻿—can’t!”

“You must, Honey!”

===Chinese===
“没事？”她茫然不解地重复道，“嗯。我想我没事。”

“那就快走，帕特！趁他——趁还没出什么事，赶紧离开这儿！把衣服穿上，快跑！”

“我走不了！”她虚弱地说，“我——走不动！”

“你必须走，亲爱的！”

===Original===
“I’m just﻿—not able to. I will soon, Nick﻿—honest. When I﻿—when I get my breath back.”

“Pat!” There was anguish in the cry. “Oh, God﻿—Pat! We mustn’t ever be together again﻿—not ever!”

“No,” she said. A bit of sanity was returning to her; comprehension of her position sent a shudder through her. “No, we mustn’t.”

“I couldn’t bear another night like this﻿—watching! I’d go mad!”

===Chinese===
“我就是——动不了。马上就能走，尼克——真的。等我——等我把这口气缓过来。”

“帕特！”这一声喊里满是绞痛，“哦，上帝——帕特！我们再也不能相守了——永远不能！”

“不能。”她说。一缕清明正回到她身上；一想明白自己的处境，她便浑身打了个寒噤。“不能，我们不能再相守。”

“再来一夜这样的——眼睁睁看着！我受不了！我会疯的！”

===Original===
“Oh!” she choked, tears starting. “If you hadn’t come back, Nick!”

“I conquered him,” he said. “I don’t think I could do it again. It was your call that gave me the strength, Pat.” He shook his head as if bewildered. “He thought it was being in love with you that weakened me, but in the end it was that which gave me the strength to subdue him.”

“I’m scared!” said the girl suddenly. “Oh, Nick! I’m frightened!”

“You’d better go. You’d better dress and leave at once, Honey. Here.” He gathered her clothes from the floor, depositing them beside her on the couch. “There are pins in the tray on the table, Pat. Fix yourself up as well as you can, dear﻿—and hurry out of here!”

===Chinese===
“哦！”她哽咽道，眼泪夺眶而出，“要是你刚才没回来，尼克！”

“我战胜了他，”他说，“我想我再也做不到第二次了。是你的呼喊给了我力量，帕特。”他摇着头，像是还迷惑不解。“他以为爱上你是我软弱的原因，可到头来，恰恰是它给了我制服他的力量。”

“我怕！”姑娘突然说，“哦，尼克！我好害怕！”

“你最好走吧。你最好穿好衣服马上离开，亲爱的。喏。”他把她的衣服从地板上拾起来，放到她身旁的长沙发上。“别针在桌上的托盘里，帕特。亲爱的，尽量把自己收拾齐整——赶紧离开这儿！”

===Original===
He turned toward the door as if to leave, and a shock of terror shook her.

“Nick!” she cried. “Don’t go away! I’m more afraid when I can’t see you﻿—afraid that *he*﻿—” She broke off sobbing.

“All right, Honey. I’ll turn my back.”

She slipped out from under the blanket, found the pins, and repaired her ruined costume. The frock was torn, crushed and bedraggled; she pinned it together at the throat, though her trembling fingers made the task difficult. She pulled it on and took a tentative step toward the door.

===Chinese===
他转向门口，像是要走，一阵恐怖震动了她。

“尼克！”她叫道，“别走开！看不见你我更害怕——怕*他*——”她泣不成声，说不下去了。

“好，亲爱的。我把背转过去。”

她从毯子下面溜出来，找到别针，修整那件糟蹋得不成样子的衣裙。裙子撕裂了、揉皱了、凌乱不堪；她在领口处把它别拢，尽管手指发抖，干得很吃力。她把裙子穿上，朝门口试探地迈了一步。

===Original===
“Nick!” she called as a wave of dizziness sent her swaying against the wall.

“What’s the matter, Honey?” He turned anxiously at her cry.

“I’m dizzy,” she moaned. “My head aches, and﻿—I’m scared!”

“Pat, darling! You can’t go out alone like this﻿—and,” he added miserably, “I can’t take you!” He slipped his arm around her tenderly, supporting her to the couch. “Honey, what’ll we do?”

===Chinese===
“尼克！”她叫道，一阵眩晕猛地袭来，把她晃得靠在了墙上。

“怎么了，亲爱的？”听见她叫，他焦急地转过身来。

“我头晕，”她呻吟道，“头也疼，而且——我好怕！”

“帕特，宝贝！你这个样子没法一个人出去——可是，”他凄然添了一句，“我又不能送你！”他温柔地伸臂揽住她，扶她到长沙发上。“亲爱的，我们怎么办呢？”

===Original===
“I’ll be﻿—all right,” she murmured. “I’ll go in a moment.” The dizziness was leaving her; strength was returning.

“You must!” he said dolefully. “What a parting, Pat! Never to see you again, and then having this to remember as farewell!”

“I know, Nick. You see, I love you too.” She turned her dark, troubled eyes on him. “Honey, kiss me goodbye! We’ll have that to remember, anyway!” Tears were again on her cheeks.

“Do I dare?” he asked despondently. “After the things these lips of mine have said, and what these arms have done to you?”

===Chinese===
“我会——没事的，”她喃喃道，“我马上就走。”眩晕正在退去；力气正在回来。

“你必须走！”他哀声说，“这样的分别，帕特！从此再也见不着你，临了还只留下这么一幅景象当作诀别的纪念！”

“我知道，尼克。你要明白，我也爱你。”她把那双幽黑的、忧惶的眼睛转向他。“亲爱的，吻别我吧！至少我们还能留下这个作纪念！”泪珠又挂上了她的脸颊。

“我敢吗？”他颓然问道，“想想我这双嘴唇说过的话，想想这双手臂对你做过的事？”

===Original===
“But you didn’t, Nick! Could I blame you for﻿—that *other*?”

“God! You’re kind, Pat! Honey, if ever I win out in this battle, if ever I know I’m the final victor, I’ll﻿—No,” he said his tones dropping abruptly. “I’ll never come back to you, Pat. It’s far too dangerous, and﻿—can I ever be certain? Can I?”

“I don’t know, Nick. Can you?”

“I can’t be, Pat! I’ll never be sure that *he* isn’t just dormant, as he was before, waiting for my weakness to betray me! I’ll never be certain, Honey! It *has* to be goodbye!”

===Chinese===
“可那不是你，尼克！难道我能为——为*那另一个*怪你吗？”

“上帝！你真好，帕特！亲爱的，万一我打赢了这一仗，万一我知道自己是最后的胜利者，我就——不，”他说着，语调陡然沉了下去。“我永远不会回到你身边了，帕特。那太危险了，而且——我几时能拿得准呢？我能吗？”

“我不知道，尼克。你拿得准吗？”

“拿不准的，帕特！我永远无法确定*他*只是蛰伏着，像从前那样，只等我一软弱就出来出卖我！我永远拿不准，亲爱的！只能是永别了！”

===Original===
“Then kiss me!”

She clung to him; the room that had been so recently a chamber of horrors was transformed. As she held him, as her lips were pressed to his, she thought suddenly of the words of the demon, that Heaven and Hell were always the same place. They had taken on a new meaning, those words; she drew away from Nick and turned her tear-bright eyes tenderly on his.

“Honey,” she murmured, “I don’t want you to leave me. I don’t want you to go!”

“Nor do I want to, Pat! But I must.”

===Chinese===
“那就吻我吧！”

她偎依着他；刚才还是一间恐怖魔窟的屋子，如今变了模样。她抱着他，唇贴着他的唇，忽然想起恶魔的话——天堂和地狱永远是同一个地方。那句话有了新的意味；她从尼克身边退开些，把泪光晶亮的眼睛温柔地转向他。

“亲爱的，”她喃喃道，“我不要你离开我。我不要你走！”

“我也不想走，帕特！可是我必须走。”

===Original===
“You mustn’t! You’re to stay, and we’ll fight it out together﻿—be married, or any way that permits us to fight it through together.”

“Pat! Do you think I’d consent to that?”

“Nick,” she said. “Nick darling﻿—It’s worth it to me! I’m realizing it now; I thought it wasn’t﻿—but it is! I can’t lose you, Nick﻿—anything, even that *other*, is better than losing you.”

“You’re sweet, Pat! You know I’d trade my very soul for that, but﻿—No. I can’t do it! And don’t Honey, torture me by suggesting it again.”

===Chinese===
“你不许走！你要留下来，我们一起斗到底——结婚，或者用什么别的法子，只要能让我们并肩斗过去。”

“帕特！你以为我会答应吗？”

“尼克，”她说，“尼克，亲爱的——这对我来说值得！我现在明白了；我原以为不值得——可它值得！我不能失去你，尼克——什么都行，哪怕是*那另一个*，也比失去你强。”

“你真好，帕特！你知道为了那个我连自己的灵魂都肯拿出来换，可是——不。我不能这么做！亲爱的，也别再提这个来折磨我了。”

===Original===
“But I will, Nick!” She was speaking softly, earnestly. “You’re worth anything to me! If *he* should kill me, you’d still be worth it!” She gazed tenderly at him. “I’d want to die anyway without you!”

“No more than I without you,” he muttered brokenly. “But I won’t do it, Pat! I won’t do that to you!”

“I love you, Nick!” she said in a low voice. “I don’t want to live without you. Do you understand me, dear? I don’t want to live without you!”

===Chinese===
“可我偏要提，尼克！”她柔声而恳切地说着，“为了你什么都值得！就算*他*杀了我，你也照样值得！”她温柔地凝视着他。“没了你，我横竖也是不想活的！”

“没了你，我也一样，”他哽咽断续地低语，“可我不肯这么做，帕特！我不能那样待你！”

“我爱你，尼克！”她低声说，“没有你我不想活。你明白我的意思吗，亲爱的？没有你我不想活！”

===Original===
He stared at her somberly. “I’ve thought of that too,” he said. “Pat﻿—if I only believed that we’d be together after, together *anywhere*, I’d say yes. If only I believed there *were* an afterwards!”

“Doesn’t he prove that by his very existence?”

“Your Doctor would deny that.”

“Doctor Carl never saw *him*, Nick. And anyway, even oblivion together would be better than being separated, and far better than this!”

===Chinese===
他阴郁地凝视着她。“这个我也想过，”他说，“帕特——只要我相信我们死后还能在一起，*不管在哪里*在一起，我就答应。只要我相信死后*真有*一个以后！”

“他本身的存在，不就是证明吗？”

“你那位医生不会认账的。”

“卡尔医生从没见过*他*，尼克。再说，哪怕一同归于寂灭，也比生生分开强，更比这样强百倍！”

===Original===
He gazed at her silently. She spoke again. “That doesn’t frighten me, Nick. It’s only losing you that frightens me, especially the fear of losing you to *him*.”

He continued his silent gaze. Suddenly he drew her close to him, held her in a tight, tender embrace.

===Chinese===
他默默地凝视着她。她又开口了。“那吓不倒我，尼克。只有失去你才让我害怕，尤其是怕把你输给*他*。”

他仍旧默默地凝视着。突然，他把她拉近身边，紧紧地、温柔地把她搂在了怀里。

## XVIII Vanished / 十八 失踪
===Original===
“He doesn’t answer! I’m too late,” thought Pat disconsolately as she replaced the telephone. The cheerfulness with which she had awakened vanished like a patch of April sunshine. Now, with the failure of her third attempt in as many hours to communicate with Nicholas Devine, she was ready to confess defeat. She had waited too long. Despite Dr. Horker’s confidence in Mueller, she should have called last night﻿—at once.
“He’s gone!” she murmured distractedly. She realized now the impossibility of finding him. His solitary habits, his dearth of friends, his lonely existence, left her without the least idea of how to commence a search. She knew, actually, so little about him﻿—not even the source of the apparently sufficient income on which he subsisted. She felt herself completely at a loss, puzzled, lonesome, and disheartened. The futile buzzing of the telephone signal symbolized her frustration.
Perhaps, she thought, Dr. Horker might suggest something to do; perhaps, even, Mueller had reported Nick’s whereabouts. She seized the hope eagerly. A glance at her wristwatch revealed the time as ten-thirty; squarely in the midst of the Doctor’s morning office hours, but no matter. If he were busy she could wait. She rose, bounding hastily down the stairs.
She glimpsed her mother opening mail in the library, and paused momentarily at the door. Mrs. Lane glanced up as she appeared.

===Chinese===
“他不接电话！我来晚了。”帕特把电话放回去，黯然想道。她醒来时那股高兴劲儿，像四月天里的一片阳光，说散就散了。前后不过几个钟头，这已是她联系尼古拉斯·德文的第三次失败，她不得不认输了。她等得太久了。霍克医生虽然信得过缪勒，她昨晚就该打电话的——立刻就打。
“他走了！”她心烦意乱地喃喃道。她如今才明白，要找到他有多不可能。他那独来独往的习惯，他那寥寥无几的朋友，他孤零零的生活，让她连从哪儿开始找都毫无头绪。实际上她对他知道得那么少——甚至连他赖以为生的那笔看来颇为充裕的收入来自哪里都不知道。她感到全然无措，困惑、孤单、灰心。电话里徒然的嗡嗡声，正是她处处碰壁的写照。
也许，她想，霍克医生能出个主意；说不定缪勒已经打听到了尼克的下落。她急忙抓住这点希望。一看腕表，十点半；正卡在医生上午应诊的正中间，可那也顾不上了。他忙她就等。她起身，匆匆奔下楼去。
她瞥见母亲正在书房里拆信，便在门口顿了顿。她一露面，莱恩太太就抬起了眼。

===Original===
“Hello,” said the mother. “You’ve been on the telephone all morning, and what did Carl want of you last night?”
“Argument,” responded Pat briefly.
“Carl’s a gem! He’s been of inestimable assistance in developing you into a very charming and clever daughter, and Heaven knows what I’d have raised without him!”
“Cain, probably,” suggested Pat. She passed into the hall and out the door, blinking in the brilliant August sunshine. She crossed the strip of turf, picked her way through the break in the hedge, and approached the Doctor’s door. It was open; it often was in summer time, especially during his brief office hours. She entered and went into the chamber used as waiting room.

===Chinese===
“好啊，”母亲说，“你一上午都占着电话，昨晚卡尔又找你干什么？”
“拌嘴。”帕特简短地答。
“卡尔是块宝！把你调教成这么个又可爱又聪明的女儿，他功不可没；没了他，天知道我会养出个什么来！”
“多半是该隐。”帕特接了一句。她穿过门厅出了门，在明晃晃的八月阳光下眯起眼。她越过那片草地，寻着树篱的缺口穿过去，走到医生的门前。门开着；夏天里门常开着，尤其是他那不长的应诊时间。她走进去，进了用作候诊室的那间屋子。

===Original===
His office door was closed; the faint hum of his voice sounded. She sat impatiently in a chair and forced herself to wait.
Fortunately, the delay was nominal; it was but a few minutes when the door opened and an opulent, middle-aged lady swept past her and away. Pat recognized her as Mrs. Lowry, some sort of cousin of the Brock pair.
“Good morning!” boomed the Doctor. “Professional call, I take it, since you’re here during office hours.” He settled his great form in a chair beside her.
“He’s gone!” said Pat plaintively. “I can’t reach him.”

===Chinese===
他诊室的门关着；隐隐传来他说话的嗡嗡声。她不耐烦地坐在椅子里，强逼自己等。
所幸等得不久；不过几分钟门就开了，一位雍容的中年太太从她身边飘然而去。帕特认出那是洛瑞太太，布罗克家姐妹的哪门子表亲。
“早上好！”医生瓮声说，“应诊时间跑来，我猜是正经事。”他把高大的身躯安顿在她旁边的椅子里。
“他走了！”帕特哀声说，“我联系不上他。”

===Original===
“Humph!” grunted Horker helpfully.
“I’ve tried all morning﻿—he’s always home in the morning.”
“Listen, you little scatterbrain!” rumbled the Doctor. “Why didn’t you tell me Mueller brought you home last night? I thought he was on the job.”
“I didn’t think of it,” she wailed. “Nick said he’d have to make some preparations, and I never dreamed he’d skip away like this.”

===Chinese===
“哼！”霍克帮腔似的哼了一声。
“我试了一早上——他上午总在家。”
“听着，你这个小迷糊！”医生咕噜道，“昨晚是缪勒送你回家的，你怎么不告诉我？我还当他盯着呢。”
“我没想到啊，”她哀叫，“尼克说他得做些准备，我做梦也没想到他会这么一走了之。”

===Original===
“He must have gone home directly after you left him, and skipped out immediately,” said the Doctor ruminatively. “Mueller never caught up with him.”
“But what’ll we do?” she cried desperately.
“He can’t have gone far with no more preparation than this,” soothed Horker. “He’ll write you in a day or two.”
“He won’t! He said he wouldn’t. He doesn’t want me to know where he is!” She was on the verge of tears.

===Chinese===
“他准是把你一放下就直接回了家，马上就溜了，”医生寻思着说，“缪勒压根没追上他。”
“可我们怎么办呢？”她绝望地喊。
“就这么点准备，他走不远，”霍克宽慰道，“一两天他就会给你写信。”
“他不会的！他说过他不会写。他不想让我知道他在哪儿！”她快要哭出来了。

===Original===
“Now, now,” said the Doctor still in his soothing tones. “It isn’t as bad as all that.”
“Take off your bedside manner!” she snapped, blinking to keep back the tears. “It’s worse! What ever can we do? Dr. Carl,” she changed to a pleading tone, “can’t you think of something?”
“Of course, Pat! I can think of several things to do if you’ll quiet down for a moment or so.”
“I’m sorry, Dr. Carl﻿—but what *can* we do?”

===Chinese===
“好了，好了，”医生仍用安抚的调子说，“没那么糟。”
“收起你哄病人的那套吧！”她厉声道，眨着眼忍住泪，“比那还糟！我们到底能干什么？卡尔医生，”她换上恳求的调子，“你就不能想个法子？”
“当然能，帕特！只要你肯安静那么一小会儿，我能想出好几件事来。”
“对不起，卡尔医生——可我们*到底*能做什么？”

===Original===
“First, perhaps Mueller can trace him. That’s his business, you know.”
“But suppose he can’t﻿—what then?”
“Well, I’d suggest you write him a letter.”
“But I don’t know where to write!” she wailed. “I don’t know his address!”

===Chinese===
“首先，缪勒也许能查到他。那是他的本行，你知道。”
“可万一他查不到呢——那怎么办？”
“那我就建议你给他写封信。”
“可我不知道往哪儿写！”她哀叫，“我不知道他的地址！”

===Original===
“Be still a moment, scatterbrain! Address it to his last residence; you know that, don’t you? Of course you do. Now, don’t you suppose he’ll leave a forwarding address? He must receive some sort of mail about his income, or estate, or whatever he lives on. Your letter’ll find him, Honey; don’t you doubt it.”
“Oh, do you think so?” she asked, suddenly hopeful. “Do you really think so?”
“I really think so. You would too if you didn’t fly into a panic every time some little difficulty confronts you. Sometimes even my psychiatry is puzzled to explain how you can be so clever and so stupid, so self-reliant and so dependent, so capable and so helpless﻿—all at one and the same time. Your Nick can’t be as much of a paradox as you are!”
“I wonder if a letter *will* reach him,” she said eagerly, ignoring the Doctor’s remarks. “I’ll try. I’ll try immediately.”

===Chinese===
“安静一会儿，小迷糊！信寄到他上个住处；那个你知道吧？当然知道。你想，他难道不会留个转信地址？他的收入也好、产业也好、不管靠什么过活，总得收点邮件吧。你的信会找到他的，孩子；别犯嘀咕。”
“哦，您真这么想？”她问，忽然生出了希望，“您真这么想？”
“我真这么想。你要不是一碰到点小难处就慌了神，你也会这么想。有时候连我的精神病学都解释不了，你怎么能这么聪明又这么蠢，这么独立又这么依赖人，这么能干又这么没用——而且是同时。你那个尼克，悖论也悖不过你！”
“不知道这信*能不能*到他手上，”她急切地说，把医生的话当了耳旁风，“我要试试。马上就试。”

===Original===
“I sort of had a feeling you would,” said Horker amiably. “I hope you succeed; and not only for your sake, Pat, because God knows how this thing will work out. But I’m anxious to examine this youngster of yours on my own account; he must be a remarkable specimen to account for all the perturbation he’s managed to cause you. And this Jekyll-and-Hyde angle sounds interesting, too.”
“Jekyll and Hyde!” echoed Pat. “Dr. Carl, is that possible?”
“Not literally,” chuckled the other, “though in a sense, Stevenson anticipated Freud in his thesis that liberating the evil serves also to release the good.”
“But﻿—It was a drug that caused that change in the story, wasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“我料到你会的，”霍克和气地说，“我希望你成功；也不光是为了你，帕特，因为这桩事会怎么收场只有天知道。不过我私心也想检查检查你这位小伙子；能把你搅成这样，他必是个人物。杰基尔与海德这个路子，听着也怪有意思的。”
“杰基尔与海德！”帕特附和道，“卡尔医生，有这种可能吗？”
“照字面讲没有，”对方轻笑道，“不过从某种意义上说，‘释放了恶也就解放了善’这个论点，斯蒂文森倒是抢在弗洛伊德前头了。”
“可是——书里引起那变化的是一种药，对吧？”

===Original===
“Well? Do you suspect your friend of being addicted to some mysterious drug? Is that the latest hypothesis?”
“*Is* there such a drug? One that could change a person’s character?”
“*All* alkaloids do that, Honey. Some of them stimulate, some depress, some breed frenzies, and some give visions of delight﻿—but all of them influence one’s mental and emotional organization, which you call character. So for that matter, does a square meal, or a cup of coffee, or even a rainy day.”
“But isn’t there a drug that can separate good qualities from evil, like the story?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样？你疑心你朋友上了什么神秘药物的瘾？这是最新的假说？”
“*到底*有没有这种药？能改变一个人性格的药？”
“*所有*生物碱都能，孩子。有的提神，有的镇静，有的令人癫狂，有的让人看见极乐幻境——但它们全都会影响人的心理和情绪组织，也就是你所说的性格。认真讲起来，一顿饱饭、一杯咖啡，甚至一个雨天，也都这样。”
“可就没有一种药，能像书里那样把善和恶分开吗？”

===Original===
“Emphatically not, Pat! That’s not the trouble with this pesky boyfriend of yours.”
“Well,” said the girl doubtfully, “I only wish I had as much faith in your psychologies as you have. If you brain-doctors know it all, why do you switch theories every year?”
“We *don’t* know it all. On the other hand, there are a few things to be said in our favor.”
“What are they?”

===Chinese===
“断然没有，帕特！你这位磨人的男朋友，毛病不出在这儿。”
“唉，”姑娘将信将疑地说，“我只盼我对你们这些心理学，能有你们自己那么大的信心。你们脑科医生既然全懂，干吗年年换理论？”
“我们*并没有*全懂。不过话说回来，有几条还是替我们说得上的。”
“哪几条？”

===Original===
“For one,” replied the Doctor, “we do cure people occasionally. You’ll admit that.”
“Sure,” said Pat. “So did the Salem witches﻿—occasionally.” She gave him a suddenly worried look. “Oh, Dr. Carl, don’t think I’m not grateful! You know how much I’m hoping from your help, but I’m miserably anxious over all this.”
“Never mind, Honey. You’re not the first one to point out the shortcomings of the medical profession. That’s a game played by plenty of physicians too.” He paused at the sound of footsteps on the porch, followed by the buzz of the doorbell. “Run along and write your letter, dear﻿—here comes that Tuesday hypochondriac of mine, and he’s rich enough for my careful attention.”

===Chinese===
“头一条，”医生答道，“我们偶尔确实治得好人。这你认吧。”
“认，”帕特说，“塞勒姆的女巫们——偶尔也治得好。”她忽然投去一个忧心忡忡的眼神。“哦，卡尔医生，别以为我不领情！您知道我多盼着您的帮忙，可这一切实在让我焦心得不行。”
“别在意，孩子。指出医疗行当的短处，你不是头一个。这游戏不少医生自己也玩。”门廊上响起脚步声，接着门铃嗡的一响，他顿住。“去吧，亲爱的，写你的信去——我那位星期二疑病症来了，他有钱得很，值得我细心伺候。”

===Original===
Pat flashed him a quick smile of farewell and slipped quietly into the hall. At the door she passed the Doctor’s patient﻿—a lean, elderly gentleman of woebegone visage﻿—and returned to her own home.
Her spirits, mercurial to a degree, had risen again. She was suddenly positive that the Doctor’s scheme would bring results, and she darted into the house almost buoyantly. Her mother had abandoned the desk, and she ensconced herself before it, finding paper and pen, and staring thoughtfully at the blank sheet.
Finally she wrote.

===Chinese===
帕特朝他飞快一笑算是道别，悄悄溜进门厅。在门口她和医生的病人擦肩而过——一位清瘦的老人家，一脸愁苦——然后回了自家。
她那喜怒无常的性子，这会儿又高涨起来。她忽然笃定医生的办法定能见效，几乎脚不点地地窜进屋里。母亲已经离开了书桌，她一屁股坐到桌前，找出纸笔，对着白纸出了会儿神。
终于，她落笔了。

===Original===
> “Dear Nick﻿—
> “Something has happened, favorable, I think, to us. I believe I have found the help we need.
> “Will you come if you can, or if that’s not possible, break that self-given promise of yours, and communicate with me?
> “I love you.”

She signed it simply “Pat,” placed it in an envelope, addressed it hastily, and hurried out to post it. On her return she spied the Doctor’s hypochondriac in the act of leaving. He walked past her with his lean, worry-smitten face like a study of Hogarth, and she heard him mumbling to himself. The elation went out of her; she mounted the steps very soberly, and went miserably inside.

===Chinese===
> “亲爱的尼克——
> “出了一件事，我想，对我们是好事。我相信我找到了我们需要的援手。
> “你能来就来；来不了的话，就破了你自己许的那个愿，跟我通个信，好不好？”
> “我爱你。”
她只签了个“帕特”，装进信封，匆匆写上地址，赶忙出去投寄。回来时，她撞见医生那位疑病症病人正往外走。他从她身边走过，清瘦的脸上满是愁苦，活像一幅贺加斯的人物习作，她还听见他喃喃自语。她的欢快劲儿一下子没了；她神情肃然地走上台阶，闷闷不乐地进了门。

## XXIII Werewolf / 二十三 狼人
===Original===
Pat awoke in rather better spirits. Somehow, the actual entrance of Dr. Horker into the case gave her a feeling of security, and her natural optimistic nature rode the pendulum back from despair to hope. Even the painful black-and-blue mark on her arm, as she examined it ruefully, failed to shake her buoyant mood.
Her mood held most of the day; it was only at evening that a recurrence of doubt assailed her. She sat in the dim living room waiting the arrival of her mother’s guests, and wondered whether, after all, the predicament was as easily solvable as she had assumed. She watched the play of lights and shadows across the ceiling, patterns cast through the windows by moving headlights in the street, and wondered anew whether her faith in Dr. Carl’s abilities was justified. Science! She had the faith of her generation in its omnipotence, but here in the dusk, the outworn superstitions of childhood became appalling realities, and some of Magda’s stories, forgotten now for years, rose out of their graves and went squeaking and maundering like sheeted ghosts in a ghastly parade across the universe of her mind. The meaningless taunts she habitually flung at Dr. Carl’s science became suddenly pregnant with truth; his patient, hard-learned science seemed in fact no more than the frenzies of a witch-doctor dancing in the heart of a Rhodesian swamp.
What was it worth﻿—this array of medical facts﻿—if it failed to cure? Was medicine falling into the state of Chinese science﻿—a vast collection of good rules for which the reasons were either unknown or long forgotten? She sighed; it was with a feeling of profound relief that she heard the voices of the Brocks outside; she played miserable bridge the whole evening, but it was less of an affliction than the solitude of her own thoughts.

===Chinese===
帕特醒来时情绪好了不少。不知怎么，霍克医生真的插手这件事，给了她一种安全感，她天生的乐观性情便随着钟摆从绝望荡回了希望。就连胳膊上那块乌青的伤痛处，她懊恼地察看时，也没能动摇她那轻快的心情。
这情绪维持了大半天；直到傍晚，疑虑才又卷土重来。她坐在昏暗的客厅里等母亲的客人们到来，心里琢磨着，这困境到底是不是她原以为的那样容易解决。她望着天花板上光影的变幻——街上移动的车灯透过窗户投下的图案——又重新怀疑起自己对卡尔医生本事的那份信心是否靠得住。科学！她有着她这代人对科学万能的信仰，可在这暮色里，童年那些陈旧的迷信变成了骇人的现实，玛格达讲过的几个故事，多年不曾想起，也从坟墓里爬了出来，像一群裹着尸布的鬼魂，在她心宇中排成一列阴森的游行队伍，尖声吱叫、语无伦次地游荡。她平日掷向卡尔医生那套科学的那些无谓讥嘲，忽然句句都应验成了真；他那门耐心苦学来的科学，看去竟不比在罗得西亚沼泽腹地手舞足蹈的巫医的狂态高明多少。
要是治不了病，这一大堆医学事实又值什么？医学是不是正落入中国科学的境地——一大堆好规矩，道理却要么无人知晓、要么早被忘光？她叹了口气；听见外面布罗克家姐妹的声音，她深深松了口气；她打了一晚上憋闷的桥牌，可这总比自己独个儿陪着心思受罪好受些。

===Original===
Saturday morning, cloudy and threatening though it was, found the pendulum once more at the other end of the arc. She found herself, if not buoyantly cheerful, at least no longer prey to the inchoate doubts and fears of the preceding evening. She couldn’t even recall their nature; they had been apart from the cool, daytime logic that preached a commonsense reliance on accepted practices. They had been, she concluded, no more than childish nightmares induced by darkness and the play of shadows.
She dressed and ate a late breakfast; her mother was already en route to the Club for her bridge-luncheon. Thereafter, she wandered into the kitchen for the company of Magda, whom she found with massive arms immersed in dishwater. Pat perched on her particular stool beside the kitchen table and watched her at her work.
“Magda,” she said finally.

===Chinese===
星期六早晨，天虽阴沉沉地酝酿着雨，钟摆却又荡到了弧线的另一头。她发现自己虽说不上欢快，至少不再被前一晚那些乱糟糟的疑虑和恐惧折磨了。她连它们的底细都想不起来了；它们本就游离于白天冷静的逻辑之外——那套逻辑宣扬的，是对现成办法的常识性信赖。她断定，它们不过是黑暗和光影的把戏催出来的小孩子噩梦罢了。
她穿好衣裳，吃了顿很晚的早饭；母亲已经动身去俱乐部吃桥牌午餐了。随后她踱进厨房找玛格达作伴，见她正把两条粗壮的胳膊泡在洗碗水里。帕特爬上厨房桌边她专用的那张凳子，看着她干活。
“玛格达，”她终于开口了。

===Original===
“I’m listening, Miss Pat.”
“Do you remember a story you told me a long time ago? Oh, years and years ago, about a man in your town who could change into something﻿—some fierce animal. A wolf, or something like that.”
“Oh, him!” said Magda, knitting her heavy brows. “You mean the werewolf.”
“That’s it! The werewolf. I remember it now﻿—how frightened I was after I went to bed. I wasn’t more than eight years old, was I?”

===Chinese===
“我听着呢，帕特小姐。”
“你还记得很久以前你给我讲过的一个故事吗？哦，好多年好多年以前，说你们镇上有个人能变成个什么——什么凶猛的野兽。一只狼，或是那一类的东西。”
“哦，他呀！”玛格达说着，拧起两道浓眉，“你说的是狼人吧。”
“就是它！狼人。我想起来了——那时候我上了床吓得要命。我那时顶多八岁，是不是？”

===Original===
“I couldn’t remember. It was years ago, though, for sure.”
“What was the story?” queried Pat. “Do you remember that?”
“Why, it was the time the sheep were being missed,” said the woman, punctuating her words with the clatter of dishes on the drainboard. “Then there was a child gone, and another, and then tales of this great wolf about the country. I didn’t see him; us little ones stayed under roof by darkness after that.”
“That wasn’t all of it,” said Pat. “You told me more than that.”

===Chinese===
“我记不清啦。不过那可真是好多年前的事了。”
“那故事是怎么讲的？”帕特问，“你还记得吗？”
“嗐，就是丢羊那阵子，”女人说，一句一响地把碗碟磕在沥水板上，“跟着丢了一个孩子，又丢了一个，后来乡下就传开了那头大狼的事。我没见过它；打那以后，天一黑我们小孩儿就不出屋了。”
“不止这些吧，”帕特说，“你讲的比这多。”

===Original===
“Well,” continued Magda, “there was my uncle, who was best hand with a rifle in the village. He and others went after the creature, and my uncle, he came back telling how he’d seen it plain against the sky, and how he’d fired at it. He couldn’t miss, he was that close, but the wolf gave him a look and ran away.”
“And then what?”
“Then the Priest came, and he said it wasn’t a natural wolf. He melted up a silver coin and cast a bullet, and he gave it to my uncle, he being the best shot in the village. And the next night he went out once more.”
“Did he get it?” asked Pat. “I don’t remember.”

===Chinese===
“后来，”玛格达接着讲，“我叔叔是村里使枪使得最好的。他和另外几个人去追那畜生，我叔回来说，他看得清清楚楚，它衬在天幕上，他还朝它开了枪。那么近，他不可能打不中，可那狼瞪了他一眼，跑了。”
“后来呢？”
“后来神甫来了，说那不是寻常的狼。他熔了一枚银币，铸成一颗子弹，交给我叔叔，因为他是村里最好的枪手。第二天夜里他又出去了。”
“他打中了吗？”帕特问，“我不记得了。”

===Original===
“He did. He came upon it by the pasture, and he aimed his gun. The creature looked straight at him with its evil red eyes, and he shot it. When he came to it, there wasn’t a wolf at all, but this man﻿—his name I forget﻿—with a hole in his head. And then the Priest, he said he was a werewolf, and only a silver bullet could kill him. But my uncle, *he* said those evil red eyes kept staring at him for many nights.”
“Evil red eyes!” said Pat suddenly. “Magda,” she asked in a faint voice, “could he change any time he wanted to?”
“Only by night, the Priest said. By sunrise he had to be back.”
“Only by night!” mused the girl. Another idea was forming in her active little mind, another conception, disturbing, impossible to phrase. “Is that worse than being possessed by a devil, Magda?”

===Chinese===
“打中了。他在牧场上撞见它，端起了枪。那畜生用它那双邪恶的红眼睛直勾勾地瞪着他，他开了枪。等他走到跟前，那儿根本没有什么狼，只有这么一个人——名字我忘了——脑袋上一个洞。于是神甫说，他是狼人，只有银子弹杀得死他。可我叔叔，*他*说那双邪恶的红眼睛，后来许多夜里都一直瞪着他。”
“邪恶的红眼睛！”帕特突然说。“玛格达，”她声音发虚地问，“他想变就能随时变吗？”
“只有夜里才行，神甫说的。日出以前他必须变回来。”
“只有夜里！”姑娘沉吟道。她那活跃的小脑袋里又有个念头在成形，又一种构想，叫人心神不宁，又没法说出口。“那比被魔鬼附体还糟吗，玛格达？”

===Original===
“Sure it’s worse! The Priest, he could cast out the devil, but I never heard no cure for being a werewolf.”
Pat said nothing further, but slid from her high perch to the floor and went soberly out of the kitchen. The fears of last night had come to life again, and now the overcast skies outside seemed a fitting symbol to her mood. She stared thoughtfully out of the living room windows, and the sudden splash of raindrops against the pane lent a final touch to the whole desolate ensemble.
“I’m just a superstitious little idiot!” she told herself. “I laugh at Mother because she always likes to play North and South, and here I’m letting myself worry over superstitions that were discarded before there was any such thing as a game called contract bridge.”

===Chinese===
“当然更糟！神甫还能把魔鬼赶出去，可狼人这事，我从来没听说过有什么解法。”
帕特没再说什么，从高凳上溜下来，神色凝重地走出了厨房。昨夜的恐惧又活了过来，此刻外面阴沉的天色，恰是她心境的写照。她若有所思地盯着客厅窗外，雨点骤然打上玻璃，给这一派萧索补上了最后一笔。
“我就是个迷信的小傻瓜！”她对自己说，“我还笑话妈妈打牌总爱坐南北，自己倒在这儿为一些迷信发愁——这些迷信被扔进垃圾堆的时候，连定约桥牌这么个玩法都还没影儿呢。”

===Original===
But her arguments failed to carry conviction. The memory of the terrible eyes of that *other* had clicked too aptly to Magda’s phrase. She couldn’t subdue the picture that haunted her, and she couldn’t cast off the apprehensiveness of her mood. She recalled gloomily that Dr. Horker was at the Club﻿—wouldn’t be home before evening, else she’d have gladly availed herself of his solid, matter-of-fact company.
She thought of Nick’s appointment with the Doctor for that evening. Suppose his psychoanalysis brought to light some such horror as these fears of hers﻿—that would forever destroy any possibility of happiness for her and Nick. Even though the Doctor refused to recognize it, called it by some polysyllabic scientific name, the thing would be there to sever them.
She wandered restlessly into the hall. The morning mail, unexamined, lay in its brazen receptacle, she moved over, fingering it idly. Abruptly she paused in astonishment﻿—a letter in familiar script had flashed at her. She pulled it out; it was! It was a letter from Nicholas Devine!

===Chinese===
可她这套道理说服不了自己。那*另一个*的可怕眼睛的记忆，跟玛格达的话扣得太严丝合缝了。她压不住萦绕心头的那幅画面，也甩不掉满心的忧惧。她闷闷地想起霍克医生去了俱乐部——天黑前不会回家，不然她倒乐意去沾沾他那种扎实、讲实际的陪伴。
她想到尼克今晚和医生的约。万一他的精神分析查出来的，正是像她这些恐惧一样的骇人东西——那就永远毁掉她跟尼克幸福的任何可能。哪怕医生不肯承认，给它安上某个多音节的科学名目，那东西也会横在那儿，把他们俩分开。
她心神不定地踱进门厅。早晨的邮件还没人动过，躺在黄铜信箱里；她走过去，懒懒地用手指翻着。蓦地她惊得顿住了——一封笔迹熟稔的信跳进了眼里。她抽出来；没错！是尼古拉斯·德文的信！

===Original===
She tore it open nervously, wondering whether he had reverted to his original refusal of Dr. Horker’s aid, whether he was unable to come, whether *that* had happened. But only a single unfolded sheet slipped from the envelope, inscribed with a few brief lines of poetry.

> “The grief that is too faint for tears,
 

 And scarcely breathes of pain,
 

 May linger on a hundred years
 

 Ere it creep forth again.
 

 But I, who love you now too well
 

 To suffer your disdain,
 

 Must try tonight that love to quell﻿—
 

 And try in vain!”

===Chinese===
她紧张地撕开信，猜着他是不是又变了卦、回绝了霍克医生的帮助，是不是来不了，是不是——*那种事*发生了。可信封里只滑出一张没折叠的信笺，上面题着短短几行诗。

> “那悲伤淡得流不出眼泪，
 

 也几乎哼不出一声痛，
 

 尽可以蛰伏上一百年，
 

 才再度悄悄爬出。
 

 可我，如今爱你爱得太深，
 

 受不了你的轻慢，
 

 今夜只得把这份爱掐熄——
 

 掐熄也是徒然！”

## XVII Witch-Doctor / 十七 巫医
===Original===
Pat had no intentions, however, of following the famous highway that evening. She stamped angrily down the Doctor’s steps, swished her way through the break in the hedge with small regard to the safety of her sheer hose, and mounted to her own porch. She found her key, opened the door and entered.
As she ascended the stairs, her fit of temper at the Doctor passed, and she felt lonely, weary, and unutterably miserable. She sank to a seat on the topmost step and gave herself over to bitter reflections.
Nick was gone! The realization came poignantly at last; there would be no more evening rides, no more conversations whose range was limited only by the scope of the universe, no more breathtaking kisses, the sweeter for his reluctance. She sat mournfully silent, and considered the miserable situation in which she found herself.

===Chinese===
然而那天晚上，帕特全无心思去走那条出名的康庄大道。她气冲冲地踏下医生门前的台阶，窸窸窣窣地穿过树篱的缺口，全不顾她那薄丝袜的死活，登上自家的门廊。她找出钥匙，开了门，走进去。
她上楼的时候，对医生的那阵脾气过去了，只觉得孤单、疲惫、说不出的凄苦。她颓然坐上最高一级台阶，任自己陷进苦涩的沉思里。
尼克走了！这个认识终于尖锐地袭来；再没有傍晚的驾车兜风，再没有那些话题只以宇宙范围为界的交谈，再没有那令人窒息的吻——因他的羞怯而愈发甘甜。她悲戚地默坐着，思量自己陷入的这悲惨处境。

===Original===
In love with a madman! Or worse﻿—in love with a demon! With a being half of whose nature worshiped her while the other half was bent on her destruction! Was anyone, she asked herself﻿—was anyone, anywhere, ever in a more hopeless predicament?
What could she do? Nothing, she realized, save sit helplessly aside while Nick battled the thing to a finish. Or possibly﻿—the only alternative﻿—take him as he was, chance the vicissitudes of his unstable nature, lay herself open to the horrors she had glimpsed so recently, and pray for her fortunes to point the way of salvation. And in the mood in which she now found herself, that seemed infinitely the preferable solution. Yet rationally she knew it was impossible; she shook her head despondently, and leaned against the wall in abject misery.
Then, thin and sharp sounded the shrill summons of the door bell, and a moment later, the patter of the maid’s footsteps in the hall below. She listened idly to distract herself from the chain of despondency that was her thoughts, and was mildly startled to recognize the booming drums of Dr. Horker’s voice. She heard his greeting and the muffled reply from the group, and then a phrase understandable because of his sonorous tones.

===Chinese===
爱上一个疯子！或者更糟——爱上一个恶魔！爱上这样一个生灵：他一半的天性崇拜着她，另一半却处心积虑要毁掉她！她问自己——古往今来，天下四方，可有人陷过比这更绝望的境地？
她能做什么呢？什么也做不了，她明白，只能无助地坐在一旁，看尼克和那东西拼个了局。再不然——唯一的另一条路——就是照他现在的样子接受他，赌一赌他那不稳定天性的阴晴变幻，把自己敞开给不久前才窥见过的那些恐怖，再祈求命运指给她得救的路。而以她此刻的心境，那条路反倒显得无限可取。可理性告诉她这不可能；她垂头丧气地摇摇头，悲惨万分地靠在墙上。
这时，门铃尖利的召唤细细地、刺耳地响起，片刻之后，楼下门厅里响起女仆轻快的脚步声。她漫不经心地听着，好把自己从那串灰暗的念头里岔开，忽而听出霍克医生那擂鼓般的嗓音，不由得微微一惊。她听见他的问好和屋里人含混的应答，接着是一句话——他嗓音洪亮，听得真切。

===Original===
“Where’s Pat?” The words drifted up the well of the stairs, followed by a scarcely audible reply from her mother. Heavy footfalls on the carpeted steps, and then his figure bulked on the landing below her. She cupped her chin on her hands, and stared down at him while he ascended to her side, sprawling his great figure beside her.
“Pat, Honey,” he rumbled, “you’re beginning to get me worried!”
“Am I?” Her voice was weary, dull. “I’ve had myself like that for a long time.”

===Chinese===
“帕特呢？”这句话顺着楼梯井飘上来，跟着是她母亲一声几不可闻的回答。铺毯的台阶上响起沉重的脚步，他的身影随即出现在她下面的楼梯平台上。她双手托着下巴，俯视着他一级级上来，到她身边，把高大的身躯摊开坐下。
“帕特，孩子，”他瓮声瓮气地说，“你都快让我担心起来了！”
“是吗？”她的嗓音疲惫而呆滞。“我让自己这么担心，可不是一天两天了。”

===Original===
“Poor kid! Are you really so miserable over this Nick problem of yours?”
“I love him.”
“Yes.” He looked at her with sympathy and calculation mingling in his expression. “I believe you do. I’m sorry, Honey; I didn’t realize until now what he means to you.”

===Chinese===
“可怜的孩子！为你这桩尼克难题，你真这么难受吗？”
“我爱他。”
“是啊。”他望着她，神情里怜悯和盘算兼而有之。“我相信你爱他。我很抱歉，孩子；直到现在我才明白他对你意味着什么。”

===Original===
“You don’t realize now,” she murmured, still with the weary intonation.
“Perhaps not, Pat, but I’m learning. If you’re in this thing as deeply as all that, I’m in too﻿—to the finish. Want me?”
She reached out her hand, plucking at his coatsleeve. Abruptly she leaned toward him, burying her face against the rough tweed of his suit; she sobbed a little, while he patted her gently with his great, delicately fingered hand. “I’m sorry, Honey,” he rumbled. “I’m sorry.”

===Chinese===
“您现在也还没明白。”她喃喃道，仍是那疲惫的语调。
“也许还没有，帕特，可我在学。你既然陷得这么深，我也入伙——奉陪到底。要不要我？”
她伸出手，扯住他的衣袖。冷不防她朝他偎过去，把脸埋进他粗花呢西装的糙面上，低低啜泣起来；他用那只手指灵巧的大手轻轻拍着她。“对不起，孩子，”他瓮声说，“对不起。”

===Original===
The girl drew herself erect and leaned back against the wall, shaking her head to drive the tears from her eyes. She gave the Doctor a wan little smile.
“Well?” she asked.
“I’ll return your compliment of the other night,” said Horker briskly. “I’ll ask a few questions﻿—purely professional, of course.”

===Chinese===
姑娘直起身子，靠着墙坐好，摇了摇头，把眼泪甩出眼眶。她朝医生惨然一笑。
“怎么样？”她问。
“礼尚往来，我也回敬你前两天晚上那一套，”霍克爽利地说，“我要问几个问题——当然，纯属业务。”

===Original===
“Fire away, Dr. Carl.”
“Good. Now, when our friend has one of these﻿—uh﻿—attacks, is he rational? Do his utterances seem to follow a logical thought sequence?”
“I﻿—think so.”

===Chinese===
“尽管问吧，卡尔医生。”
“好。那么，咱们这位朋友犯这种——呃——毛病的时候，他理智吗？他的话听上去合乎逻辑的思维顺序吗？”
“我——觉得是。”

===Original===
“In what way does he differ from his normal self?”
“Oh, every way,” she said with a tremor. “Nick’s kind and gentle and sensitive and﻿—and naive, and this﻿—other﻿—is cruel, harsh, gross, crafty, and horrible. You can’t imagine a greater difference.”
“Um. Is the difference recognizable instantly? Could you ever be in doubt as to which phase you were encountering?”

===Chinese===
“他和平时的自己有什么不同？”
“哦，哪儿都不同，”她打着颤说，“尼克善良、温柔、敏感，还——还很天真，而这个——另一个——残忍、粗暴、下作、狡诈，而且可怕。你想不出还有更大的差别了。”
“唔。这差别一眼就认得出来吗？你可曾拿不准自己面对的是哪一副？”

===Original===
“Oh, no! I can﻿—well, sort of dominate Nick, but the other﻿—Lord!” She shuddered again. “I felt like a terrified child in the presence of some powerful, evil god.”
“Humph! Perhaps the god’s name was Priapus. Well, we’ll discount your feelings, Pat, because you weren’t exactly in the best condition for﻿—let’s say *sober* judgment. Now about this story of his. What happens to his own personality when this other phase is dominant? Did he say?”
“Yes. He said his own self was compelled to sort of stand by while the﻿—the intruder used his voice and body. He knew the thoughts of the other, but only when it was dominant. The rest of the time he couldn’t tell its thoughts.”

===Chinese===
“哦，不会！我能——嗯，多少能支配尼克，可那另一个——天哪！”她又打了个寒噤。“我就像个吓坏了的小孩，站在某个强大而邪恶的神面前。”
“哼！那神没准叫普里阿波斯。好了，你的感受我们打个折扣，帕特，因为你的状态实在说不上最适合——就说是*清醒*的判断吧。再说说他讲的这套。那另一副占上风的时候，他自己的个性怎么样？他说过吗？”
“说过。他说他自己只能被迫在一边干看着，任那个——那个闯入者——用他的嗓子和身体。那一个的心思他知道，可只在它占上风的时候。其余时候，他摸不透它的念头。”

===Original===
“And how long has he suffered from these﻿—intrusions?”
“As long as he can remember. As a child he was blamed for the other’s mischief, and when he tried to explain, people thought he was lying to escape punishment.”
“Well,” observed the Doctor, “I can see how they might think that.”

===Chinese===
“那他受这种——侵扰，有多久了？”
“记事以来就有。小时候那一个闯了祸，挨骂的是他，他想解释，人们只当他撒谎耍赖，想逃脱责罚。”
“这个嘛，”医生评论道，“我看他们那么想也不奇怪。”

===Original===
“Don’t you believe it?”
“I don’t exactly disbelieve it, Honey. The human mind plays queer tricks sometimes, and this may be one of its little jokes. It’s a psychiatrist’s business to investigate such things, and to painlessly remove the point of the joke.”
“Oh, if you only can, Dr. Carl! If you only can!”

===Chinese===
“您不信他的话？”
“我也不是不信，孩子。人的头脑有时会玩些古怪的花样，这兴许是它的一则小玩笑。精神病医生的职责就是查究这类事，再把那玩笑的锋芒不痛不痒地拔掉。”
“哦，您要是真能做到，卡尔医生！要是真能做到！”

===Original===
“We’ll see.” He patted her hand comfortingly.
“Now, you say the kind, gentle, and all that, phase is the normal one. Is that usually dominant?”
“Yes. Nick can master the other, or could until recently. He says this last﻿—attack﻿—is the worst he’s ever had; the other has been gaining strength.”

===Chinese===
“走着瞧吧。”他安慰地拍拍她的手。
“那么，你说那善良啦、温柔啦等等的那一副是常态。平常都是它占上风吗？”
“是的。尼克能镇住那另一个，至少到不久前还能。他说这一回——这次发作——是他经历过的最凶的一次；那一个的力气一直在长。”

===Original===
“Strange!” mused the Doctor. “Well,” he said with a smile of encouragement, “I’ll have a look at him.”
“Do you think you can help?” Pat asked anxiously. “Have you any idea what it is?”
“It isn’t a devil, at any rate,” he smiled.

===Chinese===
“怪哉！”医生沉吟道。“好吧，”他带着鼓励的笑容说，“我会见见他。”
“您觉得帮得上忙吗？”帕特焦急地问。“您知道那是什么吗？”
“无论如何，总不是魔鬼。”他微笑道。

===Original===
“But have you any idea?”
“Naturally I have, but I can’t diagnose at second hand. I’ll have to talk to him.”
“But what do you think it is?” she persisted.

===Chinese===
“可您到底有数吗？”
“我自然有数，可隔着一层没法下诊断。我得跟他谈谈。”
“可您觉得它到底是什么？”她追问。

===Original===
“I think it’s a fixation of an idea gained in childhood, Honey. I had a patient once﻿—” He smiled at the reminiscence﻿—“who had a fixed delusion of that sort. He was perfectly rational on every point save one﻿—he believed that a pig with a pink ribbon was following him everywhere! Down town, into elevators and offices, home to bed﻿—everywhere he went this pink-ribboned prize porker pursued him!”
“And did you cure him?”
“Well, he recovered,” said the Doctor non-committally. “We got rid of the pig. And it might be something of that nature that’s troubling your boyfriend. Your description doesn’t sound like a praecox or a manic depressive, as I thought originally.”

===Chinese===
“我看是童年得下的一个观念固着，孩子。我从前进过一个病人——”他想起往事，笑了——“就有这么一种固定妄想。他在一切事情上都再理智不过，只有一条例外——他坚信一头系粉红缎带的猪到处跟着他！进城、进电梯进办公室、回家上床——他走到哪儿，这头系粉红缎带的得奖肥猪就跟到哪儿！”
“那您治好他了吗？”
“嗯，他好了。”医生不置可否地说。“我们把那头猪打发掉了。纠缠你那位男朋友的，也许正是那一类东西。照你的描述，不像我起初想的那样，是早发性痴呆或者躁郁症。”

===Original===
“Oh,” said Pat abruptly. “I forgot. He went to a doctor in New York, a very great doctor.”
“Muenster?”
“He didn’t say whom. But this doctor studied him a long time, and finally came out with this fixed idea theory of yours. Only he couldn’t cure him.”

===Chinese===
“哦，”帕特忽然说，“我忘了。他在纽约看过一位医生，一位非常了不起的医生。”
“明斯特？”
“他没说是谁。只是那位医生研究了他很久，最后得出的正是您这套固定观念的理论。只是他治不好他。”

===Original===
“Um.” Horker grunted thoughtfully.
“Do fixed ideas do things like that to people?” queried the girl. “Things like the pig and what happened to Nick?”
“They might.”

===Chinese===
“唔。”霍克若有所思地哼了一声。
“固定观念能把人弄成那样吗？”姑娘问，“弄出那头猪那样的、还有发生在尼克身上的那样的事？”
“可能。”

===Original===
“Then they’re devils!” she announced with an air of finality. “They’re just your scientific jargon for exactly what Magda means when she says a person’s possessed by a devil. So I’m right anyway!”
“That’s good orthodox theology, Pat,” chuckled the Doctor. “We’ll try a little exorcism on your devil, then.” He rose to his feet. “Bring your boyfriend around, will you?”
“Oh, Dr. Carl!” she cried. “He’s leaving! I’ll have to call him tonight!”

===Chinese===
“那它们就是魔鬼！”她一锤定音似的宣称，“你们那套科学行话，说的正是玛格达讲一个人被魔鬼附体时的那码事。反正我是对的！”
“这可是正统的神学，帕特，”医生轻笑道，“那我们就对你的魔鬼来一场小小的驱魔吧。”他站起身。“把你那位男朋友带来，行吗？”
“哦，卡尔医生！”她叫道，“他要走了！我今晚就得给他打电话！”

===Original===
“Not tonight, Honey. Mueller would let me know if anything of that sort were happening. Tomorrow’s time enough.”
The girl stood erect, mounting to the top step to bring her head level with the Doctor’s. She threw her arms about him, burying her face in his massive shoulder.
“Dr. Carl,” she murmured, “I’m a nasty, ill-tempered, vicious little shrew, and I’m sorry, and I apologize. You know I’m crazy about you, and,” she whispered in his ear, “so’s Mother!”

===Chinese===
“今晚不必，孩子。真要有那种事，缪勒会告诉我的。明天也来得及。”
姑娘站直身子，跨上最高一级台阶，好让自己的头和医生齐平。她张开双臂搂住他，把脸埋进他厚实的肩膀。
“卡尔医生，”她喃喃道，“我是个刁蛮、坏脾气、凶巴巴的小泼妇，对不起，我赔不是。您知道我迷您迷得不行，还有，”她凑着他耳朵悄声说，“妈妈也一样！”

## XI Wreckage / 十一 残骸
===Original===
Pat opened her eyes reluctantly, with the impression that something unpleasant awaited her return to full consciousness. Something, as yet she could not recall just what, had happened to her; she was not even sure where she was awakening.
However, her eyes surveyed her own familiar room; there opposite the bed grinned the jade Buddha on his stand on the mantel﻿—the one that Nick had﻿—Nick! A mass of troubled, terrible recollections thrust themselves suddenly into consciousness. She visioned a medley of disturbing pictures, as yet disconnected, unassorted, but waiting only the return of complete wakefulness. And she realized abruptly that her head ached miserably, that her mouth was parched, that twinges of pain were making themselves evident in various portions of her anatomy. She turned her head and caught a glimpse of a figure at the bedside; her startled glance revealed Dr. Horker, sitting quietly watching her.

===Chinese===
帕特不情愿地睁开眼睛，隐隐觉得有什么不愉快的事正等着她完全恢复知觉。有什么事——她一时还想不起究竟是什么——落到了她头上；她甚至拿不准自己这是在哪儿醒来的。
不过，她的双眼环顾的仍是自己那间熟悉的屋子；床对面，壁炉架的座子上，那尊玉佛正咧嘴而笑——正是尼克的那一尊——尼克！一大团纷乱可怕的回忆猛地挤进意识。她眼前闪过一堆扰人的画面，尚且东零西碎、未经归整，只等她完全醒来。她这才骤然发觉，自己的头疼得厉害，口干舌燥，身上好些部位都一阵阵显出痛来。她转过头，瞥见床边有个人影；她惊惶的一瞥认出是霍克医生，正静静坐着望她。

===Original===
“Hello, Doctor,” she said, wincing as her smile brought a sharp pain from her lips. “Or should I say, Good morning, Judge?”
“Pat!” he rumbled, his growling tones oddly gentle. “Little Pat! How do you feel, child?”
“Fair,” she said. “Just fair. Dr. Carl, what happened to me last night? I can’t seem to remember﻿—Oh!”

===Chinese===
“你好，医生，”她说，一笑牵动了嘴唇，疼得她直皱眉。“还是我该道一声：早安，法官大人？”
“帕特！”他瓮声唤道，那低沉的嗓音出奇地温和。“小帕特！觉得怎么样，孩子？”
“还行，”她说，“只是还行。卡尔医生，昨晚我出了什么事？我好像记不起来了——噢！”

===Original===
A flash of recollection pierced the obscure muddle. She remembered now﻿—not all of the events of that ghastly evening, but enough. Too much!
“Oh!” she murmured faintly. “Oh, Dr. Carl!”
“Yes,” he nodded. “ ‘Oh!’﻿—and would you mind very much telling me what that ‘Oh’ of yours implies?”

===Chinese===
一星回忆的闪光刺穿了那团混沌。她现在记起来了——不是那个可怕夜晚的全部事件，但够了。太多了！
“噢！”她微弱地喃喃道，“噢，卡尔医生！”
“是啊，”他点点头，“‘噢！’——那能不能劳驾告诉我，你这声‘噢’是什么意思？”

===Original===
“Why﻿—” She paused shuddering, as one by one the events of that sequence of horrors reassembled themselves. “Yes, I’d mind very much,” she continued. “It was nothing﻿—” She turned to him abruptly. “Oh, it was, though, Dr. Carl! It was horrible, unspeakable, incomprehensible!﻿—But I can’t talk about it! I can’t!”
“Perhaps you’re right,” said the Doctor mildly. “Don’t you really want to discuss it?”
“I do want to,” admitted the girl after a moment’s reflection. “I want to﻿—but I can’t. I’m afraid to think of all of it.”

===Chinese===
“这个——”她打了个寒噤，顿住了，那连串恐怖的事件正一件接一件自行拼拢。“是啊，我很介意，”她接着说，“那没什么——”她猛地转向他。“不，有事，卡尔医生！太可怕了，说不出口，没法理解！——可我没法谈它！没法！”
“也许你是对的，”医生温和地说，“你真的不想谈谈它吗？”
“我是想谈的，”姑娘沉吟片刻，承认道，“我想谈——可谈不了。我不敢把整件事想全。”

===Original===
“But what in Heaven’s name did you do?”
“We just started out to go dancing,” she said hesitatingly. “Then, on the way to town, Nick﻿—changed. He said someone was following us.”

===Chinese===
“可你们到底干了些什么，看在老天的份上？”
“我们本来只是出门去跳舞的，”她迟疑地说，“后来，在进城的路上，尼克——变了。他说有人跟踪我们。”

===Original===
“Someone was,” said Horker. “*I* was, with Mueller. That Nick of yours has the Devil’s own cleverness!”
“Yes,” the girl echoed soberly. “The Devil’s own!﻿—Who’s Mueller, Dr. Carl?”
“He’s a plainclothes man, friend of mine. I treated him once. What do you mean by changed?”

===Chinese===
“是有人，”霍克说，“*我*就在跟踪，带着缪勒。你那个尼克，机灵得简直像魔鬼！”
“是啊，”姑娘正色应道，“简直像魔鬼！——缪勒是谁，卡尔医生？”
“他是个便衣，我的朋友。我给他治过一回病。你说‘变了’是什么意思？”

===Original===
“His eyes,” she said. “And his mouth. His eyes got reddish and terrible, and his mouth got straight and grim. And his voice turned sort of﻿—harsh.”
“Ever happen before, that you know of?”
“Once. When﻿—” She paused.

===Chinese===
“他的眼睛，”她说，“还有他的嘴。他的眼睛变得发红、可怕，嘴巴绷得又直又狠。嗓音也变得有点儿——刺耳。”
“据你所知，以前发生过这种事吗？”
“有过一回。那时——”她顿住了。

===Original===
“Yes. Last Wednesday night, when you came over to ask those questions about pure science. What happened then?”
“We went to a place to dance.”
“And that’s the reason, I suppose,” rumbled the Doctor sardonically, “that I found you wandering about the streets in a tablecloth, step-ins, and a pair of hose! That’s why I found you on the verge of passing out from rotten liquor, and looking like the loser of a battle with an airplane propellor! What happened to your face?”

===Chinese===
“对。上星期三晚上，你跑来问那些纯科学问题的时候。那回出了什么事？”
“我们去了一个地方跳舞。”
“我猜，就因为这个，”医生讥诮地咕噜道，“我才撞见你裹着一块桌布、穿着内裤和一双长袜在街上乱晃！就因为这个，我才撞见你喝了劣质烈酒、眼看就要昏过去，那副模样活像跟飞机螺旋桨打了一仗还输了的！你的脸是怎么回事？”

===Original===
“My face? What’s wrong with it?”
The Doctor rose from his chair and seized the hand-mirror from her dressing table.
“Look at it!” he commanded, passing her the glass.
Pat gazed incredulously at the reflection the surface presented; a dark bruise colored her cheek, her lips were swollen and discolored, and her chin bore a jagged scratch. She stared at the injuries in horror.

===Chinese===
“我的脸？怎么了？”
医生从椅子里起身，从她的梳妆台上抓起那面手镜。
“你自己瞧！”他吩咐道，把镜子递给她。
帕特难以置信地瞪着镜面映出的影像；一块乌青染着她的脸颊，嘴唇肿起、变了色，下巴上一道参差的划痕。她惊恐地盯着这些伤。

===Original===
“Your knees are skinned, too,” said Horker. “Both of them.”
Pat slipped one pajamaed limb from the covers, drawing the pants-leg up for inspection. She gasped in startled fright at the great red stain on her knee.
“That’s mercurochrome,” said the Doctor. “I put it there.”

===Chinese===
“你的膝盖也擦破了，”霍克说，“两只都破了。”
帕特从被子里抽出一条穿着睡衣裤的腿，撩起裤腿来验看。膝盖上那一大块红痕，吓得她倒吸一口凉气。
“那是红药水，”医生说，“我给你涂的。”

===Original===
“*You* put it there. How did I get home last night, Dr. Carl? How did I get to bed?”
“I’m responsible for that, too. I put you to bed.” He leaned forward. “Listen, child﻿—your mother knows nothing about this as yet. She wasn’t home when I brought you in, and she’s not awake yet this morning. We’ll tell her you had an automobile accident; explain away those bruises.﻿—And now, how did you get them?”
“I fell, I guess. Two or three times.”
“That bruise on your cheek isn’t from falling.”

===Chinese===
“*你*涂的。我昨晚是怎么到家的，卡尔医生？我是怎么上的床？”
“这也归我负责。是我把你放上床的。”他俯身向前。“听着，孩子——这事你母亲还一无所知。我抱你进来的时候她不在家，今天早上她还没醒。我们就说你出了场车祸；把那些乌青搪塞过去。——现在说吧，你是怎么弄上它们的？”
“摔的吧，我猜。摔了两三回。”
“你脸颊上那块乌青可不是摔出来的。”

===Original===
The girl shuddered. Now in the calm light of morning, the events of last night seemed doubly horrible; she doubted her ability to believe them, so incredible did they seem. She was at a loss to explain even her own actions, and those of Nicholas Devine were simply beyond comprehension, a chapter from some dark and blasphemous book of ancient times﻿—the Kabbala or the Necronomicon.
“What happened, Pat?” queried the Doctor gently. “Tell me,” he urged her.
“I﻿—can’t explain it,” she said doubtfully. “He took me to that place, but drinking the liquor was my own fault. I did it out of spite because I saw he didn’t﻿—care for me. And then﻿—” She fell silent.

===Chinese===
姑娘打了个寒噤。此刻在清晨平静的光线里，昨晚的事件显得加倍可怕；她怀疑自己有没有本事相信它们，它们显得那么难以置信。她连自己的行为都解释不清，而尼古拉斯·德文的所作所为则全然无法理解，像出自某部上古黑暗渎神之书的一章——《卡巴拉》或者《死灵之书》。
“到底出了什么事，帕特？”医生温和地问。“告诉我吧。”他催她。
“我——说不清楚，”她迟疑地说，“是他带我去的那家店，可喝酒是我自己的错。我是赌气才喝的，因为我看出他不——不把我放在心上。后来——”她不说了。

===Original===
“Yes? And then?”
“Well﻿—he began to talk about the beauty of evil, the delights of evil, and his eyes glared at me, and﻿—I don’t understand it at all, Dr. Carl, but all of a sudden I was﻿—yielding. Do you see?”
“I see,” he said gently, soberly.

===Chinese===
“是吗？后来呢？”
“嗯——他开始大谈恶之美、恶之乐，他的眼睛瞪着我，还有——我一点儿也不明白，卡尔医生，可突然之间我就——顺从了。你明白吗？”
“我明白。”他温和而郑重地说。

===Original===
“Suddenly I seemed to comprehend what he meant﻿—all that about the supreme pleasure of evil. And I was sort of﻿—swept away. The dress﻿—was his fault, but I﻿—somehow I’d lost the power to resist. I guess I was drunk.”
“And the bruises? And your cut lips?” queried the Doctor grimly.
“Yes,” she said in a low voice. “He﻿—struck me. After a while I didn’t care. He could have﻿—would have done other things, only we were interrupted, and had to leave. And that’s all, Dr. Carl.”
“Isn’t that enough?” he groaned. “Pat, I should have killed the fiend there!”

===Chinese===
“我好像突然懂了他的意思——关于恶的至高快感的那一整套。我有点儿——被卷走了。衣服——是他的错，可我——不知怎么就失去了抵抗的力气。我估摸着自己是醉了。”
“那乌青呢？还有你裂开的嘴唇呢？”医生冷冷地问。
“是的，”她低声说，“他——打了我。过了一阵我就不在乎了。他本来还可以——本来还要做别的事，只是我们被人打断了，只好离开。就这些，卡尔医生。”
“这还不够吗？”他呻吟道，“帕特，我真该当场宰了那个恶魔！”

===Original===
“I’m glad you didn’t.”
“Do you mean to say you’d care?”
“I﻿—don’t know.”
“Are you intimating that you still love him?”
“No,” she said thoughtfully. “No, I don’t love him, but﻿—Dr. Carl, there’s something inexplicable about this. There’s something I don’t understand, but I’m certain of one thing!”

===Chinese===
“幸亏你没有。”
“你是说，你还会在乎？”
“我——不知道。”
“你这是在暗示，你还爱着他？”
“不，”她若有所思地说，“不，我不爱他了，可是——卡尔医生，这件事里有个说不通的地方。有我不明白的东西，可有一件事我敢肯定！”

===Original===
“What’s that?”
“That it wasn’t Nick﻿—not *my* Nick﻿—who did those things to me last night. It wasn’t, Dr. Carl!”
“Pat, you’re being a fool!”
“I know it. But I’m sure of it, Dr. Carl. I *know* Nick; I loved him, and I know he couldn’t have done﻿—that. Not the same gentle Nick that I had to beg to kiss me!”

===Chinese===
“哪件事？”
“昨晚对我做那些事的，不是尼克——不是*我的*尼克——不是他，卡尔医生！”
“帕特，你在犯傻！”
“我知道。可我敢肯定，卡尔医生。我*了解*尼克；我爱过他，我知道他做不出——那种事。同一个温柔的尼克，我求他他才肯吻我！”

===Original===
“Pat,” said the Doctor gently, “I’m a psychiatrist; it’s my business to know all the rottenness that can hide in a human being. My office is the scene of a parade of misfits, failures, potential criminals, lunatics, and mental incompetents. It’s a nasty, bitter side I see of life, but I know that side﻿—and I tell you this fellow is dangerous!”
“Do you understand this, Dr. Carl?”
He reached over, taking her hand in his great palm with its long, curious delicate fingers. “I have my theory, Pat. The man’s a sadist, a lover of cruelty, and there’s enough masochism in any woman to make him terribly dangerous. I want your promise.”

===Chinese===
“帕特，”医生温和地说，“我是精神病医生；一个人身上可能藏着的全部污浊，摸清它们正是我的本行。我的诊室就是一场游行的现场：格格不入者、失败者、潜在的罪犯、疯子和心智不健全者鱼贯而过。我看到的是生活龌龊而辛酸的一面，可这一面我了解——我告诉你，这家伙危险！”
“这个你明白吗，卡尔医生？”
他伸手过来，把她的手握进那只大手——掌上手指修长，灵巧得出奇。“我有我的一套理论，帕特。这男人是个施虐狂，嗜好残忍，而随便哪个女人身上那点受虐倾向，都足够让他危险透顶。我要你一句承诺。”

===Original===
“About what?”
“I want you to promise never to see him again.”
The girl turned serious eyes on his face; he noted with a shock of sympathy that they were filled with tears.

===Chinese===
“关于什么？”
“我要你答应，永远不再见他。”
姑娘把严肃的目光投向他的脸；他觉察到那双眼睛已噙满泪水，不禁恻然一惊。

===Original===
“You warned me I’d get burned playing with fire,” she said. “You did, didn’t you?”
“I’m an old fool, Honey. If I’d believed my own advice, I’d have seen that this never happened to you.” He patted her hand. “Have I your promise?”
She averted her eyes. “Yes,” she murmured. He winced as he perceived that the tears were on her cheeks.

===Chinese===
“你警告过我，玩火会烧了自己，”她说，“你警告过的，不是吗？”
“我是个老糊涂，亲爱的。要是我信了自己的忠告，我就会确保这种事绝不出在你身上。”他拍拍她的手。“你答应我了？”
她移开目光。“嗯。”她低声道。他看见泪水已淌上她的脸颊，不由得瑟缩了一下。

===Original===
“So!” he said, rising. “The patient can get out of bed when she feels like it﻿—and don’t forget that little fib we’ve arranged for your mother’s peace of mind.”
She stared up at him, still clinging to his hand.
“Dr. Carl,” she said, “are you sure﻿—quite sure﻿—you’re right about him? Couldn’t there be a chance that you’re mistaken﻿—that it’s something your psychiatry has overlooked or never heard of?”

===Chinese===
“好啦！”他说着起身，“病人想下床就下床吧——还有，别忘了咱们编好让你母亲安心的那个小瞎话。”
她仰头望着他，仍攥着他的手不放。
“卡尔医生，”她说，“你肯定吗——十分肯定——你对他的看法是对的？会不会有可能你弄错了——会不会那是你的精神病学漏掉了、或者从来没听说过的东西？”

===Original===
“Small chance, Pat dear.”
“But a chance?”
“Well, neither I nor any reputable medic claims to know everything, and the human mind’s a subtle sort of thing.”

===Chinese===
“机会很小，亲爱的帕特。”
“可毕竟有机会？”
“这个嘛，不论我还是哪个体面的医生，都不敢自称无所不知，而人的心智，是种微妙的东西。”
